# 03 — Target Architecture and Roadmap (Phase 4)

**Repo:** `main` @ `1e04694` (2026-09-05) · **Live project:** `djpgvcvhvgrzgaziruze`, read 2026-09-06 · **Companions:** [00-SYSTEM-INVENTORY.md](00-SYSTEM-INVENTORY.md) (what exists) · [01-ARCHITECTURE-REVIEW.md](01-ARCHITECTURE-REVIEW.md) (the assessment, grade D+, and the strangler verdict) · [02-CAPABILITY-GAP-MATRIX.md](02-CAPABILITY-GAP-MATRIX.md) (what each capability needs) · [04-OPEN-QUESTIONS.md](04-OPEN-QUESTIONS.md) (what the plan needs from you) · [findings.json](findings.json)

**What this document is.** The architecture this system should converge on, under the constraints you set: solo owner, no full-time developer, AI-assisted development, Supabase stays, the business keeps running, simple to operate today, and structurally able to carry 100+ employees without a re-architecture. Three architects designed it independently from different angles — domain-model-first, automation-backbone-first, and simplest-thing-a-solo-owner-can-run — each was critiqued line by line against your brief, and the results were reconciled against a binding decision record and then checked for internal contradictions.

**How to read it.** Part 0 is the decision record: every contested choice, what was rejected, the honest ceiling, and the places where the choice is left to you. Parts 1 through 8 are the design. Every structural decision states the scale it holds to and what would force a change; where a "holds to 100+" claim was challenged in review, the corrected ceiling is the one printed here. Assumptions carry tags `[A-<part>.<n>]` and are consolidated in [04-OPEN-QUESTIONS.md](04-OPEN-QUESTIONS.md). Citations are `path:line` at `main@1e04694`; "(live)" marks a fact read from production. Cross-references read "Decision record §n" for Part 0 and "§2.4" for the numbered parts.

**Contents**

| Part | What it covers |
|---|---|
| 0 | Decision record — the binding choices, what was rejected, ceilings, and your open options |
| 1 | Target structure — monorepo layout, packages, one data-access pattern, one design system, build and environments |
| 2 | The automation layer — the propose/approve/execute/record contract, the operation registry, the permission matrix, where agent code runs, model routing, observability |
| 3 | Target domain model — the insurance-restoration vocabulary as tables; state vs event; what carries history; what maps from today's blob |
| 4 | End-to-end flow — dry job → drafted invoice → approval → issued document → QuickBooks → collections, step by step |
| 5 | ADRs — service boundary, data access, proposals, permissions, events, job runner, documents, agent runtime, model routing, blob coexistence, tenancy, price sources, migrations, build, integrations |
| 6 | Document pipeline and on-device capture |
| 7 | Phased roadmap (E0 through P7), data migration, cutover criteria, and the first shipped automation |
| 8 | Guardrails against re-fragmentation — CLAUDE.md, lint and CI, folder rules, PR checklist, five runbooks, migration discipline |

# Part 0 — Decision record

**Status: binding.** Built on `docs/architecture/00-SYSTEM-INVENTORY.md`, the live facts read 2026-09-06, the three design attempts with their critiques, and the Phase 3 verdict (unanimous D+, path = strangler). Where an attempt and this record disagree, this record wins. Citations are `path:line` at `main@1e04694`; **(live)** = production; **[A-0.n]** = a design assumption of this record (each section numbers its own as `[A-<section>.<n>]`). Cross-references: subsections of this record are cited as "Decision record §n"; the eight sections that follow are cited "§1"…"§8" with their subsection numbers ("§2.4").

### Decision record §0 — The path

**Strangle the backend, not the app.** Typed tables and one operations door are built inside the same Supabase project beside the running apps; the office UI is strangled module by module onto them; the field PWA is wrapped, never rewritten.

Kept byte-for-byte: `push_project` (`supabase/migrations/241_merge_delete_tombstones.sql:193-282`), `merge_project_blobs` (`243_losstypes_merge_union.sql:13-144`), `apps/field/js/merge.js`, `sync.js`, `media.js`, the tombstone and self-echo rules; the pure engines (`apps/board/js/schedule.js`, `fincalc.js`, `dryingcalc.js`, `completeness.js`, `dryingwatch.js`, `buildwatch.js`, `phasematch.ts`, `emailmatch.ts`, `approve.ts`, `payments.ts`, `guards.ts`); the web agent's RPC-only, reserve-before-spend posture (`227_web_receptionist.sql:147-211`), generalised. Ported as templates plus written spec, not as code: the 22 `forms.js` renderers, the letterhead and packet conventions; `print.css` ports nearly verbatim into `packages/documents` (Decision record §8). Rejected: clean-sheet (22 renderers and a proven offline engine are expensive to relearn; the March→June rewrite already left ~20 dead tables); evolve-in-place as the primary path (a blob system of record and browser-executed writes cannot become a headless backbone without a parallel structure — so the parallel structure is built on purpose, inside the same project, and only the office UI and documents are strangled).

### Decision record §1 — Runtime split

| Tier | Runs | Why | Ceiling |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Postgres** — SECURITY DEFINER functions `op_<name>` behind the gateway | every short, DB-only operation: `proposal.approve/decline` (`op_proposal_approve`, `op_proposal_decline`), `job.set_stage`, `line_item.add/update`, `document.issue` (number + version row; render queued), `equipment.place/remove`, `reading.record`, `schedule.apply`, `submittal.record`, `emit_event`, `enqueue`, `ai_reserve/settle` | transactional; the event lands in the same transaction; the office keeps working when Fly is down. The pattern exists: `coordination_job_patch` (`246_lead_triage_grant.sql:28-78`), `push_project`, `web_*` (`227`). A Deno dispatcher cannot open a multi-statement transaction over PostgREST — the flaw the critiques found in the edge-only design | holds to 100+; the forcing condition is a rule that is a bad fit for plpgsql — estimating math stays in `packages/domain` and runs in the worker, never rewritten in SQL |
| **Deno edge** — one `roybal-ops` dispatcher plus trust-level functions (Decision record §2) | synchronous work that needs a secret and a waiting human: assistant chat turns (keep the 2-round cap — the comment at `roybal-ai-office/index.ts:1527`, the loop guard at `:1586`), voice extraction, OAuth code exchange, credential probes | request-scoped; no Chromium, no long loops; interactive chat must not ride a queue | per-invocation wall clock |
| **Node worker** `roybal-worker` on Fly — a second app, never co-hosted with `roybal-phone` | queue consumer, agent loops (Claude Agent SDK), the MCP server (streamable HTTP only), PDF render, integration pulls (QB Time, Gmail, QBO payments), blob→row projection, outbox delivery | long/heavy work; the phone agent proves Fly is operable here | one machine, one region, no HA — an **availability** ceiling, not throughput |

The facts that fix the shape: the phone agent runs `shared-cpu-1x`, `memory = "256mb"`, `min_machines_running = 1` (`services/phone-agent/fly.toml:21-23`) with per-caller limits in a process `Map` (`tools.mjs:19-27`); headless Chromium needs 300–500 MB alone. So: the worker machine is **≥1 GB** [A-0.1], rendering runs in an **isolated child process with its own memory cap** (restart on OOM, one render at a time), and the ConversationRelay socket stays on its own machine so a 20-page packet can never drop a live call. The worker holds nothing that matters in memory (leases, reservations, sessions live in Postgres); `fly scale count 2` is true for the queue poller only — MCP and Agent SDK sessions are per-connection stateful and a second machine needs sticky routing. **If the worker is down:** approvals still record — in the inbox *and* by SMS, because `roybal-webhooks` calls `op_proposal_approve`/`op_proposal_decline` directly (Decision record §2) — sends and renders queue, crews sync, the office keeps writing; only the execution of an approved op whose runtime is `worker` waits. The owner learns the worker is down from the heartbeat check (Decision record §2), never from an outbox row the absent worker would have to deliver.

### Decision record §2 — Edge functions by trust level; auth conventions; worker and MCP identity

Fourteen functions become five, grouped by who may call them:

| Trust level | Function | Caller | Auth |
|---|---|---|---|
| A — authenticated app | `roybal-ops` | the field and office browsers (`apps/field`; `apps/office` — today's `apps/admin` + `apps/board` [A-0.10]) | user JWT → `current_principal()` once; absorbs ai-office, ai-ingest, ai-narrative, the browser halves of the gmail/qbo/qb-time proxies; `sendSms` becomes an outbox row |
| B — signed vendor webhooks | `roybal-webhooks` | Twilio voice/SMS inbound + **status callback** (absent today; 174/175 outbound rows frozen `queued` **(live)**; `supabase/config.toml:53-54` claims a handler); pg_cron's dead-worker check on `/alert` | HMAC; inserts events/queue rows, **and calls exactly two SQL ops** — `op_proposal_approve` and `op_proposal_decline` — as the principal resolved from the sender's number (allowlisted approvers only), so the owner's "YES 12" records its approval without the worker; `/alert` takes a service-secret header and sends one owner SMS synchronously through the Twilio adapter |
| C — anonymous, self-protecting | `roybal-web-agent`, `roybal-lead` | www | unchanged: `verify_jwt=false`, DB-enforced caps, RPC-only |
| D — portal gateway | `roybal-portal` | customers, adjusters | share token / `contact_sessions`; kept separate because its blast radius is customer data |

Deleted at E0: `magicplan-proxy` (no callers, no auth, a paid key, `magicplan-proxy/index.ts:44-70`) and `magicplan-webhook`. **pg_cron never calls HTTP again — it inserts `jobs_queue` rows — with one documented exception, the dead-worker alert:** a SQL job every 5 minutes reads `worker_heartbeats`; when the newest row is older than 10 minutes it calls `net.http_post` to `roybal-webhooks` `/alert` (service-secret header read from `vault.decrypted_secrets`), and the function texts the owner synchronously through the Twilio adapter. The outbox is not used for this alert, because the outbox is delivered by the worker whose absence is being reported. With every other cron job SQL-only, `x-cron-secret` and the publishable key as a credential (literal in the live `cron.job` rows) disappear. The five caller-identity conventions (user JWT; service key as bearer; anon key + cron secret; machine-user password grant; publishable key + cron secret) collapse to **two**: a user JWT resolved by `current_principal()`, and the service role used only from inside a trusted runtime (webhooks function, worker) with an explicit `p_principal_id`.

**Worker identity.** The worker holds the service-role key in Fly secrets but executes every op as a named machine principal (`agents` row); `op_*` functions accept a service-role caller only when a principal is supplied and `agent_authority` grants the capability — deny by default, propose-only. The password-grant machine users (`services/phone-agent/supa.mjs:30-33`; `roybal-brief/index.ts:62-71`) remain for the phone agent until it imports packages; the 26 email-string fences (`204`, `205`) are replaced by role/agent predicates in the migration that introduces `agents`.

**MCP principal.** The worker serves MCP over **streamable HTTP only**; Claude Code on a laptop connects with a bearer token minted in the admin (`api_tokens`: principal_id, scopes, expiry, hash) — directly, or through a 10-line local stdio shim that forwards to that endpoint — never a stdio server "in the worker". A session acts **as the human who minted it** for reads and is capped at **propose** for every write action type regardless of that human's role — an MCP session is an agent harness, and the audit row says "proposed via mcp by <person>". Approval happens in the inbox or by SMS; MCP callers never hold approve or execute [A-0.3].

### Decision record §3 — Operation registry

One file per operation in `packages/domain/ops/<area>.<verb>.ts`:

```
defineOp({ name:"invoice.add_line", version:1,
  actionType:"money",            // read|capture|job|money|comms|external|schedule|admin
  description, input:<JSON Schema, additionalProperties:false>, output:<JSON Schema>,
  runtime:"sql"|"edge"|"worker",
  idempotency:(i)=>`invoice.add_line:${i.documentId}:${i.clientLineId}`,
  approval:{ default:"office", amountField:"total_usd" },
  emits:["line_item.added"], handler })
```

JSON Schema is the source of truth (not zod): it feeds Anthropic `tools[]` with `strict:true` and MCP verbatim — the shape the six read tools already use (`roybal-ai-office/personas.ts:78-146`); the 17 prose `ACTION_DEFS` (`:246-362`) transcribe one-to-one. One definition emits: `operation_catalog` rows (published by CI; the DB never invents an op), Anthropic tools (read ops as-is, writes only as `propose_<name>`) and the MCP list — both generated from the registry by `packages/ai` — a typed browser client in `packages/db`, the dispatcher and worker route tables, `role_permissions` seed rows and `docs/PERMISSIONS.md`. `runtime:"sql"` bodies live beside the definition and ship by migration; CI diffs the catalog against deployed signatures.

**Validation at three doors** with one schema: proposal insert (`op_propose`, `pg_jsonschema` [A-0.2]), execution (handler re-validates), tool call (SDK validates). **Versioning:** `name@version` on every proposal and event; additive changes do not bump, breaking ones do; an old handler stays until no open proposal references it. **Autonomy tiers** per (operation, principal): `propose_only` → `auto_below_threshold` → `auto`, held in `agent_authority` rows with conditions, widened only by the owner's `agent_authority.grant` op (an event). **Idempotency key:** computed by the registry from input; `unique` on `proposals`, `events`, `outbox`, `jobs_queue`; offline capture ops use a client uuid. **Separation of duties:** for `money`/`external`/`comms` action types `op_proposal_approve` refuses `approved_by = proposed_by`; a chip on a non-money op is a proposal the signed-in proposer may self-approve when they hold execute; agents never self-approve; the owner may override with a recorded reason. **Amount thresholds** are `approval_policies(action_type, division_id, max_amount_usd, approver_role)` rows — office approves `invoice.issue` up to a threshold the owner sets [A-0.3], owner above. **Auto-approval records its principal:** `approved_by_kind='policy'`, `approved_by_ref = approval_policies.id` — "who approved" is never empty.

Rejected: a generic `commit_operation(changes[])` RPC — an unguarded write door callable by any authenticated user unless SQL knows op→tables, which needs the per-op functions anyway.

### Decision record §4 — Proposals, events, queue, outbox

| Table | State machine | Rules |
|---|---|---|
| `proposals` (supersedes `pending_actions`, `210_pending_actions.sql:24-38`) | proposed → approved \| declined \| expired \| superseded; approved → executing → executed \| failed | `input` immutable after insert (trigger); `edited_params` separate; every transition emits an event; carries `division_id`, `job_id`, `assigned_role`, `assignee_id`, `escalate_after`; a schedule proposal is ONE row whose input holds N assignments, applied atomically. Approval channels: inbox tap, chip, policy, and SMS `YES n` — the last handled synchronously by `roybal-webhooks` calling `op_proposal_approve`/`op_proposal_decline` (Decision record §2), so approval never waits for the worker |
| `jobs_queue` | queued → leased → done \| failed (retry, `run_after` backoff) \| dead (attempts ≥ `max_attempts`, default 5) | claim = `UPDATE … WHERE id = (SELECT … FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED) SET locked_by, lease_until RETURNING` — the lease is a column, so it outlives the claiming transaction; per-kind lease (5 min default, 10 render, 30 agent run); heartbeat extends; a sweeper requeues expired leases; partition-free, indexed on `(status, run_after)`, done rows purged daily; dead rows text the owner via the outbox with a 24 h resend guard (a dead *worker* is reported by the heartbeat check of Decision record §2, not by the outbox) |
| `outbox` | pending → sending → sent → delivered \| failed (retry) \| dead | one row per external side effect, `idempotency_key unique`, `provider_id`; the Twilio callback settles `delivered`; the SMS cap counts `delivered` |
| `events` | append-only | no UPDATE/DELETE for any role (trigger raises); insert only via `emit_event()`; `idempotency_key` unique where not null; `principal`, `kind`, `aggregate`, `job_id`, `claim_id`, `proposal_id`, `correlation_id` |

**Expiry sweep** every 5 minutes is what un-jams today's spine: three `boardEdit` rows `pending` since 2026-07-25, expired 2026-07-26 **(live)**, counted forever against `MAX_LIVE_PROPOSALS = 3` (`qb-time-proxy/index.ts:285,499`). **Inbox scoping:** mine (assignee) ∪ my role in my division ∪ unassigned in my division; escalation reassigns to the owner and texts once. Holds to several office staff across divisions; cross-department SLAs force a workflow engine *on top of* these tables, never instead.

**Retention, honestly.** The live tier is a 1 GB Micro with two disk-IO incidents (Jul 24, Aug 17). `events` stays one heap with a monthly index to low millions; `jobs_queue` done rows purge daily (the projection kind alone can write ~430k rows a day at 100+); `outbox` after 90 days (`sms_messages` stays the SMS log); `capture_events` `emailPull` rows (3,500 of 4,504 **(live)**) after 30 days, and the table itself is the legacy ledger — read-only after P3, migrated into `captures` + `events` in P5 (Decision record §14); `ai_usage` and `events` are kept. **Month partitioning of `events` is a P5 item, not "later":** at 100+ people the table reaches low millions within about twelve months, so the partition arrives with the headcount; it is still deliberately absent from P1 — an operational lift a solo owner has not done — and it comes earlier if the compute upgrade (Decision record §11) fires first.

**Blob pushes do not emit semantic events.** `push_project` is unchanged; `blob_history` (`215`) is the pre-image; the projection job emits nothing and upserts rows stamped `origin='blob', blob_rev`. Semantic events come only from operations. The arithmetic: at ~300 active jobs on the 45 s sync cycle (`sync.js:623`) pushes could reach ~500k/day if every cycle pushed — an event per push would drown the audit table, and a projection per push would drown the worker.

**Projection debounce:** an AFTER UPDATE trigger on `field_projects` inserts `jobs_queue(kind='job.project', idempotency_key='job.project:'||id, run_after=now()+60s)` with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` while a row for that job is queued or leased — at most one projection per job per minute (≈430k/day worst case at 300 jobs, which is why the queue is partition-free, indexed on `(status, run_after)` and purged daily); office reads of capture data are eventually consistent (about 1–2 minutes).

### Decision record §5 — Permission model

Roles `owner, office, crew_lead, crew, viewer, agent`; external principals **customer** (portal token / `contact_sessions`) and **adjuster** (`photo_shares` token) reach only the portal gateway. Mapping: `tech→crew`, machine viewers→`agent` rows, `office→office` (the value exists since `216_individual_logins.sql:50`; no live row). The two live `admin` profiles are **not** both mapped to owner blindly: the migration maps `branden@…` to `owner` and the second admin to `office` by default, and the owner confirms the pair in the admin UI before the role claim is armed (a confirmation item in 04).

**Role enum migration without breaking sync.** `_sync_guard` admits `role in ('tech','admin','office')` (`218_sync_rpcs_and_build_gate.sql:67-69`, redefined `226_sync_client_telemetry.sql:45-49`) and `push_project` calls it on every push. Sequence: migration N adds the enum values (`ALTER TYPE … ADD VALUE` cannot be used in its own transaction); migration N+1, in one transaction, redefines `_sync_guard` and every other role gate — `coordination_job_patch` (`246:47-52`), `contact_merge`, `contact_resolve`, `contact_mark_review_asked`, `restore_photo`, the `field_photos` policies (`221:113-137`) — to accept **both** vocabularies, remaps `profiles.role`, and asserts in a `DO` block that `push_project` still resolves `_sync_guard(text)`; migration N+2 drops the old names a week later. No push fails at any instant.

**JWT claim:** a Custom Access Token hook adds `principal_id`, `role`, `division_id` and `org_id`; assigned job ids are **not** in the claim. `current_principal()` reads the claim and falls back to a `profiles` lookup when it is absent (old tokens, machine users mid-cutover). Role changes lag until refresh (the field app refreshes under 60 s before expiry, `apps/field/js/supa.js:34-86`); demotions also revoke sessions [A-0.8]. Helpers are `STABLE` and use the `(select auth.uid())` initplan form. Owner and office predicates on `events`, `photos`, `line_items` read the claim only; crew predicates call **`assigned_job_ids()`** — a `STABLE SECURITY DEFINER` helper evaluated once per statement as an initplan (`job_id = any ((select assigned_job_ids()))`), never joined per row — plus the **walk-on rule**: a crew member may read any job in their division whose stage is in the active set for 24 h after scanning its QR/asset label or being added by a crew lead. §2.4 and ADR-04 state this identically.

**`role_permissions` as data** (decision): rows editable through the owner's `role.grant` op, no deploy; a CI snapshot asserts every catalog op has a row or an explicit deny. **Owner's option — code as matrix:** generated `PERMISSIONS.md`, versioned, drift-free, but every change to who may approve is a deploy; at 100+ with several office staff and per-person overrides that cost dominates. **Assignments:** `job_assignments(job_id, principal_id|crew_id, role, from, to, source)` derived from schedule assignments plus explicit adds; the walk-on above is recorded by `job.claim_walk_on(job_id)`, a self-executed op that inserts a 24 h `source='walk_on'` assignment and notifies the office — reads widen through a recorded op, never an ad-hoc policy edit. **Machine widening:** `agents` + `agent_authority(agent_id, operation, capability, conditions{max_amount_usd, kinds, quiet_hours, division_ids}, granted_by, expires_at, revoked_at)`; grant and revoke are events. **Quiet hours are not assumed away for crews:** every outbound text, crew-line kinds included, obeys 7am–8pm AK by default; exempting crew-line kinds (`crewSchedule`, `onOurWay`, `crewLine`) is an owner option recorded in 04 and, if taken, a per-kind setting in `role_permissions`/`notification_policies`, never a rule hidden in code. Ledgers (`capture_events`, `ai_usage`, `events`) become insert-only for everyone but the service role — today any crew login can delete the month's spend (`201_ai_usage.sql:78-79`). Ceiling: 100+ people, ~10 roles; forcing conditions: per-customer isolation → the `org_id` claim doing real work; SLAs → a workflow layer.

### Decision record §6 — Blob strategy

**Additive-only** blob changes: a new key lands in `ID_COLLECTIONS` (`apps/field/js/merge.js:32-36`) and `id_cols` (`243:16-19`) in the same PR, guarded by a CI test that parses the array out of the migration and compares — the 2,033-case parity harness referenced at `243:30-36` is not in the tree and must be recovered from PR history first.

**One-directional projection** (`job.project`, worker, pure `extractProject()` in `packages/domain`): rows carry `origin='blob'`, `source_ref`, `blob_rev`; arbitration is **blob wins** for capture-owned sections (moisture maps and readings, drying logs, daily logs, photos, work auth, contents, sketches); rows are rebuildable by the idempotent `job.project_repair(job_id)`; the SQL `field_photos` trigger (`224_photo_projection_trustworthy.sql:195-219`) stays until the worker projection is zero-diff for 30 days, then drops. **Office-owned entities are born in tables** and never written into the blob: claims, carriers, policies, submittals, documents/versions, line items, estimates/invoices/COs, schedule, contacts. Ownership flips at the phase that lands them (P4 for money documents): the field app becomes read-only for that section and shows the issued PDF — the intent already recorded at `apps/field/js/model.js:26`. The nine `invoices[]`, four `reconEstimates[]` and two `changeOrders[]` **(live)** are imported on first open in the office surface; a read-only summary (`officeDocs: {invoiceNo, status, pdfPath}`) is written back under the **one-owner-per-key** rule: the worker reads the row, sets only the key it owns, pushes with the rev it just read, retries once on `merged` — it never carries a stale copy of a crew-owned key, so the Aug 6 stale-device clobber cannot recur through it.

**Rename, then a compat view — never a copy.** `unified_jobs` is renamed in place (`alter table public.unified_jobs rename to jobs`), which keeps the 33 ids that `contacts`, the portal and the phone agent already hold and needs no backfill; in the same transaction a read-only view named `unified_jobs` with the old column list is created and kept until every reader has moved — the phone agent, the brief, the portal, notify, the field `spine.js` sites and old PWA builds. §3.5 and §7.5 describe the same rename. Its one writer (`apps/field/js/spine.js:23-40`) is repointed at an op in the same PR; old builds that still POST get a 4xx that `spine.js:83` already swallows as `{skipped:true}`.

**The one risky shape change — `rooms[]`.** `rooms` unions by value in both engines (`merge.js:180-185`; `243:100-125`) and old devices keep pushing old shapes. Decision: do **not** rewrite `rooms[]`. Add `roomIds: [{id, name, aliases[]}]` as a new id-keyed collection (additive in both engines); new builds write both, `rooms[]` stays the derived string list old builds read and write; the projection resolves every free-text `room` through `roomIds.aliases` per job. Before any consumer relies on it, **arm `min_field_build`** (seeded 0 at `218:55`; the recipe is at `219_revoke_direct_field_writes.sql:32`) so the v153/v155 devices **(live)** receive the P0002 "update required" (`218:82-85`) and self-update through the service worker. Rollback: stop reading the key; nothing was rewritten.

**Offline outbox for non-blob ops** (P3): an IndexedDB `outbox` store `{id = idempotency key, op, input, attempts}` — the offline adapter of the one data client in `packages/db` — drained after each successful pull through the ops door; it replaces today's online-only, fire-and-forget writes (`apps/field/js/boardpush.js:204`; `spine.js:83`). The field `sendText` chip stays in the synchronous tap window for iOS (`apps/field/js/assist.js:229-236`) but records a proposal.

**Honest ceiling:** per-job, not per-company — whole-blob writes (INSERT 207 ms mean, max blob 227 kB, five triggers per update, a `blob_history` rewrite per push **(live)**) under a per-job `FOR UPDATE`. Forcing condition: more than ~5 devices concurrently editing one job, an office feature that must *edit* a capture-owned section, or blobs routinely over ~1 MB → that section moves to rows plus the outbox, one section at a time.

### Decision record §7 — Tenancy

`divisions(id, name, kind, calendar, qbo_class)` now; `division_id` on jobs, crews, crew members, equipment units, documents, proposals, settings, connections — a second crew, calendar, mailbox or QBO class becomes a row (today one settings uuid is hard-coded in 7 files and each provider has one token row). **Recommendation:** add `org_id uuid not null default '<this-org>'` on the spine (jobs, claims, contacts, crews, divisions) and money tables (documents, versions, line items, payments, submittals) in the migrations that create them, with `document_sequences` unique on `(org_id, kind, year)` and RLS predicates written against `current_org()` — which reads the `org_id` claim the JWT hook stamps (Decision record §5), a single defaulted value now and a real second value later. Cost now: near zero. **Owner's simpler option — divisions only:** fewer columns and nothing to explain; its cost, stated: adding `org_id` later touches every RLS policy, every unique constraint (document and claim numbers), every view and the JWT claim, plus a backfill under load — and must land before the second entity exists. Ceiling: single org plus divisions holds to 100+ in one legal entity; the defaulted `org_id` holds to a second entity with a claim change and a predicate swap.

### Decision record §8 — Document pipeline

`buildDocumentModel(kind, ids)` (pure, `packages/documents`) → `renderHtml(model, template, carrierProfile)` (the shared HTML renderer, also in `packages/documents`; `sheet()`/`letterhead()` from `apps/field/js/formkit.js:290-315` and `print.css:6,20,25-30,82-87,111,238-259` ported nearly verbatim — `print.css` lives **only** in `packages/documents` and imports its tokens from `packages/ui`) → **PDF** by Chromium in the worker's render child process, or **print** of the same HTML in the browser, so offline printing keeps working.

Tables: `documents(id, org_id, job_id, claim_id, kind, number, current_version_no, status draft→issued→superseded|void, paid_state unpaid|partial|paid, division_id)`; `document_versions(id, document_id, version_no, model_json, model_hash, html_hash, pdf_sha256, storage_path, page_count int, render_status queued|rendering|rendered|failed, rendered_at, template_version, carrier_profile_id, issued_at, issued_by, supersedes_version_id, void_reason)`; `document_sequences(org_id, kind, year, next)` — numbers never reused (today `INV-${length+1}` repeats after a delete, `apps/admin/js/finactions.js:89,137,250`). Line items are rows on drafts and are frozen into `model_json` at issue — a dual representation; a `billed_lines` view unions both so "what was billed" is one query. **Issued versions are immutable** (trigger; it exempts only the render columns — `render_status`, `rendered_at`, `page_count`, `html_hash`, `pdf_sha256`, `storage_path` — and only while `render_status <> 'rendered'`); a post-issue edit is `document.supersede` (same number, v+1) and `document.void` records a reason; QBO follows — supersede → update in place with SyncToken (`qbo-proxy/index.ts:345-352` precedent), void → QBO void, never delete. **Durability:** PITR (P4) protects the rows; the `documents` bucket is rebuildable — `model_json` + `template_version` + the pinned Chromium build in the worker image (Playwright version pinned) re-render the same HTML, and `pdf_sha256` is verified on every re-render, so a lost object is a re-render, not a restore. **Signatures** bind to an issued version — one definition, adopted by §3 and §6.8 alike: `signatures(document_version_id, signer_kind owner|contractor|adjuster|tech|lender|external, name, png_sha256, signed_model_hash, signed_at, method in_app|upload|portal, captured_by, captured_offline, device_id, ip, consent_text_version, external_ref)`; a signable document is issued unsigned as v1 and `document.sign` renders the signed v2 with `supersedes`; signatures captured offline in the blob attach through the projection to the current issued version [A-0.4]. **Paid state** comes from `payments` rows written by the QBO pull (`qbo-proxy/payments.ts:37-65` rules kept). **QBO idempotency** is keyed on the version — `outbox.idempotency_key = 'qbo.invoice:'||document_id||':v'||n` — and the adapter writes an `external_intents` row before the call; on retry with no external id it looks the invoice up by `DocNumber` before creating, because the real failure mode is a response lost after QBO created it.

**Submittals are one table with one definition** (§3, §4 and §6 adopt it): `submittals(job_id, claim_id, kind estimate|invoice|supplement|packet|certificate|drying_log|photo_packet|other, document_version_ids uuid[] ordered, packet_document_version_id nullable — the composed PDF, carrier_id, adjuster_contact_id, to_contact_id, channel email|portal|carrier_portal|mail|api, sent_at, sent_by principal, outbox_id, external_ref, parent_submittal_id for supplements and resubmissions, status)` — no `components jsonb[]`; `format` is copied from `carrier_profiles.format` at send time so the row records what the carrier required (§3.7), while `channel` and `external_ref` record how and where it was sent.

**Per-carrier profiles are a typed table:** `carrier_profiles(carrier_id, name, format pdf|xactimate|portal_upload, packet_order text[], required_documents text[], photo_rules jsonb, line_justification always|flagged|never, justification_style, cover_letter_template_id, days_to_pay_median)`; the renderer branches on `format` and `packet_order`; a line is *pushback-prone* when its (carrier, code|category) matches `learned_rules` (Decision record §10) or a default list. **ESX/XactAnalysis structured export is an explicit open boundary:** ESX files are encrypted, no export exists, and the Verisk EULA forbids redistributing price data; the pipeline produces a PDF plus a structured JSON packet, `carrier_profiles.format` records what the carrier requires and the submittal's `channel`/`external_ref` record what was sent, and a human does any Xactimate-native submission in Xactimate. `price_list` is frozen behind a `price_sources` interface and never expanded. A golden-output harness (fixture job → HTML diff + page count per kind) exists before the first template ships. Ceiling: hundreds of documents/day per render process; photo packets are the heavy case (cap images per render); over ~1,000/day or a sub-2 s SLA → a second render machine.

### Decision record §9 — Domain model completeness

The gaps the critiques named, resolved as entities (real FKs, `deleted_at`, `(actor_kind, actor_id)`):

| Gap | Entity |
|---|---|
| Policy/coverage | `policies(claim_id, policy_number, carrier_id, limits{dwelling,contents,ale}, deductible, deductible_applies_to, endorsements[], exclusions[], acv_rcv_basis, holdback_rule)` |
| Adjuster reassignment | `claim_adjusters(claim_id, contact_id, from, to, reason, desk)` — current = latest; the per-adjuster learning dimension |
| Scope baseline + observations (capability 3) | `scope_baselines(claim_id, document_version_id, approved_at, approved_amount, source)`; `observations(job_id, room_id, observed_at, by, kind, description, photo_ids, source photo_ai|daily_log|voice|manual, status)`. **`scope.compare`** (`packages/domain/detect/scope.compare.ts`, P6): inputs = the approved estimate version's line items by room and category vs observations = `photos.ai` tags + daily-log issues + change-order text + readings; a deterministic diff first, a judgment model second; output = one supplement proposal with evidence refs; idempotency key = `estimate_version_id` + observation cursor; trigger = a nightly job plus every new observation |
| Supplement ≠ change order | `supplements(claim_id, parent_document_version_id, document_id, reason, trigger_observation_ids, submittal_id, status)` is carrier-facing; a change order is a document kind with a customer signature and a contract delta |
| Crews (capability 4) | `crews(division_id, name, lead_member_id)`; `crew_members(principal_id, contact_id, crew_id, hourly_rate, qb_user_id, contact_prefs{digestOptOut, appOptOut, paperTimesheet})`; `skills`, `crew_member_skills`, `certifications(cert_code, issued, expires, evidence_document_id)`; `availability_patterns` + `availability_exceptions`; `phase_skill_requirements`; `time_entries.source ∈ qbtime|manual|board|paper` |
| Equipment | `equipment_units(asset_tag, type, model, ppd_or_cfm, serial, status, home_location_id)`; `equipment_placements(unit_id, job_id, room_id, placed_at, removed_at, by, photo_ids)`; `equipment_reservations(unit_id|type+qty, job_id, from, to, proposal_id)` |
| Geocode/travel | `jobs.lat/lng`; `locations` (yards, offices); distance buckets first, a travel matrix later [A-0.7] |
| Drying-time prediction (capability 4 input) | `drying_predictions(job_id, map_id, location_id, predicted_dry_on, confidence, basis)` computed by `packages/domain/detect/drying.predict.ts` (P7): a per-location linear/exponential trend over the last ≥3 readings toward `dry_goal`, confidence from the residual, fallback = IICRC class defaults when fewer than three readings exist; consumed by the scheduler |
| SOV/draws (capability 6) | `sov_schedules(job_id, contract_amount, lender_contact_id, retainage_pct)`; `sov_lines`; `draw_requests(number, period_to, requested, approved, paid_at, document_id)` — from the blob's `drawSchedule/lender/contractAmount` on 8 construction jobs **(live)** |
| Leads/intake | `leads(division_id, contact_id, source, channel, stage, score, scam_flags, log[], outcome, lost_reason)`. **`lead.screen`** (P5, with the Leads tab; propose-only, run by the intake agent): signals = honeypot, IP/subnet caps, phone/email validity, prior-contact match, plus an LLM classification (`claude-sonnet-5` at low effort) returning `{score, reasons}`; output = the `leads` row plus a `captures` row; it proposes triage and never declines a lead |
| Dry-standard provenance | `dry_standards(material_class, goal_pct, source IICRC_S500|reference_reading|carrier_profile)`; `moisture_locations.dry_goal_source_id`, `reference_location_id` (what `apps/field/js/core.js:705-734` derives today) |
| Person identity | `profiles` ↔ `crew_members.principal_id` (nullable — only 7 of 11 roster rows carry an email **(live)**, and the paper-timesheet member may never have a login) ↔ `contacts.principal_id`; every crew member has a **mandatory `contact_id`**; one `principals` view over humans and agents; the one-time bind matches on email where present and is confirmed by the owner for the rest |
| Automation cost per job | `agent_runs.job_id`, `claim_id` |

### Decision record §10 — AI layer

**Routing is data:** `model_routes(task_class pk, provider, model_id → model_prices, effort, thinking adaptive|budget, max_output_tokens, max_cost_usd, fallback_task_class, batch_eligible)`; the FK stops a route to an unpriced model. `model_prices` is seeded from the provider price sheet at deploy time; the facts this record fixes (bundled claude-api skill, cached 2026-06-24) are: Fable 5.1 `claude-fable-5-1` (1M, $10/$50 per MTok, thinking always on, effort low..max, **forced `tool_choice` rejected**); Opus 5 `claude-opus-5` (1M, $5/$25, adaptive thinking by default); Opus 4.8 `claude-opus-4-8` ($5/$25 — what DOC tasks run today, `roybal-ai-office/index.ts:64`; `roybal-ai-narrative/index.ts:28`); Sonnet 5 `claude-sonnet-5` (1M, $2/$10); Sonnet 4.6 `claude-sonnet-4-6` ($3/$15 — what photo, assist, ingest, concierge and phone run today, `index.ts:63,65`; `roybal-ai-ingest/index.ts:51`; `roybal-portal/index.ts:76`; `services/phone-agent/config.mjs:21` — dearer than Sonnet 5); Haiku 4.5 `claude-haiku-4-5` (200K, $1/$5, `budget_tokens`). Any column this record does not state (cache-read and batch rates among them) is filled from the price sheet at seed time, never from a design document. Verified today: four literal forced `tool_choice:{type:"tool"}` sites — `roybal-ai-office/index.ts:148` inside `forcedTool()` (called 14 times), `roybal-ai-ingest/index.ts:411`, `qb-time-proxy/aiphase.ts:123`, `roybal-portal/index.ts:665` — all break on Fable 5.1; replace with `tool_choice: auto` + `strict:true` schemas or `output_config.format`. `roybal-web-agent/guards.ts:254-256` prices `claude-sonnet-5` at $3/$15 and `claude-opus-5` at $15/$75 against the real $2/$10 and $5/$25. Assistant prefill returns 400 on 4.6+; adaptive thinking + `output_config.effort` replaces `budget_tokens`; prompt caching is prefix-based (stable system + tools first, volatile last; verify `usage.cache_read_input_tokens`); the Message Batches API at 50% is the lane for bulk photo tagging and classification. Seed routes [A-0.5]: `judgment.*` — `judgment.carrier_document`, `judgment.supplement`, **`judgment.line_justification`** — → `claude-opus-5` adaptive (Fable 5.1 at medium effort for `carrier_document` once evals justify it); `draft.*`, `chat.*`, `extract.*` and `classify.*` → `claude-sonnet-5` (classification at low effort, batch-eligible; Haiku 4.5 stays priced as the eval-justified alternative for bulk backfills, not the default). Cost per completed task is the metric; one model at lower effort beats a cascade and keeps one cache namespace. Model ids appear in exactly one file, `packages/integrations/llm/client.ts`, never in an op; `packages/ai` holds the routing-policy loader that reads `model_routes`, the versioned prompts, the metering helpers and the tool-definition generation from the ops registry.

**Prompts** are versioned files `packages/ai/prompts/<key>.v<N>.md` mirrored to a `prompts` table; `ai_usage` gains `prompt_id`, `prompt_version`, `task_class`, `route_key`, `agent_run_id`, `proposal_id`, `job_id`. **Evals** live in `packages/ai/evals/<task_class>/` [A-0.9] (fixtures + expectations; `npm run eval -- <class>` against staging; CI runs a zero-cost schema-validity subset; a full run recorded in `eval_runs` — the table lands in P3 with the first prompt version — gates activating a prompt version). **Reserve-before-spend** generalises `web_turn_begin/end` (`227:147-211`) into `ai_reserve`/`ai_settle` SECURITY DEFINER with per-lane and shared caps as `ai_caps` rows. **Per-carrier learning:** `carrier_responses` → `rejection_reasons(line_item_id|code|category, reason_code typed, text)` → weekly `carrier.learn` (deterministic aggregation) → `learned_rules(carrier_id, scope, rule_text, evidence_response_ids, active)` — propose-only, owner-approved → `retrieve_rules(carrier_id, codes[])` injects matching rules after the cached prefix of the `line_justification` prompt. Harness: the worker uses the Claude Agent SDK for open-ended loops and plain Messages calls for forced-schema jobs; Tool Runner and Managed Agents are noted, not chosen.

### Decision record §11 — Migrations and environments

Today the directory is a journal: replay fails at `105` (needs `200`) and at `233/236/244` (they scrape `cron.job`); 44 repo files were applied under timestamp versions; six live entries have no file; at least eight live tables have no DDL (inventory §6.8). **Procedure, in order:** (1) `supabase db dump` of production schema into `0000_baseline.sql` (policies, functions, triggers, grants, live enum values), cron definitions stripped; (2) archive the 70 files; (3) `supabase migration repair --status reverted` for every live entry and `--status applied` for the baseline — **before any CLI points at prod**; verify `supabase migration list` agrees on both sides; (4) forward-only timestamped files after; (5) `supabase db reset` on the baseline passes locally — the CI replay test; (6) schema reaches prod only by `db push` from CI after staging passes, never the SQL editor or MCP `apply_migration` again. **Staging = a second Supabase project** seeded from `pg_dump` (schema plus scrubbed data) — **not branching**, which replays the journal that cannot replay; cost: a second project's plan fee [A-0.6] and duplicated function secrets. **Cron:** registered post-deploy by `cron_register.sql`; jobs are SQL-only inserts into `jobs_queue`, so no secret sits in a migration or a `cron.job` row — with the one documented exception of the dead-worker check (Decision record §2), whose `net.http_post` to `roybal-webhooks` `/alert` reads its service-secret header from `vault.decrypted_secrets`; during the transition the last HTTP job reads its header the same way. **Compute tier:** Micro → Small is a P3 roadmap step with a trigger metric — `events + capture_events` over 1M rows, p95 `push_project` over 400 ms after projections land, or a third disk-IO alert, whichever first. PITR is enabled at P4 when documents and events become the legal record; it covers Postgres, and the `documents` bucket is rebuildable from `model_json` + `template_version` + the pinned Chromium build (Decision record §8).

### Decision record §12 — Build and tooling

**Layout (binding — §1.1 and §8 use these names and no others):** `packages/domain` (pure, runtime-neutral: `engines/` = the verbatim calc modules incl. `schedule.js`, `fincalc`, `dryingcalc`, `completeness`, `phasematch`, `emailmatch`, `approve`, `payments`, `guards`; `schemas/`; `vocab/`; `policies/`; `ops/` = the operation registry; `detect/` = `billing.reconcile`, `scope.compare`, `drying.predict`), `packages/db` (generated Supabase types + the one data client, including the field app's IndexedDB/offline adapter), `packages/documents` (document models, templates, the shared HTML renderer, `print.css`), `packages/ui` (tokens incl. the single navy, components, `app.css`), `packages/integrations` (adapters behind interfaces + fakes), `packages/ai` (routing policy loader, versioned prompts, metering helpers, tool-definition generation from ops); `apps/{field,office,portal,site}` (`apps/office` = today's `apps/admin` + `apps/board` [A-0.10]); `services/{worker,phone-agent}`; `supabase/{functions,migrations}`. Every Biome scope, folder rule and import boundary is written against these names.

**esbuild bundling per app** (`apps/field`, `apps/office`, `apps/portal`; `apps/site` joins CI as a build check) from `packages/*`; one `build.mjs` generates `BUILD`, `COMMIT` and the service-worker precache manifest from the bundle graph. **`BUILD` is a monotonic integer, never a SHA:** `_sync_guard` strips non-digits and compares as `int` (`caller_build := regexp_replace(p_build, '\D', '', 'g')::int`, `supabase/migrations/218_sync_rpcs_and_build_gate.sql:79-84`), so a SHA would compare as garbage digits — or, with no digits, parse to `null` and bypass the gate. `BUILD` is generated at build time from `git rev-list --count HEAD` (489 at `1e04694`; monotonic on `main`), and a separate `COMMIT` constant carries the short SHA for display and telemetry. **Bundling, `BUILD` generation, SW generation and the verbatim package moves land as P3.0 "toolchain"** — the first sub-step of P3 — not in P1 (Decision record §15). Typecheck: `tsc --noEmit --checkJs` + JSDoc for the apps (no rewrite of 17k lines), TypeScript in `packages/*` and `services/*`, `deno check` for the five functions; Biome for lint and format. **Why the no-build import-map option lost:** it keeps zero toolchain but leaves the two most recent production defects in place — the hand-bumped `BUILD` (`apps/field/js/config.js:25`, v165) vs SW cache (`apps/field/sw.js:6`, v166) drift that shipped in PR #181 with the fleet lying to `_sync_guard` **(live)**, and a hand-maintained precache that omits two statically imported modules and includes deprecated `pricing.js` (`sw.js:8-32`) — and it cannot code-split a boot path that loads 42 of 44 modules (923 KB raw). **One CI** (`ci.yml`): on PR — Biome, typecheck, `deno check`, `node --test` across field/office/functions/phone-agent (no `&&` chain — today `package.json:11` and `apps/field/package.json:10` stop at file 2), the BUILD/SW lockstep test (in CI from E0 against the hand-maintained pair; from P3.0 it asserts the generated integer), migration replay on the baseline, merge-parity (JS vs SQL array), golden document diffs; on merge — staging migrations → tests → prod `db push` → `functions deploy` from `config.toml` (never MCP; `supabase/config.toml:1-21` records why) → bundles with tests, READMEs and `serve.mjs` excluded → `fly deploy` worker. Deploy records `latest_field_build`; a weekly proposal asks the owner to raise `min_field_build` to latest−2 on that integer — a low-risk early proposal kind.

### Decision record §13 — First automation

**Backbone smoke test (P1):** the overdue-invoice reminder lane (`roybal-brief/index.ts:249-299` → `pending_actions.emailSend` → `gmail-proxy/index.ts:317-381`) and the adjuster-email draft lane (`roybal-ai-office/index.ts:760-782`) migrate onto `proposals` + `outbox`: the brief proposes `email.send`; approval in the inbox or by SMS (`op_proposal_approve` called from `roybal-webhooks`, Decision record §2); the worker delivers the outbox row with an idempotency key; a `submittals` row (the one definition of Decision record §8) is written when the email carries a document reference. Verify: approve twice → one email; a crew JWT cannot approve; an SMS approval with the worker scaled to zero still records; a killed worker resumes; `events` shows propose→approve→execute with the approver.

**First new capability (P2): mitigation billing reconciliation v0** — `billing.reconcile` at `packages/domain/detect/billing.reconcile.ts`, deterministic, zero-LLM. Per mitigation job in drying/final or closed-unbilled: (a) Σ per-day `dehu/am/scrub` strings over dated psychrometric rows (`dryingLogs[].readings[]`, `apps/field/js/model.js:353-361`) = documented unit-days by class vs invoice lines matched by the `dryingcalc.js:152-163` regexes (the live sample bills 215 air-mover-days against a log of 20/day); (b) Σ `time_entries.hours` vs Σ qty of `HR` lines; (c) reading dates per map vs monitoring-visit lines; (d) Cat-3 package assertions when `waterCategory='3'`; (e) "was this invoice already sent" from `submittals`, which ships with it; (f) `photos[].ai.equipment` tags as a **v0 hint input** — a photo whose tags name a unit class in a room where no line bills that class raises a hint with the photo id, never a priced line. Output: one `invoice.review_gaps` proposal per job with `evidence_refs` (dates, counts, entry ids, photo ids). Execution before P4 appends flagged lines to the blob's `invoices[]` through the worker's key-owner write-back (invoices are office-edited only, `model.js:26`); after P4 it writes `line_items` rows without changing the contract. **It is an internal leak detector, not carrier-grade justification** — typed count strings are not evidence a carrier accepts; that arrives with placement events and photos in P3. Jobs whose drying logs have rows on only some days (the live sample: 07-31 and 08-03) are flagged "undocumented days", not "unbilled". Collections and scheduling come second: the Gmail lane was dead five days with cron reporting success, and skills/certs do not exist.

### Decision record §14 — On-device capture

1. **QR/Code-128 asset labels first** (S): a label per `equipment_units.asset_tag`; place/remove scans create `equipment.place/remove` ops in the field outbox with a photo — and a scan of a job's label is also what grants the 24 h walk-on read of Decision record §5; decode with `BarcodeDetector` where present and a WASM decoder (zxing-class, tens of KB, in a Web Worker) elsewhere — iOS Safari has no Shape Detection API and the crew carries a company iPad. Count strings become an identity event log with no ML.
2. **Meter OCR as prefill, never commit** (M): a photo per reading is always captured as evidence (`moisture_readings.photo_id`); Tesseract.js in a worker, precached, confidence-gated, digit-whitelisted — 7-segment LCDs are weak, so an eval set of ~50 meter photos gates the prefill; online `extract.meter_ocr` re-reads and flags disagreement.
3. **Receipts: capture now, recognize online, confirm always** (S/M): offline `captures` row (kind `ocr_receipt`) plus photo; `extract.receipt` on reconnect; tech or office confirms into `receipts`/`materials`.
4. **iOS constraints:** PWA storage can be evicted and there is no background sync — the outbox flushes on foreground; offline model assets stay under ~15 MB and OCR data loads only when enabled; persistent storage is already requested in `boot()` (`apps/field/js/app.js:80`).

**`captures` is the ingest envelope from P3 on** — one kind enum, `voice|photo_ai|ocr_meter|ocr_receipt|barcode|serial|signature|email_pull` — for every on-device, voice or pulled prefill before a human confirms it. `capture_events` stays as the legacy ledger (read-only after P3) and is migrated into `captures` + `events` in P5; §3 and §6.16 use the same enum and the same handover.

### Decision record §15 — Roadmap skeleton (recommended path)

Calendar-honest for a solo owner with Claude Code at roughly a third of working time. Effort S ≈ days, M ≈ 1–3 weeks, L ≈ 1–2 months.

| Id | Phase | Weeks | Goal | Unblocks | Effort |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| E0 | Fix production now | 1–2 | reconnect Gmail + QB Time with the **interim** `integration_health` (a view over the three token tables) + owner alert; CI test gate + BUILD/SW lockstep + unchained suites (fix v165/v166 by hand); gate the proxy actions reachable with the publishable key (`pushInvoice`, `exchangeCode`, `disconnect`, `getTimesheets`…); sweep the 3 zombie proposals + expiry filter; Twilio status callback; delete Magicplan; cron secrets out of `cron.job`; arm `min_field_build`; ledgers insert-only; `capture_events` retention | all | S |
| P1 | Thin backbone + smoke test | 3–6 | baseline squash + history repair + staging; `proposals`, `events`, `jobs_queue`, `outbox`, `agents`, `agent_authority`, `role_permissions` (+ `approval_policies`, `operation_catalog`, `api_tokens`, `submittals`; `connections`, **`integration_runs`** — written by every adapter call, pull or push — with the `integration_health` view over `integration_runs ⋈ connections` and the alert rule *3 consecutive failures or no successful run in 2× the expected cadence*; `worker_heartbeats`, `external_refs`; `ai_caps` and `web_sessions` as renames of existing state; the six `agents` seed rows `agent:billing`, `agent:projection`, `agent:outbox`, `agent:brief`, `agent:phone`, `agent:web`); ~5 operations as SQL functions; the `roybal-ops` dispatcher shell; role migration + JWT hook; the worker skeleton (queue poller + outbox deliverer + heartbeat) on Fly at ≥1 GB; Approvals inbox beside "YES n"; the two migrated lanes. **No esbuild, no service-worker generation, no package extraction** | 5, 6 | M |
| P2 | First new capability | 7–10 | `billing.reconcile` v0 → `invoice.review_gaps` (with `photos.ai.equipment` tags as a v0 hint input); QB Time pull on the queue writing `integration_runs`; `time_entries` unique + expression indexes | 2 | M |
| P3 | Toolchain, then domain tables + projections | 11–18 | **P3.0 toolchain:** esbuild bundling, generated `BUILD`/`COMMIT`, generated SW precache, the verbatim package moves; then `unified_jobs` renamed in place to `jobs` + the compat view; claims/carriers/policies/adjusters; `roomIds`; equipment units/placements + QR + field outbox; `captures` as the ingest envelope; moisture locations/readings; drying days; time-entry columns (`source` qbtime\|manual\|board\|paper); crews/skills/certs/availability + `job_assignments`; `job.project` + repair; `eval_runs` with the first prompt version; era-0 → `legacy`; compute upgrade if triggered | 1, 2, 3, 4 (data) | L |
| P4 | Documents | 19–26 | documents/versions/sequences/signatures/payments; `qbo_item_map`, `internal_rates`; render process; invoice → cert of drying → drying packet → photo packet → estimate → CO; import-on-open; QBO via outbox keyed on version; carrier profiles; golden harness; PITR + the rebuildable bucket | 1 | L |
| P5 | Office UI strangling | 27–34 | office tabs rebuilt on ops (Today/Proposals → Leads → Claims → Documents → Board); `lead.screen` with the Leads tab; direct writes revoked table by table behind each op; board poll → changes feed; `schedule.apply` atomic; **reporting** = materialized views `jobs_summary`, `ar_aging`, `crew_hours`, `automation_costs` + read ops + a Reports tab; `event_subscriptions`, `on_call`; `capture_events` migrated into `captures` + `events`; month-partition `events` | 6 | L |
| P6 | Communications, carrier learning, scope | 35–44 | threads/messages; `message.draft_reply`; `classify.email`; responses/rejection reasons/learned rules; collections on the outbox; supplements, baselines, observations, `scope.compare`; QBO sync widened to bills/expenses from receipts and materials, customer creation, class per division | 5, 3, 6 | L |
| P7 | Scheduling proposals + MCP | 45–56 | `drying_predictions`; the scheduler pass — `schedule.js` gains skill/cert filters, per-person capacity, equipment reservations and drive-time from geocode; `schedule.propose` files one proposal row per schedule run with the assignment set as params, a crew lead approves their crew's assignments and office approves cross-crew; MCP over streamable HTTP (propose-only); phone agent imports packages, stays its own app | 4 | L |

Verify / rollback / what people feel. **E0** — CI green on a PR, a test alert SMS arrives from the interim view, `curl` with the publishable key to `pushInvoice` returns 401; every item is one revertible PR; crews: two stale devices see "update required"; office: the Gmail card stops lying. **P1** — the negative and idempotency tests above, plus: scale the worker to zero and text "YES n" → the approval records through `op_proposal_approve`, the send waits, and the owner's dead-worker text arrives within ~15 minutes through `/alert`; rollback = a flag routes the brief back to `pending_actions` (kept until P2); crews: nothing; office: an inbox beside "text YES 12". **P2** — three closed jobs surface the known misses; rollback = disable the queue kind; office: a weekly unbilled list. **P3** — P3.0 first: the first generated build ships with `BUILD` = the commit count and a precache equal to the module graph; then zero-diff blob vs rows for 14 days; a SQL answer to "dehu-days in the basement on job X"; rollback = drop the queue kind, blob untouched; crews: an optional scan screen and one build update. **P4** — the pipeline PDF equals the on-site print; re-issue makes v2 with the same number; a retried QBO push makes one invoice; rollback = the field print path stays until no open job uses it; crews: Print/Share yields PDFs; office: documents arrive as attachments. **P5** — cutover per tab after two weeks of daily use and 14 days of zero traffic on the old route, then delete; the Reports tab and the brief read the same materialized views. **P6/P7** — a rejection recorded Monday changes Tuesday's draft; a supplement proposal cites the observation and photo ids; a proposed week is approved in one tap and matches the crew texts, honoring `digestOptOut`, the paper-timesheet opt-out and the quiet-hours default.

**What the not-recommended variants would have looked like.** *Evolve-in-place as the primary path:* the same contract tables and a thin ops function over the existing blobs, auth fixed table by table across 48 policy sets, both merge engines kept — nobody feels anything, security lands in weeks, but every new operation stays blob-shaped, the JS↔SQL twin becomes permanent, nothing forces a crew or division entity into existence, and by month twelve the company has more features at the same distance from the north star. *Clean-sheet rewrite:* 8–14 months to parity for 22 form kinds, the offline engine, board, admin, portal, phone agent and 14 functions before crews can switch, a solo owner running two products, and a big-bang cutover for iPads on job-site LTE — the parts it discards are the best parts. *No worker (Postgres + edge only):* pgmq or the queue table drained by an edge `tick` with `EdgeRuntime.waitUntil`, a hosted HTML→PDF service, agent loops as plain Messages calls — honest to ~30 people, but the phone agent already forces a Node process, long loops are chunked around the isolate wall clock, and the Agent SDK and MCP have no home. *One Fly app for voice and worker:* fewer deploys, but a packet render can OOM-restart the process under a live call. *A Deno dispatcher as the only write door:* no transaction across PostgREST calls, an edge hop on every short write, and the office stops when one function is down.

### Decision record §16 — Corrected ceilings

| Claim in an attempt | Corrected |
|---|---|
| One 256/512 MB Fly machine runs queue + agent loops + Chromium (+ the phone socket) | separate apps; render child process; ≥1 GB; the ceiling is availability (one machine, one region, no HA), not throughput |
| Staging via Supabase branching | branching replays a journal that cannot replay; second project from `pg_dump` |
| `commit_operation(changes[])` as the write door | rejected: unguarded unless SQL knows op→tables; per-op SQL functions instead |
| Blob pushes emit events; "tens of thousands of events/day" | no; `blob_history` is the pre-image; ~500k potential pushes/day at ~300 active jobs on the 45 s cycle, and the projection is debounced to one per job per minute (≈430k/day worst case) |
| "No `org_id`; add it later, additively" | not additive — every policy, unique constraint, view and claim; defaulted `org_id` now recommended |
| "Postgres-as-queue to thousands/minute" | true of Postgres, not of the 1 GB Micro with disk-IO history; the compute upgrade is a roadmap step with a trigger metric |
| "Partition `events` at 10–50M rows" | one heap to low millions; at 100+ people that is ~12 months of events, so month partitioning is a P5 item, not "later" |
| `BUILD` generated from the git SHA (this record's first §12 wording) | wrong: `_sync_guard` strips non-digits and compares as `int` (`218:79-84`); `BUILD` = `git rev-list --count HEAD`, `COMMIT` = the short SHA |
| "Role-per-op holds to 100+" | only with `job_assignments`, amount thresholds and data-driven overrides |
| "Projection holds to hundreds of jobs" | load is per push; the ceiling is concurrently editing devices per job |
| "Stateless worker → `fly scale count 2`" | true for the poller; MCP (streamable HTTP) and Agent SDK sessions are stateful |
| "Edge dispatcher: validate → permission → run in one transaction" | not achievable over PostgREST; hence SQL functions for short ops |
| "PostgREST + RLS scale far past 100+" | RLS scales with policy complexity; claims in the JWT, `STABLE` helpers and the once-per-statement `assigned_job_ids()` initplan are the condition |
| "Document pipeline: nothing forces change" | carrier-required structured formats (ESX/XactAnalysis) and e-signature are real forces; both are explicit boundaries here |

### Decision record — assumptions

[A-0.1] Worker machine size and Fly plan. [A-0.2] `pg_jsonschema` is enabled on the project. [A-0.3] The owner accepts propose-only MCP and sets per-division amount thresholds. [A-0.4] Offline-captured signatures attach to the current issued version. [A-0.5] Seed model routes and effort levels pending evals. [A-0.6] Staging project cost and plan tier. [A-0.7] Travel starts as distance buckets. [A-0.8] Session revocation on demotion via the auth admin API. [A-0.9] Evals live beside the prompts in `packages/ai/evals/<task_class>/` (R1 places the prompts in `packages/ai` and names no eval home). [A-0.10] `apps/office` is the target home of today's `apps/admin` and `apps/board` (R1's app list has no admin or board app).

---

## 1. Target structure

Today the three internal apps share code by URL path: 56 `../../js/*.js` imports in 16 admin and board files (`apps/admin/js/admin.js:8-11`; `apps/board/js/data.js:13-14`) and one the other way (`apps/field/js/myweekcalc.js:17-18`) resolve only because `.github/workflows/deploy-field.yml:36-38` lays field, admin and board out at `/`, `/admin`, `/board` on one origin and each `serve.mjs` fakes that layout locally (`apps/field/serve.mjs:30-34`). The scheduling engine exists three ways (`apps/board/js/schedule.js` and `supabase/functions/roybal-brief/schedule.js` are md5-identical, `23189e1e…`; `services/phone-agent/Dockerfile:8-9` copies it into the Fly image), the 14 edge functions have no `_shared/`, the job merge has a plpgsql twin, and nothing type-checks, lints or tests before a deploy (`deploy-field.yml:10-52`). The target is a monorepo whose packages *are* the architecture: six packages, four apps, two Fly services, five edge functions, one replayable migration set, one CI workflow. Per Decision record §0 it is built beside the running apps and adopted file by file; nothing below asks a crew to change how they capture a job.

> **Reading conventions.** This document is assembled as Part 0, the decision record — cited as "Decision record §n" — followed by Parts 1–8, cited as "§1"…"§8" with their subsection numbers ("§2.4"); ADRs are cited by number ("ADR-04"). A bare "§n" anywhere in Parts 1–8 means the assembled section, never the record. Where a Part restates a decision the record made, this map says where to read the fuller version:
>
> | Decision record | Assembled document |
> |---|---|
> | §0 path · §1 runtime split · §2 trust levels and identity | §2.5, §2.4, ADR-08 |
> | §3 registry · §4 proposals/events/queue/outbox · §5 permissions | §2.3, §2.2, §2.4 + ADR-01/03/04/05/06 |
> | §6 blob strategy · §7 tenancy · §8 documents · §9 domain model | ADR-10 + §7.10, ADR-11 + §3 intro, §6 Part A + ADR-07, §3 |
> | §10 AI layer · §11 migrations · §12 build · §13 first automation | §2.7 + ADR-09, §8.6 + ADR-13, §1.7 + ADR-14, §7.13 |
> | §14 on-device · §15 roadmap · §16 corrected ceilings | §6 Part B, §7, §1.9 / §2.9 / §3.6 / §4.6 / §8.7 |
>
> **Assumption tags.** The record's assumptions are [A-0.1]…[A-0.10]; each Part numbers its own as [A-<part>.<n>] ([A-1.1], [A-2.3], …) and no tag is reused across Parts. The record's *Assumptions* list consolidates them. Where this Part relies on a record assumption it cites the record's tag ([A-0.6] for the staging project's cost, for example).

### 1.1 Monorepo layout

```
roybal-restoration-app/
├── packages/
│   ├── domain/          pure, runtime-neutral TypeScript, zero I/O — the rules of the business and the OPERATION REGISTRY
│   │   ├── engines/     the verbatim calc modules, tests beside them: schedule.js (887 lines, 89 tests), schedulewatch, myweekcalc,
│   │   │                fincalc, dryingcalc, completeness, dryingwatch, buildwatch, calibration, gpp (core.js:705-734),
│   │   │                xactimate, xlsx, convert, selections, zip, phasematch, emailmatch, payments, approve, guards, digest, crewdigest, weekly
│   │   ├── schemas/     JSON Schema for every op input/output, blob key and table row; model.js factories as the typed defaults beside them
│   │   ├── vocab/       stages, job types, trades, materials, channels, reasons, loss types, SETTINGS_ROW_ID, COMPANY, the FORMS registry
│   │   ├── policies/    quietHours (7 am–8 pm AK for every kind by default; the crew-line exemption is an owner option, per kind), canNotify, spend caps, approval defaults
│   │   ├── ops/         <area>.<verb>.ts — one file per operation; runtime:"sql" bodies beside them as <area>.<verb>.sql
│   │   ├── extract/     extractProject(blob) → rows; toUnifiedRow; the blob→row projection
│   │   └── detect/      billing.reconcile.ts (P2) · scope.compare.ts (P6) · drying.predict.ts (P7) · lead.screen.ts (P5) — the detectors
│   ├── db/              generated Supabase types + THE one data client: auth+refresh (supa.js moved), typed reads, ops.invoke/propose,
│   │                    the three factories forBrowser / forFunction / forService, the field app's IndexedDB/offline adapter, log
│   ├── documents/       document models (pure) · templates · the shared HTML renderer · letterhead once · print.css (its ONLY home;
│   │                    imports tokens from packages/ui) · print.js (the one window.print()) · golden fixtures
│   ├── ui/              tokens.css (ONE navy), app.css, h(), formkit editor helpers, app chrome, emblem
│   ├── integrations/    adapters behind interfaces + fakes: qbo, qbtime, gmail, twilio, deepgram, llm/client.ts (the only file naming a model id), price_sources
│   └── ai/              routing policy loader (model_routes/model_prices), prompts/<key>.v<N>.md, evals/<task_class>/, personas + tool allowlists,
│                        metering helpers (ai_reserve/settle), tool-definition generation from ops (Anthropic tools[], MCP list)
├── apps/
│   ├── field/           the PWA, wrapped not rewritten: js/ keeps sync.js, merge.js, media.js, graft.js, editors, app.js
│   ├── office/          the office app: today's admin tabs and the board, strangled in tab by tab onto ops at P5
│   │                    (Today/Proposals → Leads → Claims → Documents → Board); apps/admin and apps/board are its source until then and keep publishing
│   ├── portal/          customer/adjuster portal (Vercel), same tokens, same db client
│   └── site/            Astro marketing site — unchanged; joins CI as a build check
├── services/
│   ├── worker/          roybal-worker on Fly (≥1 GB): queue poller, outbox deliverer, heartbeat, Agent SDK, MCP over streamable HTTP, render child, pulls, projection
│   └── phone-agent/     roybal-phone on Fly — its own app; imports packages/* via the workspace (P7), no Docker COPY
├── supabase/
│   ├── functions/       FIVE: roybal-ops (A) · roybal-webhooks (B) · roybal-web-agent + roybal-lead (C) · roybal-portal (D)
│   │   └── _shared/     hand-written in P1 (one CORS, one envelope, current_principal()); from P3.0 built by esbuild from packages/db + domain + ai for Deno
│   ├── migrations/      0000_baseline.sql + forward-only timestamped files; cron_register.sql
│   ├── migrations_archive/   the 70 journal files, read-only history
│   ├── tests/           pgTAP
│   └── config.toml      verify_jwt pins (kept; records why MCP deploys are forbidden)
├── tools/               build.mjs (esbuild per app; generates BUILD/COMMIT and the service-worker precache manifest) · serve.mjs · invariants/ · drift.test.mjs · ops-catalog.mjs
├── .github/workflows/ci.yml   the one workflow
├── CLAUDE.md · docs/PERMISSIONS.md (generated) · docs/architecture/
└── biome.json · tsconfig.json (extends tsconfig.base.json — finally consumed)
```

`packages/` last existed as `packages/shared` and died with the React line on 2026-08-13 (`package-lock.json:31,79,7434` still carries its ghost). Nothing here resurrects that code; the name is reused because the workspace mechanism (`package.json:5-8`) already exists and needs only two more entries (`packages/*`, `services/*`).

**Canonical package names (fixed here; §6, §8.1's layout block, §8.2's Biome scopes and §8.3's import boundaries use exactly these).** Earlier drafts of this document wrote `packages/client`, `packages/render`, `packages/domain/{calc,schedule,model}` and put templates under `packages/domain/docs`; those names are retired, and a rule written against them is read with the substitution below.

| Package | Holds | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `packages/domain` | `engines/`, `schemas/`, `vocab/`, `policies/`, `ops/`, `extract/`, `detect/`, `ports.ts` (the interfaces — `Render`, `Llm`, `Outbox`, `Db`, and one per vendor — that the runtimes inject; this is why `domain` never imports upward) | pure and runtime-neutral; `detect/billing.reconcile.ts` is the detector's one path — never `domain/billing/reconcile`; document models, templates, prompts and evals do **not** live here |
| `packages/db` | `types.gen.ts` from `supabase gen types`; the one data client with its three factories `forBrowser(session)` / `forFunction(req)` / `forService(principalId)`; auth and refresh-on-401; typed reads; `ops.invoke/propose` with the route table the registry emits; the IndexedDB outbox adapter; `log` | the invariants `no-raw-supabase-url`, `no-rest-in-apps` and `write-is-operation` key on this path |
| `packages/documents` | `models/<kind>.ts` (`buildDocumentModel`, pure), `templates/<kind>.ts`, `render.ts`, `format.ts` (currency, dates, units — the one formatter the templates and the office UI share), `letterhead.ts`, `print.css`, `print.js`, `__golden__/` | templates are not in `domain`; `print.css` lives only here — not in `ui`, not in an app — and imports `tokens.css` from `packages/ui` |
| `packages/ui` | `tokens.css`, `app.css`, `h()`, formkit editor helpers, app chrome, the emblem | the only package that defines a colour |
| `packages/integrations` | one interface + one real adapter + one fake per vendor; `llm/client.ts`; `price_sources` | every call, pull or push, writes an `integration_runs` row (§2.6) |
| `packages/ai` | the routing policy loader, versioned prompts, evals, personas and per-persona allowlists, metering helpers, tool-definition generation from the ops registry | reads `model_routes`/`model_prices` through `packages/db`; never names a model id (that is `integrations/llm/client.ts`) |

The portal, which has no user JWT, imports only the `forBrowser` factory's anonymous-gateway variant from `packages/db` and never its REST helpers.

### 1.2 Packages and their rules

| Package | Holds | Runs in | May import | Rule | Ceiling |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `domain` | every rule that decides something; the ops registry; the projection; the detectors; the schemas, vocabularies and policies | browser, Deno, Node, and (for `runtime:"sql"` bodies) Postgres | nothing outside itself; JSON Schema types only | no `document`, `window`, `Deno`, `process`, `fetch`, `localStorage`, `indexedDB` — a lint rule, plus `node --test` proving every module loads under bare Node | holds to 100+ and hundreds of operations; a second product line with disjoint vocabulary would split it, headcount never does |
| `db` | auth + refresh + REST/RPC (the field's `supa.js` moved, still "no SDK: just fetch calls", `apps/field/js/supa.js:1-3`) [A-1.5]; `types.gen.ts` from `supabase gen types`; `ops.invoke/propose` with the route table the registry emits; the three factories `forBrowser` / `forFunction` / `forService`; the IndexedDB outbox adapter | browser, Deno, Node | `domain` (schemas, vocab) | the only package that builds a PostgREST URL; `rest(` string literals in `apps/` fail lint | holds to 100+; PostgREST + RLS scale with policy complexity, and the JWT claims of Decision record §5 (`principal_id`, `role`, `division_id`, `org_id`) plus the `assigned_job_ids()` initplan helper of §2.4 keep predicates flat |
| `ui` | `tokens.css`, `app.css`, `h()` (from `apps/field/js/core.js:8`), formkit editor helpers, app chrome, `emblem-mark.svg` once (four copies today) | browser only | `domain` (vocab) | the only file that defines a colour is `tokens.css` | none |
| `documents` | `models/<kind>.ts` (`buildDocumentModel`, pure — totals through `domain/engines/fincalc`); templates `DocumentModel → HTML`; `letterhead()`/`sheet()`/`sheetFooter()` (ported from `apps/field/js/formkit.js:290-315`); `print.css`; `print.js`, the one `window.print()`; golden fixtures | browser (preview and print) and Node (Chromium child process) | `domain`, `ui` (`tokens.css`) | a model is assembly over the engines and a template reads a `DocumentModel` and nothing else — no network, no IndexedDB, no live project | hundreds of documents/day per render process (§6.2) |
| `integrations` | one interface + one real adapter + one fake per vendor; `llm/client.ts` is the only file naming a model id (Decision record §10; §2.7); `price_sources` freezes `price_list` behind an interface and never expands it | Deno, Node | `domain`, `db` | every outbound side effect is an `outbox` row with an idempotency key; every adapter call — a pull run from `jobs_queue` or a push run from `outbox` — ends by writing an `integration_runs` row (`connection_id`, `kind`, `started_at`, `finished_at`, `ok`, `error`, `rows_affected`, `external_ref`; §2.6); adapters never write blobs | 100+; a second LLM provider or a licensed price feed is one adapter |
| `ai` | the routing policy loader over `model_routes`/`model_prices` (60 s cache); `prompts/<key>.v<N>.md`, immutable once merged and mirrored to the `prompts` table by CI; `evals/<task_class>/`; personas and per-persona op allowlists (from `personas.ts`); metering helpers around `ai_reserve`/`ai_settle`; generation of Anthropic `tools[]` and the MCP tool list from the ops registry | Deno, Node | `domain`, `db`, `integrations` (`llm`) | a prompt or a route never appears inline in a function or an op; a task class reaches a model only through a `model_routes` row | 100+; a second provider is a route row plus an adapter |

Apps import `domain`, `db`, `ui`, `documents`; never each other and never `../../`. Functions import `_shared/` (hand-written in P1; the built `db` + `domain` + `ai` from P3.0) and Deno std. The worker and the phone agent import the packages through the workspace — Node 22 strips types at load, as the phone agent already relies on (`services/phone-agent/package.json:7`), so the worker needs no bundler [A-1.8]. Seven sharing mechanisms (URL-path imports, Docker `COPY`, byte copies, SQL re-implementations, mirrored constants, npm workspaces that share nothing, hand-mirrored vocabularies) become one, and the one has a guard: the CI import-boundary rule (§8.3).

### 1.3 Where domain logic lives

Three homes, and only three:

1. **`packages/domain`** — pure functions with typed inputs. The modules that already meet this bar move verbatim into `engines/`, tests included: `fincalc.js`, `dryingcalc.js`, `completeness.js`, `dryingwatch.js`, `buildwatch.js`, `calibration.js`, `convert.js`, `xactimate.js`/`xlsx.js`, `selections.js`, `ai.js`, `myweekcalc.js`, `portal.js`'s projection, `narrative.js`'s fact builders, the GPP math and `DRY_STANDARDS` at `apps/field/js/core.js:705-734`, and `apps/board/js/schedule.js` — which is already consumed by four runtimes and proves the headless pattern works here. `spine.js`'s `toUnifiedRow` (`apps/field/js/spine.js:23-40`) goes to `extract/` with the projection; `model.js`'s factories to `schemas/`, its vocabularies and `COMPANY` to `vocab/`. From the functions: `phasematch.ts`, `aiphase.ts`, `emailmatch.ts`, `payments.ts`, `approve.ts`, `guards.ts`, `digest.ts`, `crewdigest.ts`, `weekly.ts`, `selections.ts`, `crewtoday.mjs`. The document models in `packages/documents/models` are assembly over these engines and compute nothing themselves (§6.2); the prompts in `packages/ai` are text, not rules.
2. **`op_<name>` SQL bodies** — the short, transactional operations of §2.3 and §2.5, written beside their `defineOp` file and shipped by migration. They are business logic in SQL by design (the `coordination_job_patch` and `push_project` pattern) and the registry knows it: CI diffs the catalog against deployed signatures, so an op body cannot drift from its declaration the way `merge_project_blobs` drifted through three definitions (217, 241, 243).
3. **`packages/domain/ops/*.ts` handlers** for `runtime:"edge"` and `runtime:"worker"` — estimating math, the detectors, agent loops (their prompts from `packages/ai`). Never rewritten in SQL.

What no longer holds logic: renderer closures (`invoice()` computes totals, pushes to QuickBooks, replaces items with an AI draft and paints print nodes in one ~530-line closure, `apps/field/js/forms.js:1378-1912`), browser chip executors (`apps/admin/js/finactions.js:74-303`; `apps/board/js/actions.js:399-414`; `apps/field/js/assist.js:179-193`), inline rules in edge functions (three totals ladders, five quiet-hour copies — one `policies/quietHours` from now on, 7 am–8 pm AK for every kind by default with the crew-line exemption an owner option per kind (§8.1 rule 14) — and six `LLM_PRICES` tables — Phase 2 §5 Table A), inert SQL seed rows (`200_ai_backbone.sql:279-315`, zero evaluators), and prose contracts (`roybal-ai-office/personas.ts:246-362`, which transcribe one-to-one into op schemas per Decision record §3). The 25 rules that exist in two or more places today collapse to one definition each; the two that already diverged silently (the QBO tax base and the selections over/under rule) stop being possible.

**The one deliberate exception:** `apps/field/js/merge.js` and `merge_project_blobs` stay twins (Decision record §6).
