The engine is kept byte-for-byte, so it does not move into `packages/domain`; it stays where the decision record cites it (`merge.js:32-36`), and the CI merge-parity test reads the SQL array out of the migration and compares. The twin ends when the last capture-owned section moves to rows, not before.

**Ceiling:** holds to 100+ people. The forcing condition is a rule that is a bad fit for plpgsql — it stays in the worker; and deploy coupling: one `domain` change rebuilds four bundles, `_shared/`, and the worker. esbuild makes that a 200 ms cost per bundle, not a ceiling.

### 1.4 One data-access pattern

Today the field app alone has eight access patterns (`apps/field/js/supa.js:74-86,101-129,133-194,211-265,278-294`), the board five, the admin six, the functions two (supabase-js ×4, raw fetch ×10), and `coordination_jobs` is protected by a rev guard implemented five times. The target is one pattern with three faces.

| Access | Pattern | Who | Replaces | Ceiling |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Reads** | PostgREST `SELECT` through `packages/db` typed helpers over tables and views, under RLS with the JWT claims of Decision record §5 (`principal_id`, `role`, `division_id`, `org_id`) and, for crew, the `assigned_job_ids()` initplan helper plus the 24-hour walk-on rule of §2.4; cursor pagination on every list (today `limit=1000` silently drops ~190 of 1,193 `time_entries`, `roybal-ai-office/index.ts:1409-1411`); aggregates as SQL views or `read` ops | browsers, functions, worker, agents (as the calling principal) | 50 inline query strings in 13 field files; 38 REST sites in `roybal-portal`; the board's three-table full read every 20 s | holds to 100+ with claims in the JWT and `STABLE` helpers; reporting is SQL views (`jobs_summary`, `ar_aging`, `crew_hours`, `automation_costs`) exposed as `read` ops behind the office Reports tab (P5, §7.7), materialized at hundreds of jobs; a read replica or warehouse export is the step after that |
| **Writes** | **operations only.** `db.ops.<name>(input)` routes by the registry's `runtime`: `sql` → `POST /rest/v1/rpc/op_<name>` (no edge hop, one transaction, event in the same transaction); `edge` → `POST /functions/v1/roybal-ops {op, input, idempotency_key}`; `worker` → `op_propose`/`enqueue` in SQL. **The browser never calls Fly.** Direct `INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE` privileges are revoked table by table behind each op, the way `219_revoke_direct_field_writes.sql:44-46` already did for `field_projects` | everyone, including agents (`propose_<name>` only, §2.4) | five rev-guard implementations; `finactions.js` IndexedDB writes; `boardpush.js` same-rev annotation PATCHes; the service-role PATCH that bypasses the merge (`qbo-proxy/index.ts:429-444`); four function-to-function auth conventions | holds to 100+; an op that exceeds ~50 writes/s sustained moves its body to SQL or the worker, nothing else changes |
| **Offline capture** | the blob keeps its door: `push_project` (`241_merge_delete_tombstones.sql:193-282`) unchanged, listed in the catalog as `job.push_blob` for description and permission only — not wrapped, and it emits no events (Decision record §4). Capture-owned sections (moisture maps, drying logs, daily logs, photos, work auth, contents, sketches) ride it; `job.project` turns them into rows | field app | nothing; this is the part that works | per job, not per company (Decision record §6): >~5 devices editing one job, an office feature that must *edit* a capture section, or blobs over ~1 MB move that section to rows |
| **Offline ops** | `packages/db` in the field bundle has an outbox mode: an op called while `likelyOffline()` (or failing on network) is written to an IndexedDB `outbox` store `{id = idempotency key, op, input, attempts}` and drained after each successful pull (P3). `actionType:"capture"` ops (`equipment.place/remove`, `reading.record`) carry a client uuid and crew `execute` rights, so they never wait for approval | field app | today's online-only, silently skipped writes (`apps/field/js/boardpush.js:204`; `spine.js:83`) | iOS has no background sync — the outbox flushes on foreground (Decision record §14; §6.17) |

The office-app replica (every admin tab boots the field sync engine and pulls every blob with media, `apps/admin/js/admin.js:8-11,34`) ends at P5 when the office app reads rows through `packages/db`; until then it keeps working. `sendText` in the field stays inside the synchronous tap window iOS requires (`apps/field/js/assist.js:229-236`) and records a proposal.

### 1.5 One document pipeline (summary — detail in §6 Part A)

`buildDocumentModel(kind, ids)` in `packages/documents/models` (pure; totals through `domain/engines/fincalc`) → `renderHtml(model, template, carrierProfile)` in `packages/documents` → PDF by Chromium in the worker's isolated render child process, **or** browser print of the same HTML, so offline printing keeps working. Playwright and the Chromium build are pinned in the worker image, so an issued version re-renders from `model_json` + `template_version` to the same `pdf_sha256` and the `documents` bucket is rebuildable rather than backed up (§6.7). Numbers come from `document_sequences`, never from array length (`apps/admin/js/finactions.js:89,137,250` repeat after a delete); issued versions are immutable; the letterhead is defined once instead of six times (`formkit.js:290-307`, `model.js:6-24`, `print.css:33-55`, `app.css:476-488`, `photoshare.js:277-279`, `board.css:356-390`). The six `window.print()` sites (`apps/field/js/app.js:1552,1625,1936,2400,2413,2458`) are repointed at the pipeline's HTML through `packages/documents/print.js`; the DOM-snapshot share (`apps/field/js/photoshare.js:154-174`) is deleted once packets are PDFs. **Ceiling:** hundreds of documents/day per render process; >1,000/day or a sub-2 s SLA → a second render machine.

### 1.6 One design system

Two navies have been in production since 2026-06-18: `#16395a` on every screen (`apps/field/css/app.css:29`, inherited by admin and board, hand-copied into `apps/portal/css/portal.css:4`) and `#0f1b2d` in print (13 lines of `apps/field/css/print.css`: 35, 43, 60, 95-96, 109, 149, 159, 170, 218, 228, 232, 250), on the site (`apps/site/src/styles/brand.css:9`), in `README.md:44`, and asserted wrongly by `apps/board/css/board.css:5`. The orange literal `#f26a21` appears 51 times across 20 files.

Decisions:

- **One navy: `#0f1b2d`** [A-1.1]. It is the brand value the README and the site carry and the value every printed document already uses; the screens are the drift. Printed output does not change; the office sees a darker header.
- **`packages/ui/tokens.css` is the only file that defines a colour.** The 31 custom properties of `app.css:6-48` become it; `portal.css:3-10` and the three legal pages' inline copies link it; `apps/site/src/styles/brand.css` imports it at Astro build time and keeps only its typography scale. Literals become `var(--navy)` / `var(--orange)`; the letterhead's inline `color:var(--orange,#f26a21)` (`formkit.js:298`) becomes a class.
- **`print.css` is owned by `packages/documents` — not by an app and not by `packages/ui`.** It imports `tokens.css` from `packages/ui`; the field app links it as `media="print"` from the package; the render child process inlines `tokens.css` + `print.css` into the HTML it hands Chromium; the portal's packet print uses the same file. Its 13 navy literals become the token; every other rule ports verbatim, because the pagination rules at `print.css:6,20,25-30,82-87,111,238-259` were learned from adjuster pushback and are the asset. A change to `print.css` must pass the golden harness (§6.12).
- **`design-system/`** (20 static pages nothing imports, loading unpinned `cdn.tailwindcss.com`) is deleted; a gallery is regenerated from `tokens.css` when someone needs one.

**Ceiling:** none.

### 1.7 Build and tooling

**Decision: esbuild bundling per app** (field, office — admin and board until P5 — and portal) from `packages/*`, one `tools/build.mjs`, no framework. The no-build import-map option lost because it leaves the two most recent production defects in place: the hand-bumped `BUILD` (`apps/field/js/config.js:25`, `v165`) versus the service-worker cache (`apps/field/sw.js:6`, `v166`) that shipped red in PR #181 with `build.test.mjs` failing — and, because `apps/field/package.json:10` and `package.json:11` chain every suite with `&&`, silencing 26 field files, 4 board files and 13 function tests behind it; and a hand-maintained precache (`sw.js:8-32`) that omits two statically imported modules and includes deprecated `pricing.js`. Import maps also cannot code-split a boot path that loads 42 of 44 modules (923 KB raw).

**When: P3.0 "toolchain", the first sub-step of P3 (§7.5) — not P1.** E0 fixes the v165/v166 pair by hand and puts `build.test.mjs` behind the PR gate; P1 ships no bundler — its ~5 operations are SQL functions, its worker imports the workspace directly, its `_shared/` is hand-written — and no existing module leaves its directory. P3.0 then lands `tools/build.mjs`, the generated `BUILD`/`COMMIT`, the generated service worker and the verbatim package moves of §1.10 in one sub-step, because P3's `roomIds` build must be a generated build and P3's projection imports the engines from the package. Its effort is counted inside P3's L; nothing in P1 or P2 depends on it.

What `tools/build.mjs` does, and how it meets the service worker:

| Step | Output | What it retires |
|---|---|---|
| `esbuild --bundle --format=esm --splitting` per app, content-hashed chunks | `dist/field`, `dist/office` (`dist/admin` + `dist/board` until P5), `dist/portal` | the `../../js` coupling; `board/js/schedule.js` leaves the field precache (`sw.js:27`) because the field bundle carries `packages/domain/engines/schedule`; the board's `?v=64`/`?v=39` hand bumps (`apps/board/index.html:16,53`) and its 3.5 s stale-module reload guard (`index.html:34-48`) |
| generate `config.generated.js` with `BUILD` and `COMMIT` | one constant, two consumers | the hand bump |
| generate `sw.js` with `CACHE = "roybal-field-" + BUILD` and the precache list from esbuild's metafile plus assets; pdf.js stays vendored, lazy (`apps/field/js/pdf.js:10-14`) and in `OPTIONAL` | precache equals the module graph by construction | the hand-maintained `CORE` list |
| `build.test.mjs` stays, asserting a *generated* constant | a guard against hand edits, in CI | the red-on-main state |

**BUILD must stay a monotonic integer.** `_sync_guard` strips non-digits and compares as `int` (`218_sync_rpcs_and_build_gate.sql:79-84`, redefined identically at `226_sync_client_telemetry.sql:57-59`), and `supa.js:102` sends `p_build: BUILD` on every sync RPC. A build tag derived from a git SHA would compare as garbage (or parse to `null` and bypass the gate), so `BUILD = "v" + git rev-list --count HEAD` (489 today; monotonic on `main` because merges only add commits) and the SHA rides separately as `COMMIT`, for display and telemetry [A-1.7]. A rebuild of the same commit yields the same `BUILD`, so CI asserts the number is *strictly greater* than `origin/main`'s only when a PR touches `apps/{field,admin,board,office}`; a hotfix branch that rebases is rebuilt before deploy; `config.generated.js` is a build output, never committed. The weekly `min_field_build → latest−2` proposal of Decision record §12 works on that count.

The field bundle and today's admin and board bundles (the office app from P5) keep one origin and one service worker at scope `/` (`apps/field/js/app.js:2675`) through P5 [A-1.6]; because all of them publish from one commit and navigations are network-first, version skew between them is at most one open. Typecheck (P3.0, with today's violators allowlisted and a `TODO(types)` counter that may only fall, §8.2): `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` with `checkJs` + JSDoc for the 69 app files (no rewrite of 17k lines), strict TypeScript in `packages/*` and `services/*` (the flags in `tsconfig.base.json:1-14` finally have a consumer), `deno check` for the five functions; Biome for lint, format and the import boundaries; `node --test` across globs, no `&&` chains; `apps/field/js/calibration.test.mjs` moves to `test/` so it stops shipping to the public origin. **Ceiling:** none for scale; a build of this size is ~200 ms.

**Option left to the owner:** no-build import maps serving `packages/` at `/lib/`, JSDoc + `checkJs`, and drift tests (lockstep, precache, merge parity). Zero toolchain to operate; it keeps the URL coupling, keeps the lockstep and the precache as human duties, and cannot split the boot path. Viable while the fleet is six devices; it is not the recommendation.

### 1.8 Environments and CI

| Environment | What | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **Production** | Supabase `djpgvcvhvgrzgaziruze` (Micro, 1 GB; the compute upgrade is a P3 step with a trigger metric, §7.12); Fly `roybal-phone` and `roybal-worker`; GitHub Pages for field/admin/board (the office app from P5); Vercel `roybal-restoration-app-web` for the portal (never delete); Cloudflare Pages `roybal-site-pages` | schema reaches it only by `db push` from CI after staging passes; never the SQL editor or MCP `apply_migration` (`supabase/config.toml:1-21` records why MCP deploys are forbidden) |
| **Staging** | a **second Supabase project** seeded from `pg_dump` (schema plus scrubbed data) — not branching, which would replay a journal that cannot replay (`105` needs `200`; `233/236/244` scrape `cron.job`) | function secrets duplicated; the worker runs against staging from CI or a laptop, no second Fly app [A-1.2]; cost = the project's plan fee [A-0.6] |
| **CI (`ci.yml`)** | **on PR:** Biome · `tsc` · `deno check` · `node --test` across field/board/functions/phone-agent · BUILD/SW lockstep · migration replay on the baseline (`supabase start` + `db reset`) · merge-parity (JS vs SQL array) · golden document diffs · `operation_catalog` diff · `PERMISSIONS.md` regeneration must be clean · site build. **on merge:** staging `db push` → tests against staging → prod `db push` → `functions deploy` from `config.toml` → bundles (tests, READMEs, `serve.mjs` excluded — today `deploy-field.yml:36-38` copies whole directories) → `fly deploy` worker → record `latest_field_build` | the assembly step of `deploy-field.yml` survives as the publish job with gates in front of it; the workflow's trigger on the deleted branch (`:13`) goes |
| **Local** | `node tools/build.mjs --watch` + one `tools/serve.mjs` over `dist/` (replacing four `serve.mjs` copies); `supabase start` for the baseline | the site keeps manual `wrangler pages deploy` until the owner puts a Cloudflare token in GitHub [A-1.3] |

The gates arrive in the order the phases need them, each a required check from the day it lands: **E0** — every `node --test` suite without `&&` chains and the BUILD/SW lockstep test; **P1** — migration replay on the baseline, merge-parity, the `operation_catalog` diff, `PERMISSIONS.md` regeneration, the staging → prod `db push` chain and `fly deploy` for the worker; **P3.0** — Biome, `tsc`, `deno check`, the import boundaries, the drift guards and the `build` job that publishes `dist/`; **P4** — golden document diffs.

**Ceiling:** 100+ people; two environments and one deploy discipline. A second region or project is per-environment config, nothing structural.

### 1.9 Decision → holds to → forces change

| Decision | Holds to | What forces change |
|---|---|---|
| One monorepo, six real packages (`domain`, `db`, `documents`, `ui`, `integrations`, `ai`), apps bundled from them | 100+ people | a second product line sold outside → publish the packages; deploy coupling (one `domain` change rebuilds everything) is a cost, not a ceiling |
| Domain logic only in `packages/domain`, `op_*` SQL bodies beside their definitions, worker handlers for the rest | 100+ | a rule that is a bad fit for plpgsql stays in the worker; the `merge.js`↔SQL twin ends when the last capture section moves to rows |
| Typed reads under RLS; **all** writes as operations; blob door for capture; field outbox for non-blob ops | 100+ with JWT claims, `STABLE` helpers and the `assigned_job_ids()` initplan (Decision record §5; §2.4) | reporting at hundreds of jobs → materialized views, then a replica; per-element multi-device offline editing → that section moves to rows plus the outbox (Decision record §6) |
| One document pipeline, HTML shared by browser print and worker PDF | hundreds/day per render process | >1,000/day or sub-2 s SLA → second render machine; ESX/XactAnalysis stays a human step recorded on the submittal (§6.10) |
| One design system: `tokens.css`, one navy, `print.css` owned by `documents` | none | none |
| esbuild + generated `BUILD` (monotonic integer) + generated precache, landing at P3.0 | none for scale | never for headcount |
| Field app wrapped, never rewritten | per job: hundreds of concurrent jobs with a handful of devices each | >~5 devices on one job, an office edit of a capture section, blobs over ~1 MB — one section at a time |
| Five edge functions by trust level; worker a second Fly app, never co-hosted with the phone socket | 100+ for throughput; **availability** is one machine, one region, no HA | HA → a second machine for the poller only; MCP (streamable HTTP) and Agent SDK sessions need sticky routing (§2.5) |
| Prod + staging project + one CI | 100+ | a second region or legal entity → per-environment config and the `org_id` claim (Decision record §7; ADR-11) |

Modes: **verbatim** = moved, content unchanged, tests move with it · **as-spec** = rebuilt from the existing code as its specification · **repoint** = kept, its calls redirected · **rewrite** = replaced · **delete**. Phases refer to §7 (Decision record §15); **P3.0** is the toolchain sub-step that opens P3 (§1.7) — no existing module changes directory before it, and P1 (weeks 3–6, M) is the contract tables, ~5 SQL operations, the `roybal-ops` shell, the worker skeleton, `submittals` and the smoke test, nothing more.
