| `qbo-proxy/index.ts` (+`payments.ts`) | `integrations/qbo` adapter + outbox deliverer keyed on document version; service-role PATCH around the merge (`:429-444`) deleted; `payments.ts` → `domain/engines`; bills/expenses from receipts and materials, customer creation and class per division follow as further outbox channels behind the same adapter | split | E0 (gates) · P4 · P6 (scope, §7.8) |
| `gmail-proxy/index.ts` (+`emailmatch.ts`) | `integrations/gmail` (pull job + outbox send — inline in the worker for P1's reminder lane, lifted at P3.0); `emailmatch.ts` → `domain/engines` | split | E0 · P1 · P3.0 |
| `magicplan-proxy` `magicplan-webhook` + `MAGICPLAN_*` secrets | — | delete | E0 |
| CORS ×11, `x-cron-secret` ×6, `json()` ×5, `monthSpend` ×6, `LLM_PRICES` ×5 + `PRICES` ×1 | `_shared/` (one CORS, one `current_principal()`, one envelope — hand-written in P1, built from the packages at P3.0) or gone | delete | P1 |
| **services** | | | |
| `phone-agent/server.mjs` `brain.mjs` `tools.mjs` | stay; own Fly app forever; `Dockerfile:8-9` `COPY` → workspace imports; in-memory rate-limit `Map` (`tools.mjs:19-27`) → Postgres | repoint | P7 |
| `phone-agent/supa.mjs` · `config.mjs` price table | `packages/db` · `model_prices` | verbatim / delete | P7 |
| `services/worker` | new: `roybal-worker`. P1 skeleton = queue poller + outbox deliverer + heartbeat (`worker_heartbeats`), with its Gmail, Twilio and QB Time adapters inline under `services/worker/adapters/` until P3.0 lifts them into `packages/integrations`; the stale-heartbeat alert is pg_cron every 5 min → `net.http_post` → `roybal-webhooks /alert` (service-secret header) → the owner's SMS through the Twilio adapter, the one documented exception to "pg_cron never calls HTTP" (§2.5) — it does not ride the outbox; Agent SDK, MCP over streamable HTTP, the render child and the projection arrive with their phases | new | P1 |
| **SQL** | | | |
| `push_project` `merge_project_blobs` `_sync_guard` `tombstone_project` `revive_project` | unchanged (`_sync_guard`'s role list is redefined once, §2.4) | verbatim | — |
| `coordination_job_patch`, `web_*` | the pattern for `op_<name>`; `web_turn_begin/end` → `ai_reserve/settle` | as-spec | P1 |
| `unified_jobs` | `jobs` by in-place `alter table … rename`, plus a compatibility view named `unified_jobs` kept until every reader has moved (phone agent, brief, portal, field `spine.js`, old PWA builds) — never a copy (§3.5) | rename | P3 |
| `project_field_photos`, `repair_field_photos`, nightly cron | retire after 30 zero-diff days of `job.project` | delete | P3 |
| era-0 tables (20), 6 enums, `is_assigned_to_job`, `generate_job_number`, `set_invoice_number`, `compute_is_dry` | schema `legacy` | move | P3 |
| `phase_templates` `required_forms` `field_requirements` `phase_instances` `completeness_state` (zero app references; the 29 JS rules are the spec) | schema `legacy` [A-1.4] | move | P3 |
| `migrations/001…246` | `migrations_archive/`; `0000_baseline.sql` from `supabase db dump` | archive | P1 |
| HTTP `cron.job` rows with literal secrets | `cron_register.sql` inserting `jobs_queue` rows (the heartbeat check above is the one HTTP job that stays) | rewrite | E0 / P1 |
| **tooling** | | | |
| `.github/workflows/deploy-field.yml` | `ci.yml` (assembly step kept as the publish job) | rewrite | E0 (test gate) · P1 (migration and deploy chain) · P3.0 (build job) |
| `tsconfig.base.json` · `deno.lock` | consumed by `tsconfig.json` · regenerated, consumed by `deno check` | keep | P3.0 |
| `wrangler.jsonc` (unused Workers project) · `design-system/` · lockfile ghosts for `apps/web`, `apps/mobile`, `packages/shared` | — | delete | E0 / P3.0 |
| `.site-archive/` | kept — it is a build input for the site's parity check | keep | — |

The table has one property worth stating: every row marked **verbatim** is a file that already runs under bare Node with tests, and every row marked **rewrite** is a file that today can only run in a signed-in browser tab or a copy-pasted function. The strangler moves the first group in P3.0 without changing a line of behaviour — nothing in P1 or P2 waits for it — and replaces the second group only when its op exists and has run beside it.

---

## 2. The automation layer

Today the company has five disconnected pieces of an approval loop and no loop. A server-verified proposal ledger exists for three action kinds (`supabase/migrations/210_pending_actions.sql:24-38`) and is approved only by a text from one phone number (`supabase/functions/roybal-notify/index.ts:400-401`); it has been jammed since 2026-07-26 by three rows written on 2026-07-25 that expired the next day, because nothing ever writes `expired` (`210:33`) and `qb-time-proxy` counts them against `MAX_LIVE_PROPOSALS = 3` with no expiry filter (`supabase/functions/qb-time-proxy/index.ts:285`, `:494-499`) **(live)**. Every other write the assistant may propose — 17 action types — is a prose contract (`supabase/functions/roybal-ai-office/personas.ts:244-245`: "each desc IS the params spec") executed by a browser tab with a best-effort audit PATCH (`apps/field/js/assist.js:179-223`). A cost ledger exists but any crew login can delete it (`supabase/migrations/201_ai_usage.sql:78-79`). A reserve-before-spend gate exists for one lane only (`supabase/migrations/227_web_receptionist.sql:147-211`). Background work is pg_cron firing `net.http_post`, whose failures are invisible: two integration lanes have been dead for days while `cron.job_run_details` reports success **(live)**. Nothing in the system can wake an agent when state changes, and nothing can render a document without a human's print click.

This section defines the one loop that replaces those five, as tables in the same Supabase project, one operation registry, and one worker. Nothing below is thrown away: §2.8 shows where each existing piece lands. The domain entities the loop acts on are in §3; the walk of one invoice through it is in §4; the ADRs behind each choice are in §5; the phases that build it are in §7. Section numbers below are the assembled document's (§1–§8); the decision record is cited as "Decision record §n"; assumptions made in this section are tagged [A-2.n] and the record's as [A-0.n].

### 2.1 The loop, end to end

```
 READ ──────────► PROPOSE ─────────────► APPROVE ────────────────► EXECUTE ───────────► RECORD
 typed views,     proposals row           op_proposal_approve()     op_<name>() (sql)     events row in the
 read ops under   (inert; input           inbox tap | chip |        or jobs_queue →       same transaction;
 RLS as the       immutable; sms_code)    SMS YES n (sync, via      worker/edge handler   outbox row for every
 principal                                roybal-webhooks) | policy re-verifies the row   external side effect
                                                                                              │
      agents (worker) · cron (SQL insert) · UI · MCP · webhooks all enter here                ▼
                                                                              deliverer: Twilio / Gmail / QBO
                                                                              with idempotency_key + provider_id
```

Three rules make it one system:

1. **A proposal is data, never code.** It names an operation and carries a validated input; approving it changes nothing until the operation runs. This is the posture `210:4-8` already states ("Nothing in this table DOES anything by existing"), generalised from three kinds to every write.
2. **Every state change is an `events` row, appended in the same transaction as the change.** Today `pending_actions.result` is overwritten in place (`roybal-notify/index.ts:465-468`) and chip execution is a client PATCH that "never blocks the chip" (`assist.js:211-223`). Blob pushes are the one exception (Decision record §6): `push_project` stays byte-for-byte and `blob_history` remains its pre-image; semantic events come only from operations (see §2.2, `events`).
3. **Every external side effect is an `outbox` row with an idempotency key.** `sendSms` inserts a row and then posts to Twilio with no key (`roybal-notify/index.ts:335-368`), browser `sendEmail` has none (`supabase/functions/gmail-proxy/index.ts:340-349`), and `pushInvoice` creates a new QBO invoice whenever `qboInvoiceId` is absent (`supabase/functions/qbo-proxy/index.ts:345-356`).

The five verbs and who performs them:

| Verb | Who | Where it runs | What it writes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Read | any principal holding `read` on the action type, scoped by role and assignment (§2.4) | PostgREST views under RLS; read ops as Anthropic/MCP tools | nothing (agent reads are counted in `agent_runs.tool_calls`) |
| Propose | humans (inbox, chip, voice), agents (worker), cron rules, MCP sessions | `op_propose()` SECURITY DEFINER | `proposals` (status `proposed`) + `events(proposal.created)` |
| Approve | a human holding `approve` (inbox tap, chip, SMS `YES n`) or an `approval_policies` row | `op_proposal_approve()` / `op_proposal_decline()` — the SQL bodies of `proposal.approve@1` and `proposal.decline@1`, written `op_approve`/`op_decline` for short below. The SMS channel calls them **synchronously from `roybal-webhooks`** as the principal resolved from the sender's number (§2.4), so approval never waits for the worker | `proposals` transition + `events(proposal.approved)`; for `runtime:"sql"` the execution happens inside the same call |
| Execute | the per-op body: `op_<name>()` in Postgres, an edge handler, or the worker under a machine principal | per `operation_catalog.runtime` | domain rows + `events(<op>.executed)` + `outbox` rows |
| Record | automatic | trigger-enforced | `events` (append-only), `ai_usage`, `agent_runs`, `integration_runs`, `outbox` status settled by provider callbacks |

**Ceiling.** The loop is per-row and human- or rule-bounded: hundreds of proposals a day and several thousand queue jobs a day on one worker hold to 100+ people (the arithmetic is at the top of §2.2). The forcing conditions are organisational, not technical: cross-department SLAs force a workflow engine layered *on top of* these tables, never instead of them; strict FIFO across several workers forces `pgmq` or a broker behind the same `claimJob()` seam.

### 2.2 The contract in Postgres

**Load arithmetic at 100+ people, stated before the tables.** Two ceilings below say "several thousand queue jobs a day" and "one heap to low millions of events"; this is what those numbers mean against the brief's target (100 people, ~60 field devices, hundreds of concurrent jobs), so that each "holds to 100+" is a computed claim rather than a phrase [A-2.7].

| Quantity | Today (live) | At 100+ | Where it lands |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sync cycles | 6 devices; the 45 s timer at `apps/field/js/sync.js:623` is a **pull** ("catch others' changes"); pushes fire on save through `schedulePush()` (`:617`) | ~60 devices × 8 h ÷ 45 s ≈ **38k pulls/day**; pushes ≈ saves, ~2–5k/day. ADR-05's "~190k pushes/day" is the pull cadence at 100 devices, and pulls are cursor reads | Postgres read load on the Micro; p95 `push_project` is the tripwire in the compute-tier metric (Decision record §11) |
| Projection jobs | 0 | **Worst case, the bound the queue is built for:** ~300 active jobs × one push per 45 s cycle ≈ **500k projection jobs/day** if unbatched. The debounce — `run_after = now() + 60 s` with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` while a row for that job is queued or leased — caps it at **≤1 projection per job per minute**, ≈ 430k/day. **Expected:** pushes fire on save, so ~300 jobs × ~20 edit bursts/day ≈ **6k jobs/day**, each running `extractProject()` over a ≤227 kB blob | the table stays partition-free, indexed on `(status, run_after)`, and purged daily (`job.project` `done` rows after 24 h; other kinds after 14 days). Even the expected load reaches the "several thousand jobs/day" ceiling on its own, so **worker CPU** for projection is the first thing to measure and `job.project` is the first kind to move to a second poller machine |
| Semantic events | 0 | ~100 people × ~150 ops/day ≈ **15–20k events/day** ≈ 5M in **9–12 months** | month partitioning arrives *with* the headcount, inside the first year at 100+: it is a **P5 roadmap item** (§7.7), not "later"; the ~5M-rows-or-compute-upgrade trigger stays as the earlier-if-sooner condition |
| Proposals | 3 (jammed) | ~200–500/day (billing, schedule, comms, captures for review) | hundreds/day is the stated ceiling; SLA routing is the forcing condition |
| Outbox rows | 175 SMS frozen | ~1–2k/day (crew texts, reminders, QBO, email) | Twilio and Intuit throttles bound it, not the table |
| Renders | 0 | ~50–150/day; photo packets ~10% | under the "hundreds/day per process" ceiling (§6) |

Two corrections follow. ADR-05's "~190k potential pushes/day" overstates the write side — the sync engine pushes only dirty jobs — but the *read* side at that cadence is real and is what the Micro feels first, which is why the compute upgrade trigger includes p95 `push_project`; the design still tolerates the worst case, which is why the projection is debounced per job rather than merely "one per burst". And the queue's "several thousand jobs/day on one worker" is met by the projection alone at hundreds of active jobs, so the honest statement is: `jobs_queue` as a table holds to 100+; **one worker machine** holds to roughly 100–150 *actively edited* jobs of projection plus everything else, after which the projection kind gets its own poller — the `fly scale count 2` case the record already allows.

All tables live in `public` with `org_id` defaulted (Decision record §7, tenancy) and `division_id` where the row is job-scoped. Every actor column is the pair `(principal_kind, principal_id)` with `principal_kind ∈ {human, agent, policy, system}`.

| Table | Columns | State machine and rules |
|---|---|---|
| **`proposals`** (supersedes `pending_actions`) | `id`, `operation` (`name@version`), `action_type`, `input jsonb`, `input_schema_hash`, `edited_params jsonb`, `proposed_by_kind/_id`, `proposed_via` (`ui`/`chip`/`agent`/`cron`/`sms`/`mcp`/`voice`), `agent_run_id`, `rationale`, `evidence_refs jsonb`, `division_id`, `job_id`, `claim_id`, `assigned_role`, `assignee_id`, `escalate_after`, `sms_code int`, `status`, `expires_at`, `approved_by_kind/_ref`, `approved_via` (`inbox`/`chip`/`sms`/`policy`/`override`), `approved_at`, `decline_reason`, `execution_job_id`, `executed_event_id`, `result jsonb`, `error`, `supersedes_id`, `idempotency_key unique` | `proposed → approved \| declined \| expired \| superseded`; `approved → executing → executed \| failed`. `input` is immutable after insert (trigger raises); edits before approval go to `edited_params`. Every transition emits an event. `sms_code` is allocated by a sequence inside `op_propose` with a partial unique index `where status = 'proposed'` — this deletes the two app-side "lowest free integer" allocators (`qb-time-proxy/index.ts:345-356`; `supabase/functions/roybal-brief/index.ts:262-266`). `approved_via = 'sms'` rows are written synchronously by `roybal-webhooks` calling `op_proposal_approve`/`op_proposal_decline` (§2.4) — never through the queue. A schedule proposal is ONE row whose input holds N assignments and is applied atomically. A repeat `idempotency_key` returns the existing row. |
| **`events`** | `id bigint identity`, `at`, `principal_kind/_id`, `kind` (`proposal.approved`, `equipment.placed`, `document.issued`…), `operation`, `aggregate_type`, `aggregate_id`, `job_id`, `claim_id`, `division_id`, `proposal_id`, `correlation_id`, `causation_id`, `data jsonb`, `idempotency_key unique where not null` | Append-only: a trigger raises on UPDATE or DELETE for every role including the service role; INSERT is revoked from `authenticated` and happens only through `emit_event()` SECURITY DEFINER, which every `op_*` body calls in its own transaction. RLS read is scoped by the JWT claims of §2.4 (`role`, `division_id`, `org_id`) for office and owner, and by the `assigned_job_ids()` initplan helper for crew — one evaluation per statement, never a per-row join. |
| **`jobs_queue`** | `id`, `kind`, `payload jsonb`, `principal_id`, `priority`, `run_after`, `attempts`, `max_attempts` (default 5), `status`, `locked_by`, `lease_until`, `heartbeat_at`, `last_error`, `idempotency_key unique`, `job_id`, `correlation_id`, `created_at`, `finished_at` | `queued → leased → done \| failed (retry: run_after = now() + 2^attempts min, capped 1 h) \| dead (attempts ≥ max_attempts)`. Claim (below) sets `locked_by` and `lease_until` as columns, so the lease outlives the claiming transaction — `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` alone releases at commit and a dead worker would orphan the row. Per-kind lease: 5 min default, 10 min `document.render`, 30 min `agent.run`; the worker heartbeats every 30 s and extends `lease_until` [A-2.2]; a pg_cron sweeper every minute requeues `leased` rows past their lease. Indexed on `(status, run_after)`; partition-free by design (the arithmetic above). Dead rows text the owner through the outbox with a 24 h resend guard [A-2.6] — the worker is alive in that case; the dead-*worker* alert takes the separate path in §2.5, Amendment 0. |
| **`outbox`** | `id`, `channel` (`sms`/`email`/`qbo`/`portal`), `operation`, `payload jsonb`, `idempotency_key unique`, `status`, `provider_id` (Twilio SID, Gmail id, QBO Id + SyncToken), `provider_status`, `attempts`, `next_attempt_at`, `sent_at`, `delivered_at`, `error`, `principal_id`, `proposal_id`, `job_id`, `cost_usd` | `pending → sending → sent → delivered \| failed (retry) \| dead`. One row per external side effect; the deliverer in the worker claims like the queue. The Twilio status callback (absent today — 174 of 175 outbound rows frozen at `queued` **(live)**; `supabase/config.toml:53-54` claims a handler) settles `delivered`; the monthly SMS cap counts `delivered`, not `queued`. `sms_messages` stays as the SMS log; `outbox` is the delivery state. Every delivery also writes an `integration_runs` row (§2.6). The outbox is never the path for the dead-worker alert (§2.5). |
| **`agent_runs`** | `id`, `agent_id`, `trigger` (`queue`/`chat`/`cron`/`mcp`/`webhook`), `task_class`, `route_key`, `model_id`, `prompt_id`, `prompt_version`, `input_refs jsonb`, `tool_calls`, `proposals_created`, `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_read_tokens`, `cost_usd`, `status` (`running`/`succeeded`/`failed`/`capped`/`refused`), `refusal_category`, `error`, `started_at`, `finished_at`, `job_id`, `claim_id`, `proposal_id`, `queue_job_id`, `trace jsonb` | One row per loop; `ai_usage` rows reference it. `trace` holds tool names, durations and row ids — never customer text or model output [A-2.4]. `job_id`/`claim_id` are what make "what did automation cost on this claim" one query. |
| **`agents`** + **`agent_authority`** | `agents(id, name, kind, auth_user_id nullable, enabled, created_by)`; `agent_authority(id, agent_id, operation` or `action_type:*`, `capability` (`read`/`propose`/`execute`), `conditions jsonb {max_amount_usd, kinds[], quiet_hours, division_ids[]}`, `granted_by`, `granted_at`, `expires_at`, `revoked_at`, `revoked_by`, `reason)` | Deny by default: a new agent can read what its grant says and propose; it can never approve. `agent_authority.grant` and `.revoke` are operations that emit events. Replaces the 26 restrictive policies naming two literal emails (`supabase/migrations/204_phone_agent_rls.sql:12-31`; `205_brief_machine_rls.sql:11-24`). **Seed rows (P1):** `agent:billing`, `agent:projection`, `agent:outbox`, `agent:brief`, `agent:phone`, `agent:web` — the worker selects its principal per queue kind from a table in `services/worker`, never from a default. |
| **`role_permissions`** + **`approval_policies`** | `role_permissions(role, operation` or `action_type:*`, `capability` (`read`/`propose`/`approve`/`execute`), `scope` (`all`/`division`/`assigned`/`own`), `conditions jsonb)`; `approval_policies(id, action_type, operation nullable, division_id nullable, max_amount_usd, approver_role, auto bool, active)` | Data, not code: edited only through the owner's `role.grant` op (an event). A CI snapshot asserts every catalog operation has a row or an explicit deny. `approval_policies` gives money its magnitude dimension: office approves `invoice.issue` up to a threshold the owner sets, owner above [A-0.3]; a row with `auto = true` approves below its threshold and stamps `approved_by_kind = 'policy'`, `approved_by_ref = approval_policies.id` — "who approved" is never empty. The per-kind quiet-hours setting of §2.4 (which comms kinds, if any, may leave the 7 am–8 pm window) also lives in `conditions` here — a `notification_policies` table if the owner wants it per division. |
| **`operation_catalog`** | `name`, `version`, `action_type`, `description`, `input_schema jsonb`, `output_schema jsonb`, `runtime` (`sql`/`edge`/`worker`), `approval_default`, `amount_field`, `emits text[]`, `idempotency_template`, `definition_sha`, `published_at`, `deprecated_at` | Published by CI from `packages/domain/ops`; the DB never invents an operation. `op_propose` validates `input` against `input_schema` with `pg_jsonschema` [A-0.2]. CI diffs the catalog against deployed `op_*` signatures. |
| **`external_refs`** + **`external_intents`** | `external_refs(entity_type, entity_id, system` (`qbo`/`qbtime`/`gmail`/`twilio`/`portal`), `external_id`, `sync_token`, `last_synced_at`, `status)` unique on `(system, external_id)` and on `(entity_type, entity_id, system)`; `external_intents(id, outbox_id, system, operation, idempotency_key, request_digest, created_at, external_id, resolved_at)` | One home for every integration id — replaces `qboInvoiceId` in the blob, `qbTimesheetId` in JSON with no unique index (`supabase/migrations/102_time_entries.sql:14-36`), `contacts.qbo_customer_id`. An adapter writes the intent row *before* the provider call; on retry with no `external_id` it looks the object up (QBO by `DocNumber`) before creating, because the real failure is a response lost after the provider created it. |
| **`api_tokens`** | `id`, `principal_id`, `name`, `scopes text[]`, `token_hash`, `expires_at` (default 30 days [A-2.3]), `last_used_at`, `revoked_at`, `created_by` | Minted in the admin by the owner; presented as a bearer to the MCP endpoint the worker serves over streamable HTTP (§2.3). See §2.4, MCP identity. |
| **`worker_heartbeats`** | `worker_id`, `version`, `at`, `queue_depth`, `leased` | Upserted every 30 s by each worker process. "Is the worker alive" is a query, not a Fly dashboard. A pg_cron SQL check every 5 minutes reads it; when the newest row is older than **10 minutes** it calls `net.http_post` to `roybal-webhooks` `/alert` with a service-secret header, and that function sends the owner one SMS **synchronously** through the Twilio adapter — the one documented exception to "pg_cron never calls HTTP"; the outbox is not used for this alert (§2.5, Amendment 0). |
| **`ai_caps`** | `lane`, `period` (`day`/`month`), `cap_usd`, `enabled` | Caps as rows, generalising the five `SPEND_CAP_USD` reads (`roybal-ai-office/index.ts:68`) and the web lane's three-tier cap (`227:147-165`). `enabled = false` is the kill switch. Lands in P1 as a rename of the existing cap state. |

The claim statement, which is the only code that knows the queue is a table:

```sql
update jobs_queue j
   set status = 'leased', locked_by = $worker, lease_until = now() + $lease, attempts = attempts + 1
 where j.id = (select id from jobs_queue
                where status = 'queued' and run_after <= now()
                order by priority desc, run_after
                for update skip locked limit 1)
returning j.*;
```

**The expiry sweep** (pg_cron, every 5 minutes, SQL only) writes `expired` where `status = 'proposed' and expires_at < now()` and emits the event. This is what un-jams today's spine on day one. **Inbox scoping:** a person sees `mine (assignee_id) ∪ my role in my division ∪ unassigned in my division`; when `escalate_after` passes, the row is reassigned to the owner and one text goes out.

**When each table lands** (§7): P1 — `proposals`, `events`, `jobs_queue`, `outbox`, `agents` (with the six seed rows), `agent_authority`, `role_permissions`, `approval_policies`, `operation_catalog`, `api_tokens`, `external_refs`, `connections`, `integration_runs`, `worker_heartbeats`, and `ai_caps` + `web_sessions` as renames of state that already exists; P3 — `eval_runs`, with the first versioned prompt; P4 — `external_intents`, `qbo_item_map`, `internal_rates`; P5 — `event_subscriptions`, `on_call`.

**Retention, honestly.** The live tier is a 1 GB Micro with two disk-IO incidents (Jul 24, Aug 17). `events` stays one heap with a monthly index to low millions of rows; `jobs_queue` `done` rows purge after 24 h for `job.project` and after 14 days for every other kind, by a daily job; `outbox` after 90 days; `capture_events` `emailPull` rows (3,500 of 4,504 **(live)**) after 30 days; `ai_usage`, `agent_runs`, `integration_runs` and `events` are kept, `agent_runs.trace` trimmed after 90 days [A-2.4]. Month-partitioning `events` is a **P5 roadmap item** (§7.7) — the arithmetic above puts 5M rows inside the first year at 100+ — and fires earlier if ~5M rows or the compute upgrade (§7, P3) arrives first; it is an operational lift a solo owner has not done, deliberately absent from P1.

**Blob pushes emit no semantic events** (Decision record §6). At 100 devices the 45 s cycle (`apps/field/js/sync.js:623`) is ~190k *pulls*/day and pushes fire on save (`:617`); even at the expected ~2–5k pushes/day an event per push would be the largest writer of the audit table, and the worst case — every cycle pushing — would drown it. The projection job (§3; Decision record §6) upserts rows stamped `origin='blob', blob_rev` and emits nothing; an AFTER UPDATE trigger on `field_projects` enqueues `jobs_queue(kind='job.project', idempotency_key='job.project:'||id, run_after = now() + 60 s)` with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` while a row for that job is queued or leased — at most one projection per job per minute, one per burst in practice, office reads of capture data eventually consistent by about 1–2 minutes.

**Ceilings.** `proposals`/`events`/`outbox` hold to 100+ people at hundreds of writes per minute on a Small tier. `jobs_queue` as a plain table holds to several thousand jobs/day on one worker — *option left to the owner:* plain table (chosen: inspectable, one migration) versus `pgmq` (managed visibility timeouts, one more extension to learn); `claimJob()` is the only code that knows which. The queue's forcing condition is >50 jobs/s sustained or strict FIFO across workers. `role_permissions` as data holds to 100+ and ~10 roles; the *option* of code-as-matrix (a generated `PERMISSIONS.md`, versioned, drift-free) is viable while the office is two people and costs a deploy for every change to who may approve.

### 2.3 The operation registry

**Where the MCP server runs.** One deployment, one transport: the worker serves MCP over **streamable HTTP only**, on an internal port behind Fly's proxy, with the tool list the registry generates (below). Claude Code on the owner's laptop connects to that endpoint with a minted `api_tokens` bearer (`claude mcp add roybal --transport http <url> --header "Authorization: Bearer $ROYBAL_MCP_TOKEN"`); a client that speaks only stdio uses a ten-line local shim that forwards each message to the same endpoint with the same bearer — there is never a stdio server "in the worker", and nothing runs on the laptop that holds state or a service key. Hosted clients (claude.ai, a future partner harness) connect to the same endpoint. Every session is propose-only on writes and writes `proposed_via='mcp'` (§2.4). Sticky routing (§2.5, Amendment 4) applies to these sessions because they are per-connection stateful; the P7 verify step ("an MCP session as the owner … cannot approve it") exercises this endpoint.

One file per operation in `packages/domain/ops/<area>.<verb>.ts`:

```ts
export const invoiceAddLine = defineOp({
  name: "invoice.add_line", version: 1,
  actionType: "money",                       // read|capture|job|money|comms|external|schedule|admin
  description: "Add one line to a draft invoice with its code, quantity, unit price, justification and evidence refs.",
  input:  { type: "object", additionalProperties: false, required: ["documentId", "clientLineId", "line"],
            properties: { documentId: { type: "string", format: "uuid" }, clientLineId: { type: "string", format: "uuid" },
                          line: { $ref: "#/$defs/LineItem" } }, $defs: { LineItem: { /* code, qty, unit, unit_price, justification, evidence_refs */ } } },
  output: { type: "object", additionalProperties: false, properties: { lineItemId: { type: "string" }, total_usd: { type: "number" } } },
  runtime: "sql",                             // sql | edge | worker
  idempotency: (i) => `invoice.add_line:${i.documentId}:${i.clientLineId}`,
  approval: { default: "office", amountField: "total_usd" },
  emits: ["line_item.added"],
  handler,                                    // sql: the plpgsql body shipped by migration; edge/worker: TS
});
```

JSON Schema is the source of truth, not zod, because the same object must feed Anthropic `tools[]` and MCP verbatim — the shape the six read tools already use (`personas.ts:78-146`) and the five phone tools (`personas.ts:166-221`). The 17 prose `ACTION_DEFS` (`personas.ts:246-362`) transcribe one-to-one: every `desc` already enumerates its parameters, types and enums.

**What one definition emits** (generated in CI; nothing is hand-maintained twice):

| Artifact | Consumer | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| `operation_catalog` row | `op_propose` validation, the admin's operation list | published on merge; `definition_sha` changes force a re-publish |
| `op_<name>` plpgsql signature check | CI | for `runtime:"sql"` the body lives beside the definition and ships by migration; CI asserts the deployed signature matches the schema |
| `roybal-ops` route table | the Deno dispatcher | `runtime:"edge"` ops only |
| worker route table | `services/worker` | `runtime:"worker"` ops and every queue kind |
| Anthropic `tools[]` | worker agent loops, `roybal-ops` chat turns; generated by `packages/ai` from the ops | read ops as-is; write ops only as `propose_<name>` with `strict: true` and `additionalProperties: false` — the model can never execute |
| MCP tool list | the MCP endpoint the worker serves over streamable HTTP (above) | same list, same schemas, same `propose_` prefix on writes |
| typed client (`packages/db`) | field/admin/board bundles, the worker, the functions through `_shared/` | `ops.invoiceAddLine(input)` calls `rpc/op_invoice_add_line` for sql ops, `roybal-ops` for edge ops, `rpc/enqueue` for worker ops; the same client carries the field app's IndexedDB/offline adapter |
| `role_permissions` seed rows + `docs/PERMISSIONS.md` | migration + docs | seeds only; the rows are data thereafter |

**Validation at three doors, one schema:** proposal insert (`op_propose`, `pg_jsonschema` [A-0.2]); execution (the handler re-validates `input` merged with `edited_params`); tool call (the SDK validates against the strict schema before the model's call reaches the registry). **Versioning:** `name@version` is stored on every proposal and event; additive input changes do not bump, breaking ones do; an old handler stays until no open proposal references it. **Idempotency:** the key is computed by the registry from the input, never typed by a caller; it is `unique` on `proposals`, `events`, `outbox` and `jobs_queue`; offline capture ops carry a client uuid in the input so the key is stable across retries. **Autonomy tiers** are per (operation, principal): `propose_only → auto_below_threshold → auto`, held in `agent_authority` (machines) and `approval_policies` (rules), widened only by the owner's grant ops. **Separation of duties:** for `money`, `external` and `comms`, `op_approve` refuses `approved_by = proposed_by`; a chip on a non-money op is a proposal the signed-in proposer may self-approve when they hold `execute`; agents never self-approve; the owner may override with a recorded reason (`approved_via = 'override'`).

**Anthropic tool definitions, specifically.** Four literal forced-tool sites exist today — `roybal-ai-office/index.ts:148` inside `forcedTool()` (15 `forcedTool(` occurrences in the file), `supabase/functions/roybal-ai-ingest/index.ts:411`, `supabase/functions/qb-time-proxy/aiphase.ts:123`, `supabase/functions/roybal-portal/index.ts:665`. Forced `tool_choice` (`{type:"tool"}` and `{type:"any"}`) returns a 400 on Fable 5.1; assistant prefill returns a 400 on every 4.6+ model. The registry therefore emits `tool_choice: {type:"auto"}` plus an instruction naming the tool, `strict: true` on every tool for schema-valid arguments, and `output_config.format` where a forced call only existed to get JSON back. That is one rule in one client file (§2.7), not four edits.

**Rejected:** a generic `commit_operation(changes[])` RPC. It is an unguarded write door callable by any authenticated user unless SQL knows op → tables, which requires the per-op functions anyway. The pattern chosen is the one the codebase already proves: `coordination_job_patch` (`supabase/migrations/246_lead_triage_grant.sql:28-78`), `push_project`, and the `web_*` guards (`227:349-359`).

**Ceiling.** One registry holds to hundreds of operations and 100+ people; a new capability is a new file plus a migration for its SQL body. A second implementation language would need a second registry — do not; a public partner API adds a versioned gateway in front of `roybal-ops`, not a second registry.

### 2.4 The permission matrix

**How crew scoping avoids a per-row join.** The JWT claim carries `principal_id`, `role`, `division_id`, `org_id`; assigned job ids are not in the claim — RLS predicates call a `STABLE` SECURITY DEFINER helper `assigned_job_ids()` (one initplan evaluation per statement, not per row) plus the walk-on rule: crew may read any job in their division with `stage` in the active set for 24 h after scanning its QR/asset label or being added by a crew lead. (ADR-04 states this identically.) The mechanics: assigned job ids cannot ride in the token — a crew lead on a busy week has dozens, and a claim has a size and a refresh lag — so `assigned_job_ids()` returns `uuid[]` from one indexed read of `job_assignments (principal_id, job_id) where from_at <= now() and (to_at is null or to_at > now())`, unioned with the caller's crew's rows and with the walk-on rows (`job.claim_walk_on(job_id)`, the self-executed op that records the assignment with a 24 h `to_at` and notifies the office), and every crew-facing policy is written `job_id = any ((select assigned_job_ids()))`. Postgres evaluates the sub-select once per statement — the same mechanism the record prescribes for `(select auth.uid())`. Office and owner predicates stay claim-only (`division_id = (select current_division_id())` or `org_id`). The pgTAP suite asserts that `explain` for `select * from photos` as a crew principal shows one InitPlan and no nested loop over `job_assignments`. Ceiling: one lookup per statement at any headcount; the forcing condition is a principal assigned to thousands of jobs at once, which does not occur.

**The two `admin` profiles.** `admin → owner` is not applied blindly to both live rows: the brief says the company has an owner *and* an office role today, and the office person may well be one of those two logins. The migration maps `branden@roybalconstruction.com` to `owner` and the second admin profile to `office` by default, and the owner confirms both in the admin UI **before the role claim is armed** — a confirmation item in 04. The remap is an explicit `(profile_id, old_role, new_role)` table for all live rows, reviewed in the PR, never a blanket `update profiles set role = 'owner' where role = 'admin'`. The two machine `viewer` rows become `agents` rows in the same migration and their `profiles.role` becomes `agent`.

Roles: `owner, office, crew_lead, crew, viewer, agent`. Mapping from today's `user_role`: `admin → owner` (branden@…) and `admin → office` (the second live admin, by default, owner-confirmed as above), `office → office` (the value exists since `supabase/migrations/216_individual_logins.sql:50`; no live row), `tech → crew`, the two machine viewers → `agents` rows. External principals — **customer** (portal share token / `contact_sessions`) and **adjuster** (`photo_shares` token) — reach only the portal gateway and never hold a JWT role. Capabilities: **R** read, **P** propose, **A** approve, **E** execute.

| Action type (example ops) | owner | office | crew_lead | crew | viewer | agent (`agent_authority`) | customer (portal) | adjuster (`photo_shares`) | MCP session (`api_tokens`) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **read** (`job.get`, `claim.get`, `hoursLookup`…) | R all | R division | R own crews' assigned jobs | R assigned jobs + walk-on (`assigned_job_ids()`) | R division | R per grant | R own job projection | R shared photos/packet | R as the minting human |
| **capture** (`reading.record`, `equipment.place/remove`, `photo.add`, `daily_log.add`, `job.claim_walk_on`) | E | E | E | E on assigned jobs (offline outbox); `job.claim_walk_on` self-executes and notifies office | — | P (voice/OCR prefill) | — | — | P |
| **job** (`job.set_stage`, `job.certify_drying`, `claim.update`, `room.upsert`) | E | E | E own crews' jobs, P otherwise | P | — | P | — | — | P |
| **money** (`invoice.add_line`, `document.issue/supersede/void`, `invoice.review_gaps`, `co.create`, `payment.record`) | A+E; override with reason | E on drafts; A+E ≤ `approval_policies.max_amount_usd` [A-0.3]; P above | P | — | — | P; E only by grant, never `document.issue` | E `co.sign` only | — | P |
| **comms** (`email.send`, `sms.send`, `portal.post`, `submittal.record`, `message.draft_reply`) | A+E | A+E | P; E crew-kind texts to own crew, inside the 7 am–8 pm AK window | P; E `onOurWay`/`crewLine` kinds, inside the window | — | P; E by grant for `kinds ∈ {crewSchedule, onOurWay}`, inside the window by default — leaving it is a per-kind setting the owner turns on (below) | E `portal.message` on own thread | — | P |
| **external** (`qbo.invoice_upsert`, `qbtime.pull`, `gmail.pull`, `connection.connect/disconnect`) | A+E; connect/disconnect owner only | A; E pulls | — | — | — | E pulls by grant (integration principal) | — | — | P |
| **schedule** (`schedule.apply`, `phase.update`, `availability.set`) | A+E | A+E | A+E own crews; E own availability | E own availability; P else | — | P (`schedule.propose`) | — | — | P |
| **admin** (`role.grant`, `agent_authority.grant`, `route.set`, `cap.set`, `policy.set`, `api_token.mint`) | E | R | — | — | — | — | — | — | — |
| **ledgers** (`events`, `ai_usage`, `agent_runs`, `outbox`, `integration_runs`) | R | R | R own crews | R own | R | R own runs | — | — | R as the human |

Rules that sit under the table:

- **Amount thresholds** are `approval_policies` rows keyed by `(action_type, operation?, division_id?)`. Below the threshold office approves; above it the owner does; a row with `auto = true` approves without a human and records `approved_by_kind = 'policy'` with the policy id.
- **Separation of duties** (§2.3): `money`/`external`/`comms` refuse approver = proposer; agents never approve anything; the owner's override is an event with a reason.
- **SMS approval** stays a channel of `op_proposal_approve`, not a separate path — and it does not depend on the worker. The owner's "YES 12" is handled **synchronously by `roybal-webhooks`** (trust level B): after the Twilio signature check (kept), the function resolves the sender's number to a principal through `profiles.phone` (`supabase/migrations/001_initial_schema.sql:51`) against the allowlist of approvers — principals holding `approve` for that proposal's action type; today only the owner's cell, `OWNER_CELL` at `roybal-notify/index.ts:400-401` [A-2.1] — and is allowed to call **exactly two SQL ops, `op_proposal_approve` and `op_proposal_decline`**, as that principal. Trust level B's rule is therefore "inserts events/queue rows, or calls those two ops"; a test asserts no other `op_*` is reachable from the function. A second `YES n` for a consumed code is a no-op (the `:438-443` guard, kept). Approval records without the worker; only the *execution* of an approved op whose `runtime` is `worker` waits for the worker. Matching any contact or crew phone would be a permission widening, and is not done.
- **Machine principals and the widening path.** A new agent has no writes. The owner adds `agent_authority` rows through `agent_authority.grant` (an event) with conditions: `max_amount_usd` for `money`, `kinds[]` for `comms`, `quiet_hours` — the 7 am–8 pm Alaska window today implemented three ways (`roybal-notify/index.ts:217-232`, `qb-time-proxy`, hard-coded in `apps/admin/js/campaigns.js:36-39`) becomes one predicate in the `sms.send` handler — `division_ids[]`, and `expires_at`. **Crew-line kinds obey the window by default:** the brief states 7 am–8 pm as a flat constraint, so no kind is assumed exempt; the exemption for `crewSchedule`/`onOurWay`/`crewLine` is an **owner option** (a confirmation item in 04) and, once taken, a per-kind setting in `role_permissions.conditions` / `agent_authority.conditions` (a `notification_policies` table if the owner wants it per division) — never a constant. Revocation is `agent_authority.revoke`, also an event, effective on the next `op_*` call because the worker checks the row, not a cached claim. Every comms op checks `crew_members.contact_prefs` (`digestOptOut`, `appOptOut`, `paperTimesheet`) — the paper-timesheet crew member's lane survives exactly as `roybal-brief/crewdigest.ts:149` honors it today.
- **Worker identity.** The worker holds the service-role key in Fly secrets but executes every op as a named `agents` row with an explicit `p_principal_id`; `op_*` functions accept a service-role caller only when a principal is supplied and `agent_authority` grants the capability. The password-grant machine users (`services/phone-agent/supa.mjs:30-33`; `roybal-brief/index.ts:62-71`) remain for the phone agent until it imports packages (§7, P7); the 26 email-string fences are replaced by role and agent predicates in the migration that introduces `agents`.
- **MCP caller identity.** The MCP endpoint the worker serves over streamable HTTP (§2.3) requires a bearer minted in the admin (`api_tokens`: principal, scopes, expiry, hash) — the same bearer whether Claude Code connects directly or through a local stdio shim. A session acts **as the human who minted it** for reads and is capped at **propose** for every write action type regardless of that human's role — an MCP session is an agent harness, and the audit row says "proposed via mcp by <person>". Approval happens in the inbox or by SMS; MCP callers never hold approve or execute [A-0.3]. *Option left to the owner:* whether tokens may ever be widened past propose for a read-only role's delegate — recommendation: never.
- **RLS mechanics.** A Custom Access Token hook adds the four claims — `principal_id`, `role`, `division_id`, `org_id`; `current_principal()` reads them and falls back to a `profiles` lookup when they are absent (old tokens, machine users mid-cutover). Helpers are `STABLE` and use the `(select auth.uid())` initplan form (36 `auth_rls_initplan` advisor warnings show the wrong form live); crew-facing predicates use `assigned_job_ids()` in the same form (above). Role changes lag until refresh — the field app refreshes under 60 s before expiry (`apps/field/js/supa.js:71-73`) — and demotions also revoke sessions [A-0.8]. Crew read scope is `job_assignments` (derived from schedule assignments plus explicit adds) ∪ the 24 h walk-on rows; reads widen through a recorded op, never an ad-hoc policy edit. Ledgers become insert-only for everyone but the service role — today any crew login can delete the month's spend (`201:78-79`) and rewrite the audit envelope (`supabase/migrations/200_ai_backbone.sql:229-240`).
- **The role-enum migration must not stop a single push.** `_sync_guard` admits `role in ('tech','admin','office')` (`supabase/migrations/218_sync_rpcs_and_build_gate.sql:67-69`, redefined at `226_sync_client_telemetry.sql:45-49`) and `push_project` calls it on every push. Migration N adds the enum values (`ALTER TYPE … ADD VALUE` cannot run in its own transaction); N+1, in one transaction, redefines `_sync_guard` and every other role gate (`coordination_job_patch` `246:47-52`, `contact_merge`, `contact_resolve`, `contact_mark_review_asked`, `restore_photo`, the `field_photos` policies `221_field_photos_table.sql:113-137`) to accept both vocabularies, remaps `profiles.role` from the reviewed remap table, and asserts in a `DO` block that `push_project` still resolves `_sync_guard(text)`; N+2 drops the old names a week later.

**Ceiling.** Roles × action types × four capabilities with `job_assignments`, amount thresholds and data-driven overrides holds to 100+ people and ~10 roles. Forcing conditions: per-customer or franchise isolation → the `org_id` claim (Decision record §7); multi-step approvals with SLAs → an approvals child table and a workflow layer. Without `job_assignments` the matrix holds only to "everyone sees every job", which is today's posture — that table lands in P3 with crews.

### 2.5 Where agent code runs — the owner's hybrid default, amended

**Amendment 0 — two things that must not depend on the worker, settled before the tiers.**

(1) *The dead-worker alert.* "If the worker is down … the owner gets one text" would be circular if that text were an `outbox` row, because the outbox is delivered by the worker. The liveness alert is therefore the **one permanent, documented exception** to "pg_cron never calls HTTP": a pg_cron job every 5 minutes runs a SQL check over `worker_heartbeats`; when the newest heartbeat is older than **10 minutes** it calls `net.http_post` to `roybal-webhooks` `/alert` with a service-secret header (read from `vault.decrypted_secrets`, the mechanism the record already names for the last transitional HTTP job), and that function sends the owner one SMS **synchronously through the Twilio adapter**, with a 24 h resend guard in `app_settings`. The outbox is not used for this alert; the row it writes afterwards to `sms_messages` and `integration_runs` is a record, not a delivery. Everything else pg_cron does stays SQL-only.

(2) *SMS approval.* "YES n" is the owner's channel during P1, and it is **not a queue kind**. `roybal-webhooks` (trust level B) handles the text synchronously and is allowed to call exactly two SQL ops — `op_proposal_approve` and `op_proposal_decline` — as the principal resolved from the sender's number, allowlisted approvers only (§2.4). Approval therefore records without the worker, exactly as an inbox tap does; only the execution of the approved op may wait for the worker, and only when that op's `runtime` is `worker`. The trust-level-B rule reads "inserts events/queue rows, or calls those two ops", the op names are allowlisted in the function, and a test asserts no other op is reachable from it.

The owner's default — Postgres holds the contract, edge functions do thin inbound work, one small long-running worker runs agent loops, rendering and integrations — is endorsed with those two settlements and five amendments drawn from this codebase.

| Tier | Runs | Why | Ceiling |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Postgres** — `op_<name>()` SECURITY DEFINER behind the Supabase gateway (`rest/v1/rpc/`) | every short, DB-only operation: `proposal.approve/decline`, `job.set_stage`, `line_item.add/update`, `document.issue` (number + version row; render queued), `equipment.place/remove`, `reading.record`, `schedule.apply`, `submittal.record`, `emit_event`, `enqueue`, `ai_reserve/settle` | transactional; the event lands in the same transaction; no edge hop on a short write; the office keeps working when Fly or an edge function is down. A Deno dispatcher cannot open a multi-statement transaction over PostgREST — the flaw the critiques found in the edge-only design | holds to 100+; the forcing condition is a rule that is a bad fit for plpgsql — estimating math stays in `packages/domain` and runs in the worker, never rewritten in SQL |
| **Deno edge** — one `roybal-ops` dispatcher plus the trust-level functions (Decision record §2) | synchronous work that needs a secret and a waiting human: assistant chat turns (keep the 2-round cap — the comment at `roybal-ai-office/index.ts:1527`, the loop guard at `:1586`), voice extraction, OAuth code exchange, credential probes, the two synchronous webhook paths of Amendment 0; the web receptionist's single-turn lane stays where it is | request-scoped; no Chromium; interactive chat must not ride a queue | per-invocation wall clock; no loops longer than a chat turn |
| **Node worker** `roybal-worker` on Fly — a second app, never co-hosted with `roybal-phone` | queue consumer, agent loops (Claude Agent SDK), the MCP endpoint (streamable HTTP), PDF render, integration pulls (QB Time, Gmail, QBO payments), blob → row projection, outbox delivery | long or heavy work; the phone agent proves Fly is operable here | one machine, one region, no HA — an **availability** ceiling, not throughput |

*Amendment 1 — short ops execute in Postgres, not in the worker or the edge.* Approvals, stage changes, line edits and captures are 10–50 ms transactions; routing them through Fly would make Fly a hard dependency for every office click, and routing them through one edge function would make that function the office's only write door.

*Amendment 2 — the worker polls; cron never pushes (with the one exception above).* Seven HTTP crons fire `net.http_post` and `cron.job_run_details` reports "succeeded" for jobs that have returned 400 for days, because the post is asynchronous and `net._http_response` is purged in ~6 h (inventory §6.5). pg_cron becomes SQL-only: it inserts `jobs_queue` rows, the worker claims them, and the outcome is a row. `x-cron-secret` and the publishable key as a credential (literal in the live `cron.job` rows) disappear with it. The single HTTP call pg_cron keeps is the dead-worker alert of Amendment 0, precisely because it must not depend on the worker it is reporting on. The worker polls every 5 s and heartbeats every 30 s [A-2.2].

*Amendment 3 — "small" means ≥1 GB, and rendering runs in a child process.* The phone agent is `shared-cpu-1x`, `memory = "256mb"` (`services/phone-agent/fly.toml:21-23`) with per-caller limits in a process `Map` (`services/phone-agent/tools.mjs:19-27`). Headless Chromium needs 300–500 MB alone. So: the worker machine is ≥1 GB [A-0.1], PDF rendering runs in an isolated child process with its own memory cap (restart on OOM, one render at a time), and the ConversationRelay socket stays on its own machine so a 20-page photo packet can never drop a live call.

*Amendment 4 — the worker holds nothing that matters in memory.* Leases, reservations, sessions, rate limits live in Postgres. `fly scale count 2` is true for the queue poller only; MCP and Agent SDK sessions are per-connection stateful and a second machine needs sticky routing.

*Amendment 5 — model selection is data the client reads, never a constant an op imports* (§2.7).

**If the worker is down:** approvals still record — inbox taps and SMS `YES n` are both SQL, the SMS through `roybal-webhooks` (Amendment 0); sends and renders queue; crews sync; the office keeps writing; execution of approved `worker`-runtime ops waits; and the owner gets one text from the pg_cron heartbeat check through `roybal-webhooks` `/alert`, not through the outbox. **If Fly and Supabase edge are both down:** the office still approves and edits through the gateway RPCs; only the SMS channel and the liveness text are lost until the edge returns, and the inbox is the fallback the P1 verify step already exercises.

**Rejected shapes, one line each** (the full argument is in §5, the ADRs): *edge-only with a Deno dispatcher as the only write door* — no transaction across PostgREST, an edge hop on every short write, and the office stops when one function is down; *co-hosting the worker with the voice socket on the 256 MB phone machine* — a packet render can OOM-restart a live call; *routing every office write through the Fly worker* — Fly becomes a hard availability dependency for a solo operator who today depends on it only for the phone lane; *SMS approval as a queue kind* — it makes the owner's P1 approval channel worker-dependent while the inbox is not.

### 2.6 Cost, failure and outcome logging — what "observability" means

**Integration health, one shape.** Three definitions appeared in this document — a view over `connections ⋈ outbox`, a table "populated by every adapter call", and an E0 view over token rows. One shape, in two stages:

- **E0 (no new tables):** `integration_health_v0` is a view over the three existing token tables and the newest successful row per lane in `capture_events` (`emailPull`), `time_entries` (`qbtime`) and `sms_messages`; it is what the E0 alert reads until P1, and it is replaced, not evolved.
- **P1 onward:** a **table `integration_runs`** — one row per adapter call, written by every call in `packages/integrations` whether it ran from `jobs_queue` (pulls, refreshes) or from the outbox (pushes) or from a webhook — and a **view `integration_health`** over `integration_runs ⋈ connections` that derives `last_ok_at`, `last_error`, `consecutive_failures` and carries `token_expires_at`. The outbox-joined view is withdrawn: pulls (QB Time, Gmail pull, QBO payments) are `jobs_queue` kinds and never write `outbox`, so a view joined to `outbox` alone could not have seen the two lanes that actually died; rows written by the adapter see every call regardless of direction.

**Cost.** `ai_usage` keeps its columns (`201:21-45`) and gains `prompt_id`, `prompt_version`, `task_class`, `route_key`, `agent_run_id`, `proposal_id`, `job_id`, `cache_read_tokens`, `reservation_state` (`reserved`/`settled` — moved out of the free-text `note` where `227:167-173` keeps it today). It is written only by `ai_reserve()`/`ai_settle()` SECURITY DEFINER, which generalise `web_turn_begin`/`web_turn_end` (`227:147-211`): reserve a pessimistic estimate against the lane cap, the daily cap and the shared cap from `ai_caps` rows, refuse at the cap with a `capped` row, settle to actuals afterwards; a failed settle leaves the reservation standing, which is the safe direction. The error-path metering in `roybal-ai-office/index.ts:1817-1838` (billed tokens on a failed call still land) is kept as a rule of `ai_settle`. The four disagreeing `monthSpend` implementations (paged at `index.ts:110-126`, unpaged in narrative, `.limit(5000)` in qb-time, fail-open in portal) collapse into the cap check inside `ai_reserve`.

**Failures.** `jobs_queue.dead`, `outbox.dead`, `agent_runs.status ∈ {failed, capped, refused}`, `integration_runs.ok = false`, and `integration_health`:

```sql
create table connections (
  id uuid primary key, org_id uuid not null, division_id uuid,
  provider text not null,                       -- qbo | qbtime | gmail | twilio | portal
  account text, token_ref text,                 -- token_ref points at Vault, never a token column
  token_expires_at timestamptz, expected_cadence interval not null,
  updated_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  unique (org_id, division_id, provider));

create table integration_runs (
  id bigint generated always as identity primary key,
  connection_id uuid not null references connections(id),
  kind text not null check (kind in ('pull','push','refresh','webhook')),
  started_at timestamptz not null default now(), finished_at timestamptz,
  ok boolean, error text, rows_affected int, external_ref text,
  queue_job_id bigint, outbox_id bigint);      -- whichever lane ran it
create index on integration_runs (connection_id, finished_at desc);

create view integration_health as
with last_ok as (
  select connection_id, max(finished_at) as at from integration_runs where ok group by connection_id),
fails as (
  select r.connection_id, count(*) as n, (array_agg(r.error order by r.finished_at desc))[1] as last_error
    from integration_runs r left join last_ok k using (connection_id)
   where not r.ok and r.finished_at > coalesce(k.at, '-infinity') group by r.connection_id)
select c.id as connection_id, c.provider, c.division_id, c.token_expires_at,
       k.at as last_ok_at, f.last_error, coalesce(f.n, 0) as consecutive_failures,
       case when c.token_expires_at < now()                                     then 'dead'
            when coalesce(f.n, 0) >= 3                                          then 'failed'
            when coalesce(k.at, '-infinity') < now() - 2 * c.expected_cadence   then 'silent'
            else 'ok' end as status
  from connections c
  left join last_ok k on k.connection_id = c.id
  left join fails   f on f.connection_id = c.id;
```

`connections` replaces "latest token row wins" (`gmail-proxy/index.ts:110-111`; `qbo-proxy/index.ts:76-77`); pulls run as queue kinds so a failed pull is a `failed` queue row *and* an `integration_runs` row with `ok = false`, not a green cron. **The alert rule:** `consecutive_failures ≥ 3` **or** no successful run within `2 × expected_cadence` (`status in ('failed','silent','dead')`). A pg_cron rule every 15 minutes inserts an `outbox` SMS to the owner for any such lane, with a 24 h resend guard keyed `alert:<provider>:<division>:<date>` [A-2.5]; a token expiring within 7 days is surfaced on the admin card and in the morning brief rather than by text. That is the text that would have caught Gmail on 09-01 and QB Time on 09-04. When the failing lane *is* the worker, the alert takes the worker-independent path of §2.5, Amendment 0. The admin card reads the view, so it can no longer say "● Connected" because a token row exists (`apps/admin/js/gmailconnect.js:64-68`).

**Outcomes.** `proposals ⋈ events ⋈ agent_runs` answers "what did the AI propose, who approved, what ran, what did it cost". The Sunday brief's `buildWeekly` (`roybal-brief/weekly.ts:25-58`) reads these instead of `capture_events` — today it counts a `source_type = 'assist_action'` that no code writes (`weekly.ts:46`).

**Concretely, the queries the owner can run** (the admin *Automation* tab is these and nothing else; at P5 they become the `automation_costs` materialized view and its read op behind the admin *Reports* tab, §7.7, so the tab and the brief cannot disagree):

| Question | Query |
|---|---|
| What did automation do this week? | `select kind, count(*) from events where principal_kind = 'agent' and at > now() - interval '7 days' group by 1 order by 2 desc` |
| Who approved what, and how? | `select operation, approved_by_kind, approved_by_ref, approved_via, approved_at from proposals where status in ('executed','failed') order by approved_at desc limit 50` |
| Cost by task class this month | `select task_class, route_key, count(*), sum(cost_usd) from ai_usage where billing_month = to_char(now(),'YYYY-MM') group by 1,2 order by 4 desc` |
| Cost per job / per claim | `select job_id, sum(cost_usd) from ai_usage where job_id is not null group by 1 order by 2 desc` |
| Which lane is dead right now? | `select provider, status, last_ok_at, last_error from integration_health where status <> 'ok'` |
| Every integration call in the last hour | `select c.provider, r.kind, r.ok, r.rows_affected, r.error from integration_runs r join connections c on c.id = r.connection_id where r.started_at > now() - interval '1 hour' order by r.started_at desc` |
| Dead letters | `select kind, attempts, last_error from jobs_queue where status = 'dead'` · `select channel, error from outbox where status = 'dead'` |
| Stuck work | `select id, kind, locked_by, lease_until from jobs_queue where status = 'leased' and lease_until < now()` |
| Is the worker alive? | `select worker_id, version, at, queue_depth from worker_heartbeats where at > now() - interval '2 minutes'` |
| Texts sent but never delivered | `select count(*) from outbox where channel = 'sms' and status = 'sent' and sent_at < now() - interval '1 hour'` |
| Which prompt version is in production? | `select task_class, prompt_id, prompt_version, count(*) from ai_usage where created_at > now() - interval '1 day' group by 1,2,3` |
| Proposals past their escalation time | `select count(*) from proposals where status = 'proposed' and escalate_after < now()` |
| The full audit trail for one job | `select at, principal_kind, principal_id, kind, operation, proposal_id from events where job_id = $1 order by id` |

Structured worker logs (pino) go to Fly logs; edge functions keep Supabase logs; neither is the record — the tables are. Alert routing is the owner's cell through the existing SMS lane (zero new infrastructure); at 100+ an `on_call(role, phone, from, to)` row, landing in P5, replaces the single number [A-2.6].

**Ceiling.** Every signal is a row in the same database, so it holds to 100+ people and scales with `events`. Forcing condition: a hosted error tracker with stack traces becomes worth a second console when there are several developers, not before.

### 2.7 Model routing as policy

Model ids appear in exactly one file, `packages/integrations/llm/client.ts` (the SDK adapter), and in two tables; the route loader that reads those tables, the versioned prompts and the metering helpers around `ai_reserve`/`ai_settle` live in `packages/ai`. Today ten differently named secrets choose a model per file with hard-coded defaults (`roybal-ai-office/index.ts:63-65`; `roybal-ai-ingest/index.ts:51`; `roybal-ai-narrative/index.ts:28`; `roybal-portal/index.ts:76`; `services/phone-agent/config.mjs:21`), the price table is copied six times (`roybal-ai-office/index.ts:83-87` and five more), and `roybal-web-agent/guards.ts:253-257` prices `claude-sonnet-5` at $3/$15 and `claude-opus-5` at $15/$75 against the real $2/$10 and $5/$25.

**`model_prices`** (`model_id pk`, `provider`, `in_per_mtok`, `out_per_mtok`, `cache_read_per_mtok`, `batch_discount`, `context_tokens`, `thinking_mode` ∈ {`always`, `adaptive`, `budget`}, `forced_tool_choice bool`, `prefill bool`, `effort_levels text[]`, `effective_from`, `retired_at`). The rows are **seeded from the provider's price sheet at deploy time** by a seed step CI runs, never typed into a migration by hand; the in/out figures below are the decision record's model facts (bundled `claude-api` skill, cached 2026-06-24), and every other price column — cache reads, batch discount — is filled from the price sheet at seed time, not from this document.

| Model | id | Context | $/MTok in / out | API behaviour that the client must know |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Fable 5.1 | `claude-fable-5-1` | 1M | 10 / 50 | thinking always on (omit the param); effort `low`…`max`; **forced `tool_choice` rejected**; `refusal` stop reason must be handled |
| Opus 5 | `claude-opus-5` | 1M | 5 / 25 | adaptive thinking by default; `budget_tokens` rejected; effort `low`…`max` |
| Opus 4.8 | `claude-opus-4-8` | 1M | 5 / 25 | what DOC tasks run today (`index.ts:64`; narrative `:28`); adaptive thinking must be set explicitly |
| Sonnet 5 | `claude-sonnet-5` | 1M | 2 / 10 | adaptive; `budget_tokens` rejected |
| Sonnet 4.6 | `claude-sonnet-4-6` | 1M | 3 / 15 | what photo, assist, ingest, concierge and phone run today (`index.ts:63,65`; ingest `:51`; portal `:76`; `config.mjs:21`) — dearer than Sonnet 5 |
| Haiku 4.5 | `claude-haiku-4-5` | 200K | 1 / 5 | `budget_tokens` form of thinking; no `effort` |

**`model_routes`** (`task_class pk`, `provider`, `model_id → model_prices` FK, `effort`, `thinking` ∈ {`adaptive`, `budget`}, `max_output_tokens`, `max_cost_usd`, `fallback_task_class`, `batch_eligible`, `cache_ttl`, `updated_by`, `updated_at`). The FK makes a route to an unpriced model impossible — the metering the owner cares about cannot silently break. Routes are edited only by the owner's `route.set` op (an event); there is no env override, because an env override is a model id outside the two tables. Seed routes [A-0.5]:

| Task class | Model | Effort / thinking | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `judgment.carrier_document`, `judgment.supplement`, `judgment.line_justification` | `claude-opus-5` | adaptive, `high` | the judgment class; Fable 5.1 at `medium` for `carrier_document` once evals justify it |
| `draft.correspondence`, `draft.narrative`, `chat.assist`, `chat.phone`, `chat.web`, `extract.voice_form`, `extract.receipt`, `extract.meter_ocr` | `claude-sonnet-5` | adaptive, `medium` | replaces every `claude-sonnet-4-6` lane at a lower price |
| `classify.photo`, `classify.phase`, `classify.email`, `classify.intake` | `claude-sonnet-5` | adaptive, `low` | Sonnet 5 is the default for extraction and classification classes; `batch_eligible = true`. *Option left to the owner:* `claude-haiku-4-5` (`budget_tokens`) for the bulk photo backfill once evals show parity on that class |

**The one client, `packages/integrations/llm/client.ts`, owns the SDK rules:** the official `@anthropic-ai/sdk`, never raw `fetch` (seven files today); `tool_choice: {type:"auto"}` + `strict: true` schemas or `output_config.format` — never a forced tool; adaptive thinking + `output_config.effort` — never `budget_tokens` except a Haiku route; no assistant prefill; prompt caching is prefix-based, so the request is assembled `tools → stable system → volatile context` with carrier-learned rules injected *after* the cached prefix, and `usage.cache_read_input_tokens` is written to `ai_usage.cache_read_tokens` so a zero hit rate is visible in one query; every response checks `stop_reason` before reading content and a `refusal` settles the reservation, records the category on `agent_runs`, and files a proposal for a human instead of retrying; `max_cost_usd` from the route bounds the reservation. **Cost per completed task is the metric**, not cost per request: one model at lower effort beats a cascade and keeps one cache namespace.

**The batch lane.** `classify.*` jobs are `batch_eligible` whatever model the route names: the worker groups queued rows into one Message Batches request (50% of list price), keys results by `custom_id = jobs_queue.id`, and settles each `ai_usage` row from the batch result. Bulk photo tagging and the photo backfill ride this lane; nothing interactive does.

**Prompts and the eval gate.** Prompts are versioned files `packages/ai/prompts/<key>.v<N>.md` mirrored to a `prompts(key, version, body_sha, task_class, active_from, activated_by, eval_run_id)` table; a change is a new version, never an edit; `ai_usage.prompt_id/prompt_version` say what ran (today: zero prompt versioning, 13 inline template strings in an 1,842-line file). Evals live in `packages/ai/evals/<task_class>/` (fixtures + expectations); CI runs a zero-cost schema-validity subset on every PR; `npm run eval -- <class>` runs the full set against staging; `prompt.activate` refuses without a passing `eval_runs` row for that class. `eval_runs` lands in P3 with the first versioned prompt; the P1–P2 lanes run the prompts they have today, registered as `v1`, and the gate applies from the first change to any of them.

**Per-carrier learning hooks into the same lane.** `carrier_responses → rejection_reasons` (typed; §3) feed a weekly `carrier.learn` (deterministic aggregation, no model) into `learned_rules(carrier_id, scope, rule_text, evidence_response_ids, active)` — propose-only, owner-approved; `retrieve_rules(carrier_id, codes[])` injects matching rules after the cached prefix of the `line_justification` prompt. Harness choice: the worker uses the Claude Agent SDK for open-ended loops (supplement detection over a claim file, a scheduling proposal that iterates) and plain Messages calls for forced-schema jobs; Tool Runner and Managed Agents are noted, not chosen.

**Ceiling.** Routing as data holds to 100+ people; cost by task type is one `GROUP BY`. A second provider is one adapter plus route rows; a model deprecation is one price row and one route row. *Option left to the owner:* which task classes graduate to Fable 5.1 and at what effort, decided by evals; whether the bulk photo backfill runs on Haiku through the batch lane.

### 2.8 How each existing piece evolves — nothing is thrown away

| Today | Evidence | Becomes |
|---|---|---|
| `pending_actions` — inert proposals, owner-cell SMS approval, executor re-verifies the row | `210:24-55`; `roybal-notify/index.ts:396-443`; `gmail-proxy/index.ts:317-326` | `proposals` is a superset; the three zombie `boardEdit` rows of 2026-07-25 migrate as `expired`; `code` becomes `sms_code` allocated by sequence; SMS `YES n` stays as `approved_via = 'sms'`, handled synchronously by `roybal-webhooks` calling `op_proposal_approve`/`op_proposal_decline` as the sender's principal [A-2.1]; the table is kept until P2 as the P1 rollback target |

Kept exception: the field `sendText` chip stays in the synchronous tap window for iOS (`assist.js:229-236`) but records the proposal and the event |
| `capture_events` — ingest envelope + audit + web-agent rate state | `200:148-163`; `227:177-182` | until P3 it keeps its voice-ingest envelope role and the envelope-before-spend rule (`roybal-ai-office/index.ts:1771-1775`); **from P3 on, `captures` is the ingest envelope** — `kind ∈ {voice, photo_ai, ocr_meter, ocr_receipt, barcode, serial, signature, email_pull}` — and `capture_events` stays as the legacy ledger, read-only after P3, migrated into `captures` + `events` in P5 and then dropped; automated-organ audit rows move to `events`; web-session state moves to `web_sessions` (P1); `emailPull` rows get 30-day retention; the `for all using(true)` policy (`200:229-240`) is dropped |

| `gmail-proxy` approval executor: re-verify, send, consume | `gmail-proxy/index.ts:317-326`, `:376-381` | the re-verify-under-lock rule is the template for every `op_*` body; the send becomes an `outbox` row delivered by the worker with an idempotency key, an `integration_runs` row, and a `submittals` row when a document is attached |
| Admin `finactions.js` executing money writes in the browser into IndexedDB; numbers from array length | `apps/admin/js/finactions.js:89,137,250` | the five executors become operations with server-side validation; numbers come from `document_sequences` (§3) |
| pg_cron → `net.http_post` with secrets in `cron.job.command` | inventory §6.5 | pg_cron inserts `jobs_queue` rows; `cron_register.sql` runs post-deploy; the last transitional HTTP job reads its header from `vault.decrypted_secrets`; the one permanent HTTP job is the dead-worker check that posts to `roybal-webhooks` `/alert` (§2.5, Amendment 0) |

### 2.9 Ceilings, in one place

| Decision | Holds to | What forces change |
|---|---|---|
| Proposals as data with one state machine and four approval channels | 100+ people; hundreds of proposals/day | cross-department SLAs → workflow engine on top of the tables |
| SMS approval synchronous in `roybal-webhooks`, two allowlisted SQL ops | any headcount; approvers are the allowlist, today one number | more approver phones → rows in the allowlist, not code; a second inbound provider → a second route in the same function |
| `events` append-only via `emit_event`, one heap | low millions of rows on Small — reached inside the first year at 100+ | month partitions as a P5 item; earlier at ~5 M rows or the compute upgrade |
| `jobs_queue` plain table, lease columns, SKIP LOCKED, per-job projection debounce | several thousand jobs/day, one worker; poller scales to N machines; the table tolerates the ~430k/day worst case with daily purge | >50 jobs/s or strict FIFO → `pgmq`/broker behind `claimJob()`; >~100–150 actively edited jobs → `job.project` on its own poller |
| `outbox` with idempotency key + provider id + callbacks | 100+; per-provider rate limits are the bound | none structural |
| Dead-worker alert: pg_cron SQL check → one HTTP post → synchronous Twilio send | any headcount; the one HTTP exception is a single 5-minute job | an `on_call` roster at 100+ (P5) changes the recipient, not the path |
| Operation registry, JSON Schema as source of truth | hundreds of operations | a second implementation language — do not |
| `role_permissions` + `approval_policies` + `agent_authority` as data; `assigned_job_ids()` initplan for crew reads | 100+ people, ~10 roles; one lookup per statement | per-customer isolation → `org_id` claim; multi-step approvals → approvals child table |
| Runtime split: SQL for short ops, edge for waiting humans, one worker for long work | 100+ for throughput | worker availability (one machine, one region); sustained render/agent backlog → second machine (poller only) or dedicated render machine |
| `integration_runs` rows + `integration_health` view | 100+; per-division connections | a partner API → a gateway, not a second health table |
| Routing as data with a price FK | 100+; one `GROUP BY` for cost | none; a provider or model change is rows |
| Observability as tables + owner SMS | 100+ | several developers → hosted error tracker |
