## 3. Target domain model

This is the vocabulary the six capabilities read and write. Every entity below is a Postgres table in the same Supabase project, born beside the running apps (Decision record §0). Office-owned entities are created in tables and never written into the blob; capture-owned sections stay in `field_projects.data` and are projected one way into the rows marked `origin='blob'` (Decision record §6). Citations are `path:line` at `main@1e04694`; **(live)** = production read 2026-09-06; **[A-3.n]** = design assumptions introduced by this section (numbered apart from the decision record's [A-0.1]–[A-0.10]). Cross-references follow the assembled document: "Decision record §n" is Part 0; "§1"–"§8" with their subsection numbers are the sections.

**Tenancy, stated first.** `divisions` now, with `division_id` on jobs, crews, crew members, equipment units, documents, proposals, settings and connections; `org_id uuid not null default '<this-org>'` on the spine and money tables in the migrations that create them, `document_sequences` unique on `(org_id, kind, year)`, and RLS predicates written against `current_org()` — a constant today, a JWT claim later (Decision record §7). Today there is no division: `jobType` is a per-job flag that chooses which forms show (`apps/field/js/model.js:100-102,162`), the board's `TYPES` list mixes division with loss type (`apps/board/js/board.js:30-38`), the spine records a rebuild as `loss_type='construction'` (`apps/field/js/spine.js:36`), and one settings tile `00000000-…-01` holding `{workDays, hoursPerDay, holidays}` **(live)** serves the whole company from seven hard-coded sites (inventory §8). Ceiling: one legal entity with several divisions and offices holds to 100+; a second entity is a claim change and a predicate swap because the column already exists. The owner's simpler option (divisions only) and its stated cost are in Decision record §7.

### 3.1 How to read the entity tables

- **Kind.** *state* = current truth, updated in place by an operation; *event* = an append-only fact, corrected by a compensating event, never edited; *reference* = slow-changing catalog; *projection* = derived and rebuildable; *contract* = the backbone tables of §2.2 (Decision record §4).
- **History.** **E** = `events` rows emitted by the operation that changed the row (§2.2); **V** = explicit version rows (issued documents, policies, scope baselines); **A** = the row is itself append-only, so the fact is the history; **P** = `blob_history` pre-images (`215_blob_history.sql`) while the section still lives in the blob and rows carry `origin='blob'`; **—** = reference data changed by migration or an `admin`-class op.
- **Today.** Where the concept lives at `1e04694`: a path under `field_projects.data` (with its `model.js` factory line), a table, or *absent*.
- **Every table** carries `id uuid` (client-generatable, server default `gen_random_uuid()`), `created_at`, `updated_at`, the actor pair of §3.4, and `org_id`/`division_id` where the row is job- or money-scoped. Blob-origin rows add `origin`, `source_ref`, `blob_rev`.

### 3.2 Entities

#### A. Company, division, people

| Entity | Kind | Key fields | Relationships | History | Today |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `divisions` | reference | id, org_id, name, kind (mitigation \| reconstruction \| remodel \| new_build), calendar jsonb {workDays, hoursPerDay, holidays}, qbo_class, mailbox_connection_id | ← jobs, crews, crew_members, equipment_units, documents, proposals, settings, connections | E | absent; the settings tile **(live)**, `jobType` `model.js:162`, board `TYPES` `board.js:30-38` |
| `locations` | reference | id, division_id, kind (office \| yard \| warehouse), address, lat, lng | ← equipment_units.home_location_id; travel buckets [A-0.7] | E | absent |
| `profiles` | state | id → auth.users, role (owner \| office \| crew_lead \| crew \| viewer \| agent after the remap of §2.4), default_division_id, display_name | ↔ crew_members.principal_id, contacts.principal_id | E | `001_initial_schema.sql:47-56`; 11 rows {admin 2, tech 7, viewer 2} **(live)**; `office` added `216_individual_logins.sql:50`, no live row; gate `_sync_guard` `218_sync_rpcs_and_build_gate.sql:67-69` |
| `agents` | state | id, name, kind (worker \| phone \| web \| mcp \| intake), auth_user_id, active | ← agent_authority, agent_runs (§2.2, §2.4); seeded in P1 with `agent:billing`, `agent:projection`, `agent:outbox`, `agent:brief`, `agent:phone`, `agent:web` (§3.8) | E | two `viewer` profiles fenced by 26 email-string policies (`204`, `205`); password grants `services/phone-agent/supa.mjs:30-33`, `roybal-brief/index.ts:62-71` |
| `principals` | projection (view) | id, kind (human \| agent), display_name, role, division_ids | union of `profiles` and `agents`; the one identity every actor column resolves through | — | absent |
| `contacts` | state | existing (`228_contacts.sql:51-70`): kind, role (customer \| adjuster \| agent \| property_manager \| sub \| other → **add** `lender`, `carrier_desk`), phone_norm, email_norm, merged_into, qbo_customer_id; **add** `principal_id uuid null` (staff who are also contacts), `carrier_id` (adjusters, desks) | ← claims.customer_contact_id, claim_adjusters.contact_id, sov_schedules.lender_contact_id, threads.contact_id, crew_members.contact_id | E; `contact_timeline` view `229_contact_links.sql:49-62` | exists, 40 rows **(live)**; adjuster rows exist (`228:54`) but no claim links them |
| `crew_members` | state | id, org_id, division_id, crew_id, principal_id **null** (the login, absent for a member who never signs in), contact_id **not null**, name, phone, email, hourly_rate_usd, qb_user_id, contact_prefs jsonb {digestOptOut, appOptOut, paperTimesheet}, active, deleted_at | → crews, contacts; ← crew_member_skills, certifications, availability_*, schedule_assignments, time_entries | E | `crew_members.data` blob, 11 rows, last-write-wins, no rev (`101_coordination.sql:53-59`); live keys role, hourlyRate, qbUserId, outDays, digestOptOut (2), bioCerts (1); `digestOptOut` honored at `roybal-brief/crewdigest.ts:149` |

**Role remap, stated here because `profiles` is the live role source.** The two live `admin` profiles are not both mapped to `owner` blindly: the migration maps `branden@…` to `owner` and the second `admin` to `office` by default, and the owner confirms the pair in the admin UI before the role claim is armed (a confirmation item in 04; the three-step enum migration is §2.4). `tech` → `crew`; the two machine `viewer` rows become `agents` rows and their `profiles.role` becomes `agent`.

**Person identity.** Three identities exist with no link between them: `profiles` (auth), `crew_members` (roster; `email` on 7 of 11 rows **(live)**), `contacts` (CRM). The target is one uuid: `crew_members.principal_id` and `contacts.principal_id` both point at `profiles.id`, humans and agents are one `principals` view, and every crew member has a mandatory `contact_id` so the roster row exists whether or not a login does. `principal_id` is **nullable** on purpose: only 7 of the 11 roster rows carry an email, and the paper-timesheet member may never have a login. The one-time bind matches on email where an email is present and is confirmed by the owner for the rest — a reviewed `(crew_member_id, profile_id | null)` table in the P3 migration, never a blanket match; a null `principal_id` is treated by `canNotify` as opt-out of every app lane. Ceiling: 100+ — attribution, assignment-scoped reads (§2.4) and payroll join on one column. `contact_prefs` is checked by every crew-facing send, so the paper-timesheet, phone-only crew member is a row, not an exception in code.

Ceiling of the spine: one physical `jobs` row per job with real FKs from every child holds to 100+ people and hundreds of concurrent jobs; the four identities a job has today (`field_projects`, `coordination_jobs`, `unified_jobs`, `portal_jobs`, soft-linked by uuids inside JSON) are what respawned the Hebard tile six times. Forcing condition: none structural; the board tile column goes away when the board reads `jobs` (P5). A job that spans two claims is not modeled — it is two jobs linked by `linked_job_id`.

Ceiling: the loop is append-only rows keyed by claim, carrier and adjuster; `carrier.learn` is a weekly `GROUP BY` over `rejection_reasons` and holds to hundreds of claims a year without a search index. Forcing condition: free-text similarity across thousands of rejection texts → a `pg_trgm` index on `rejection_reasons.text` (the extension is already installed).

Ceiling of the capture model: per-location readings, placement events and typed time entries are the unit of work capabilities 1–4 need, and rows at this grain are small (a 52-location map for 30 days is 1,560 readings). Forcing condition, stated in Decision record §6: more than ~5 devices editing one job's blob concurrently, or an office feature that must edit a capture-owned section — that section moves to rows plus the field outbox, one section at a time. The `rooms[]` identity migration is the one risky shape change and is done additively (`roomIds`, Decision record §6); the projection resolves every free-text `room` through `aliases` per job [A-3.2].

| `payments` | event | id, org_id, document_id, amount_usd, received_on, method, qbo_payment_id unique, source (qbo_pull \| manual) | drives `documents.paid_state`, collections (§4 step 12) | A | `invoices[].payments[]` written by the nightly pull `supabase/functions/qbo-proxy/payments.ts:37-65` |
| `external_refs` | state | entity_kind, entity_id, system (qbo \| qbtime \| gmail \| twilio), external_id, sync_token, last_synced_at | one home for integration ids; QBO writes go through `external_intents` (§2.2; §4 step 9) | E | `qboInvoiceId` in the blob, `contacts.qbo_customer_id` `228:62`, `qbTimesheetId` in JSON |
| `sov_schedules` | state | id, job_id, contract_amount_usd, lender_contact_id, retainage_pct, notes | ← sov_lines, draw_requests | E | `drawSchedule`, `lender`, `contractAmount` on 8 construction jobs **(live)**; `model.js:164,168,664-669`; editor `forms.js:3221-3232` |
| `sov_lines` | state | id, sov_id, sort, description, pct, amount_usd | | E | `drawSchedule.rows[{desc, pct, amount, invoicedDate, paidDate, invoiceId}]` `model.js:668` |
| `draw_requests` | event | id, sov_id, number, period_to, requested_usd, approved_usd, document_id (kind=draw_request), submitted_at, paid_at | | A | `rows[].invoicedDate/paidDate/invoiceId` |

Ceiling of the document model: one generic `documents` table with a `kind` column (the option chosen in Decision record §8 and §6.7) holds every kind to hundreds of documents a day; the dual representation (rows on drafts, `model_json` when issued) is the cost, paid by the `billed_lines` view. Forcing condition: a kind with validation the generic row cannot express (a lender's draw form with fixed fields) → a per-kind terms table, which `change_order_terms` already demonstrates.

Ceiling: the scheduling entities are the inputs `schedule.js` (887 lines, pure, four runtimes) already consumes plus the four it lacks — skills/certs, availability, equipment, travel — expressed as rows; what the P7 pass adds to the engine (skill/cert filters, per-person capacity, equipment reservations, drive-time from geocode) is stated in §7.9, and the engine's ceiling is compute per proposal, not data. Forcing condition: distance buckets stop being good enough → a travel-matrix provider [A-0.7]; crews across time zones → `divisions.calendar` per crew.

#### H. Contract tables, integration health and projections (cross-references only)

`proposals`, `events`, `jobs_queue`, `outbox`, `agent_runs`, `agents`, `agent_authority`, `role_permissions`, `approval_policies`, `operation_catalog`, `api_tokens`, `worker_heartbeats`, `ai_caps`, `web_sessions` (§2.2, §2.3, §2.4); `model_routes`, `model_prices`, `prompts`, `eval_runs`, `ai_usage` (§2.7); `carrier_profiles` (§6.9), `external_intents` (§2.2), `qbo_item_map` and `internal_rates` (§3.7, §3.8); `event_subscriptions` and `on_call` (§3.8); the per-kind quiet-hours settings in `role_permissions` / `notification_policies` (§2.4). Integration health is two things, not one: `connections` (provider, division, token, cadence) and **`integration_runs`**`(connection_id, kind pull|push|refresh|webhook, started_at, finished_at, ok, error, rows_affected, external_ref)`, written by **every** adapter call whether it ran from `jobs_queue` (pulls) or `outbox` (pushes) — with `integration_health` a **view** over `integration_runs ⋈ connections` (last_ok_at, last_error, consecutive_failures, token_expires_at) whose alert rule is 3 consecutive failures or no successful run in 2× the expected cadence (§2.6); E0's interim is a simpler view over the three token tables.

Projections that stay projections: `unified_jobs` (view, §3.5), `principals` (view), `billed_lines` (view), `contact_timeline` (view, `229:49-62`), `drying_predictions`, the reporting **materialized views** `jobs_summary`, `ar_aging`, `crew_hours`, `automation_costs` with their read ops and the admin Reports tab (P5, §7.7), and `portal_jobs` — the customer-safe table with nine jsonb columns (7 rows **(live)**, `107_portal_jobs.sql:24-39`) is rebuilt by the publish op from rows, never hand-written.

### 3.3 Core-table DDL sketch

The twenty tables the capabilities cannot do without, key columns only; `pk`, `fk`, `uq` abbreviate constraints; every table also carries `created_at`, `updated_at`, `created_by_kind/created_by_id` (§3.4). Amounts are `numeric(12,2)` in USD; quantities `numeric(12,3)`; enums are `text` with `check` constraints.

```sql
divisions      (id pk, org_id, name, kind check, calendar jsonb, qbo_class, deleted_at)
profiles       (id pk fk auth.users, role user_role, default_division_id fk divisions, display_name)
contacts       (id pk, kind, role check, name, phone_norm gen, email_norm gen, merged_into fk contacts,
                principal_id fk profiles null, carrier_id fk carriers null, qbo_customer_id, deleted_at)
carriers       (id pk, org_id, name, aliases text[], default_profile_id fk carrier_profiles, days_to_pay_median)
claims         (id pk, org_id, claim_number, claim_number_norm, carrier_id fk carriers,
                customer_contact_id fk contacts, date_of_loss, loss_types text[], water_category, water_class,
                cause_of_loss, status check, deleted_at)
policies       (id pk, claim_id fk claims, carrier_id fk carriers, policy_number, limits jsonb, sublimits jsonb,
                deductible_usd, deductible_applies_to check, endorsements text[], exclusions text[],
                acv_rcv_basis check, holdback_rule jsonb, effective_from, effective_to)
claim_adjusters(id pk, claim_id fk claims, contact_id fk contacts, carrier_id fk carriers, desk check,
                from_at, to_at null, reason check)
jobs           (id pk, org_id, division_id fk divisions, job_number uq(org_id, job_number), kind check, stage check,
                customer_contact_id fk contacts, claim_id fk claims null, linked_job_id fk jobs null,
                property_address, lat, lng, field_project_id uq null, board_tile_id null, qb_jobcode_id, deleted_at)
rooms          (id pk, job_id fk jobs, name, level, aliases text[], floor_sf, perim_lf, ceiling_ft,
                source check, origin check, source_ref, blob_rev, deleted_at,
                uq(job_id, lower(name)) where deleted_at is null)
moisture_locations(id pk, map_id, job_id fk jobs, room_id fk rooms, marker_no, label, material_class,
                dry_goal_pct, dry_goal_source_id fk dry_standards, reference_location_id fk moisture_locations null,
                uq(map_id, marker_no))
moisture_readings(id pk, location_id fk moisture_locations, job_id fk jobs, read_on date, value_pct numeric(5,1),
                meter, tech_kind, tech_id, photo_id fk photos null, source check, origin, source_ref, blob_rev,
                uq(location_id, read_on, source_ref))
equipment_units(id pk, org_id, division_id fk divisions, asset_tag uq, type check, model, serial, ppd_or_cfm,
                owned_or_rented check, vendor_contact_id fk contacts null, status check,
                home_location_id fk locations)
equipment_placements(id pk, unit_id fk equipment_units null, type check, job_id fk jobs, room_id fk rooms,
                placed_at, removed_at null, placed_by_kind, placed_by_id, removed_by_kind, removed_by_id,
                photo_ids uuid[], source check, origin, source_ref, blob_rev)
drying_days    (id pk, job_id fk jobs, log_id, day date, time, tech_kind, tech_id, out_t, out_rh, out_gpp,
                ref_t, ref_rh, ref_gpp, aff_t, aff_rh, aff_gpp, gd, dehu_count int, am_count int, scrub_count int,
                heater_count int, notes, origin, blob_rev, uq(job_id, log_id, day, time))
time_entries   (id pk, org_id, job_id fk jobs, crew_member_id fk crew_members null, work_date, hours numeric(6,2),
                start_time, finish_time, service_item, task, note,
                source check (source in ('qbtime','manual','board','paper')),
                qb_timesheet_id uq where not null, qb_jobcode_id, qb_user_id, phase_id fk job_phases null,
                phase_match jsonb, entered_by_kind, entered_by_id, deleted_at)
photos         (id pk, job_id fk jobs, room_id fk rooms null, stage, caption, taken_at, taken_by_kind, taken_by_id,
                media_sha, cloud_path, ai jsonb, ai_version, origin, blob_rev, deleted_at, deleted_by)
documents      (id pk, org_id, division_id fk divisions, job_id fk jobs, claim_id fk claims null, kind check,
                number, current_version_no, status check, paid_state check, issued_at, deleted_at,
                uq(org_id, kind, number) where number is not null)
document_versions(id pk, document_id fk documents, version_no, model_json jsonb, model_hash, html_hash,
                pdf_sha256, storage_path, page_count int,
                render_status check (render_status in ('queued','rendering','rendered','failed')), rendered_at,
                template_version, carrier_profile_id fk carrier_profiles null,
                issued_at, issued_by_kind, issued_by_id, supersedes_version_id fk document_versions null,
                void_reason, uq(document_id, version_no))
                -- immutability trigger: no UPDATE/DELETE on a version of an issued document, except the render
                -- columns (html_hash, pdf_sha256, storage_path, page_count, rendered_at, render_status)
                -- while render_status <> 'rendered'
line_items     (id pk, document_id fk documents, job_id fk jobs, room_id fk rooms null, sort, code, category,
                description, qty numeric(12,3), unit, unit_price_usd, total_usd generated,
                price_source_id fk price_sources, price_ref, price_basis check, justification text,
                evidence_refs jsonb, flag, origin, deleted_at)
submittals     (id pk, org_id, job_id fk jobs, claim_id fk claims, kind check, document_version_ids uuid[],
                packet_document_version_id fk document_versions null, carrier_id fk carriers,
                adjuster_contact_id fk contacts null, to_contact_id fk contacts, channel check, format check,
                sent_at, sent_by_kind, sent_by_id, outbox_id fk outbox, external_ref,
                parent_submittal_id fk submittals null, status check)
carrier_responses(id pk, submittal_id fk submittals, received_at, kind check, approved_amount_usd,
                message_id fk messages null, recorded_by_kind, recorded_by_id, source check)
rejection_reasons(id pk, response_id fk carrier_responses, line_item_id fk line_items null, code, category,
                reason_code check, text, carrier_id fk carriers, adjuster_contact_id fk contacts null)
crews          (id pk, org_id, division_id fk divisions, name, lead_member_id fk crew_members null, active)
crew_members   (id pk, org_id, division_id fk divisions, crew_id fk crews null, principal_id fk profiles null,
                contact_id fk contacts not null, name, phone, email, hourly_rate_usd, qb_user_id uq null,
                contact_prefs jsonb, active, deleted_at)
job_phases     (id pk, job_id fk jobs, sort, name, template_key, duration_days, lag_days, estimated_hours,
                planned_start, planned_finish, done bool, completed_on, deleted_at)
schedule_assignments(id pk, job_id fk jobs, phase_id fk job_phases null, crew_id fk crews null,
                crew_member_id fk crew_members null, work_date, hours_planned, source check,
                proposal_id fk proposals, applied_at, check (crew_id is not null or crew_member_id is not null))
```

`carrier_profiles`, `price_sources`, `dry_standards`, `skills`, `certifications`, `availability_*`, `leads`, `sov_*`, `payments`, `signatures`, `threads`/`messages`, `scope_baselines`, `observations`, `supplements`, `external_refs`, `captures`, `connections`/`integration_runs` follow the shapes in §3.2 and §3.7 with the same conventions.

### 3.4 Conventions (one each)

- **Naming.** Tables are plural `snake_case`; foreign keys are `<singular>_id`; timestamps end in `_at` (`timestamptz`), dates in `_on` (`date`); money columns end in `_usd numeric(12,2)`, quantities are `numeric(12,3)`; enumerations are `text` with a `check` constraint, never a Postgres enum — `ALTER TYPE … ADD VALUE` cannot run inside a transaction, which is exactly what makes the one existing enum (`user_role`) need the three-step migration in §2.4 (Decision record §5); `jsonb` columns hold shapes documented as JSON Schemas in `packages/domain/schemas` with their vocabularies in `packages/domain/vocab`, and camelCase keys appear only inside blob-origin `jsonb` (`model_json`, `phase_match`, `ai`), never as column names. Today snake_case columns are mapped to camelCase blob keys by hand (`spine.js:23-40`).
- **Soft delete.** State tables carry `deleted_at timestamptz` and `deleted_by`; event tables never do — a wrong fact is corrected by a compensating event; the blob's `deletedIds` tombstones (`merge.js:59-64`; `243:46-62`) become `deleted_at` at projection; restore is an op that clears `deleted_at` and emits an event; hard purge only by the retention job of §2.2. This replaces the six idioms live today (`deleted bool`, `deleted_at`+`purged_at`, `merged_into`, `revoked_at`, `enabled`, JSON `archived`/`deletedIds` — inventory §6.1).
- **Identity and actor.** Every actor column is a pair `(<verb>_by_kind text check in ('human','agent','policy','integration','system'), <verb>_by_id uuid)`; humans and agents resolve through `principals`; policies resolve to `approval_policies.id` (§2.2); no email strings and no free labels (`'qb-time'`, `'quickbooks-time'`, `'web-agent'`, `'office'`, which appear in `pending_actions.proposed_by`, `time_entries.data.enteredBy` and `sms_messages.sent_by` today) — each becomes an `agents` row. Row ids are uuid v4, generatable on the device for offline capture, with `gen_random_uuid()` as the server default; business numbers (`job_number`, document numbers) come only from `document_sequences`.

### 3.5 Migration collision: era-0 names become schema `legacy`

Seven of the target names already exist as live tables from the deleted React app — `jobs`, `rooms`, `photos`, `moisture_readings`, `line_items`, `invoices`, `documents` — beside `equipment_logs`, `floor_plans`, `canvas_plans`, `manual_floor_plans`, `floor_plan_rooms`, `floor_plan_openings`, `room_openings`, `room_markers`, `tasks`, `communications`, `work_authorizations`, `tech_checkins`, `reconstruction_items`: twenty tables holding single-digit March rows (`jobs` 2, `rooms` 6, `photos` 9, `moisture_readings` 6, `equipment_logs` 5, `line_items` 2, `invoices` 1 **(live)**), with no live writer once `magicplan-webhook` is deleted at E0. They carry what a fresh `create table` would collide with: the `RC-YYYY-NNN` sequence trigger `set_job_number` (`001:108-119`), `compute_is_dry` (`001:181-196`), `set_invoice_number` **(live, no repo DDL)**, six enums (`job_status` with 18 live values against the repo's 6, `loss_type`, `loss_category`, `equipment_type`, `photo_category`, `billing_type` — `001:20-42`), the `is_assigned_to_job()` policies that produce most of the 255 `multiple_permissive_policies` advisor warnings, 24 FK edges (`live-fks`), and live-only columns the repo never saw — `jobs.policy_number`, `deductible_amount`, `xactimate_file_number`, `portal_token`, `property_manager_*` (35 live columns against 21 in `001:83-105`), `documents.doc_type/signed_at/signed_by_name`, `invoices.invoice_type/amount_cents/paid_cents/xactimate_ref`, `work_authorizations.owner_sig_data_url` **(live)**.

The step, in order, and it precedes every `create table` in P3:

1. It runs **after** the baseline squash of Decision record §11 (§8.6; a P1 item), so `0000_baseline.sql` captures the live-only columns and `set_invoice_number` before anything moves.
2. One migration: `create schema legacy; alter table public.<t> set schema legacy` for the twenty tables (FKs, triggers, indexes and policies travel with them); `alter type public.<e> set schema legacy` for the six enums; `alter function` for `generate_job_number`, `compute_is_dry`, `set_invoice_number`, `is_assigned_to_job`; `revoke all on schema legacy from anon, authenticated` (closes the 49-table pg_graphql exposure for this set). `profiles` stays — it is the live role source. `qb_time_tokens` and `qb_time_jobcodes` (`003_qb_time.sql:6,22`) stay — era 1 reuses them. The two era-0 buckets `job-photos` and `floor-plans` (19 objects **(live)**) stay untouched; a bucket has no schema.
3. Verify: `select count(*) from legacy.jobs` = 2 and the other counts above; `grep -rn "from('jobs')\|/rest/v1/jobs\|rest/v1/rooms\|rest/v1/line_items" apps supabase services` returns only the deleted Magicplan functions; `supabase db reset` on the baseline plus this migration passes in CI.
4. Only then, in one transaction: `alter table public.unified_jobs rename to jobs` — an **in-place rename, never a copy** — followed by `alter table … add column` for the columns of §3.2-B, and `create view public.unified_jobs as select …` with the old column list so the five field sites, `spine.js`, the phone agent, the brief, the portal, notify and old PWA builds keep reading (Decision record §6). The rename preserves the 33 ids that `contacts` links (`229:31`) and that the portal and phone agent already hold, so nothing is backfilled; the view is kept until every reader has moved and §7.5 reads the same way. `photos` is created fresh; `field_photos` stays trigger-fed until the worker projection is zero-diff for 30 days, then drops (Decision record §6; §7.11). `rooms`, `moisture_readings`, `line_items`, `documents`, `invoices` (as a `documents` kind) are created fresh in P3/P4 with the DDL of §3.3 — era-0's DDL is the first draft (`001:137-247`), not the table.

Rollback at any point is `alter table legacy.<t> set schema public`; nothing in the twenty tables is rewritten. Ceiling: not a scale question — a one-time move that removes the trap where an agent reading the schema writes `line_items` and hits the wrong table.

### 3.6 Ceilings per structural decision

| Decision | Holds to | What forces change |
|---|---|---|
| One physical `jobs` spine with real FKs; `unified_jobs` renamed in place, compat view kept until the last reader moves | 100+ people, hundreds of concurrent jobs | nothing structural; the view drops when the last reader (phone agent, brief, portal, `spine.js`, old PWA builds) moves |
| `claims` 1:n `jobs`; `policies` versioned; `claim_adjusters` as history | hundreds of concurrent claims | a job on two claims (not modeled; use two linked jobs) |
| `submittals` (one row per sending, ordered version ids, optional composed packet) → `carrier_responses` → `rejection_reasons` → `learned_rules`, append-only | thousands of rows a year with weekly aggregation | free-text similarity at scale → `pg_trgm` index on reason text |
| `rooms` by id with `aliases`, identity arriving through the additive `roomIds` collection | every live job; old builds keep pushing strings | none; unresolved names create `source='blob'` rooms for the office to merge [A-3.2] |
| `moisture_locations` + `moisture_readings` per location per date | a 52-location map for 30 days is 1,560 rows; hundreds of jobs are low millions | none on Small; the blob's per-map goal is coerced with a `parse_warning` until per-location goals are captured [A-3.1] |
| `equipment_placements` as the billable event, `drying_days` counts as the legacy source | any fleet; per-day counts remain valid evidence until P3 scans exist | none; the reconciliation contract (§7.13) does not change |
| `time_entries` as columns with `qb_timesheet_id` unique and `source` incl. `paper` | 1,193 rows today; 100+ people ≈ 50k rows a year | none; expression indexes on `(job_id, work_date)` from P2 |
| `captures` as the one ingest envelope from P3; `capture_events` legacy, read-only, folded into `captures` + `events` in P5 | one row per capture at any device count | none |
| Documents: one generic table + immutable versions (render columns exempt only until `rendered`) + rows-on-draft/`model_json`-on-issue | hundreds of documents a day; one `billed_lines` query | a kind needing fixed validated fields → a per-kind terms table |
| Crews, skills, certs, availability patterns + exceptions, `schedule_assignments` proposal-managed (one proposal per run per crew) | multiple crews and divisions; engine compute per proposal | travel beyond distance buckets → a matrix provider [A-0.7] |
| Person identity via nullable `principal_id` and mandatory `contact_id` on `crew_members`, `principal_id` on `contacts`, `principals` view | 100+ people, agents and integrations; members with no login | none |
| Tenancy: `divisions` now, defaulted `org_id` on spine and money tables | one legal entity, several divisions and offices | a second entity → set the claim in the hook, swap `current_org()`; no schema change |
| History: events for state, versions for issued documents/policies/baselines, `blob_history` for blob-owned sections | low millions of events unpartitioned (§2.2) — reached within ~12 months at 100+ people | month partitioning is a **P5** item, not "later"; the compute upgrade brings it forward if it fires first |
| Era-0 tables moved to schema `legacy` before P3 | n/a (one-time) | none; rollback is `set schema public` |

### 3.7 Reconciled definitions — the one definition per entity; §4 and §6 adopt these

Where earlier drafts of §4 and §6 described these entities differently, the definition below is the one the assembled document carries; §3.2 and §3.3 already read this way, and the other sections are rewritten to match.

| Entity | What differed | One definition |
|---|---|---|
| `signatures` | a four-value `signer_kind`, `png_sha` and a `pad` method value in one place; `lender`/`external`, `signed_model_hash`, `captured_by`, `device_id`, `ip`, `consent_text_version`, `external_ref` in another | `signatures(id, document_version_id, signer_kind owner\|contractor\|adjuster\|tech\|lender\|external, signer_name, png_sha256, signed_model_hash, signed_at, method in_app\|upload\|portal, captured_by, captured_offline, device_id, ip, consent_text_version, external_ref)`; `captured_offline` is kept because the projection needs it to tell a blob-captured PNG from a pad write into the outbox (§6.8) |
| `submittals` | `document_version_ids uuid[]` + `kind` + `packet_json_path` in one place; singular `document_version_id` + `components jsonb[]` + `carrier_id`/`adjuster_contact_id`/`external_ref` in another | one row per *sending*: `job_id, claim_id, kind (estimate\|invoice\|supplement\|packet\|certificate\|drying_log\|photo_packet\|other), document_version_ids uuid[] (ordered), packet_document_version_id (the composed PDF, nullable), carrier_id, adjuster_contact_id, to_contact_id, channel (email\|portal\|carrier_portal\|mail\|api), sent_at, sent_by (principal), outbox_id, external_ref, parent_submittal_id, status`; `format` rides beside them because Decision record §8 requires the row to record what the carrier required. `components jsonb[]` is dropped — page ranges are the packet version's concern; `packet_json_path` is dropped — `packet.json` sits beside the packet version's PDF (§6.10) |
| `captures` / `capture_events` | two `kind` enums; the voice envelope in `capture_events` in one place and in `captures` in another | one enum: `kind voice\|photo_ai\|ocr_meter\|ocr_receipt\|barcode\|serial\|signature\|email_pull`, on the column shape of §6.16 (client-generated id, `typed_value`/`ocr_result`/`server_result`/`confirmed_value`, five-state `status`, `target_kind`/`target_id`). `captures` is the ingest envelope **from P3 on**; `capture_events` stays as the legacy ledger, **read-only after P3**, and is migrated into `captures` + `events` in **P5**. Web-agent rate state moves to `web_sessions` in P1 |
| `document_versions` | the §3.3 DDL lacked the render columns the §6.7 trigger depends on | `page_count int`, `render_status queued\|rendering\|rendered\|failed`, `rendered_at`, `html_hash`, `pdf_sha256`, `storage_path`, `model_json`, `template_version`; the immutability trigger exempts only the render columns while `render_status <> 'rendered'` |
| `time_entries.source` | `(qbtime\|manual\|board)` vs a `paper` lane written from the office | `source in ('qbtime','manual','board','paper')` |
| `unified_jobs` → `jobs` | rename in §3.5, copy in §7.5 | an in-place `alter table … rename`, plus a compatibility view named `unified_jobs` created in the same transaction and kept until every reader has moved (phone agent, brief, portal, field `spine.js`, old PWA builds); never a copy. §7.5 reads the same way |
| `crew_members` identity | "a one-time email match binds the 11 roster rows" | `principal_id` nullable, `contact_id` mandatory; the bind matches on email where present (7 of 11 rows **(live)**) and the owner confirms the rest through a reviewed `(crew_member_id, profile_id \| null)` table in the P3 migration; a member with no login keeps `principal_id` null and `canNotify` treats null as opt-out of every app lane |
| crew read scope | "read claims, never join per row" vs `job_assignments` ∪ walk-on | the JWT claim carries `principal_id`, `role`, `division_id`, `org_id` and **not** assigned job ids; predicates call the `STABLE` SECURITY DEFINER `assigned_job_ids()` (one initplan evaluation per statement) plus the walk-on rule — stated once in §2.4 and identically in ADR-04; §3.2-F only points there |
| `agents` seed rows | named throughout §4, never listed | `supabase/seed.sql` carries **six** rows in P1 — `agent:billing`, `agent:projection`, `agent:outbox`, `agent:brief`, `agent:phone`, `agent:web`; the principals §4 names for later lanes (`agent:integrations`, `agent:correspondence`, `agent:collections`, `agent:worker`, `agent:intake`, `agent:mcp`) are added by the migration of the phase whose lane first runs under them; the worker selects its principal per queue kind from a table in `services/worker`, never from a default |
| `qbo_item_map` | named in §4 step 9 only | `qbo_item_map(org_id, category, qbo_item_id, active)` (P4), seeded from the 48 QBO items ever used; a category with no row falls back to the one generic service item **and** flags the line "unmapped" on the document card |

### 3.8 Every table, its phase

Tables that appear in this document but were assigned no phase in §7 are placed here; the roadmap tables in §7.3–§7.9 are read with these additions. P1 contains no package extraction and no build-tool work (those open P3 as its "toolchain" sub-step, §7.3); the tables below are migrations only.

| Phase | Tables and views added beyond the phase's own list |
|---|---|
| E0 | none — the interim `integration_health` is a view over the three existing token tables |
| P1 | `connections`, `integration_runs`, the `integration_health` view over `integration_runs ⋈ connections`; `worker_heartbeats` (read every 5 min by the pg_cron liveness check that posts to `roybal-webhooks /alert`, the one HTTP call pg_cron keeps, §2.5); `external_refs` (the outbox deliverer needs it for the migrated lanes' Gmail ids); `ai_caps` and `web_sessions` as renames of the existing cap and web-agent rate state; the six `agents` seed rows |
| P2 | none — `photos.ai.equipment[]` / `ai.materials[]` become a `billing.reconcile` hint input, which is a read, not a table |
| P3 | `captures` (the ingest envelope from here on), `locations`, `job_assignments`, `equipment_reservations` (created here, first written in P7), `eval_runs` with the first prompt version; the era-0 `legacy` move (§3.5) precedes them |
| P4 | `external_intents`, `qbo_item_map`, `change_order_terms`, `signatures`, `payments`, `price_sources` + `internal_rates`; PITR for Postgres, with the `documents` bucket rebuildable from `model_json` + `template_version` + the pinned Chromium build (§6.7) |
| P5 | `leads` (with `lead.screen`), `event_subscriptions`, `on_call`, the reporting materialized views `jobs_summary`, `ar_aging`, `crew_hours`, `automation_costs` with read ops and the Reports tab; `capture_events` folded into `captures` + `events`; month partitioning of `events` |
| P6 | `threads`, `messages`, `carrier_responses`, `rejection_reasons`, `learned_rules`, `supplements`, `scope_baselines`, `observations` (with `scope.compare`); QBO bills/expenses from `receipts` and `materials`, customer creation and class per division; **`notification_policies`** (`kind`, `channel`, `quiet_start`, `quiet_end`, `opt_out_field`) — only if the owner takes the crew-line quiet-hours option in [04-OPEN-QUESTIONS.md](04-OPEN-QUESTIONS.md); until then the flat 7am–8pm AK window is enforced in `packages/domain/policies/quiet_hours.ts` and the table is not created |
| P7 | `drying_predictions` (method in §7.9); `schedule_assignments` first written by `schedule.propose` |

---

## 4. End-to-end flow: mitigation job marked dry → agent drafts invoice → office approves → invoice issues → PDF → QuickBooks → collections

This is the flow the backbone exists for. It is walked here as it runs once the documents phase (P4) has landed — invoices born in tables, one issue transaction, one render pipeline — with a note per step on what runs earlier (P1–P3) and a table at the end that says which step exists in which phase. Conventions: **principal** is who the audit row names (`profiles` for people, `agents` rows for machines); **runtime** is SQL (a `SECURITY DEFINER` `op_<name>` function behind the gateway — `op_proposal_approve` for `proposal.approve@1`, and so on), edge (`roybal-ops`; or `roybal-webhooks` for the two SQL ops it alone may call, Step 6), or worker (`roybal-worker` on Fly); **key** is the idempotency key the registry computes from the input and that is `unique` on `proposals`, `events`, `outbox` and `jobs_queue`. Citations are `path:line` at `main@1e04694`; **(live)** is production; **[A-4.n]** marks assumptions this section introduces; the decision record's assumptions are cited as [A-0.n]. Cross-references: "Decision record §n" is Part 0 of this document; a bare "§n" or "§n.m" is one of the assembled sections §1–§8. Package paths follow §1.1: the calc engines are `packages/domain/engines/`, the detectors `packages/domain/detect/`, the document models, templates and renderer `packages/documents/`, the vendor adapters `packages/integrations/`. Machine principals: the P1 `agents` seed is six rows — `agent:billing`, `agent:projection`, `agent:outbox`, `agent:brief`, `agent:phone`, `agent:web` (§3); the principals this flow names beyond them — `agent:worker` (render) and `agent:integrations` (payment pulls) at P4, `agent:correspondence` and `agent:collections` at P6 — are seeded by the migration of the phase that first grants them `agent_authority`, and until then the lane's earlier principal, named per step, does the work.

### 4.0 What the same ten steps look like today

| Step | Today | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Job marked dry | a signature PNG (or an uploaded signed scan) inside the blob's `certDrying` is the state; `isCertified` is the only predicate and it runs in the browser | `apps/field/js/dryingwatch.js:30-33`; `apps/field/js/model.js:520-535` |
| Draft | an office user clicks "Draft from documentation"; the browser assembles `invoiceFacts`, the edge function returns lines with a required `basis` per line, and the client stores `code/priced/flag` and drops `basis` after rendering it once | `apps/field/js/officeai.js:139-181`; `supabase/functions/roybal-ai-office/index.ts:369-393`; `apps/field/js/forms.js:1484-1488` vs `:1505` |
| Quantities | typed strings — the live sample bills 215 air-mover-days against a drying log that shows `am:"20"` per day on rows dated 07-31 and 08-03 only **(live)**; the certificate itself carries typed "# × days" strings | `model.js:353-361`, `:526`; `forms.js:1170-1175` |
| Review / approve | none — the chip writes IndexedDB and a blob push carries it; "only an approved estimate can be invoiced" lives in client JS | `apps/admin/js/finactions.js:127-128`, `:137` |
| Issue | `INV-${invoices.length + 1}` (repeats after a delete) or a hand-typed number; no issued state, no version | `finactions.js:137`; `forms.js:1866`; `fincalc.js:16` |
| PDF | `window.print()` over the live DOM; no server can produce the document | `apps/field/js/app.js:1552,1936,2413` |
| QuickBooks | one generic service item for every line, `DocNumber = invoiceNo.slice(0,21)`, update-in-place when `qboInvoiceId` is known, otherwise a **new** invoice every time; the browser appends an O&P line | `supabase/functions/qbo-proxy/index.ts:52-54,316-321,336,345-355`; `apps/field/js/qbo.js:50-54` |
| Submittal | an email drafted by `adjusterEmail`, sent text-only through the `gmailSend` chip; nothing records that a packet went out or which version | `roybal-ai-office/index.ts:760-782`; `apps/admin/js/assistctx.js:194-220`; `gmail-proxy/index.ts:345-349` |
| Payments | nightly balance pull applies `payments.ts` rules and writes the blob with a service-role PATCH that bypasses `push_project` | `qbo-proxy/index.ts:375-456`, `:429-444`; `qbo-proxy/payments.ts:37-65` |
| Collections | the morning brief proposes at most two reminder emails into `pending_actions`, approved only by a text from `OWNER_CELL`; codes are picked app-side; expiry is never written; three zombie rows `pending` since 2026-07-25 (expired 07-26) have jammed the queue **(live)** | `roybal-brief/index.ts:249-299`, `:264-266`; `roybal-notify/index.ts:400-401`, `:438-443`; `210_pending_actions.sql:24-38`; `qb-time-proxy/index.ts:285,494-499` |
| Delivery | `twilioPost` sends no `StatusCallback`; the router serves only `/inbound` and `sendSms`; 174 of 175 outbound rows sit at `queued` **(live)** although `config.toml` claims a callback handler | `roybal-notify/index.ts:247-260`, `:646`; `supabase/config.toml:53-54` |

### 4.1 The walk

**Step 1 — Job marked dry.**
*Trigger.* The tech signs the Certificate of Drying on the device (offline is fine) or the office uploads a wet-signed scan — the same two facts `isCertified` accepts today (`dryingwatch.js:30-33`). The blob pushes through `push_project` unchanged (`241_merge_delete_tombstones.sql:193-282`); the `field_projects` AFTER UPDATE trigger inserts one debounced `jobs_queue(kind='job.project')` row — at most one projection per job per minute (§2.2); the worker projection upserts `moisture_readings`, `drying_days`, `equipment_placements` (P3) and the certificate's signature as a `signatures` row in the one shape of §3 (`signer_kind='tech'`, `method='in_app'` for the pad or `'upload'` for the scan, `captured_offline`, `png_sha256`, `signed_model_hash`) bound to the current issued `cert_drying` version (P4, [A-0.4]) — all rows `origin='blob'`, no semantic event.
*Operation.* `job.mark_dry@1` (action type `job`), input `{jobId, certSignedAt, source: 'cert_signed'|'manual'}`.
*Runtime.* SQL: `op_job_mark_dry` sets `jobs.stage='dry'`, `jobs.dry_certified_at`, and — in the same transaction — enqueues Step 2.
*Who invokes it.* Two paths. (a) A crew lead or tech taps "Mark dry" in the field app: online, straight through the ops door; offline, an IndexedDB outbox row drained after the next successful pull (P3). Permission: `(crew_lead|crew, job, execute)` on a job in `assigned_job_ids()` — the `STABLE` `SECURITY DEFINER` helper that returns `job_assignments` ∪ the walk-on rule (any job in the crew's division with a stage in the active set, for 24 h after scanning its QR/asset label or being added by a crew lead), evaluated once per statement, never per row (§2.4; Decision record §5). (b) The projection worker notices the certificate signature landed and the job is not `dry`: it files a proposal as `agent:projection`; an `approval_policies` row auto-approves `job.mark_dry` when `source='cert_signed'`, stamping `approved_by_kind='policy'`, `approved_by_ref=<policy id>` [A-4.1]. The human approval already happened on the device — the signature is it. A crew member who never touches the app (paper timesheet, phone only) is covered by (b) through the office's scan upload; nothing in this step texts or emails a crew member.
*Reads → writes.* `jobs(stage, dry_certified_at)`, `documents(kind='cert_drying', status, current_version_no)`; writes `jobs`, `events`, `jobs_queue`.
*Key.* `job.mark_dry:{job_id}:{cert_signed_at}` — a re-signature on a new date is a new fact; the same signature re-pushed by a second device is a no-op.
*Recorded.* `events(kind='job.marked_dry', principal, job_id, claim_id, proposal_id?)`; the proposal row when path (b) ran.
*Failure.* Worker down: the blob still syncs, the projection queue waits, the office is not blocked; the certificate pushes on save (`apps/field/js/sync.js:617`) and is visible to the office and every other device on the 45 s pull (`sync.js:623`), and the office may mark dry by hand (path a, office holds execute). **Re-wet after dry:** `job.set_stage(drying)` from `dry` requires a reason and emits `job.rewet`; the SQL body marks any open `invoice.issue` proposal for the job `superseded` (event) so nothing stale can be approved; Step 2's key (below) decides whether the next dry produces a new draft or refreshes the old one.
*What people see.* Crew: the certificate form exactly as today, then a "Dry — office notified" badge on the job card after sync. Office: the job moves to `dry` on Today; nothing to do yet.
*Ceiling.* Per-job serialization is `push_project`'s `FOR UPDATE` (`241:208-213`) plus the projection debounce (≤1 projection per job per minute); holds to hundreds of concurrent jobs with a handful of devices each. The forcing condition is the one in the blob strategy (Decision record §6): more than ~5 devices editing one job. The load behind that number: ~300 active jobs pushing on a 45 s cadence would be ≈500k projection jobs a day if unbatched; the per-job debounce caps it at ≈430k in the worst case (§2.2), which is why `jobs_queue` stays partition-free, is indexed on `(status, run_after)`, and is purged by a daily job.

**Step 2 — Fan-out: the draft job is enqueued.**
*Trigger.* Inside `op_job_mark_dry`'s transaction. The fan-out is declared on the op definition (`enqueues: ['billing.draft_invoice']`) and executed by the SQL body — no trigger on `events`, nothing over HTTP; a data-driven `event_subscriptions` table is the P5 upgrade when automation kinds multiply (§3; §7.7) [A-4.2].
*Runtime.* SQL `enqueue(kind, input, idempotency_key, run_after)`.
*Writes.* `jobs_queue(kind='billing.draft_invoice', input{job_id, claim_id, period_start}, idempotency_key, status='queued', max_attempts=5, lease_kind='agent')`.
*Key.* `billing.draft_invoice:{job_id}:{period_start}` where `period_start` = the later of the job's mitigation start and the `period_end` of the last **issued** mitigation invoice on the job [A-4.3]. This is the **second-draft guard**: a re-wet and re-dry inside an unbilled period hits the same key — `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` while a row for that key is queued or leased, and the worker refreshes the existing draft instead of creating a second one; a re-dry after an invoice was issued is a new billing period, a new key, a new draft that cites the prior number.
*Recorded.* `events(kind='queue.enqueued')` is not emitted (the queue row is its own record); the `job.marked_dry` event carries `caused_job_ids[]`.
*Failure.* None possible outside the transaction; if the worker is down the row waits with `run_after=now()`.

**Step 3 — The worker reconciles the facts and drafts the invoice.**
*Trigger.* The worker's poller claims the row: `UPDATE jobs_queue … WHERE id = (SELECT … FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED) SET locked_by, lease_until = now() + 30 min RETURNING`; the worker's 30 s heartbeat (`worker_heartbeats`, §2.2) extends the lease; a sweeper requeues rows whose lease expired.
*Operation.* `billing.draft_invoice@1` (worker) — two halves. **Deterministic** (`packages/domain/detect/billing.reconcile.ts`, the P2 module, zero-LLM; it imports `dryingcalc` and `completeness` from `packages/domain/engines/` once P3.0 moves them there verbatim): unit-days by equipment class from `equipment_placements` (P3; before P3 from the per-day `dehu/am/scrub` strings over dated psychrometric rows, `model.js:353-361`, and the regex classifier `dryingcalc.js:152-163`), labor hours from `time_entries` grouped by service — every `source` (`qbtime|manual|board|paper`), so the paper-timesheet member's office-entered hours bill like anyone's — monitoring visits from reading dates per map, the Cat 3 package assertion from `claims.water_category='3'` (the rule the prompt states today, `roybal-ai-office/index.ts:537`), the billable gate from `completeness.js:205-231` running in the worker, prices from `price_sources` — own rates for T&M, a code plus `price_source_id` on every line; `price_list` is one frozen source and is never expanded — and, from v0 on (P2), the photo tagger's `ai.equipment[]` tags (`roybal-ai-office/index.ts:241,248`) as a **hint**: a room whose photos show an air mover, dehumidifier or scrubber that no placement, count string or line covers is flagged `documented_not_billed` with the photo ids. A hint is evidence for a finding, never a quantity. **Judgment** (LLM): a loss summary and a justification string for every pushback-prone line — a line whose `(carrier, code|category)` matches `learned_rules` (P6) or the default list — through `judgment.line_justification` routed to `claude-opus-5` with adaptive thinking, `tool_choice: auto` and a `strict` schema (never a forced tool: `roybal-ai-office/index.ts:148` breaks on Fable 5.1); `retrieve_rules(carrier_id, codes[])` is appended after the cached prefix. Quantities never come from the model.
*Reads.* `jobs`, `claims(carrier_id, water_category, deductible via policies)`, `carrier_profiles(line_justification, justification_style)`, `equipment_placements`, `drying_days`, `moisture_readings`, `time_entries`, `photos(room_id, ai)`, `price_sources`, `learned_rules`, `prompts(key='invoice.mitigation', active)`, `model_routes(task_class)`.
*Writes.* `ai_reserve()` then `ai_settle()` on `ai_usage` (the `web_turn_begin/end` pattern, `227_web_receptionist.sql:147-211`, generalized; rows carry `prompt_id`, `prompt_version`, `task_class`, `route_key`, `agent_run_id`, `job_id`); `agent_runs(job_id, claim_id, trigger='queue', model, cost_usd, proposals_created)`; `documents(kind='invoice', status='draft', job_id, claim_id, division_id, org_id)`; `line_items(document_id, room_id, code, category, description, qty, unit, unit_price, price_source_id, justification, evidence_refs jsonb, pushback_prone bool)`; then Step 4's proposal.
*Permission.* `agent:billing` holds `read` on the job's tables, `execute` on `document.draft` and `line_item.add` **for draft documents only** — a draft binds nothing — and `propose` on `invoice.issue` (`agent_authority` rows, granted by the owner, expiring, revocable) [A-4.4]. It holds no `approve`, no `execute` on `invoice.issue`, ever.
*Key.* The queue row's key; the agent run is keyed by `jobs_queue.id`; line inserts use `line_item.add:{document_id}:{client_line_id}` so a retried run after a crash does not duplicate lines.
*Recorded.* `events(kind='document.drafted', principal=agent:billing, aggregate=documents/{id}, correlation_id=<queue job id>)`; `agent_runs` closed with cost; `ai_usage` settled.
*Failure.* A killed worker: the lease expires, the sweeper requeues, the run resumes idempotently (the draft row exists by id, lines by client id). Cap exhausted: `ai_reserve` returns null, the job fails with `retry_after` the next month boundary and the owner is texted once (an outbox row with the 24 h resend guard — the worker is alive in this case). The reconciliation's "undocumented days" (drying-log rows on only some days — the live sample) are flagged as gaps, never billed. Before P4 this whole step files `invoice.review_gaps` (P2) instead of a draft document, and execution appends flagged lines to the blob's office-owned `invoices[]` through the worker's one-owner-per-key write-back.
*Ceiling.* One worker, one region: an availability ceiling, not throughput; a draft is seconds of CPU and one model call. `fly scale count 2` is honest for this poller.

**Step 4 — The proposal is filed.**
*Operation.* `op_propose(operation='invoice.issue@1', input{document_id, model_hash, total_usd, invoice_date, terms}, evidence_refs[], rationale, division_id, job_id, claim_id, assigned_role='office', escalate_after='48h')`, called by the worker with `p_principal_id = agent:billing`.
*Runtime.* SQL. Validation door 1 of the three in §2.3: `pg_jsonschema` against the catalog's input schema [A-0.2]; the `approval_policies(action_type='money', division_id, max_amount_usd)` row sets `required_role` — office up to the owner's threshold, owner above [A-0.3].
*Writes.* `proposals(status='proposed', input immutable, expires_at = now() + 7 days)`; `events(kind='proposal.created')`.
*Key.* `invoice.issue:{document_id}:{model_hash}` [A-4.5] — what the approver approves is the frozen content hash of the lines and totals, not "the invoice" in the abstract. Any later edit produces a new hash, a new proposal, and supersedes this one (Step 5).
*What people see.* Office: a card in Today → Proposals: job, customer, carrier, "INV draft · $7,412.60 · 14 lines · 3 flagged pushback-prone", the evidence summary from the reconciliation ("20 dehu-days from 10 dated rows · 41.5 h from 23 time entries · 2 undocumented days · Cat 3 package complete"), and four buttons — Preview, Edit lines, Approve, Decline. Inbox scoping (§2.2): mine ∪ my role in my division ∪ unassigned in my division; after `escalate_after` it reassigns to the owner and texts once.
*Ceiling.* Hundreds of proposals a day, several office staff across divisions; cross-department SLAs force a workflow layer on top of these tables, never instead of them.

**Step 5 — The office reviews and edits.**
*Trigger.* An office user opens Preview (the same HTML the pipeline renders, produced in the browser by the shared renderer in `packages/documents` — the letterhead and totals ladder from `formkit.js:290-315` and `fincalc.js:27-38` ported) and edits lines.
*Operation.* `line_item.update@1` / `line_item.add` / `line_item.remove` on the draft document (action type `money`).
*Runtime.* SQL, one transaction per edit.
*Permission.* `(office|owner, money, execute)` **on drafts** — `role_permissions` rows (§2.4); `op_line_item_update` refuses when `documents.status <> 'draft'`.
*Key.* `line_item.update:{line_id}:{client_edit_id}` (a uuid the browser mints per edit, so a retried PATCH after a dropped connection is one edit).
*Recorded.* One `events` row per edit with `proposal_id`, `before`, `after`, and the editor as principal — the audit answer to "who changed the air-mover quantity from 215 to 20 and when".
*Edits before approval.* The document's `model_hash` changes; the SQL body records the delta in `proposals.edited_params` (the office's changes against the agent's proposed input) and, because the key changed, files a superseding proposal `invoice.issue:{document_id}:{new_hash}` with `proposed_by = agent:billing` carried forward, `edited_by = <office user>`; the old row becomes `superseded` with `superseded_by`. `op_proposal_approve` refuses a proposal whose `model_hash` no longer matches the document — a stale approval cannot issue edited content.
*Failure.* None external. Two office people editing the same draft: each edit is its own transaction on its own line row; there is no whole-blob write to clobber.
*What people see.* The edit toolbar and the live totals ladder; the card's amount and hash-suffix update; the Approve button reads "Approve v-hash e41c…".

**Step 6 — Approval, from the inbox or by text.**
*Trigger.* Office taps Approve; or the owner texts "YES 12" and `roybal-webhooks` (trust level B, Twilio HMAC) handles it **synchronously**: it resolves the sender's number to a principal through `profiles.phone`, refuses any sender not on the allowlist of approvers holding `approve` for that proposal's action type — today the owner's cell only (`roybal-notify/index.ts:400-401`), and it stays so unless the owner grants an office phone [A-4.6] — and calls `op_proposal_approve` (or `op_proposal_decline` for "NO 12") as that principal. Those two SQL ops are the only ones the function may call; trust level B is otherwise "inserts rows only" (§2.2, §2.5). The approval therefore records without the worker; only the *execution* of an approved op waits for the worker, and only when that op's runtime is `worker` — `invoice.issue` is SQL, so all of Step 7 runs here too. The reply ("Approved: …") goes back through the same synchronous `twilioPost` the function makes today (`roybal-notify/index.ts:405-407`). Codes are allocated by a sequence inside `op_propose`, not the app-side "lowest free integer" (`roybal-brief/index.ts:264-266`).
*Operation.* `proposal.approve@1` → `op_proposal_approve(proposal_id, approver principal, channel 'inbox'|'sms', reason?)`.
*Runtime.* SQL, one transaction: `SELECT … FROM proposals WHERE id = $1 AND status = 'proposed' FOR UPDATE` (the guarded flip `roybal-notify/index.ts:438-443` already does, kept); `role_permissions(role, 'invoice.issue', 'approve')`; `approval_policies` threshold against `input.total_usd` [A-0.3]; **separation of duties:** for `money`, `external` and `comms` action types the function refuses `approved_by = proposed_by` — an agent never approves its own proposal, and a person who proposed cannot approve; a person who holds `execute` on an op runs it directly instead of proposing it, which is not an approval [A-4.7]; the owner may override with a recorded reason (an event). Then, because `invoice.issue` is `runtime:'sql'`, the same transaction runs Step 7 and marks the proposal `executed`.
*Key.* `proposal.approve:{proposal_id}` — a second Approve or a second YES returns the standing result; the P1 verify is exactly "approve twice → one send".
*Recorded.* `events(kind='proposal.approved', principal=<approver>, channel)`, `proposal.executed`; on refusal, `proposal.approve_refused` with the reason (`separation_of_duties`, `over_threshold`, `stale_hash`, `expired`).
*Failure.* Worker down: irrelevant — approval and issue are SQL from both channels, and the SMS path never touches `jobs_queue`. The 5-minute expiry sweep marks unanswered proposals `expired` (the transition `pending_actions` never wrote, `210:33,37`), so nothing is counted forever against a cap. Ambiguous SMS code → a reply asking which, as `approve.ts` does today.
*What people see.* Office: the card flips to "Issued INV-2026-0042 v1 · PDF rendering · QuickBooks queued". Owner by SMS: "Approved: INV-2026-0042 $7,412.60 to State Farm — Smith / 1911 Central".

**Step 7 — Issue (inside the approval transaction).**
*Operation.* `document.issue@1` (SQL, `runtime:'sql'`).
*Writes, in order.* `document_sequences(org_id, kind='invoice', year)` `FOR UPDATE`, `next` incremented — numbers are never reused, unlike `INV-${length+1}` (`finactions.js:137`); `document_versions(document_id, version_no=1, model_json = frozen lines + totals + parties, model_hash, template_version, carrier_profile_id, issued_at, issued_by = <approver>, render_status='queued', page_count = null)`; `documents.status='issued', number, current_version_no=1, paid_state='unpaid', issued_at`; `line_items.frozen_in_version_id` set (the `billed_lines` view unions live and frozen rows so "what was billed" is one query); an immutability trigger from here on refuses UPDATE on an issued version, exempting only the render columns — `render_status`, `rendered_at`, `page_count`, `html_hash`, `pdf_sha256`, `storage_path` — and only while `render_status <> 'rendered'` (the `document_versions` shape of §3); `jobs_queue(kind='document.render', key='document.render:{version_id}', lease_kind='render')`; `outbox(channel='qbo', operation='qbo.invoice_upsert', key='qbo.invoice:{document_id}:v1', proposal_id)`; `jobs_queue(kind='collections.schedule', key='collections.schedule:{document_id}', run_after=now())` (P6; in P1–P5 the brief's daily job finds issued-unpaid documents itself).
*Recorded.* `events(kind='invoice.issued', aggregate=documents/{id}, data{number, version_no, total_usd}, proposal_id)`.
*Failure.* Any failure rolls the whole thing back including the approval — the proposal stays `proposed` and the card shows the error; there is no half-issued invoice. A post-issue correction is never an edit: `document.supersede` (same number, v2, `supersedes_version_id`) or `document.void(reason)`, and QuickBooks follows — update-in-place with `SyncToken` (`qbo-proxy/index.ts:345-352`) or QBO void, never delete.
*What people see.* Field app: the invoice section is read-only and shows the issued document (the intent at `model.js:26`); Print/Share yields the PDF once Step 8 lands, or prints the same HTML offline. Office: the document card with number and version.
*Ceiling.* The sequence row `(org_id, kind, year)` is one hot row per kind per year; hundreds of issues a day are nothing, thousands a minute would contend — the option then is a per-division sequence, which the `division_id` column already permits.

**Step 8 — Render.**
*Trigger.* Worker claims `document.render` (10-minute lease); `document_versions.render_status` goes `queued → rendering`.
*Operation.* `buildDocumentModel` is skipped — the model is the frozen `model_json`; `renderHtml(model, template@version, carrierProfile)` from `packages/documents` runs in the worker; the PDF is produced by the Chromium build pinned in the worker image (§6.7) in an **isolated child process with its own memory cap**, one render at a time, restarted on OOM; the machine is ≥1 GB [A-0.1], never the 256 MB phone machine (`services/phone-agent/fly.toml:21-23`).
*Writes.* Storage `documents/{org}/{job}/{number}-v{n}.pdf`; `document_versions(render_status='rendered', rendered_at, page_count, html_hash, pdf_sha256, storage_path)`; `events(kind='document.rendered')`.
*Permission.* `agent:worker` (seeded at P4) holds `execute` on `document.render` (an internal op, no approval).
*Key.* `document.render:{version_id}`; a re-render of the same version must produce the same `html_hash` (the golden-output harness asserts it) and the same `pdf_sha256` under the pinned Chromium build, and overwrites the same storage path — which is what makes the `documents` bucket rebuildable from `model_json` + `template_version` rather than a second thing to back up (§6.7).
*Failure.* OOM → the child dies, `render_status='failed'`, the job fails with backoff, attempts count; after 5 the row is `dead` and the owner is texted once. The HTML preview and the browser print path keep working the whole time — a dead render never blocks issuing, sending a link, or QuickBooks.
*What people see.* Office: "PDF ready" and a Download / Attach control; the same file is what the submittal (Step 10) attaches. Customer portal: the invoice appears in Documents with a payment link once Step 9 records the QBO id (the `invoiceLink` precedent, `qbo-proxy/index.ts:363`).
*Ceiling.* Hundreds of documents a day per render process; photo packets are the heavy case (images per render are capped); over ~1,000 renders a day or a sub-2 s SLA means a second render machine.

**Step 9 — QuickBooks.**
*Trigger.* The outbox deliverer picks up `qbo.invoice:{document_id}:v1` (`pending → sending`).
*Operation.* `qbo.invoice_upsert@1` (worker, `packages/integrations/qbo`).
*Sequence.* (1) `external_intents(idempotency_key, provider='qbo', entity='invoice', entity_ref=document_id, request_digest, status='attempting')` is written **before** the call. (2) `external_refs` lookup: an existing QBO id → update-in-place with the current `SyncToken` (the `qbo-proxy/index.ts:345-352` rule, kept); none → query QBO by `DocNumber = documents.number` (now unique and stable, no longer `invoiceNo.slice(0,21)` from a free-text field, `:336`) and **adopt** a match before creating — because the real failure mode is a response lost after QBO created the invoice. (3) Lines map to QBO items by `line_items.category` through a `qbo_item_map(category, qbo_item_id)` table [A-4.8] instead of one generic "Restoration Services" item (`:52-54`, `:316-321`); the O&P line rides as its own line as the browser does today (`apps/field/js/qbo.js:50-54`); the customer resolves through `contacts.qbo_customer_id`, with the proxy's find-or-create fallback kept inside the invoice adapter (`ensureCustomer`, `qbo-proxy/index.ts:129-190`: stored id first, `DisplayName` second). (4) Success: `external_refs(system='qbo', entity=document, external_id, sync_token, last_synced_at)`, `external_intents.status='done'`, `outbox.status='sent'`, `provider_id`; and — as after **every** adapter call, push or pull — one `integration_runs(connection_id, kind='push', started_at, finished_at, ok, error, rows_affected, external_ref)` row (§2.6).
*Scope beyond this row.* Assigned to **P6**: customer creation as its own lane (`qbo.customer_upsert`, proposed when a contact has no QBO customer, independent of an invoice push), `divisions.qbo_class` stamped on every pushed line (class per division), and bills/expenses from confirmed `receipts` and `materials` as a `qbo.purchase_create` outbox channel — the same adapter, the same intents-and-refs pattern.
*Permission.* `agent:outbox` executes with the issuing `proposal_id` on the row — the approval that authorizes the side effect is the invoice approval; no second human tap.
*Recorded.* `events(kind='qbo.invoice.synced', data{external_id, doc_number, updated: bool})`; on failure `outbox.failed` with `last_error` and `run_after` backoff.
*Failure.* Lost response → retry finds the intent row `attempting` with no external id → DocNumber lookup adopts the invoice QBO already created → one invoice, never two. Token invalid → the adapter writes `integration_runs(ok=false, error)`; the `integration_health` view over `integration_runs ⋈ connections` (`last_ok_at`, `last_error`, `consecutive_failures`, `token_expires_at`) flags the lane after 3 consecutive failures or when no run succeeded within 2× its expected cadence (§2.6); the row waits, the owner gets one text (24 h guard) and the admin Connections card says "needs reconnect" instead of "Connected" — the honest QB Time card behaviour, not the Gmail card's. Intuit throttles a realm [A-4.9] → backoff, no data loss. Worker down → the row waits; the office sees "QuickBooks: queued".
*What people see.* Office: "In QuickBooks as #1163 · open in QBO" — a link built from the realm id and external id on the document card; the field app's `officeDocs` summary (read-only, written back under the one-owner-per-key rule) shows the same number.
*Ceiling.* One outbox row per side effect and one deliverer; Intuit's per-realm throttle is the external ceiling; a second QBO company is a `connections` row keyed by division, not code.

**Step 10 — Submittal to the carrier.**
*Trigger.* The office (or the packet-complete rule at P6) asks for the cover email; `draft.correspondence` (`claude-sonnet-5`) drafts it from claim context — carrier, adjuster (current row of `claim_adjusters`), claim number, the documents attached — the lane that runs today as `adjusterEmail` (`roybal-ai-office/index.ts:760-782`).
*Operation.* `email.send@1` (action type `comms`) proposed by `agent:correspondence` (P6) with `attachments = [document_version_id …]`, approved by office; in P1–P5 the same proposal is the chip the assistant renders for the office user — proposer = that user, self-approved by the tap because `email.send` is not a money op and the user holds `execute` (§2.3) — or executed directly by an office user who holds `execute` on `email.send` (a hand-written email is not a proposal, [A-4.7]). Delivery is an `outbox(channel='email')` row the worker delivers through the Gmail adapter as `multipart/mixed` with the PDF attached — today `buildRfc822` takes text only (`gmail-proxy/index.ts:345-349`).
*Writes.* `submittals` in the one shape of §3: `job_id`, `claim_id`, `kind='invoice'`, `document_version_ids uuid[]` (ordered — the invoice, then the certificate), `packet_document_version_id` (the composed PDF when the packet was rendered as one document, else null), `carrier_id`, `adjuster_contact_id`, `to_contact_id`, `channel='email'`, `sent_at`, `sent_by` (the approver's principal), `outbox_id`, `external_ref`, `parent_submittal_id` (a resubmission or supplement points at the first), `status` — written by the outbox deliverer when the email carries a document reference (the P1 smoke test's third assertion); `threads/messages(direction='out')` — the thread is reachable from the submittal through `outbox_id → messages.outbox_id`; `email_messages` stays the Gmail mirror; the Gmail adapter writes its `integration_runs` row.
*Key.* `email.send:{proposal_id}`; the submittal is unique on `(outbox_id)`.
*Recorded.* `events(kind='submittal.sent', claim_id, data{document numbers, versions, to})`. From now on "was this invoice already sent, which version, to whom" is one row — the datum capability 5 learns from.
*Failure.* Gmail token dead → the same `integration_runs` / `integration_health` alert as Step 9; the proposal stays approved, the row retries; nothing is re-sent because the row is keyed. ESX/XactAnalysis-native submission is an explicit boundary: the pipeline emits the PDF plus a structured JSON packet, `carrier_profiles.format` records what the carrier requires, the submittal's `channel` (`carrier_portal`, `api`, `mail`) and `external_ref` record how and where it went, and a person does any Xactimate-native step in Xactimate.
*What people see.* Office: "Submitted 09-08 09:41 to J. Smith (State Farm) · INV-2026-0042 v1 + Cert of Drying v2" on the claim and on the document card; a reply from the adjuster threads under it.

**Step 11 — Payments arrive.**
*Trigger.* Nightly `jobs_queue(kind='qbo.pull_payments')` inserted by pg_cron (SQL only; no HTTP from cron — the dead-worker alert of §2.5 is the single documented exception). The QBO adapter writes an `integration_runs(kind='pull')` row whether the pull succeeds or fails, so a dead token shows in `integration_health` exactly as a failed push does — a pull is never a green cron again.
*Operation.* `payment.record@1` executed by `agent:integrations` (seeded at P4; `execute` granted — it records a fact QuickBooks already holds).
*Rules.* `payments.ts:37-65` unchanged — a balance drop is a payment, adopt-don't-double-count on first sight, never backward. Writes `payments(document_id, amount, received_at, method='QuickBooks', external_ref)`, `documents.paid_state` (`unpaid → partial → paid`), and no longer a service-role PATCH into the blob (`qbo-proxy/index.ts:429-444`).
*Key.* `payment.record:qbo:{external_invoice_id}:{as_of_date}:{balance}`.
*Recorded.* `events(kind='payment.recorded')` and, when the balance reaches zero, `invoice.paid` — which Step 12 consumes.
*What people see.* Office: the document card's paid state; the morning brief's payment line reads `events`, not `capture_events` — the legacy ledger, read-only after P3, migrated into `captures` + `events` in P5 (§3).

**Step 12 — Collections runs itself.**
*Trigger.* Step 7 enqueued `collections.schedule`; it inserts reminder jobs at `run_after = due_date + 7, +21, +45 days` [A-4.10], keys `collections:{document_id}:{stage}`. Each job checks `documents.paid_state` and the balance from `payments`; balance > 0 → a proposal.
*Operation.* `email.send` or `sms.send` (`comms`) proposed by `agent:collections` (P6; in P1–P5 the brief's daily job proposes the reminder itself as `agent:brief`) with the reminder template and the QBO payment link; office approves from the inbox (the brief's "text YES 12" lane, migrated in P1); stage 1 may auto-execute only if the owner adds `agent_authority(agent:collections, email.send, auto, conditions{kinds:['reminder1'], max_amount_usd})` — recorded as `approved_by_kind='policy'`. Stage 3 proposes a call task instead of a message.
*Quiet hours.* Live in one place: the outbox deliverer holds an `sms` row outside 07:00–20:00 Alaska and sends at the window's open — today `assertSendWindow` refuses and the caller retries by hand (`roybal-notify/index.ts:224-231`). Crew-line kinds (`crewSchedule`, `onOurWay`, `crewLine`) obey the same window **by default** — the exemption `assertSendWindow` grants them today (`:225`) is not carried over as a given; it becomes an owner option in 04 and, when taken, a per-kind setting in `role_permissions` / `notification_policies` that the deliverer reads. `contact_prefs.digestOptOut` and `appOptOut` are checked on every comms op, so the paper-timesheet crew member is never addressed by an automated lane.
*Cancellation.* `invoice.paid` (Step 11) runs `collections.cancel`: queued rows for the document become `done` with `reason='paid'`; an open reminder proposal is `superseded`.
*Recorded.* `events(kind='collections.scheduled' | 'collections.reminder_proposed' | 'collections.cancelled')`; each sent reminder is an `outbox` row and a `messages` row on the customer thread.
*Ceiling.* Machine-bounded — automations × trigger rates, not headcount; one `GROUP BY kind` on `outbox` answers "how many reminders did we send and how many were delivered".

**Step 13 — Delivery reconciled and the loop closes.**
*Twilio status callback.* `roybal-webhooks` (HMAC) inserts `jobs_queue(kind='outbox.settle', input{provider_id, status})`, key `outbox.settle:{provider_id}:{status}`; the worker sets `outbox.status='delivered'|'failed'` and `messages.delivered_at`. The monthly SMS cap counts `delivered`. An undelivered reminder (`failed`, `undelivered`) files a proposal to retry by email or to call — it never silently stands as "sent". This is the route that is absent today (`roybal-notify/index.ts:646`; `config.toml:53-54`).
*Carrier response (P6).* An inbound adjuster email — fetched by the Gmail pull, whose envelope is a `captures(kind='email_pull')` row from P3 on (§3) — matched to the thread → `classify.email` (`claude-sonnet-5` at low effort, batch-eligible — the default for classification classes, §2.7) → `carrier_response.record` proposed by `agent:correspondence`, confirmed by office → `carrier_responses(submittal_id, kind, approved_amount)` and typed `rejection_reasons(line_item_id|code, reason_code)` → the weekly deterministic `carrier.learn` proposes `learned_rules` the owner approves → `retrieve_rules` changes the justification the next draft writes for that carrier. A rejection recorded Monday changes Tuesday's draft; that is the whole point of the flow.

### 4.2 The failure modes, named

| Failure | Where it is caught | What the rows say | Who is told |
|---|---|---|---|
| Re-wet after dry, then dry again | Step 2 key `billing.draft_invoice:{job}:{period_start}`; Step 1 `job.rewet` supersedes the open proposal | one draft per unbilled period; the superseded proposal names its successor | office sees "re-wet since draft" on the card, then the refreshed card |
| Worker down | Steps 1, 5, 6 (inbox **and** SMS), 7 are SQL; 3, 8, 9, 10, 12 are queue/outbox rows | approvals and issues recorded; `jobs_queue`/`outbox` rows wait; leases expire and requeue; `worker_heartbeats` goes stale | the owner, by the dead-worker alert: pg_cron checks `worker_heartbeats` every 5 minutes and, when the newest row is older than 10 minutes, calls `net.http_post` to `roybal-webhooks` `/alert` (service-secret header), which sends the SMS synchronously through the Twilio adapter — the one documented exception to "pg_cron never calls HTTP"; the outbox is not used for it (§2.5) |
| Integration lane dead (token, throttle, silence) | every adapter call — a push from `outbox` or a pull from `jobs_queue` — writes `integration_runs`; the `integration_health` view flags 3 consecutive failures or no success within 2× the expected cadence (§2.6) | `integration_runs(ok=false, error)`; the outbox or queue row waits with backoff; nothing is lost | owner texted once per 24 h through the outbox (the worker is up in this case); the Connections card says "needs reconnect" |
| QBO created the invoice, response lost | Step 9 `external_intents` row before the call + `DocNumber` lookup on retry | one QBO invoice adopted into `external_refs` | nobody needs to be |
| SMS undelivered | Step 13 status callback settles `outbox` | `delivered`/`failed` instead of `queued` forever; cap counts `delivered` | a retry-by-email proposal in the inbox |
| Office edits before approval | Step 5 `edited_params` + superseding proposal keyed on `model_hash`; `op_proposal_approve` refuses a stale hash | every edit an event with `proposal_id`; the approved hash equals the issued version's hash | the approver sees the hash suffix they approved |
| Proposer = approver on money/comms/external | `op_proposal_approve` separation-of-duties check | `proposal.approve_refused(reason='separation_of_duties')`; owner override is an event with a reason | the refused user, in the inbox |
| Spend cap reached mid-draft | `ai_reserve` returns null before the call | queue row `failed` with `retry_after`; no half-charged run | owner, once per 24 h |
| Render OOM | child process cap | `render_status='failed'`, job retried, `dead` after 5; HTML preview and browser print unaffected | owner, once |
| Stale approval by SMS for an expired proposal | 5-minute expiry sweep; `op_proposal_approve` requires `status='proposed'` | `expired`, never counted against a live cap | the texter gets "that one expired" |

### 4.3 The same tables, a construction draw instead of a mitigation invoice

Today the draw schedule is a blob key on 8 construction jobs **(live)** — `drawSchedule.rows[{desc, pct, amount, invoicedDate, paidDate, invoiceId}]` (`model.js:204`, `:667-669`), `contractAmount` and a free-text `lender` (`model.js:164,168`) — and "→ Invoice" pre-fills a `DRAW-n` invoice into `invoices[]` (`forms.js:3256-3262`). In the target the same thirteen steps run with three substitutions and no new contract tables:

| Step | Mitigation invoice | Construction draw |
|---|---|---|
| 1 trigger | `job.mark_dry` from the signed certificate | `draw.request` proposed by `progress.assess` (worker, reads `daily_logs`, `photos`, `inspections` against `sov_lines.pct_complete`) or filed by office at the period end; `sov_schedules(job_id, contract_amount, lender_contact_id, retainage_pct)` replaces `contractAmount`+`lender` |
| 3 draft | lines from placements, readings, hours | lines = `sov_lines × pct this period − previously billed`, a retainage line, change-order deltas from issued `change_order` documents; no LLM for quantities, `draft.correspondence` only for the cover note |
