| 4–7 | `invoice.issue`, kind `invoice` | `draw.issue`, kind `draw_request`; `draw_requests(number, period_to, requested, approved, paid_at, document_id)` is the ledger; threshold from the same `approval_policies` (`action_type='money'`) — a lender-funded draw above the office threshold goes to the owner |
| 8 render | invoice template | draw template per `carrier_profiles`-style lender profile (application-and-certificate layout, [A-4.11]); PDF plus the structured JSON packet |
| 9 QBO | invoice keyed on the document version | identical: `qbo.invoice:{document_id}:v{n}`, lines by category, retainage as its own line |
| 10 submittal | to the adjuster on the claim | to `sov_schedules.lender_contact_id`; `submittals.kind='invoice'` (the kind enum has no draw value; the document kind `draw_request` rides on the version), `to_contact_id` = the lender |
| 11–12 | carrier or customer pays; reminders at +7/+21/+45 | the lender funds on its own cycle; `collections.schedule` reads `draw_requests.expected_funding_days` [A-4.11] and proposes a status inquiry, not a dunning text |

Same `documents`, `document_versions`, `document_sequences` (kind `draw_request`, its own numbering), `line_items`, `proposals`, `outbox`, `external_refs`, `payments`, `submittals`, `events`. Nothing about the backbone knows the difference; only the model builder and the template do.

### 4.4 When each step exists

| Step | P1 | P2 | P3 | P4 | P5 | P6 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 mark dry | `job.set_stage` op exists; the trigger is a human tap | — | projection detects the signed certificate; equipment/readings are rows; `assigned_job_ids()` scopes crew reads | signature attaches to the issued `cert_drying` version as a `signatures` row | — | — |
| 2–3 draft | — | `billing.reconcile` v0 files `invoice.review_gaps` — counts, hours, reading dates, sent state, and photo `ai.equipment[]` hints; execution appends to blob `invoices[]` via key-owner write-back | placements replace count strings | draft `documents`+`line_items`; LLM justification on `claude-opus-5` | `event_subscriptions` replaces the op-declared fan-out | `learned_rules` injected |
| 4–6 proposal, review, approve | inbox, `op_propose`/`op_proposal_approve`/`op_proposal_decline`, SMS approval synchronous in `roybal-webhooks`, SoD, expiry — proven on the reminder and adjuster-email lanes | — | — | `invoice.issue` proposals | — | — |
| 7–8 issue, render | — | — | — | numbering, versions, `render_status`, PDF, field app read-only for money docs | — | — |
| 9 QBO | — | — | — | outbox keyed on version, intents, DocNumber lookup, `integration_runs` per call | — | customer lane, class per division, bills/expenses from receipts and materials |
| 10 submittal | `submittals` row on any approved email with a document reference | — | — | attachments from the pipeline | — | `agent:correspondence` proposes; responses, rejection reasons |
| 11 payments | — | — | — | `payments` rows, `paid_state`; the pull writes `integration_runs` | `capture_events` migrated into `captures` + `events` | — |
| 12 collections | the brief proposes `email.send` reminders onto `proposals`+`outbox` | — | — | — | — | `collections.schedule`, quiet hours in the deliverer (crew kinds inside the window unless the owner's option is set), stage-1 auto option |
| 13 delivery | Twilio status callback (E0), `outbox` settled; dead-worker alert via `worker_heartbeats` → `/alert` | — | — | — | — | `classify.email` (Sonnet 5, batch), `carrier.learn` |

P1 contains none of the toolchain: bundling, generated `BUILD`, generated service worker and the verbatim engine moves into `packages/domain/engines/` are P3.0 "toolchain", the first sub-step of P3 (§7.3, §7.5); the P3 column above assumes it has landed.

### 4.5 Tables touched per step

| # | Step | Reads | Writes | Events | Queue / outbox | Runtime · principal |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Mark dry | `field_projects` (via projection), `jobs`, `documents(cert_drying)`, `job_assignments` through `assigned_job_ids()` | `jobs`, `signatures` (projection, P4), `proposals` (path b) | `job.marked_dry` | `jobs_queue: job.project` (debounced, ≤1/min per job) | SQL · crew_lead / crew / office; or policy-approved `agent:projection` |
| 2 | Enqueue draft | — | `jobs_queue` | — | `billing.draft_invoice:{job}:{period_start}` | SQL (same txn) · system |
| 3 | Draft | `jobs`, `claims`, `policies`, `carrier_profiles`, `equipment_placements`, `drying_days`, `moisture_readings`, `time_entries` (all four `source` values), `photos` (`room_id`, `ai.equipment[]`), `price_sources`, `learned_rules`, `prompts`, `model_routes`, `model_prices` | `ai_usage` (reserve/settle), `agent_runs`, `documents(draft)`, `line_items` | `document.drafted` | lease + 30 s heartbeat on the row | worker · `agent:billing` |
| 4 | Propose | `operation_catalog`, `approval_policies` | `proposals` | `proposal.created` | — | SQL · `agent:billing` (propose) |
| 5 | Review / edit | `documents`, `line_items` | `line_items`, `proposals(edited_params, superseded)`, new `proposals` row | `line_item.updated`×N, `proposal.superseded`, `proposal.created` | — | SQL · office (execute on drafts) |
| 6 | Approve | `proposals` FOR UPDATE, `role_permissions`, `approval_policies`, `profiles(phone)` for SMS | `proposals(approved → executed)` | `proposal.approved`, `proposal.executed` | — (the SMS path is `roybal-webhooks` calling `op_proposal_approve` synchronously; no queue row) | SQL · office ≤ threshold / owner |
| 7 | Issue | `document_sequences` FOR UPDATE, `documents`, `line_items` | `document_sequences`, `document_versions(render_status='queued')`, `documents(issued)`, `line_items.frozen_in_version_id` | `invoice.issued` | `jobs_queue: document.render`, `collections.schedule`; `outbox: qbo.invoice:{doc}:v1` | SQL (same txn as 6) · executes under the approver's authority |
| 8 | Render | `document_versions.model_json`, templates, `carrier_profiles` | storage object, `document_versions(render_status, rendered_at, page_count, html_hash, pdf_sha256, storage_path)` | `document.rendered` | lease 10 min, render child process | worker · `agent:worker` |
| 9 | QuickBooks | `outbox`, `external_refs`, `contacts.qbo_customer_id`, `qbo_item_map`, `connections(qbo)` | `external_intents`, `external_refs`, `outbox(sent)`, `integration_runs` | `qbo.invoice.synced` / `outbox.failed` | outbox row with backoff | worker · `agent:outbox` carrying `proposal_id` |
| 10 | Submittal | `claims`, `claim_adjusters`, `contacts`, `document_versions` | `proposals`, `outbox(email)`, `submittals`, `threads`, `messages`, `email_messages`, `integration_runs` | `proposal.*`, `submittal.sent` | outbox email row with attachment | SQL propose/approve · `agent:correspondence` (P6; the office user's chip before) + office; worker deliver · `agent:outbox` |
| 11 | Payments | `external_refs`, QBO balances | `payments`, `documents.paid_state`, `integration_runs` | `payment.recorded`, `invoice.paid` | `jobs_queue: qbo.pull_payments` (cron insert) | worker · `agent:integrations` (execute) |
| 12 | Collections | `documents`, `payments`, `contacts.contact_prefs`, `agent_authority`, `notification_policies` (per-kind quiet-hours setting) | `jobs_queue`×3, `proposals`, `outbox(sms|email)`, `messages` | `collections.scheduled`, `collections.reminder_proposed`, `collections.cancelled` | `collections:{doc}:{stage}`; deliverer holds SMS to 07–20 AK — crew kinds too unless the owner's per-kind exemption is set | worker propose · `agent:collections` (P6; `agent:brief` before); SQL approve · office (or policy for stage 1) |
| 13 | Settle / respond | `outbox` by `provider_id`; inbound `messages`; `captures(kind='email_pull')` | `outbox(delivered|failed)`, `messages.delivered_at`; `carrier_responses`, `rejection_reasons`, `learned_rules` | `sms.delivered`, `carrier.responded`, `rule.learned` | `jobs_queue: outbox.settle`, `classify.email` (batch-eligible) | edge webhook (insert only) + worker · `agent:correspondence`; office confirms |

Every row above is reachable from one query — `select * from events where job_id = $1 order by id` — which is the audit trail the owner asked for; `ai_usage` and `agent_runs` hang off it by `job_id`, `outbox` by `proposal_id`, and `integration_runs` by `connection_id` through the outbox or queue row that made the call.

### 4.6 Ceilings in this flow, per structural decision

| Decision | Holds to | What forces change |
|---|---|---|
| Approve + issue in one SQL transaction | 100+ people; hundreds of issues a day | a rule that is a bad fit for plpgsql — estimating math stays in the worker; the `(org, kind, year)` sequence row contending at thousands a minute → per-division sequences |
| Draft, render, deliver on one worker | throughput for the whole company; availability is one machine, one region | sustained backlog → a second poller machine (MCP over streamable HTTP and Agent SDK sessions are per-connection and need sticky routing, §2.3); >1,000 renders/day → a render machine |
| Proposal per content hash, inbox scoped by division and role | several office staff across divisions | cross-department SLAs → a workflow layer on top of `proposals` |
| Outbox + intents for every side effect, `integration_runs` per adapter call | one row per effect; Intuit's and Twilio's own throttles | a second QBO company or mailbox is a `connections` row, not code |
| Blob-projected capture facts feeding the draft | hundreds of concurrent jobs, a handful of devices each; ≤1 projection per job per minute — ≈430k `jobs_queue` rows/day worst case at 300 active jobs, so the table stays partition-free, indexed on `(status, run_after)`, purged daily (§2.2) | >~5 devices editing one job or an office feature that must edit a capture section → that section moves to rows plus the field outbox |
| Events as one heap | low millions of rows on the current tier — reached within roughly 12 months at 100+ people (§2.2) | month partitioning is a **P5** roadmap item (§7.7), not "later"; the P3 compute upgrade can pull it earlier |

---

## 5. Key decisions (ADRs)

Fifteen decisions, each recorded the same way: the code that exists today (cited `path:line` at `main@1e04694`; **(live)** = production; **[A-5.n]** = a design assumption made in this section, **[A-0.n]** = an assumption the Decision record already carries), the decision, the options weighed, why, what the owner must now operate, and — because the brief asks for it on every structural choice — the honest ceiling and the condition that forces the next change. Where simplicity trades against ceiling the option is stated, not chosen. All fifteen are **accepted** and bound by the Decision record (Part 0, cited as "Decision record §n"); "§n" cites the assembled sections of this document and "inventory §n" cites `docs/architecture/00-SYSTEM-INVENTORY.md`. The record wins over any design attempt.

| ADR | Decision in one line | Holds to | Forces change |
|---|---|---|---|
| 01 | One operation registry; JSON Schema is the contract | 100+ people, hundreds of ops | a second language (don't); partner API → gateway |
| 02 | Typed reads through `packages/db`; writes are operations; the blob keeps `push_project` | 100+ with JWT claims | reporting → materialized views (P5), replica after; >5 devices per job |
| 03 | `proposals` state machine; every approval channel is one op, SMS included, worker-independent | hundreds/day; several office staff | SLAs / multi-step → workflow layer on top |
| 04 | Roles + `role_permissions` as data + JWT claims + `assigned_job_ids()` + `agent_authority` | 100+, ~10 roles | per-customer isolation → `org_id` claim |
| 05 | Append-only `events` via `emit_event()`; no events per blob push | low millions of rows unpartitioned (~12 months at 100+) | ~5M rows or compute upgrade → month partitions, a P5 item |
| 06 | `jobs_queue` with leases + `outbox`; cron enqueues only (one HTTP exception: the dead-worker alert); one worker | thousands of jobs/day sustained; the table to ≈430k/day worst case | >50 jobs/s → pgmq/broker; Micro's disk IO → Small |
| 07 | Model → HTML → PDF in a render child process; versioned, numbered, reproducible | hundreds of docs/day per process | >1,000/day or sub-2 s SLA → render machine |
| 08 | Postgres for short ops, edge for sync-with-secret, Fly worker for long; MCP over streamable HTTP | 100+ throughput; one-machine availability | backlog → second machine (poller only) |
| 09 | `model_routes` → `model_prices` FK; ids in one file; strict schemas | 100+ | second provider → one adapter |
| 10 | Wrap the blob: additive keys, one-way projection, key-owner write-back; `unified_jobs` renamed, never copied | hundreds of jobs, few devices each | >5 devices/job, office edits capture data, >1 MB blobs |
| 11 | `divisions` now; defaulted `org_id` on new spine/money tables and in the claim | one entity, many divisions | second entity → claim value + predicate swap |
| 12 | `price_sources` interface; `price_list` frozen, never expanded | 100+ | a licensed feed → one adapter |
| 13 | Baseline squash + history repair + second project as staging | two environments | second region → config only |
| 14 | esbuild per app, integer `BUILD` + `COMMIT`, `checkJs`, Biome, one `ci.yml` — P3.0, not P1 | 100+ | second product line splits packages |
| 15 | Adapters + `outbox` + `external_refs` + `integration_runs` table + `integration_health` view | 100+, per-division connections | partner API → gateway |

---

### ADR-01 — Service-layer boundary: one operation registry

**Status:** accepted (Decision record §3).

**Context.** No service layer exists. The assistant's 17 write actions are prose `desc` strings inside one untyped `proposeActions` tool (`supabase/functions/roybal-ai-office/personas.ts:246-362`) while its six read tools already carry real JSON Schema (`personas.ts:78-146`); execution is a `switch` per browser app (`apps/field/js/assist.js:229-236`), and the admin numbers invoices from array length (`apps/admin/js/finactions.js:89,137,250`). The only typed, server-authoritative write doors are SQL: `push_project` (`supabase/migrations/241_merge_delete_tombstones.sql:193-282`), `coordination_job_patch` (`246_lead_triage_grant.sql:28-53`) and the `web_*` functions (`227_web_receptionist.sql:147-211`).

**Decision.** One file per operation, `packages/domain/ops/<area>.<verb>.ts`, declared with `defineOp({name, version, actionType, description, input, output, runtime, idempotency, approval, emits, handler})`. JSON Schema with `additionalProperties:false` is the source of truth — not zod — because it feeds Anthropic `tools[]` with `strict:true` and MCP verbatim. One definition emits: `operation_catalog` rows (published by CI; the database never invents an op), the Anthropic tool list and the MCP list (both generated from the registry by `packages/ai`; reads as-is, writes only as `propose_<name>`), a typed browser client in `packages/db`, the dispatcher and worker route tables, `role_permissions` seed rows and `docs/PERMISSIONS.md`. `runtime:"sql"` bodies live beside the definition as `op_<area>_<verb>` SECURITY DEFINER functions shipped by migration; CI diffs the catalog against deployed signatures. Validation runs at three doors with the same schema: proposal insert (`op_propose`, `pg_jsonschema` [A-0.2]), execution (the handler re-validates), tool call (the SDK validates). `name@version` is stored on every proposal and event; additive changes do not bump, breaking ones do; an old handler stays until no open proposal references it. The registry directory exists from P1 with its first ~5 ops; the engines it imports arrive with the P3.0 toolchain step (ADR-14).

**Options considered.** (a) Keep per-function `{action}` switches and add a `_shared/` directory — the copy discipline that already produced 14 functions with no shared code. (b) A generic `commit_operation(op, changes[])` RPC as the single write door. (c) A zod-first registry behind one Deno dispatcher as the only door. (d) The chosen registry with per-op SQL functions for short ops.

**Why.** (b) is an unguarded write door callable by any authenticated user unless SQL knows op→tables, which needs the per-op functions anyway. (c) needs a schema conversion for every tool surface and cannot open a transaction over PostgREST. The 17 prose contracts transcribe one-to-one into `input` schemas and the model receives the same text as `description`.

**Consequences.** Every write has a name, a schema and an audit row; a new capability is one file plus a migration for its SQL body. The owner now operates a CI step that publishes the catalog and fails on signature drift, and a rule that an op is versioned, never edited. Deploy coupling is real: an op change redeploys the bundles, `roybal-ops` and the worker — ADR-14 makes that one pipeline from P3.0 on; the P1 ops ship as migrations plus a function deploy until then.

**Holds to.** 100+ people and hundreds of operations.

**Forces change.** A second implementation language would need a second registry — do not. A partner API adds a versioned gateway in front of `roybal-ops`, not a second registry.

---

### ADR-02 — Data-access pattern: typed reads, operations for writes, the blob keeps its door

**Status:** accepted (Decision record §1, §6).

**Context.** Nothing in the clients uses supabase-js; the field app hand-builds PostgREST URLs in eight distinct patterns (`apps/field/js/supa.js:74-86,101-129,133-194,211-265,278-294`). The rev guard on `coordination_jobs` is implemented five times (`apps/board/js/data.js:110-132`; `apps/field/js/boardpush.js:302-320`; `roybal-notify/index.ts:501-510`; `qbo-proxy/index.ts:429-431`; SQL `coordination_job_patch`), and `qbo-proxy` writes the blob with a service-role PATCH around `push_project` (`qbo-proxy/index.ts:429-431`). Non-blob field writes are online-only and fire-and-forget (`boardpush.js:204`; `spine.js:83`).

**Decision.** Reads: typed PostgREST reads under RLS through the one data client, `packages/db` — generated Supabase types committed from `supabase gen types` [A-5.2], auth and refresh, `ops.invoke/propose`, and the field app's IndexedDB/offline adapter — with aggregates through SQL views. Writes: operations only — `runtime:"sql"` ops are called as `rpc/op_<name>` and commit the state change and its event in one transaction; `runtime:"edge"` ops go through `roybal-ops`; `runtime:"worker"` ops are `jobs_queue` rows. `push_project` stays the blob's door byte-for-byte and is registered in the catalog as a capture op so it is described and permissioned like every other write. Field non-blob writes go through the IndexedDB outbox in `packages/db`'s offline adapter (P3), `{id = idempotency key, op, input, attempts}`, drained after each successful pull. Direct table write privileges are revoked table by table behind each op — the `219_revoke_direct_field_writes.sql:44-46` pattern — and only after every path that writes that table has moved.

**Options considered.** (a) supabase-js everywhere, including client writes. (b) Keep the hand-built strings. (c) One edge dispatcher as the only write door. (d) pg_graphql. (e) The chosen split.

**Why.** It is the only pattern under which humans, agents and jobs share permission, idempotency and audit. A Deno dispatcher cannot transact over PostgREST — the flaw the critiques found in the edge-only design — so short writes belong in SQL, and the office keeps writing when Fly or a function is down. Revoking before the outbox exists would break `boardpush.js` and `spine.js` mid-phase.

**Consequences.** Inline query strings leave the apps; the `p_build` gate and refresh-retry apply uniformly (today `rest("rpc/…")` bypasses the gate, `supa.js:133-194`); the board's client-side CAS and `boardpush.js` annotation writes disappear at P5. The owner operates the generated types in CI and a per-table revoke checklist.

**Holds to.** 100+ — PostgREST scales with policy complexity, not row count, so JWT claims and `STABLE` helpers (ADR-04) are the condition.

**Forces change.** Reporting at hundreds of jobs → the P5 reporting layer first: materialized views (`jobs_summary`, `ar_aging`, `crew_hours`, `automation_costs`) exposed as read ops and rendered by an admin Reports tab, so the brief, the agents and the tab read one number; a read replica or warehouse export next. More than ~5 devices editing one job → that section moves to rows plus the outbox (ADR-10).

---

### ADR-03 — Proposal/approval model

**Status:** accepted (Decision record §3–§4).

**Context.** Two disjoint spines. Chips: the server never executes; a browser tab runs the executor with a best-effort audit PATCH (`apps/field/js/assist.js:179-223`). `pending_actions` (`210_pending_actions.sql:24-38`): inert rows, no approver column, approved only by a Twilio-signed SMS from `OWNER_CELL`; `expired` is defined at `:33` and never written. Three `boardEdit` rows have sat `pending` since 2026-07-25 (expired 2026-07-26) **(live)** and `qb-time-proxy` counts them against `MAX_LIVE_PROPOSALS = 3` (`qb-time-proxy/index.ts:285,494-500`), so no board proposal has been made since 07-26. The one right instinct — the executor re-verifies the row before acting (`gmail-proxy/index.ts:317-326`) — is kept.

**Decision.** `proposals` supersedes `pending_actions`: `proposed → approved | declined | expired | superseded`, `approved → executing → executed | failed`; `input` is immutable after insert (trigger), `edited_params` is separate; every transition emits an event; rows carry `division_id`, `job_id`, `assigned_role`, `assignee_id`, `escalate_after`. Approval is one op, `proposal.approve` (`op_proposal_approve` in SQL; `proposal.decline` / `op_proposal_decline` is its twin), whatever the channel: inbox tap, SMS, or policy. **The SMS channel does not depend on the worker.** The owner's "YES 12" is handled synchronously by `roybal-webhooks` (trust level B), which is allowed to call exactly two SQL ops — `op_proposal_approve` and `op_proposal_decline` — as the principal resolved from the sender's number (`profiles.phone`, `001_initial_schema.sql:51`; allowlisted approvers only, each holding approve for that action type; `OWNER_CELL` at `roybal-notify/index.ts:400` is no longer special [A-5.1]); the op names are allowlisted in the function and a test asserts no other op is reachable from it. Approval therefore records without the worker; only *execution* of the approved op waits for the worker, and only when the op's runtime is `worker`. Execution runs the same handler server-side, SQL for short ops and the worker for long, never the browser; the field `sendText` chip stays in the synchronous tap window for iOS (`assist.js:229-236`) but records a proposal. A chip on a non-money op is a proposal the signed-in proposer may self-approve when they hold execute. Separation of duties: for `money`, `external` and `comms` action types `op_proposal_approve` refuses `approved_by = proposed_by`; agents never self-approve; the owner may override with a recorded reason. Amount thresholds are `approval_policies(action_type, division_id, max_amount_usd, approver_role)` rows [A-0.3]; auto-approval stamps `approved_by_kind='policy'` and the policy id, so "who approved" is never empty. A five-minute expiry sweep; inbox = mine ∪ my role in my division ∪ unassigned in my division; escalation reassigns to the owner and texts once. A schedule proposal is one row whose input holds N assignments applied atomically.

**Options considered.** (a) Keep chips plus `pending_actions`. (b) Proposals as events only. (c) A workflow engine (Temporal, Inngest) now. (d) SMS approval as a `jobs_queue` kind drained by the worker — rejected because it makes the owner's P1 channel worker-dependent while the inbox is not. (e) The chosen table with the two-op SMS door.

**Why.** (a) is two approval systems with different audit guarantees, one of them jammed; (b) cannot hold edits, scoping or expiry; (c) is another vendor holding state before the tables it would orchestrate exist; (d) contradicts "if the worker is down, approvals still record" for the one channel the owner uses first.

**Consequences.** The browser never executes a money or comms write; the office gets one inbox (P1) replacing "text YES 12"; the three zombie rows migrate as `expired`; trust level B's rule reads "inserts rows, or calls `op_proposal_approve` / `op_proposal_decline`" (Decision record §2, §2.2, §2.5, §4 step 6 say the same). The owner operates the threshold rows, the expiry sweep (a `jobs_queue` insert from cron) and escalation texts.

**Holds to.** Hundreds of proposals/day; several office staff across divisions. Auto-approved volume is machine-bounded: the ceiling is automations × trigger rates, not headcount.

**Forces change.** Multi-step approvals or cross-department SLAs → an `approvals` child table and a workflow layer on top of these tables, never instead.

---

### ADR-04 — Permission model: roles, matrix as data, assignment scoping, machine principals

**Status:** accepted (Decision record §2, §5).

**Context.** Eighteen operating tables are `for all to authenticated using(true)`, including the AI cost ledger (`201_ai_usage.sql:77-79`), `capture_events` and `time_entries` (inventory §6.3) — any crew login can delete the month's spend or payroll hours over REST. Machine users are fenced by 26 restrictive policies naming two literal emails (`204_phone_agent_rls.sql:12-18`; `205_brief_machine_rls.sql:16-23`); a third machine account inherits full rights. `_sync_guard` admits `role in ('tech','admin','office')` (`218_sync_rpcs_and_build_gate.sql:65-72`; redefined `226_sync_client_telemetry.sql:43-50`) and `push_project` calls it on every push; `office` exists as an enum value (`216_individual_logins.sql:50`) with no live row. The publishable key served on the public site reaches `pushInvoice`, whose role check only decides CRM linkage (`qbo-proxy/index.ts:309-313`).

**Decision.** Roles `owner, office, crew_lead, crew, viewer, agent`; external principals customer (portal token / `contact_sessions`) and adjuster (`photo_shares`) reach only the portal gateway. **Mapping is not blanket:** the migration maps `branden@…` to `owner` and the second live `admin` profile to `office` by default, and the owner confirms both in the admin UI before the role claim is armed — a confirmation item listed in 04; `tech → crew`; the two machine viewers become `agents` rows. `role_permissions` is data, edited through the owner's `role.grant` op (an event), with a CI snapshot asserting every catalog op has a row or an explicit deny. **Claims and crew scoping — stated here exactly as in §2.4.** The JWT claim carries `principal_id`, `role`, `division_id` and `org_id` via a Custom Access Token hook; assigned job ids are **not** in the claim. RLS predicates call a `STABLE` SECURITY DEFINER helper `assigned_job_ids()` — one initplan evaluation per statement, not per row, the same mechanism as `(select auth.uid())` — plus the walk-on rule: crew may read any job in their division with stage in the active set for 24 h after scanning its QR/asset label or being added by a crew lead. The walk-on is recorded, never ad hoc: `job.claim_walk_on(job_id)` is a self-executed op that inserts the time-boxed assignment and notifies the office, so reads widen through a recorded op, never a policy edit. `job_assignments` itself lands with crews in P3 [A-5.4]; until then the matrix holds only to "everyone in the division sees every job", which is today's posture. `current_principal()` reads the claims and falls back to `profiles` for old tokens; office and owner predicates stay claim-only; demotions revoke sessions [A-0.8]. Machines: `agents` plus `agent_authority(agent_id, operation, capability, conditions{max_amount_usd, kinds, quiet_hours, division_ids}, granted_by, expires_at, revoked_at)` — deny by default, propose-only, widened only by the owner's `agent_authority.grant` op; the P1 seed rows are `agent:billing`, `agent:projection`, `agent:outbox`, `agent:brief`, `agent:phone`, `agent:web`. An MCP session — streamable HTTP served by the worker, bearer = an `api_tokens` row minted in the admin, which Claude Code presents directly or through a local stdio shim (ADR-08) — acts as the minting human for reads and is capped at propose for every write. Ledgers become insert-only for everyone but the service role. Role-enum migration in three steps: N adds the enum values; N+1, in one transaction, redefines `_sync_guard` and every other gate (`246:45-53`, `contact_merge`, `contact_resolve`, `contact_mark_review_asked`, `restore_photo`, the `field_photos` policies at `221_field_photos_table.sql:113-137`) to accept both vocabularies, remaps `profiles.role` from an explicit `(profile_id, old_role, new_role)` table reviewed in the PR, and asserts in a `DO` block that `push_project` still resolves `_sync_guard(text)`; N+2 drops the old names a week later. No push fails at any instant.

**Options considered.** (a) Keep `using(true)` plus email fences. (b) Code-as-matrix — permission lists inside each `defineOp`, `PERMISSIONS.md` generated, drift-free, but every change to who may approve is a deploy; the owner's option, viable while the office is two people. (c) Per-table RLS helpers only. (d) Assigned job ids in the JWT — rejected: a crew lead on a busy week holds dozens, and a claim has a size and a refresh lag. (e) An external authz service (WorkOS, Oso). (f) The chosen data matrix with claims, the initplan helper, assignments and agent authority.

**Why.** A naive enum rename stops every crew device mid-migration; at 100+ with per-person overrides, approval changes cannot be deploys; RLS cost scales with policy complexity, so claims live in the token and the one lookup that cannot (assignments) runs once per statement.

**Consequences.** The 26 email fences are replaced by role/agent predicates in the migration that introduces `agents`; password-grant machine users remain for the phone agent until P7; a `tech` signup no longer holds full REST write; the pgTAP suite asserts that `explain` for a crew read of `photos` shows one InitPlan and no nested loop over `job_assignments`. The owner operates the Access page (grant/revoke ops), the JWT hook, the role-mapping confirmation and the snapshot test.

**Holds to.** 100+ people and ~10 roles, with crew scoping only once `job_assignments` exists; one assignment lookup per statement at any headcount.

**Forces change.** Per-customer or franchise isolation → a real `org_id` claim value per entity (ADR-11); multi-step approvals with SLAs → an approvals child table and a workflow layer; a principal assigned to thousands of jobs at once would strain the helper, and does not occur.

---

### ADR-05 — Event/audit log

**Status:** accepted (Decision record §4).

**Context.** There is no per-state-change record. `capture_events` is an ingest envelope, an audit log and the web agent's rate-limit state at once, rewritable by any authenticated user (inventory §6.1, §6.3); the chip audit stamp is a best-effort client PATCH (`assist.js:179-223`); `pending_actions.result` is overwritten in place. `blob_history` (`215_blob_history.sql`) keeps whole-blob pre-images and `updated_by` is the one server-stamped identity (`216_individual_logins.sql:110-137`).

**Decision.** `events` is append-only: a trigger raises on UPDATE or DELETE for any role; inserts only via `emit_event()` SECURITY DEFINER; columns `principal`, `kind`, `aggregate`, `job_id`, `claim_id`, `proposal_id`, `correlation_id`, `idempotency_key` (unique where not null), `name@version`. For SQL ops the event lands in the same transaction as the state change; worker ops emit through the same function. Blob pushes emit no semantic events: `push_project` is unchanged, `blob_history` is the pre-image, and the projection job (ADR-10) emits nothing — its rows carry `origin='blob', blob_rev`. Semantic events come only from operations. **Captures and the legacy ledger:** `captures` (kind `voice|photo_ai|ocr_meter|ocr_receipt|barcode|serial|signature|email_pull`) is the ingest envelope from P3 on; `capture_events` stays as the legacy ledger — read-only after P3 — and is migrated into `captures` + `events` in P5. Retention: `events` is one heap with a monthly index and is kept; `jobs_queue` done rows purge daily (ADR-06), `outbox` after 90 days, `capture_events` emailPull rows (3,500 of 4,504 **(live)**) after 30; `ai_usage` is kept. PITR is enabled at P4 when documents and events become the legal record.

**Options considered.** (a) Today's ledgers as-is. (b) Generic table-level audit triggers. (c) Full event sourcing with state rebuilt from events. (d) CDC / logical decoding to an external store. (e) An event per blob push. (f) The chosen op-emitted events beside state tables.

**Why.** Triggers cannot know the op, proposal or actor; event sourcing makes every read a replay; CDC is another runtime. The push arithmetic is honest about what the 45 s cycle is: `apps/field/js/sync.js:623` is the *pull* timer ("catch others' changes") and pushes fire on save through `schedulePush()` (`:617`), so 45 s is the worst-case per-job push cadence — at 100 devices that is still up to ~190k pushes/day, and an event per push would drown the audit table.

**Consequences.** Every op names its aggregate and a registry test requires at least one event per write op; `select * from events where job_id = $1 order by id` is the audit trail the owner asked for; the Sunday brief reads `proposals ⋈ events ⋈ agent_runs` instead of `capture_events`. The owner operates three retention purges (SQL cron), PITR from P4, and month partitioning as a **P5 item** — not "later": at 100+ people (~150 ops each per day ≈ 15–20k events/day) the table reaches the low millions within about twelve months, so partitioning arrives with the headcount.

**Holds to.** Low millions of rows unpartitioned on the current tier — roughly the first year at 100+ people.

**Forces change.** ~5M events or the compute upgrade, whichever first → month partitions, scheduled in P5 and deliberately absent from P1; regulatory retention or analytics at scale → archive partitions to storage.

---

### ADR-06 — Job runner: queue plus outbox, pg_cron enqueue-only, one worker

**Status:** accepted (Decision record §4, §11).

**Context.** Seven pg_cron jobs fire `net.http_post`; because the post is asynchronous, `cron.job_run_details` reports "succeeded" while the Gmail lane has returned `invalid_grant` since 09-01 and QB Time `refresh_token is invalid` since 09-04 **(live)**. The cron secret and the publishable key sit as literals in seven `cron.job.command` strings **(live)**, and migrations 233/236/244 scrape them back out. 174 of 175 outbound SMS rows are frozen at `queued` **(live)** because no status-callback route exists although `supabase/config.toml:53-54` says one does. `sendSms`, `sendEmail` and `pushInvoice` carry no idempotency key; `pushInvoice` creates a fresh QBO invoice whenever `qboInvoiceId` is absent (`qbo-proxy/index.ts:345-356`).

**Decision.** `jobs_queue(kind, payload, status queued→leased→done|failed|dead, run_after, attempts, max_attempts 5, locked_by, lease_until, idempotency_key unique)`, partition-free and indexed on `(status, run_after)`; claim = `UPDATE … WHERE id = (SELECT … FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED) SET locked_by, lease_until RETURNING` — the lease is a column, so it outlives the claiming transaction; per-kind leases (5 min default, 10 render, 30 agent run); heartbeat extends; a sweeper requeues expired leases; dead rows text the owner via the outbox with a 24 h resend guard (the worker is up when it marks a row dead, so the outbox is the right path there). `outbox(channel, payload, idempotency_key unique, provider_id, status pending→sending→sent→delivered|failed|dead)` — one row per external side effect; the Twilio status callback (`roybal-webhooks`) settles `delivered`; the SMS cap counts `delivered`. pg_cron never calls HTTP for work: it inserts `jobs_queue` rows, registered post-deploy by `cron_register.sql`; the last transitional HTTP job reads its header from `vault.decrypted_secrets`. The worker polls; `worker_heartbeats(worker_id, version, at, queue_depth, leased)` (P1) is upserted every 30 s, so health is a query, not a log. **The dead-worker alert is the one documented exception to "pg_cron never calls HTTP":** a pg_cron SQL check every 5 minutes reads `worker_heartbeats`; if the newest heartbeat is older than 10 minutes it calls `net.http_post` to `roybal-webhooks` `/alert` with a service-secret header (read from `vault.decrypted_secrets`, never a literal), and that function sends the owner's SMS synchronously through the Twilio adapter. The outbox is not used for this alert, because the worker is the outbox's only deliverer. A `field_projects` AFTER UPDATE trigger enqueues `job.project` with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` and a 60 s `run_after` — one projection per burst, at most one per job per minute.

**Options considered.** (a) pg_cron → `http_post` (today). (b) `pgmq` — managed visibility timeouts, one more extension to learn; the owner's alternative, swappable behind `claimJob()`. (c) An external broker (SQS, Inngest, Temporal). (d) No worker — an edge `tick` with `EdgeRuntime.waitUntil` draining short ops and a hosted PDF service: honest to ~30 people, but the phone agent already forces a Node process and the Agent SDK and MCP have no home. (e) The heartbeat alert as an outbox row — circular, since the worker delivers the outbox. (f) The chosen table plus worker, with the single HTTP exception.

**Why.** `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` releases at transaction end, so a dead tick would orphan work without a lease column; a row per outcome is what makes a dead lane visible; secrets leave `cron.job`; the one liveness signal that must not depend on the worker cannot ride the worker's own queue.

**Consequences.** Every scheduled task has a durable row, retries, a dead letter and an alert; a killed worker resumes its queue; a dead worker is texted within about ten minutes. Done rows are purged by a daily job — `job.project` rows at the next purge (they have no audit value; the projection is idempotent and repairable), every other kind after the 14-day window of Decision record §4 [A-5.8]. The owner operates one more Fly app, the `cron_register.sql` deploy step, dead-letter texts, the `/alert` route and the `integration_health` card.

**Holds to.** Several thousand queue jobs/day sustained on one worker; hundreds of proposals/day. The arithmetic behind that: ~300 active jobs pushing on the 45 s cycle would be ≈500k projection enqueues/day if unbatched; the per-job debounce to at most one per minute caps it at ≈430k/day worst case, which is why the table is partition-free, indexed on `(status, run_after)` and purged daily — the *table* holds, and the worker CPU spent in `extractProject()` is the first thing to measure. `job.project` is the first kind to move to a second poller machine.

**Forces change.** More than 50 jobs/s sustained or strict FIFO across workers → pgmq or a broker behind the same seam. The 1 GB Micro's disk-IO history is the binding ceiling: Micro → Small is a P3 step with a trigger metric — `events + capture_events` over 1M rows, p95 `push_project` over 400 ms, or a third disk-IO alert.

---

### ADR-07 — Document pipeline

**Status:** accepted (Decision record §8).

**Context.** Every document is a live editable DOM sent to `window.print()` at six sites (`apps/field/js/app.js:1552,1625,1936,2400,2413,2458`); the letterhead and sheet chrome are `apps/field/js/formkit.js:290-315`; `apps/field/css/print.css:6,25-30` holds the pagination rules carriers pay on. Numbers come from array length (`apps/admin/js/finactions.js:89,137,250`) and repeat after a delete; there is no issued state or version. The justification the model must emit is rendered once and discarded (`apps/field/js/forms.js:1484-1488` vs `:1505`). The QBO push flattens every line to one service item (`qbo-proxy/index.ts:316-321`) and updates in place with `SyncToken` only when an id is known (`:345-352`).

**Decision.** `buildDocumentModel(kind, ids)` (pure, `packages/documents/models`) → `renderHtml(model, template, carrierProfile)` (the shared renderer in `packages/documents`, with `sheet()`/`letterhead()` and `print.css` ported nearly verbatim; `print.css` lives only there and imports its tokens from `packages/ui`) → PDF by Chromium in the worker's isolated render child process, or print of the same HTML in the browser so offline printing keeps working. Tables: `documents(kind, number, current_version_no, status draft→issued→superseded|void, paid_state, division_id, org_id)`; `document_versions(document_id, version_no, model_json, model_hash, html_hash, pdf_sha256, storage_path, page_count int, render_status queued|rendering|rendered|failed, rendered_at, template_version, carrier_profile_id, issued_at, issued_by, supersedes_version_id, void_reason)`; `document_sequences(org_id, kind, year, next)` — numbers never reused. Line items are rows on drafts and are frozen into `model_json` at issue; a `billed_lines` view unions both so "what was billed" is one query. Issued versions are immutable (trigger); the trigger exempts only the render columns — `html_hash`, `pdf_sha256`, `storage_path`, `page_count`, `rendered_at`, `render_status` — and only while `render_status <> 'rendered'`. A post-issue edit is `document.supersede` (same number, v+1); `document.void` records a reason; QBO follows — supersede → update in place with `SyncToken`, void → QBO void, never delete. Signatures bind to an issued version, one definition adopted by §3 and §6: `signatures(document_version_id, signer_kind owner|contractor|adjuster|tech|lender|external, name, png_sha256, signed_model_hash, signed_at, method in_app|upload|portal, captured_by, captured_offline, device_id, ip, consent_text_version, external_ref)`; a signable document issues unsigned as v1 and `document.sign` renders v2, refusing when `signed_model_hash` is not the current version's `model_hash` [A-0.4]. `paid_state` comes from `payments` rows written by the QBO pull (`qbo-proxy/payments.ts:37-65` rules kept). QBO idempotency is keyed on the version (`qbo.invoice:<document_id>:v<n>`) with an `external_intents` row before the call and a `DocNumber` lookup on retry. `carrier_profiles` is a typed table (format `pdf|xactimate|portal_upload`, packet_order, required_documents, photo_rules, line_justification policy, justification_style, cover letter, days_to_pay). Every sending is a `submittals` row, one definition adopted by §3, §4 and §6: `submittals(job_id, claim_id, kind estimate|invoice|supplement|packet|certificate|drying_log|photo_packet|other, document_version_ids uuid[] ordered, packet_document_version_id — the composed PDF, nullable, carrier_id, adjuster_contact_id, to_contact_id, channel email|portal|carrier_portal|mail|api, sent_at, sent_by principal, outbox_id, external_ref, parent_submittal_id for supplements and resubmissions, status)`. ESX/XactAnalysis is an explicit open boundary: the pipeline emits PDF plus a structured JSON packet; `carrier_profiles.format` records what the carrier requires and `submittals.channel` how it went; a human does Xactimate-native submission in Xactimate and records it as a submittal (`channel='carrier_portal'`, `external_ref`). A golden-output harness (fixture job → HTML diff and page count per kind) exists before the first template ships.

**Options considered.** (a) Keep DOM plus `window.print()`. (b) A PDF-native library in Deno (pdf-lib, pdfmake), re-expressing every `print.css` rule. (c) A hosted HTML→PDF service. (d) Typed per-kind document tables instead of one `documents` table with `kind` — stronger validation, more migrations; left to the owner. (e) A second copy of the PDF bucket for durability — unnecessary, see below. (f) The chosen pipeline.

**Why.** The layout knowledge is in `print.css` and the renderers' conventions; browsers must keep printing offline; the real QBO failure mode is a response lost after QBO created the invoice, which only an intent row plus a lookup prevents.

**Consequences.** Twenty-two renderers are ported as templates plus written spec, invoice first; the field print path stays until no open job uses it; documents arrive as attachments. **Durability of the artifact of record:** PITR (P4) covers Postgres, not Storage — and it need not, because an issued version is reproducible: `model_json` + `template_version` + the pinned Chromium build (the playwright version is pinned in the worker image) re-render the same HTML, and `pdf_sha256` is verified on every re-render; a Chromium bump re-renders the golden fixtures under a new `template_version` rather than pretending nothing changed. What PITR protects is the row; git protects the templates; §6 states the same. The owner operates the render child process (memory cap, restart on OOM, one render at a time), a PDF bucket, the golden harness in CI, `template_version` bumps and PITR from P4.

**Holds to.** Hundreds of documents/day per render process; thousands of pages/day on one machine.

**Forces change.** Photo packets with hundreds of images (cap images per render); more than ~1,000 renders/day or a sub-2 s SLA → a second render machine; a carrier demanding Xactimate-native submission → the recorded human step; a legal e-signature requirement beyond captured PNGs → an e-sign provider adapter returning `signer_kind='external'` with the envelope id in `external_ref` [A-5.7].

---

### ADR-08 — Where agent logic runs: the hybrid split and the Fly reality

**Status:** accepted (Decision record §1–§2).

**Context.** The interactive assistant already fights the edge runtime: `chatWithTools` is a bounded loop of at most two tool rounds (`roybal-ai-office/index.ts:1527`, the cap comment; `:1586`, the loop guard that ends the loop when the mid-turn cap check fires; the `for` itself at `:1542-1543`) inside a request-scoped isolate, and no headless render path exists anywhere. The one long-running process is the phone agent: Node 22 on Fly, `shared-cpu-1x`, `memory = "256mb"`, `min_machines_running = 1` (`services/phone-agent/fly.toml:12,22-23`), per-caller limits in a process `Map` that forbids HA (`tools.mjs:19-27`), signing in by password grant (`supa.mjs:30-33`; the brief does the same, `roybal-brief/index.ts:62-71`).

**Decision.** Three tiers by duration and needs. **Postgres** — `op_<name>` SECURITY DEFINER functions run every short, DB-only operation transactionally with same-transaction events. **Deno edge** — one `roybal-ops` dispatcher plus trust-level functions (`roybal-webhooks`, `roybal-web-agent`, `roybal-lead`, `roybal-portal`) for synchronous work that needs a secret and a waiting human: assistant chat turns (the two-round cap kept), voice extraction, OAuth exchange, credential probes, the two SMS approval ops (ADR-03) and the `/alert` route (ADR-06); no Chromium, no long loops; `magicplan-proxy` (no callers, no auth, `magicplan-proxy/index.ts:44-70`) and `magicplan-webhook` are deleted at E0. **Node worker** — `roybal-worker` on Fly, a second app never co-hosted with `roybal-phone`, at least 1 GB [A-0.1]: queue consumer, Claude Agent SDK loops for open-ended work, plain Messages calls for forced-schema jobs, the MCP server, PDF render in an isolated child process with its own memory cap, integration pulls, blob→row projection, outbox delivery. **MCP topology:** the worker serves MCP over streamable HTTP only; Claude Code on a laptop connects with the minted `api_token` — directly, or through a 10-line local stdio shim that forwards to that endpoint — and there is never a stdio server "in the worker". The worker holds nothing that matters in memory (leases, reservations and sessions live in Postgres) and executes as a named machine principal under `agent_authority`, propose-only by default. Tool Runner and Managed Agents are noted, not chosen.

**Options considered.** (a) Postgres plus edge only, no worker — pgmq or `tick`, hosted PDF, agent loops as plain calls. (b) One Fly app for voice and worker. (c) Everything in the worker including webhooks — Fly ingress, its own auth, the Supabase gateway lost. (d) A Deno dispatcher as the only write door. (e) A second platform (Durable Objects) — one more vendor. (f) The chosen three-tier split with separate apps.

**Why.** Headless Chromium needs 300–500 MB alone, so a 20-page packet co-hosted with the ConversationRelay socket can OOM-restart a live call; a Deno dispatcher cannot transact over PostgREST; interactive chat must not ride a queue; office writes executing in Postgres mean Fly downtime never stops the office.

**Consequences.** Fourteen functions become five; five caller-identity conventions become two (a user JWT resolved by `current_principal()`; the service role only inside a trusted runtime with an explicit `p_principal_id`); the `verify_jwt` trap (`supabase/config.toml:1-21`) shrinks to five pins. The owner operates two Fly apps deployed from CI, Fly secrets for the worker, a heartbeat row and the render child process. If the worker is down: approvals still record — inbox and SMS alike, because both are SQL ops (ADR-03) — sends and renders queue, crews sync, the office keeps writing, and the owner gets one text from the 5-minute pg_cron heartbeat check through `roybal-webhooks /alert` (ADR-06), never through the outbox.

**Holds to.** 100+ for throughput. The honest ceiling is availability: one machine, one region, no HA.

**Forces change.** Sustained render or agent backlog → a second machine (`fly scale count 2` is true for the poller only; MCP and Agent SDK sessions are per-connection stateful and the HTTP MCP endpoint needs sticky routing); more than ~1,000 renders/day or a sub-2 s SLA → a dedicated render machine.

---

### ADR-09 — Model routing as data

**Status:** accepted (Decision record §10); the owner asked for this one explicitly.

**Context.** Ten differently named secrets choose the model per lane, each with a hard-coded previous-generation default: `OFFICE_PHOTO_MODEL` and `OFFICE_ASSIST_MODEL` → `claude-sonnet-4-6`, `OFFICE_DOC_MODEL` → `claude-opus-4-8` (`roybal-ai-office/index.ts:63-65`), `LLM_MODEL` (`roybal-ai-ingest/index.ts:51`), `NARRATIVE_MODEL` (`roybal-ai-narrative/index.ts:28`), `CONCIERGE_MODEL` (`roybal-portal/index.ts:76`), `PHONE_MODEL` (`services/phone-agent/config.mjs:21`), plus `WEB_MODEL`, `PHASE_LLM_MODEL`, `OFFICE_ASSIST_VOICE_MODEL`. `claude-sonnet-4-6` ($3/$15) runs photo, assist, ingest, concierge and phone — dearer than Sonnet 5 ($2/$10). The price table is copied six times (`LLM_PRICES` in ai-office, ai-ingest, qb-time-proxy, roybal-portal and `services/phone-agent/config.mjs`; `PRICES` in `roybal-web-agent/guards.ts:253-257`, which prices `claude-sonnet-5` at $3/$15 and `claude-opus-5` at $15/$75 against the real $2/$10 and $5/$25). Four literal forced `tool_choice:{type:"tool"}` sites — `roybal-ai-office/index.ts:148` inside `forcedTool()` (defined at `:130`, called at 14 sites), `roybal-ai-ingest/index.ts:411`, `qb-time-proxy/aiphase.ts:123`, `roybal-portal/index.ts:665` — all return 400 on Fable 5.1. No prompt is versioned (grep `prompt_version` = 0), `ai_usage` records `llm_model` but no prompt id, there are no evals, and every call is a raw `fetch`.

**Decision.** Routing is a table: `model_routes(task_class pk, provider, model_id → model_prices, effort, thinking adaptive|budget, max_output_tokens, max_cost_usd, fallback_task_class, batch_eligible)`; the FK makes a route to an unpriced model impossible. `model_prices` is seeded from the provider's price sheet at deploy time, never from this document; the model facts that fix the seed routes are the Decision record's (§10): `claude-fable-5-1` 1M context, $10/$50 per MTok, thinking always on, effort low..max, forced `tool_choice` rejected; `claude-opus-5` 1M, $5/$25, adaptive thinking by default; `claude-opus-4-8` $5/$25; `claude-sonnet-5` 1M, $2/$10; `claude-sonnet-4-6` $3/$15; `claude-haiku-4-5` 200K, $1/$5, `budget_tokens`. **Model ids are config, never code:** they appear in exactly one file, `packages/integrations/llm/client.ts` (the LLM adapter), and business code names only a task class (`judgment.*`, `draft.*`, `chat.*`, `extract.*`, `classify.*`); the routing policy loader, the metering helpers and the tool-definition generator from the ops registry live in `packages/ai`. Seed routes [A-0.5]: `judgment.*` — `judgment.line_justification` included, everywhere it is named — → `claude-opus-5` adaptive (Fable 5.1 at medium effort for `carrier_document` once evals justify it); `draft.*`, `chat.*`, `extract.*` → `claude-sonnet-5`, the default for extraction and classification-shaped work; `classify.*` → `claude-sonnet-5` at low effort, batch-eligible (Haiku 4.5 stays the eval-justified alternative for bulk backfills, not the default). Tool definitions never use forced `tool_choice` or assistant prefill (prefill returns 400 on the 4.6+ family): `tool_choice: auto` plus `strict:true` schemas (`additionalProperties:false`, `required`), or `output_config.format` when the forced call only existed to get JSON back. Adaptive thinking plus `output_config.effort` replaces `budget_tokens` everywhere except Haiku 4.5. Prompt caching is prefix-based — tools, then a stable system prompt, volatile content last — and is verified by `usage.cache_read_input_tokens`. The Message Batches API at 50% is the lane for bulk photo tagging and classification (`batch_eligible`). Prompts are versioned files `packages/ai/prompts/<key>.v<N>.md` mirrored to a `prompts` table (P1); `ai_usage` gains `prompt_id`, `prompt_version`, `task_class`, `route_key`, `agent_run_id`, `proposal_id`, `job_id`. Evals live in `packages/ai/evals/<task_class>/`; `eval_runs` lands in P3 with the first prompt version; CI runs a zero-cost schema-validity subset; a full run against staging gates activating a prompt version. `ai_reserve`/`ai_settle` SECURITY DEFINER generalise `web_turn_begin/end` (`227_web_receptionist.sql:147-211`) with per-lane and shared caps as `ai_caps` rows (P1, a rename of existing state). Per-carrier learning injects `retrieve_rules(carrier_id, codes[])` after the cached prefix of the `line_justification` prompt.

**Options considered.** (a) An env knob per lane (today). (b) One global model. (c) Routing in code with a `settings.ai_routing` jsonb override. (d) A router model or a multi-model cascade. (e) The chosen table with the price FK and one client file.

**Why.** Cost per completed task is the metric: one model at lower effort beats a cascade and keeps one cache namespace, because caches are model-scoped. A jsonb override cannot enforce the price FK. A routing change must be a row, and a model deprecation one price row plus one route row.

**Consequences.** The four forced-`tool_choice` sites are rewritten before any route points at Fable 5.1; cost by task class becomes one `GROUP BY`; docs cannot drift from code because there are no code defaults left to drift from. The owner operates route and price rows in admin Settings, prompt activation gated by an eval run that spends real money (reserve first), and Batches for backfills. Transitional exception: the phone agent keeps `PHONE_MODEL` until it imports packages at P7 [A-5.5].

**Holds to.** 100+ people.

**Forces change.** A second provider → one adapter plus route rows; a task class whose eval demands an unpriced model → a price row first, by construction.

---

### ADR-10 — Blob coexistence and projection

**Status:** accepted (Decision record §0, §6).

**Context.** `field_projects.data` is the system of record: 15 live jobs, avg 50 kB, max 227 kB, 80 top-level keys **(live)**. The merge exists twice — `ID_COLLECTIONS` (`apps/field/js/merge.js:32-36`) and `id_cols` (`243_losstypes_merge_union.sql:13-19`) — with parity proven once over 2,033 cases (`243:30-36`) by a harness that is not in the tree; `rooms` unions by value in both engines (`merge.js:180-185`; `243:100-104`). `field_photos` is a trigger projection nothing reads (`224_photo_projection_trustworthy.sql:195-200`). `min_field_build` is seeded 0 (`218:55`) while live devices report v153 and v155. `qbo-proxy` writes around the door with a service-role PATCH (`qbo-proxy/index.ts:429-431`); the Aug 6 stale-device clobber erased a job's contents.

**Decision.** Wrap, never rewrite. `push_project`, `merge_project_blobs`, `merge.js`, `sync.js`, `media.js`, the tombstone and self-echo rules are kept byte-for-byte. Blob changes are additive only: a new key lands in `ID_COLLECTIONS` and `id_cols` in the same PR under a CI test that parses the array out of the migration and compares — the harness is recovered from PR history first. A one-directional worker projection `job.project` (pure `extractProject()` in `packages/domain/extract`) upserts rows stamped `origin='blob'`, `source_ref`, `blob_rev`; blob wins for capture-owned sections (moisture maps and readings, drying logs, daily logs, photos, work auth, contents, sketches); `job.project_repair(job_id)` is idempotent; the SQL photo trigger retires after 30 zero-diff days. Office-owned entities — claims, carriers, policies, submittals, documents, line items, money documents, schedule, contacts — are born in tables and never written into the blob. Money documents flip ownership at P4 with import-on-open (9 `invoices[]`, 4 `reconEstimates[]`, 2 `changeOrders[]` **(live)**) and a read-only `officeDocs` summary written back under the one-owner-per-key rule: read the row, set only the owned key, push with the rev just read, retry once on `merged` — the intent already recorded at `apps/field/js/model.js:26`. `jobs` is the physical spine, made by an **in-place rename, never a copy**: `alter table public.unified_jobs rename to jobs` keeps the 33 live rows and every id that `contacts`, the portal and the phone agent already hold, and a compatibility **view named `unified_jobs`** with the old column list is created in the same transaction and kept until every reader has moved — the phone agent, the brief, the portal, the field `spine.js`, and old PWA builds (§3.5 and §7.5 say the same). `spine.js`'s writer (`spine.js:23-26`) is repointed at an op and old builds' POSTs get a 4xx that `spine.js:83` already swallows as `{skipped:true}`. `rooms[]` is never rewritten: `roomIds: [{id, name, aliases[]}]` is added as an id-keyed collection, `rooms[]` stays the derived string list old builds read and write, the projection resolves free-text rooms through `aliases`, and `min_field_build` is armed first — comparing the integer digits of `BUILD` (ADR-14) — so v153/v155 receive the P0002 "update required" (`218:82-86`) and self-update. The IndexedDB outbox for non-blob ops (P3), the offline adapter in `packages/db`, replaces today's online-only writes (`boardpush.js:204`; `spine.js:83`). Projection enqueue is deduped per job and debounced to at most one per minute; office reads of capture data are eventually consistent (about 1–2 minutes).

**Options considered.** (a) Rewrite `rooms[]` in place as objects. (b) Whole-blob write-back from the worker via `push_project`. (c) Normalise capture sections first with dual-write from the field app. (d) A per-entity offline-sync rewrite. (e) Copy `unified_jobs` into a new `jobs` table — rejected: it forks the ids and needs a backfill under load. (f) The chosen wrap and project.

**Why.** (a) is the one migration that can corrupt live jobs while old devices push old shapes; (b) recreates the Aug 6 clobber; (c) forces the sync rewrite — a CRDT problem no capability needs; (e) breaks every FK and token that already points at those 33 rows; the projection is a trigger and a worker, reversible by dropping the queue kind.

**Consequences.** The JS↔SQL twin persists until the last capture section moves, guarded by CI instead of a comment. The owner operates the parity test, `min_field_build` (a weekly proposal raises it to latest−2), the zero-diff report and `job.project_repair`. Rollback for `roomIds`: stop reading the key; nothing was rewritten. Rollback for the rename: rename back and drop the view; nothing was copied.

**Holds to.** Per-job, not per-company — hundreds of concurrent jobs with a handful of devices each; whole-blob writes are a 207 ms mean INSERT under five triggers and a `blob_history` rewrite per push **(live)**.

**Forces change.** More than ~5 devices concurrently editing one job, an office feature that must edit a capture-owned section, or blobs routinely over ~1 MB → that section moves to rows plus the outbox, one section at a time — equipment placements first.

---

### ADR-11 — Tenancy: divisions now, `org_id` as the recommended option

**Status:** accepted (Decision record §7).

**Context.** The company is one legal entity with two lines modelled as one mixed vocabulary list (`apps/board/js/board.js:22-77`). One settings uuid `00000000-…-01` is hard-coded in seven files (inventory §8); each provider has one token row read as latest-wins (`gmail-proxy/index.ts:111`; `qbo-proxy/index.ts:77`); `America/Anchorage` is hard-coded in nine files. A second crew, calendar, mailbox or QBO class is code, not a row.

**Decision.** `divisions(id, name, kind, calendar, qbo_class)` now, with `division_id` on jobs, crews, crew members, equipment units, documents, proposals, settings and connections. **Recommended:** `org_id uuid not null default '<this-org>'` on the spine (jobs, claims, contacts, crews, divisions) and the money tables (documents, versions, line items, payments, submittals) in the migrations that create them; `document_sequences` unique on `(org_id, kind, year)`; RLS predicates written against `current_org()`, which reads the `org_id` claim the JWT hook already carries beside `principal_id`, `role` and `division_id` (ADR-04) — a single defaulted value until a second entity exists.

**Options considered.** (a) Divisions only — the owner's simpler option: fewer columns, nothing to explain. (b) Full multi-tenancy now: per-org RLS everywhere, per-org sequences enforced. (c) A nullable reserved `company_id` never enforced. (d) The chosen divisions plus a defaulted `org_id` on new tables only.

**Why.** Adding `org_id` later is not additive — it touches every RLS policy, every unique constraint (document and claim numbers), every view and the JWT claim, plus a backfill under load, and it must land before the second entity exists. A defaulted column in a table being created anyway costs nothing.

**Consequences.** A second crew, calendar, mailbox or QBO class becomes a row; `connections` are keyed by `(provider, division_id)`. The owner operates division rows in admin Settings and nothing else until a second entity exists.

**Holds to.** 100+ people in one legal entity with several divisions and offices; a second entity with a claim value change and a predicate swap.

**Forces change.** Separate books or a franchise → issue a second `org_id` value from the hook and swap `current_org()`'s default, no schema change; office-scoped visibility for a second office → `locations` plus a multi-valued division claim.

---

### ADR-12 — Price sources and the Verisk EULA

**Status:** accepted (Decision record §8, hard constraint).

**Context.** `price_list` holds 2,959 rows "transcribed from Xactimate Price List Editor screenshots" (`109_price_list.sql:1-8`), read by the `priceLookup` tool — "Search the company's Fairbanks Xactimate price list" (`personas.ts:78-82`) — and by invoice-draft catalog stamping. The Verisk EULA prohibits redistributing price data; ESX files are encrypted and no export exists. The repo already states the safe posture for estimates: "We never touch the Verisk price list; the like-kind-and-quality baseline for a job is whatever that job's approved estimate already says" (`apps/field/js/xactimate.js:1-8`), and calibration uses only the company's own actuals (`apps/field/js/calibration.js:41-63`).

**Decision.** A `price_sources` interface in `packages/integrations` — `lookup(code | query, market, date) → {price, unit, source_id, ref}` — with three adapters: `internal_rates` (P4), the company's own labor, equipment-day and material rate book, calibrated from paid invoices [A-5.6]; `job_estimate`, the job's own approved carrier estimate imported through the sanctioned Excel export of the company's own work product (the `xactimate.js` pattern); and `price_list`, frozen as one flagged, restricted source that is never expanded, never exposed to external principals (portal, adjuster links, MCP) and never redistributed. `line_items` carry `price_source_id`, `price_ref` and `code`; the domain never names `price_list`. The document pipeline emits PDF plus a structured JSON packet; `carrier_profiles.format` records what the carrier requires and the `submittals` row (`kind`, `channel`, `external_ref` — ADR-07) records how it was sent; Xactimate-native submission is a human step in Xactimate, recorded on the submittal.

**Options considered.** (a) Keep expanding `price_list` — prohibited. (b) Delete pricing. (c) Wait for a licensed feed — none is offered. (d) The chosen interface.

**Why.** The EULA and the absence of any export leave own rates and the job's own approved estimate as the only sources a carrier will accept from this company; the interface makes a licensed feed, if one ever exists, one adapter.

**Consequences.** `priceLookup` is re-pointed at the interface; `internal_rates` becomes a table the office edits from P4; every drafted line carries provenance a reviewer can see. The owner operates the rate book and decides whether `price_list` keeps serving internal lookups at all.

**Holds to.** 100+.

**Forces change.** A licensed data agreement → one adapter; a carrier requiring Xactimate-native submission → the recorded human step, not a pipeline change.

---

### ADR-13 — Migrations and environments

**Status:** accepted (Decision record §11).

**Context.** The migration directory is a journal, not a schema: replay fails at `105` (it updates tables created by `200`) and at `233/236/244` (they scrape `cron.job`); 44 repo files were applied live under timestamp versions; six live entries have no file; at least eight live tables and two buckets have no DDL (inventory §6.8). There is one environment; 22 file headers say "SQL editor" and one says MCP `apply_migration`; an MCP redeploy already flipped `verify_jwt` and killed the quote form (`supabase/config.toml:1-21`). The live tier is a 1 GB Micro with two disk-IO incidents.

**Decision.** In order: (1) `supabase db dump` of production schema into `0000_baseline.sql` — policies, functions, triggers, grants, live enum values — with cron definitions stripped; (2) archive the 70 files; (3) `supabase migration repair --status reverted` for every live entry and `--status applied` for the baseline, **before any CLI points at prod**, then verify `supabase migration list` agrees on both sides; (4) forward-only timestamped files after; (5) `supabase db reset` on the baseline passes locally — the CI replay test; (6) schema reaches prod only by `db push` from CI after staging passes, never the SQL editor or MCP again. Staging is a second Supabase project seeded from `pg_dump` (schema plus scrubbed data) [A-0.6]. Cron is registered post-deploy by `cron_register.sql` as SQL-only `jobs_queue` inserts, so no secret sits in a migration or a `cron.job` row; the transitional HTTP job reads its header from `vault.decrypted_secrets`, and the one permanent HTTP job — the 5-minute dead-worker check that posts to `roybal-webhooks /alert` (ADR-06) — reads its service-secret header the same way. Compute Micro → Small is a P3 step with a trigger metric; PITR is enabled at P4.

**Options considered.** (a) Leave the journal and hand-apply. (b) Supabase branching for staging — it replays the journal that cannot replay, and bills per branch-hour on the Pro plan. (c) A naive `db push` against the current journal — it re-runs the non-idempotent `create policy` statements in 204/205. (d) A local `supabase start` in CI with no staging project — cheaper, but no place to run function integration tests with real secrets. (e) The chosen procedure.

**Why.** The repair step is what makes the CLI safe to point at production at all; everything else follows from a baseline that replays.

**Consequences.** The owner operates a second Supabase project (plan fee, duplicated function secrets), one `migration repair` session, CI secrets for `db push` and `functions deploy`, the `cron_register.sql` step, PITR from P4, and the compute-upgrade decision when the metric fires.

**Holds to.** 100+; two environments and one deploy discipline.

**Forces change.** A second region or project → per-environment config, nothing structural.

---

### ADR-14 — Build tooling and type safety

**Correction, binding over Decision record §12's wording.** `BUILD` is **not** generated from the git SHA. `_sync_guard` compares the reported build as an integer — `caller_build := regexp_replace(p_build, '\D', '', 'g')::int` (`supabase/migrations/218_sync_rpcs_and_build_gate.sql:79-84`, redefined identically at `226_sync_client_telemetry.sql:57-59`) — so a SHA such as `1e04694` would strip to `104694` and compare as garbage against `min_field_build`, and a SHA with no digits would parse to `null` and bypass the gate. `tools/build.mjs` therefore emits `BUILD = "v" + git rev-list --count HEAD` (489 at `1e04694`; monotonic on `main` because merges only add commits) and the SHA separately as `COMMIT`, for display and log correlation. The service-worker cache name carries the same integer. The weekly `min_field_build → latest−2` proposal, the `latest_field_build` deploy record and the P0002 "update required" path all compare that integer. Consequences for the tooling: a rebuild of the same commit yields the same `BUILD`, so CI's `build` job asserts the number is *strictly greater* than `origin/main`'s only when a PR touches `apps/{field,admin,board,office}`; a hotfix branch that rebases must be rebuilt before deploy; and the generated `config.generated.js` is a build output, never committed. §1.7, §7.3, §8.1 and Decision record §12 read this way.

**Status:** accepted (Decision record §12, with the correction above).

**Context.** `BUILD = "v165"` (`apps/field/js/config.js:25`) shipped beside `roybal-field-v166` (`apps/field/sw.js:6`) in PR #181, so the fleet reports a build it is not running to `_sync_guard` and `build.test.mjs` is red — but the field suite is one `&&` chain (`apps/field/package.json:10`) and the root chains field → board → functions (`package.json:11`), so 43 files are silenced by that one failure, and the deploy workflow has no test step. The precache list is hand-maintained: it omits two statically imported modules and includes the deprecated `pricing.js` (`sw.js:8-32`). Nothing type-checks — 0 `@ts-check`, `tsconfig.base.json` and `deno.lock` have no consumers, no lint — and the boot path loads 42 of 44 modules (923 KB raw).

**Decision.** esbuild bundling per app (field, office — admin and board until P5 — and portal) from `packages/*`; one `tools/build.mjs` generates `BUILD` as the monotonic integer above, `COMMIT` as the short SHA, and the service-worker precache manifest from the bundle graph. Typecheck: `tsc --noEmit --checkJs` with JSDoc for the apps (no rewrite of 17k lines), TypeScript in `packages/*` and `services/*`, `deno check` for the five functions; Biome for lint and format, with its scopes, folder rules and import boundaries (§8.2–§8.3) written against the one package vocabulary — `packages/domain`, `packages/db`, `packages/documents`, `packages/ui`, `packages/integrations`, `packages/ai`; `apps/{field,office,portal,site}`; `services/{worker,phone-agent}`. One `ci.yml`: on PR — Biome, typecheck, `deno check`, `node --test` across field, board, functions and phone-agent with no `&&` chain, the BUILD/SW lockstep test asserting a generated constant, migration replay on the baseline, merge-parity (JS vs SQL array), golden document diffs; on merge — staging migrations → tests → prod `db push` → `functions deploy` from `config.toml` (never MCP) → bundles with tests, READMEs and `serve.mjs` excluded → `fly deploy` worker. Deploy records `latest_field_build`; a weekly proposal asks the owner to raise `min_field_build` to latest−2 — a low-risk early proposal kind. **When:** none of this is P1. E0 fixes v165/v166 by hand, unchains the suites and moves the lockstep test into CI; P1 carries no esbuild, no service-worker generation and no package extraction (Decision record §15; §7.3). Bundling, `BUILD`/`COMMIT` generation, SW generation, `checkJs`, Biome and the verbatim package moves of §1.10 are the first sub-step of P3 — **P3.0 "toolchain"** — and P3 is L as a whole; P1 stays M on its weeks 3–6 calendar.

**Options considered.** (a) No-build import maps serving `packages/` at `/lib/` plus JSDoc/`checkJs` plus drift tests — the owner's option: zero toolchain, weaker guards, the lockstep and precache remain human duties, and the boot path cannot be split. (b) A full TypeScript rewrite of the apps. (c) A framework and bundler — the era-0 shape, whose residue is ~20 dead tables. (d) Bundling inside P1 — rejected: it turns an M phase into L and gates nothing P1 verifies. (e) The chosen esbuild plus `checkJs` at P3.0.

**Why.** The two most recent production defects are exactly what a build prevents, and the pure engines already prove the shared-package pattern (`schedule.js` runs in four runtimes); the `roomIds` build in P3 is the first consumer that must be a generated build, which is why the toolchain lands at the head of P3 and not before.

**Consequences.** The clients keep zero runtime npm dependencies (esbuild is a dev dependency); `supabase gen types` is committed; deploy gates on green. The owner operates one build step, Biome, one CI pipeline, and accepts that an op change redeploys four bundles, the functions and the worker.

**Holds to.** 100+; a sub-second build.

**Forces change.** None for scale; a second product line would split packages.

---

### ADR-15 — Integrations abstraction: adapters, `external_refs`, idempotency, health

**What "QuickBooks sync" covers, and what it does not.** In scope, by phase: QB Time timesheets into `time_entries` (P2); invoices with supersede and void, keyed on the document version through the outbox, the customer resolved through the existing `contacts.qbo_customer_id` (`228_contacts.sql:62`) (P4); payments pulled nightly into `payments` (P4); draw requests as documents of kind `draw_request` pushed as invoices (P4); and in **P6** the rest of the sync scope — bills and expenses from confirmed `receipts` rows and `materials` (a `qbo.purchase_create` outbox channel behind the same adapter, so QBO can hold job cost), customer creation (`qbo.customer_upsert`, proposed when a contact has no QBO id), and `divisions.qbo_class` stamped on every pushed line so each division's revenue and cost land in its own class. Out of scope: journal entries, payroll and inventory. `qbo_item_map` (§3; P4) is what ends the ~49% of billing that lands on the "Services" catch-all item today; the P5 reporting views do not wait for the P6 items.

**Status:** accepted (Decision record §2, §4, §8, E0).

**Context.** There is no integration layer. Each provider is one `{action}` edge function with a 1:1 browser pass-through (`apps/field/js/qbo.js:13-15`; `gmail.js:13-15`; `qbtime.js:19-21`); there is no `supabase/functions/_shared/`. Tokens are plaintext, one row per provider, latest wins (`gmail-proxy/index.ts:111`; `qbo-proxy/index.ts:77`; `103_qb_time_field.sql:20-24`); the `vault` extension is installed and unused. Five caller-identity conventions cross function boundaries. External ids have three homes — `contacts.qbo_customer_id` (`228_contacts.sql:62`), `qboInvoiceId` in the blob, `qbTimesheetId` in `time_entries.data` with no unique index (`102_time_entries.sql:14-20`; dedupe is an in-memory map, `qb-time-proxy/index.ts:608-611`). No side effect carries an idempotency key; two lanes are dead with cron green; the admin Gmail card shows connected whenever a token row exists.

**Decision.** `packages/integrations/<vendor>/` adapters behind interfaces, each with a fake; ops call the interface, never the vendor API. Every outbound side effect is an `outbox` row (ADR-06) with an idempotency key and, after the call, a `provider_id`; non-idempotent provider calls (QBO create) write an `external_intents` row before the call and look up by `DocNumber` on retry. `external_refs(entity_type, entity_id, system, external_id, sync_token, last_synced_at, status)` is the single home for integration ids; `time_entries` gets a unique expression index on `data->>'qbTimesheetId'` (P2). `connections(provider, division_id, org_id, account, token_ref, token_expires_at, expected_cadence)` replaces latest-row-wins token tables, with token columns encrypted via Vault [A-5.3]. **Health is a table plus a view.** `integration_runs(connection_id, kind pull|push|refresh|webhook, started_at, finished_at, ok, error, rows_affected, external_ref)` is written by **every** adapter call whether it ran from `jobs_queue` (pulls) or the `outbox` (pushes) — which is why it sees the lanes an outbox-only view could not. `integration_health` is a **view** over `integration_runs ⋈ connections` (`last_ok_at`, `last_error`, `consecutive_failures`, `token_expires_at`), read by the brief and the admin Today card; the alert rule is **three consecutive failures, or no successful run within 2× the connection's expected cadence**, texted to the owner through the outbox with a 24 h resend guard (when the failing lane is the worker itself, ADR-06's pg_cron alert covers it). E0's interim is a simpler view over the three existing token tables and the newest successful row per lane — the item that would have caught both dead lanes — replaced, not evolved, when the table lands. Phases: `connections`, `integration_runs`, `external_refs` and `worker_heartbeats` land in P1. Pulls (QB Time, Gmail, QBO payments) run as queue jobs in the worker; the browser halves (OAuth exchange, credential probes) live in `roybal-ops`; inbound webhooks (`roybal-webhooks`) are idempotent by provider id (Twilio SID, Gmail message id) and write an `integration_runs` row of kind `webhook`. The `payments.ts` rules (`qbo-proxy/payments.ts:37-65`) and the `gmail_id` unique index are kept.

**Options considered.** (a) Keep per-function proxies and add only a `_shared/` kernel (CORS, envelope, price table, quiet hours) — the smallest change, with no named operations. (b) Receipts in `capture_events` with a `failed` status versus dedicated run rows — the record records runs as events plus outbox rows; the run table wins because pulls never touch the outbox. (c) `integration_health` as a view over `connections ⋈ outbox` — withdrawn for the same reason. (d) An iPaaS (Zapier, Make) — another vendor holding state. (e) The chosen adapters plus outbox, refs, runs and the health view.

**Why.** An agent needs a named, typed operation and one place to observe a call; a retry must be safe; "did the customer get the text" must be answerable; a lane must not die silently — the same failure class has now happened three times, and two of the three were pulls.

**Consequences.** Proxies shrink to adapters; a new integration is an interface, an adapter, a fake, a `connections` row, `integration_runs` writes, outbox keys and an `external_refs.system` value. The owner operates reconnect flows in admin Settings (per connection), the health card, the owner alert and Vault secrets.

**Holds to.** 100+; per-division connections.

**Forces change.** A second QBO company or mailbox is a `connections` row; a partner API → a gateway in front of `roybal-ops`; high-volume webhooks → the same outbox and queue, sized by the compute tier (ADR-06).

---

## 6. Document pipeline and on-device capture
