Part A is the one pipeline every carrier-facing artifact leaves through. Part B is the separate assessment of what a crew device can recognise at the point of capture. They meet in one place: a capture (a label scan, a meter photo, a signature) becomes a row with provenance, and a document is built from rows and frozen. Nothing here touches the sync engine; the blob stays the capture path for each section until that section moves (Decision record §6; §7.10). Citations are `path:line` at `main@1e04694`; **(live)** = production read 2026-09-06; **[A-6.n]** = design assumptions introduced by this section; the decision record's own assumptions are cited as [A-0.n].

### 6.1 Today, in one paragraph

Every document is the screen. `FORMS` (`apps/field/js/model.js:31-98`, 22 entries) maps to `RENDERERS` (`apps/field/js/forms.js:3910-3933`, 22 keys, 21 functions — `reconEstimates` reuses `invoice`); each renderer returns a `sheet()` of bound inputs (`apps/field/js/formkit.js:310-315`) that the browser prints under `print.css` (`apps/field/index.html:17`; `window.print()` at `apps/field/js/app.js:1552,1625,1936,2400,2413,2458`). `h()` needs `document` (`apps/field/js/core.js:8-9`), so no server can render one. Numbers are typed (`forms.js:1146,1211,1866`) or counted from array length (`apps/admin/js/finactions.js:89,137,250`; `forms.js:3258`), so they repeat after a delete; there is no issued state, no version, and no record of what a carrier received. The one AI-produced justification per line, `basis` (`supabase/functions/roybal-ai-office/index.ts:378-385`), is rendered once and dropped when the draft is stored (`forms.js:1484-1488` vs `:1505`), although the owner's own rules say it must "survive adjuster review" (`docs/Estimating_Rules_Draft.md:113-114`). The share link is a DOM snapshot built in the browser (`apps/field/js/photoshare.js:257-296`) and printed from an iframe on the portal (`apps/portal/js/packet.js:76-81`). The email lane is `text/plain` with no attachment path (`supabase/functions/gmail-proxy/emailmatch.ts:104`), and the adjuster-email prompt promises a letterhead "added by the document" that no server can produce (`roybal-ai-office/index.ts:814`). The conventions on the page are what carriers pay on; the code that paints them is a liability (Phase 3, all three judges).

### Part A — the document pipeline

#### 6.2 Shape

Three stages, one direction, one template set:

```
sources (rows; the blob through the projection during transition)
  → buildDocumentModel(kind, ids, opts)     pure · packages/documents/models/<kind>.ts → DocumentModel (JSON)
  → renderHtml(model, template, profile)    pure string templates · packages/documents/templates/<kind>.ts
  → PDF   worker render child process, Chromium, letter @page     |   print   browser mounts the same HTML, window.print()
  → document_versions: model_json, model_hash, html_hash, pdf_sha256, storage_path, page_count, template_version,
                       render_status, rendered_at
```

`packages/documents` is the one home of document models, templates, the shared HTML renderer and `print.css` (§1.1); it imports the pure engines and the JSON Schemas from `packages/domain` and the tokens from `packages/ui`, and nothing else — a template reads a `DocumentModel` and never a database.

The rules that make it one pipeline:

- **The model is the contract.** A template reads only the model; it never queries. Everything a document says is in `model_json`, which is why an issued version re-renders byte-for-byte a year later.
- **Templates are strings, not DOM.** A tagged `html\`\`` helper with escaping replaces `h()`; the same function runs in Node, Deno and the browser. `moistureChartSvg()` already returns a string (`forms.js:64-110`); every other renderer is rewritten to that shape.
- **Media by reference.** The model carries `{sha, kind, w, h}` for photos, sketches, plan pages and signature PNGs — the content-addressed keys the sync engine already writes (`apps/field/js/media.js:35-37`; `apps/field/js/supa.js:208-222`). The renderer resolves them: in the worker from storage into `data:` URIs or files; in the browser from IndexedDB when offline. `model_json` stays under ~1 MB for a 300-photo packet.
- **Browser print stays.** `buildDocumentModel` and the templates are bundled into the field and admin apps by the toolchain that lands in P3.0 (§1.7; §7.5); the field app runs them over its local copy of the job, mounts the HTML into a print host (the `srcdoc` iframe the portal packet page already uses, `apps/portal/js/packet.js:1-8`) and calls `window.print()` through `packages/documents/print.js`, the one `window.print()` in the tree (§8.2). A tech with no signal still hands a customer a printed work authorization. The worker's PDF is the artifact of record; the browser print is a convenience of the same HTML.
- **Issue before render.** `document.issue` is a SQL op (Decision record §1; §2.5): number, version row, hash and event land in one transaction, and it enqueues `document.render`. The office sees "issued · rendering" for a few seconds; nothing waits on Chromium to hand out a number, and a Fly outage never blocks issuing.

Ceiling: hundreds of documents/day per render process; thousands of pages/day on one machine. Photo packets are the heavy case — cap images per render at 300 at the "medium" tier (900 px, `forms.js:1908-1915`) and emit continuation volumes past that [A-6.1]. Over ~1,000 renders/day or a sub-2 s document SLA → a second render machine; no table changes.

#### 6.3 DocumentModel per kind

Every model shares one envelope; the kind-specific `body` is validated by `packages/domain/schemas/document.<kind>.json` (JSON Schema, `additionalProperties:false` — the same schema source the ops registry uses, §2.3):

```
{ kind, kindVersion, org, division,
  company:   { name, address, phone, email, web, tagline, licenses[], signatory, signatoryTitle },   // from `app_settings` key `company`, seeded at P3 from `packages/domain/vocab/company.ts` (today's COMPANY, `model.js:6-24`); a rebrand or a second entity is a row edit, not a deploy
  job:       { id, workOrderNo, address, customer, phone, email, jobType },
  claim:     { number, carrier, adjuster, policyNumber, dateOfLoss, lossCause, lossTypes[], waterCategory, waterClass, dryingSystem } | null,
  parties:   { customer, adjuster, carrier, lender } — contact snapshots at build time,
  body:      <kind-specific>,
  attachments: [{ role, label, pages: [mediaRef] }],          // scans, plan pages, support docs — printed full page
  signatures:  [{ signerKind, name, date, png: mediaRef | null }],
  meta:      { templateVersion, carrierProfileId, generatedAt, clock, sourceRefs: [{ table, id, rev } | { blobPath, blobRev }] } }
```

`sourceRefs` is the provenance line: every fact in the body points at the row it came from — or, during transition, the blob path and `blob_rev`. It is what lets `billing.reconcile` (`packages/domain/detect/billing.reconcile.ts`, §7.13) say "line 12 was billed from readings dated 08-01 and 08-02, entries 4a1…".

| Kind | Prefix | Body — built from | Pure engines it calls | Attachments-as-pages |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `estimate` (reconstruction / repair) | `EST` | `line_items` grouped by room in first-appearance order; O&P mode; valid-until; scope-interview summary | totals ladder + GC O&P rule (`apps/field/js/fincalc.js:27-38,51-63`; `docs/Estimating_Rules_Draft.md:29-39`) | subcontractor quotes |
| `mitigation_invoice` | `INV` | T&M or contract lines with `code`, `category`, `priceBasis`, `justification`, `evidence[]`; deductible, prior payments, tax; Recap by Room | `fincalc`; the completeness gate before issue (`apps/field/js/completeness.js:49-135,205-231`); `dryingcalc.deployedCounts` (`apps/field/js/dryingcalc.js:152-163`) | receipts, sub invoices |
| `drying_log` (packet) | `DRL` | psychrometric rows by date (out/ref/aff T·RH·GPP, GD); equipment on site by day — from placements after P3, from count strings before; WRT worksheet and deviation notes | `gpp`/`grainDepression` (`apps/field/js/core.js:705-720`); sizing (`dryingcalc.js:45-148`); `equipmentSizingSummary` (`apps/field/js/narrative.js:140-163`) | moisture-map sketches with markers; trend chart (`moistureChartSvg`) |
| `certificate_of_drying` | `COD` | affected areas; per-material verification rows (material, meter, goal, final, reference, dry) with goal provenance (`dry_standards`, §3.2-D); start/complete; unit-day summary; the S500 statement (`forms.js:1176-1177`) | `isCertified` predicate (`apps/field/js/dryingwatch.js:30-33`); `DRY_STANDARDS`/`goalFor` (`core.js:722-734`) | an uploaded wet-signed copy replaces the body (`app.js:1439`; `print.css:22-30`) |
| `photo_packet` | `PHO` | photos grouped by room then stage; captions, timestamps, taken-by; optional line-item refs; size tier | sort and tier rules (`forms.js:1908-1915`) | — (the photos are the body) |
| `change_order` | `CO` | reason codes, description, line deltas, days added, original/revised completion, contract before/after; customer-facing; three signature slots | `fincalc.lineSubtotal` | — |
| `supplement` | `SUP` | carrier-facing: parent estimate version, trigger observations, added/changed lines with justification and evidence, delta to approved amount (`supplements`, `scope_baselines`, §3.2-E) | `fincalc`; `scope.compare` (`packages/domain/detect/scope.compare.ts`, §7.8) | observation photos |
| `draw_request` | `DRW` | SOV lines, % complete, this draw, retainage, previously billed, balance to finish; lender party (`sov_schedules`, `draw_requests`, §3.2-E) | draw math (today `forms.js:3220-3298`) | lien waivers, inspection reports |
| `work_authorization` | `WA` | pre-checked scope items (`model.js:537-544`), the six terms (`forms.js:838-844`), SMS consent, owner + representative signatures | — | uploaded signed copy replaces the body |
| `daily_log` (field report) | `DLG` | date, crew, notes, issues, materials, photos, signature; internal, never in a packet (`app.js:1442-1446`) | — | — |
| `submittal_packet` | `PKT` | ordered issued versions of what the carrier profile requires; cover letter; narrative cover sheet; index with page numbers; the component versions are the packet's `document_version_ids[]` in `packet_order`, and the composed PDF is itself an issued version that `submittals.packet_document_version_id` names (§6.9) | `narrativeFacts` (`narrative.js:241-282`) for the cover; `mdToNodes` grammar (`forms.js:2813-2837`) | every component's attachments, in profile order |
| also | `NAR` narrative cover · `CNT` contents inventory + pack-back receipt (`forms.js:2612-2613`) · `COC` certificate of completion (`forms.js:3320-3321`) · `LAB` labor log from `time_entries` · `box_label` / `equipment_label` QR sheets (`app.js:2385-2401`; `print.css:228-236`) · `PRG` progress update | | | |

Three kinds are new entities, not ports: `supplement` (carrier-facing, distinct from `change_order`), `submittal_packet` (today `packetGroups()` assembles live DOM, `app.js:1437-1494`), and `equipment_label` (Part B). `daily_log` and `labor_log` are kinds so the packet can cite them by version, not so they print differently.

#### 6.4 Building the model — from tables, and from the blob during transition

`buildDocumentModel(kind, ids, opts)` (`packages/documents/models/<kind>.ts`) reads through one `DocumentSources` port with typed getters (`job`, `claim`, `lineItems`, `readingsByLocation`, `placements`, `psychroRows`, `photos`, `signatures`, `timeEntries`, `sov`). Two implementations, one interface:

- **Rows** — supplied by `packages/db`'s `documentSources()` factory (the one data client, §1.1, so `packages/documents` itself performs no I/O): the P3/P4 tables of §3, read by the worker under its machine principal and by the office app under the user's JWT. After P4 it is the only implementation for money kinds.
- **Blob** (`packages/documents/sources/blob.ts`, pure): reads `extractProject()` output — the same pure function the `job.project` projection uses (`packages/domain/extract`, Decision record §6) — so a document built from the blob and one built from projected rows agree by construction; both stamp `sourceRefs` with `blob_rev`. It serves capture-owned sections (maps, drying logs, daily logs, photos, work auth, contents) for as long as they stay in the blob, and the import-on-open of the nine `invoices[]`, four `reconEstimates[]` and two `changeOrders[]` **(live)** into `documents` drafts at P4.

P4 template order: invoice → certificate of drying → drying-log packet → photo packet → estimate → change order (§7.6). Office-owned kinds (invoice, estimate, CO, supplement, draw, packet) flip to rows at their first issue; from then on the field app shows the issued PDF and the `officeDocs` summary (Decision record §6; §7.10). Capture-owned kinds keep reading the blob until their section moves; `job.project_repair` rebuilds their inputs.

Ceiling: an interface, not a runtime — holds to 100+ and to every kind. Forcing change: none; a new source (a licensed feed behind `price_sources`, a lender portal) is one more getter.

#### 6.5 Renderer, letterhead, tokens, `print.css` — ownership

| Asset | Today | Owner in the target | Ported how |
|---|---|---|---|
| Letterhead structure | `letterhead()` DOM with inline `color:var(--orange,#f26a21)` (`formkit.js:290-303`) | `packages/documents/templates/_partials/letterhead.ts` | nearly verbatim: same class names, string output |
| Company data | `COMPANY` (`model.js:6-24`); `licenses` printed nowhere — `sheetFooter` prints only the label (`formkit.js:305-307`) | `company` settings row → `model.company`; licenses in the footer line | data, not code |
| Sheet + footer | `sheet()`/`sheetFooter()` (`formkit.js:305-315`) | `_partials/sheet.ts` | verbatim |
| Print rules | `apps/field/css/print.css` (267 lines; `#0f1b2d` hard-coded 13 times) | `packages/documents/print.css` — its only home (§1.1): the apps link it as `media="print"`, the render child inlines it with `tokens.css` into every rendered HTML, the portal packet page uses the same file | verbatim, literals replaced by tokens imported from `packages/ui/tokens.css` |
| Screen tokens | `app.css` 31 vars with navy `#16395a`; print and site use `#0f1b2d` (inventory §8) | `packages/ui/tokens.css`, one navy `#0f1b2d` [A-6.2] (§1.6) | one file |
| Full-page attachment pages | `uploadedDocSheet` (`forms.js:2803-2808`) + `.sheet--doc` (`print.css:22-30`) | `_partials/attachment-page.ts` | verbatim |
| Signature block layout | `sigBlock` (`formkit.js:101-113`) | `_partials/signature.ts` (render only; the pad stays in the app) | layout as spec |
| Filename | `pdfName()` (`app.js:224-234`) | `documentFileName(model)` | verbatim rule |

The pagination knowledge in `print.css` — `@page` letter (`:6`), one sheet per page (`:20`), full-page scans (`:22-30`), the `.ta-print` mirror that stopped textareas clipping (`:78-87`), repeating table headers (`:111`), inline-block photo cards because Chrome ignores `break-inside` on grid items (`:238-259`) — is the accumulated cost of packets that got paid. It crosses unchanged and is pinned by the golden harness (§6.12). Chromium in the worker honors it exactly; the iPad's WebKit print is the same HTML with the one cosmetic difference the file already documents (`:244-248`).

Render process: Playwright — its package version pinned in `services/worker/package.json`, which pins the Chromium build (§6.7) — driving Chromium in a **child process** of the worker with its own memory cap, one render at a time, restarted on OOM (Decision record §1; §2.5, Amendment 3); Chromium needs 300–500 MB alone, so the worker machine is ≥1 GB [A-0.1]. Output: the PDF (letter, backgrounds on, no browser headers — the template prints its own), `page_count`, and the rendered HTML saved beside the PDF so the portal keeps serving a token-gated HTML view: `roybal-portal/index.ts:24-25,197-201` already serves a stored HTML skeleton; the worker becomes its producer instead of the browser.

Ceiling: as §6.2.

#### 6.6 Numbering

`document_sequences(org_id, kind, year, next)`, unique on `(org_id, kind, year)`. `op_document_issue` runs `UPDATE … SET next = next + 1 … RETURNING` inside the issuing transaction, so a number is never handed out without a version row committing beside it. Format `PREFIX-YYYY-NNNN` (`INV-2026-0042`). Drafts have no number and print **DRAFT** in the number box. A voided number stays voided — never reused, never renumbered. Draw requests carry the sequence number and the per-job ordinal lenders read ("Draw 3 of 7"). QuickBooks receives the number as `DocNumber` (today `invoiceNo.slice(0,21)`, `supabase/functions/qbo-proxy/index.ts:336`; the format is 13 characters; §4 step 9). One series per kind per year per org by default; a per-division series is an owner option (`divisions.number_series`) [A-6.3].

Ceiling: one row lock per (kind, year); holds to thousands of issues/day. Forcing change: a second legal entity — `org_id` is already in the key (Decision record §7; ADR-11).

#### 6.7 Versions, immutability, supersede, void

`documents(id, org_id, job_id, claim_id, kind, number, current_version_no, status draft→issued→superseded|void, paid_state unpaid|partial|paid, division_id)` and `document_versions(id, document_id, version_no, model_json, model_hash, html_hash, pdf_sha256, storage_path, page_count int, template_version, carrier_profile_id, issued_at, issued_by, supersedes_version_id, void_reason, render_status queued|rendering|rendered|failed, rendered_at)` (Decision record §8; this column list is the one §3.3 carries).

- `model_hash` = SHA-256 of the canonical JSON of `model_json` (sorted keys, no whitespace, media by sha). Two versions with the same hash are the same document; `document.supersede` refuses a no-op.
- `html_hash`, `pdf_sha256`, `storage_path`, `page_count`, `render_status` and `rendered_at` — the **render columns** — are written by the render job; `storage_path` = `documents/<org_id>/<job_id>/<document_id>/v<version_no>.pdf` in the private `documents` bucket (exists, empty **(live)**), `.html` beside it; service-role-only policy, no client write path [A-6.6].
- A trigger forbids UPDATE and DELETE on any `document_versions` row of an issued document, exempting only the render columns while `render_status <> 'rendered'`. A post-issue edit is `document.supersede(document_id, model)`: same number, `version_no + 1`, `supersedes_version_id`, prior version `superseded`. `document.void(document_id, reason)` closes the number with a recorded reason and no new version. Events: `document.issued`, `document.superseded`, `document.voided`, `document.rendered`.
- Drafts: `line_items` rows are the editable state; at issue they freeze into `model_json.body.lines[]` with `justification` and `evidence[]`. The `billed_lines` view unions issued snapshots and open drafts so "what was billed on this claim" is one query (Decision record §8; §3.2-E).
- QBO follows the version: supersede → update in place with `SyncToken` (`qbo-proxy/index.ts:345-352` is the precedent), void → QBO void, never delete; outbox key `qbo.invoice:<document_id>:v<n>`, the `external_intents` row before the call, and the `DocNumber` lookup on retry (Decision record §8; §4 step 9).

**Durability of the artifact of record.** PITR (P4) covers Postgres, not Storage: a deleted or corrupted object in the `documents` bucket is not restored by a point-in-time recovery. The design already contains the answer, stated here so nobody buys a second copy of the bucket by reflex: an issued version is **reproducible** — `model_json` + `template_version` + the pinned Chromium build in the worker image (the Playwright package version is pinned in `services/worker/package.json`, and that pin fixes the Chromium build) re-render the same HTML (`html_hash` is asserted equal by the golden harness on every re-render), and `pdf_sha256` says whether the stored file is the one that was issued — it is verified on every re-render. Recovery is `document.rerender(version_id)`, an internal op that refuses to overwrite a file whose sha already matches and fails loudly when the re-rendered sha differs from the recorded one — the row is immutable, so drift is reported, never absorbed. What must therefore be protected is the *row*, which PITR does, and the template history, which git does. Two residual risks are handled explicitly: (1) a Chromium upgrade can change PDF bytes for identical HTML — the worker image pins the Chromium build, and a bump re-renders the golden fixtures and records the new `pdf_sha256` values under a new `template_version` rather than pretending nothing changed; (2) evidence media (photos, signature PNGs, scans) is not reproducible — it stays under the sync engine's content-addressed offload with verify-before-drop (`apps/field/js/media.js:35-37`), and the P4 PITR step also turns on the soft-delete window for the `documents` and media buckets [A-6.8]. Ceiling: none; a second-region copy of the buckets is a storage setting if the owner ever wants one.

Ceiling: hundreds of thousands of versions on one heap; holds to 100+. Forcing change: none structural. Owner's option, stated: typed per-kind tables (`invoices`, `estimates`, `change_orders`) give stronger column validation and cleaner tool schemas at the cost of a migration per kind; the generic `documents` + a JSON Schema per kind is chosen because the schema already validates the body at the ops door.

#### 6.8 Signatures

A signature is evidence bound to what the signer saw, so it binds to an **issued version**, and a signed document is a **new version**.

`signatures(id, document_version_id, signer_kind owner|contractor|adjuster|tech|lender|external, name, png_sha256, signed_at, signed_model_hash, method in_app|upload|portal, captured_by, captured_offline, device_id, ip, consent_text_version, external_ref)`. This is the one definition of the table; §3.2-E adopts it. `external` is a signer who is none of the named parties (a witness, a notary, a property manager's agent); `captured_offline` is set when the PNG arrived through the blob projection rather than the pad's outbox write, which is how the projection tells the two apart.

- **Issue unsigned, sign after.** A signable kind (work authorization, certificate of drying, change order, certificate of completion, pack-back receipt) issues as v1 with empty slots. `document.sign(document_id, signer_kind, png|pages, signed_model_hash)` — action type `capture`, executable by crew on an assigned job (`assigned_job_ids()` ∪ the walk-on rule, §2.4) — inserts the row against the current issued version and renders v2 with `supersedes_version_id = v1` and the block filled. The op refuses when `signed_model_hash ≠` the current version's `model_hash`: nobody signs a document that changed under them.
- **Upload replaces.** `method='upload'` carries scanned pages; v2's body is those pages printed full page — the packet rule today (`app.js:1439`; `formkit.js:115-135`).
- **Offline.** Until the field outbox lands (P3) the capture path is unchanged: the pad writes `sigTech`/`sigOwner`/`signature` into the blob (`formkit.js:101-113`; `core.js:322`), inline under 60,000 characters because "they are the legal artefact" (`media.js:31-37`). The projection sees a new signature PNG on a section whose document is issued and enqueues `document.sign` against the current issued version with `captured_offline = true` [A-0.4]; if only a draft exists the signature waits as a `captures(kind='signature')` row (§6.16) and attaches at issue. After P3 the pad writes `document.sign` into the IndexedDB outbox with the hash of the version it displayed, `method='in_app'`.
- **Portal e-sign** for change orders (already promised in the form blurb, `model.js:87-89`) is `method='portal'` through the portal gateway (`roybal-portal`, trust level D — Decision record §2): the customer sees v1's PDF, signs, and the same op runs with `ip` and `consent_text_version` recorded [A-6.7]. A vendor e-signature envelope is a boundary, not a design input: if a lender or carrier requires one, the executed PDF comes back as `method='upload'` with the envelope id in `external_ref`.

Ceiling: holds to 100+. Forcing change: a legal requirement for vendor-audited e-signatures on a document class → that class routes to the envelope path by a settings row, not a schema change.

#### 6.9 Per-carrier submittal profiles

`carrier_profiles` is a typed table, not a JSON bag. The columns the renderer and the packet builder read:

| Column | Type | Read by | Default row |
|---|---|---|---|
| `carrier_id`, `name` | fk, text | — | `default` |
| `format` | `pdf \| xactimate \| portal_upload` | packet builder — what the carrier requires; a sending records its `channel` and `external_ref` on `submittals` | `pdf` |
| `packet_order` | `text[]` of kinds | `submittal_packet` model | `[narrative, mitigation_invoice, certificate_of_drying, drying_log, photo_packet, work_authorization, labor_log]` |
| `required_documents` | `text[]` | `submittal.validate` gate | `[mitigation_invoice, certificate_of_drying, drying_log, photo_packet]` |
| `photo_rules` | jsonb `{minPerRoom, stages[], tier, captionsRequired, maxPerPacket}` | `photo_packet` model | `{minPerRoom:2, stages:[before,during,after], tier:'medium', maxPerPacket:300}` |
| `line_justification` | `always \| flagged \| never` | invoice / estimate / supplement templates | `flagged` |
| `justification_style` | `sentence \| bullet \| reference_only` | same | `sentence` |
| `line_code_conventions` | jsonb `{requireCategoryAndCode, allowedUnits[], noLumpSum, monitoringLinePerVisit}` | draft validation, template | `{requireCategoryAndCode:true, allowedUnits:[EA,SF,LF,HR,DA,LS]}` |
| `cover_letter_template_id` | fk | packet builder | `cover.default.v1` |
| `submission_channel` | jsonb `{email, portalUrl, adjusterDirect}` | outbox | `{adjusterDirect:true}` |
| `days_to_pay_median` | int | collections, brief | `34` |
| rules | via `learned_rules(carrier_id, scope, rule_text, …)` (§3.2-C; §2.7) | `retrieve_rules` | — |

A line is **pushback-prone** when any of: `(carrier_id, code|category)` matches an active `learned_rules` row; `priceBasis='estimate'` (no catalog match); equipment days on the line exceed documented placement-days (or count-days before P3); a monitoring line has no reading on that date; an O&P line on a self-performed job; a lump-sum unit. Under `flagged`, those lines render a justification sentence beneath the description and carry `evidence[]` into the JSON packet; `always` renders every line's; `never` omits the text but keeps it in `model_json`. Justification text is produced by the `judgment.line_justification` route — `claude-opus-5`, the judgment class of the seed routes (§2.7) — at draft time and frozen at issue; never regenerated on re-render.

`submittals(id, org_id, job_id, claim_id, kind estimate|invoice|supplement|packet|certificate|drying_log|photo_packet|other, document_version_ids uuid[] — ordered, packet_document_version_id — the composed PDF, null when a single version was sent, carrier_id, adjuster_contact_id, to_contact_id, channel email|portal|carrier_portal|mail|api, format pdf|xactimate|portal_upload — copied from `carrier_profiles.format` at send time (§3.7), sent_at, sent_by principal, outbox_id, external_ref, parent_submittal_id — the prior submittal a supplement or resubmission answers, status — advanced from sent by carrier_responses rows)` is one row per sending, written by `submittal.record` whenever an issued version leaves the building — email with attachment, portal upload, a carrier-portal upload, or a human's Xactimate upload recorded after the fact. This is the one definition of the table; §3.2-C and §4 step 10 adopt it. `carrier_responses` and `rejection_reasons` hang off it (§3.2-C).

Ceiling: dozens of carriers, hundreds of rules; holds to 100+. Forcing change: a requirement these columns cannot express → a column, not a mechanism.

#### 6.10 The ESX / XactAnalysis boundary — explicit limitation

Some carriers require Xactimate-native submission. This pipeline does not produce it and is designed not to try: ESX files are encrypted, Verisk publishes no export, and the EULA forbids redistributing price data — so `price_list` stays frozen behind `price_sources` (ADR-12) and no structured Xactimate output is generated from it. Every packet emits two artifacts: the PDF and `packet.json` (the frozen models with `evidence[]`, stored beside the composed PDF at the packet version's `storage_path`), so the data is already shaped if a legitimate structured channel ever appears. For a `format='xactimate'` carrier the owner builds the estimate in Xactimate as today, uploads through XactAnalysis, and `submittal.record` captures what was sent — `channel='carrier_portal'`, the XactAnalysis reference in `external_ref`, the PDF export attached as the sent version — while the requirement itself stays on `carrier_profiles.format`. The one legitimate structured *inbound* stays: Xactimate's own Excel export of the owner's estimate, which `apps/field/js/xactimate.js:1-19` already consumes for selections. The open question is data, not design: which of the company's carriers require native submission for which claim types — collected into `carrier_profiles.format` as profiles are seeded.

#### 6.12 Golden-fixture harness

Exists before the first template ships (P4 gate). `packages/documents/__golden__/<kind>/` holds `fixture.json` (a `DocumentModel`; two per kind — a minimal one and a scrubbed copy of the live sample, e.g. the invoice that bills 215 air-mover-days against a log of 20/day **(live)**), `expected.html`, `expected.txt`, `expected.pages`. The PR job (`documents` in `ci.yml`, §8.2) renders HTML with `meta.clock` fixed and diffs it. The merge job (worker image, Chromium present) renders the PDF, extracts its text with pdf.js (already vendored at `apps/field/assets/vendor/pdfjs`) and asserts text equality, page count and — for a fixture that mirrors an issued version — the recorded `pdf_sha256`, which is what makes a Chromium bump visible (§6.7). `npm run golden:update -- <kind>` rewrites expectations and the diff travels in the PR. During P4 a third check compares the pipeline's invoice text against the on-site print of the same fixture — the cutover criterion in §7.6 ("the pipeline PDF equals the on-site print").

### Part B — on-device capture, assessed separately

#### 6.13 Principle

Evidence first, recognition second, commitment by a person always. A meter photo attached to a reading is worth more to an adjuster than a correctly recognised digit without one; a scanned asset label is worth more than both, because it needs no recognition at all. Every lane writes a `captures` row (§6.16 — the ingest envelope from P3 on) and, through it, a typed op — nothing bypasses the ops door (§2.3) — and nothing is required of any crew member: a paper lane records the same ops from the office with `time_entries.source='paper'` (the fourth value of that enum beside `qbtime`, `manual`, `board`, §3.2-D), honoring `crew_members.contact_prefs.appOptOut` and `paperTimesheet` (§3.2-A).

#### 6.14 What the PWA can do today

Camera through `<input type=file accept=image/*>` — one shot, no stream (`forms.js:2365-2370`) — resized on a canvas to 1200 px / q0.6 (`core.js:272-290`); IndexedDB persistence (`core.js:80-93`) with persistent storage requested at boot (`app.js:161-163`); a service worker that precaches the core modules and caches large vendor assets on first use (`sw.js:8-38`; pdf.js is 1.7 MB); a vendored QR *generator* for pack-out box labels (`qr.js:1-15`); content-addressed media offload with verify-before-drop (`media.js:35-37`; `apps/field/js/photoexport.js:84-106`). No decoder, no OCR: a grep for `Tesseract|BarcodeDetector|TextDetector|zxing|jsQR|onnx` across `apps/field` returns nothing. Non-blob writes are online-only and silently skipped offline (`apps/field/js/boardpush.js:204`; `apps/field/js/spine.js:83`) — the field outbox of Decision record §6 (§1.4, P3) is the precondition for every lane below.

#### 6.15 The lanes

| Lane | Do | Recognition | Commit rule | Effort · phase |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1. Asset labels + place/remove scan** (`captures.kind='barcode'`) | A QR (Code-128 fallback) label per `equipment_units.asset_tag`, printed through the `equipment_label` kind — the box-label print path already exists (`app.js:2385-2401`). A scan screen on a live `getUserMedia` stream: scan → pick room → optional photo → `equipment.place`; scan again → `equipment.remove`. | `BarcodeDetector` where present (Chromium/Android); a WASM decoder (zxing-class) in a Web Worker elsewhere — iOS Safari has no Shape Detection API and the crew carries a company iPad. Decoder budget ≤300 KB, lazy-loaded, cached under the `OPTIONAL` list (`sw.js:33-38`). | Nothing to gate: a decoded tag is an identity. The op validates server-side; a unit already placed elsewhere becomes an `equipment.reconcile` proposal for the office, never a rejected scan. Typed tags remain the fallback (`bumpAsset` convention, `forms.js:641-648`). | S · P3, with `equipment_units` / `equipment_placements` |
| **2. Meter reading OCR** (`kind='ocr_meter'`) | A photo per reading, always (`moisture_readings.photo_id`); the tech frames the LCD in a crop box. | Tesseract.js in a Web Worker, single-threaded build (the threaded build needs `SharedArrayBuffer`, which needs COOP/COEP headers GitHub Pages cannot set — `.github/workflows/deploy-field.yml:32-40`); `eng` data with the whitelist `0123456789.%`; grayscale + threshold; accepted only above a confidence floor and inside the meter's range [A-6.4]. Online, `extract.meter_ocr` (an `extract.*` class → `claude-sonnet-5` by the seed routes, §2.7) re-reads and flags disagreement. | **Prefill, never commit.** The value lands amber in the input; the tech confirms or retypes; the typed value is the reading. Typed / on-device / server disagreements become `captures.status='disagree'` for office review, never an auto-change. | M · after P3 (`moisture_locations` first); prefill enabled only when the eval passes |
| **3. Receipts** (`kind='ocr_receipt'`) | Photo offline; optional typed vendor and amount. | None on device. On reconnect `extract.receipt` (vision; `extract.*` → `claude-sonnet-5`, §2.7). | Tech or office confirms into `receipts`/`materials` through a proposal; the photo stays as evidence. | S capture · P3; M extraction · P6 |
| **4. Serial numbers** (`kind='serial'`) | At unit registration (office, usually online): a photo of the plate. | The plate's barcode reads through lane 1's decoder; printed serials through Tesseract.js (adequate on printed text) or `extract.serial` online (same class as lane 2). | Prefill into `equipment.register`; a person confirms. | S · with lane 1 |

Seven-segment LCDs are where stock OCR is weakest (segment gaps read as spaces; `1`/`7` and `5`/`6` confusion). An eval set of ~50 meter photos in `packages/ai/evals/extract.meter_ocr/` (beside the versioned prompts, §1.1; the Tramex MEX5 in the live sample, pin meters, the thermo-hygrometer) gates the on-device prefill: it ships when exact-match on the set is high enough that a wrong prefill is rarer than a typo [A-6.4]. Until then the lane is photo plus typed value — already the carrier-defensible part.

#### 6.16 `captures`

`captures(id uuid — client-generated, the idempotency key; org_id; job_id; room_id; kind voice|photo_ai|ocr_meter|ocr_receipt|barcode|serial|signature|email_pull; photo_sha; captured_by; captured_at; device_id; build; typed_value jsonb; ocr_result jsonb {engine, version, text, confidence, bbox, ms}; server_result jsonb {route_key, model, text, confidence, ai_usage_id}; confirmed_value jsonb; confirmed_by; confirmed_at; status captured|recognized|disagree|confirmed|discarded; target_kind; target_id — the reading, placement, receipt or signature the capture fed; deleted_at)`. Inserted by the capture op; status moves only through `capture.confirm` / `capture.discard`; photos are never deleted (they are the evidence). Rows are kept: `ocr_result` and `server_result` are the eval corpus for the next model.

This is the one `kind` enum and the one definition; §3.2-D adopts it. `captures` is the **ingest envelope from P3 on**: the four on-device lanes above (`barcode`, `ocr_meter`, `ocr_receipt`, `serial`), the signature pad (`signature`, §6.8), and the server-side lanes — `voice` from the field app's dictation and the phone agent, `photo_ai` from the photo tagger, `email_pull` from the Gmail pull job — all land here before a person confirms them. `capture_events` (`supabase/migrations/200_ai_backbone.sql:148-163`; 4,504 rows, 3,500 of them `emailPull` **(live)**) stays as the **legacy ledger**: read-only after P3, migrated into `captures` + `events` in P5, and dropped after that migration is verified.

Ceiling: one row per capture; holds to 100+ and any device count. Forcing change: none.

#### 6.17 iOS PWA constraints

- No Shape Detection API, no Web Bluetooth (BLE meters rejected), no background or periodic sync: the outbox flushes on foreground and after each successful pull (Decision record §6).
- Storage can be evicted; `navigator.storage.persist()` is requested at boot (`app.js:163`) and Safari honors it for installed PWAs [A-6.5]. Model assets stay under ~15 MB total: the decoder (≤300 KB) caches on first use; Tesseract core plus the digit-whitelisted `eng` data (~6–8 MB [A-6.9]) loads only when the owner enables meter OCR in settings, from the same OPTIONAL cache-first list.
- `getUserMedia` needs a secure context and works in standalone PWAs; the file-input camera remains the path for photos.
- Web Workers and WASM are fine; `SharedArrayBuffer` is not available without cross-origin isolation, hence the single-threaded builds.
- Every worker script and WASM asset is versioned with `BUILD` — the monotonic integer `tools/build.mjs` derives from `git rev-list --count HEAD` (§1.7; ADR-14), never a SHA — by the bundler that lands in P3.0 (§7.5), so a device on an old build cannot load a decoder the server no longer knows.

#### 6.18 What Part B needs from the PWA, and its ceiling

From the PWA: (1) the IndexedDB `outbox` for non-blob ops (Decision record §6; §1.4, P3) carrying `equipment.place/remove`, `reading.record`, `capture.record`, `document.sign`; (2) a `captures` local store mirrored to the table; (3) a lazy Web Worker host and the bundler's precache manifest for WASM (P3.0, §1.7); (4) a scanner screen on `getUserMedia`; (5) the existing content-addressed media path for evidence photos (`captures.photo_sha` is the same sha the sync engine uploads); (6) `equipment_units` and `moisture_locations` on the server (P3).

Ceiling: per device — any fleet size; the label lane scales with units, not people. Forcing change: a passing 7-segment eval turns the prefill on by default, still never auto-commit; a BLE meter class would be an Android-only opt-in adapter, never a requirement. The `appOptOut` and paper lanes hold at 100+: the office records what the paper says through the same ops, and the document pipeline cannot tell the difference except by `source`.

---

## 7. Phased roadmap and the first shipped automation

The roadmap turns the decisions above into eight phases a solo owner can run with Claude Code while the company keeps working. Two things fix its shape. First, the foundations — the operations door, the contract tables, the audit trail, the permission model — come before any new capability, but the first slice is deliberately thin: by week 6 a real proposal is approved in an inbox or by text and executed by the worker on two lanes whose execute legs already exist today, and by week 10 the first new capability is finding money — with no bundler, no package moves and no generated build in the way, because the toolchain is the first sub-step of P3 (P3.0), the phase that first needs a generated build. Second, every phase is additive: it adds tables, a queue kind, a worker path or a tab beside a working one and removes nothing until a stated cutover criterion is met, so any phase can be paused after its verify step without a rollback.

**Calendar assumption.** Weeks are elapsed calendar weeks at roughly one third of the owner's working time, with Claude Code doing the typing [A-7.1]. Effort: S ≈ days, M ≈ 1–3 weeks, L ≈ 1–2 months. The eight phases span about 56 weeks. A busy season pauses the current phase after its verify step; the calendar slides, the order does not. P1 is M because it contains nothing from the toolchain; P3 is L as a whole because it opens with it. Assumptions made in this section are tagged [A-7.n]; assumptions inherited from the decision record are cited as [A-0.n], and those made by other sections by their own prefix.

**Rules that hold in every phase.**

1. One revertible PR per item in E0; one PR per op or table afterwards.
2. From P1 on, schema reaches production only by `db push` from CI after staging passes (Decision record §11; §8.6); never the SQL editor or MCP `apply_migration` again.
3. No new writes into `field_projects.data` for a section once it has an owner in rows. The merge-parity test (`apps/field/js/merge.js:32-36` against the `id_cols` array of the live merge, `supabase/migrations/243_losstypes_merge_union.sql:16-19`) and a no-direct-table-write lint enforce it in CI.
4. Every phase names how the owner verifies it, how it rolls back, and what the crews and the office feel. "Crews feel nothing" is measured, not hoped: `sync_clients` keeps reporting every device's build and pushes keep succeeding through every migration window.
5. Cutover of a strangled surface is by criterion — two weeks of daily use on the new path, fourteen days of zero traffic on the old route — never by date.

### 7.1 Phase overview

| Id | Phase | Weeks | Goal in one line | Unblocks | Effort |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| E0 | Fix production now | 1–2 | dead lanes alerting through the interim `integration_health_v0` view, CI gate, publishable-key exposures closed, jammed spine swept, cron secrets out of `cron.job`, build gate armed, ledgers insert-only | all (precondition) | S |
| P1 | Thin backbone + smoke test | 3–6 | contract tables (`proposals`, `events`, `jobs_queue`, `outbox`, `agents` + six seed rows, `agent_authority`, `role_permissions`, …) with `connections`, `integration_runs`, `worker_heartbeats`, `external_refs`; ~5 operations as SQL functions; roles + JWT claim; the `roybal-ops` shell; the worker skeleton delivering the outbox; Approvals inbox; `submittals`; two migrated lanes — no bundler, no package moves | 5, 6 | M |
| P2 | First new capability | 7–10 | `billing.reconcile` v0 (with `photos.ai.equipment[]` hints) → `invoice.review_gaps`; QB Time pull on the queue writing `integration_runs` | 2 | M |
| P3 | Toolchain, then domain tables + projections | 11–18 | **P3.0** esbuild, generated `BUILD`/`COMMIT`/precache, the verbatim package moves; then `jobs` by in-place rename + compat view, claims/carriers/policies/adjusters, `roomIds`, `captures`, equipment + QR + field outbox, moisture rows, crews/skills/certs + `job_assignments`, `job.project`, `eval_runs` | 1, 2, 3, 4 (data) | L |
| P4 | Documents | 19–26 | documents/versions/sequences/signatures/payments, `qbo_item_map`, `internal_rates`, render process, six templates, QBO by version, carrier profiles, PITR + the rebuildable bucket | 1 | L |
| P5 | Office UI strangling | 27–34 | office tabs on ops, direct writes revoked table by table, changes feed, `schedule.apply`, `lead.screen`, Reports (four materialized views + read ops), `event_subscriptions`/`on_call`, `capture_events` folded into `captures` + `events`, `events` partitioned | 6 | L |
| P6 | Communications, carrier learning, scope | 35–44 | threads/messages, draft replies, responses → rejection reasons → learned rules, collections on the outbox, supplements/baselines/observations + `scope.compare`, QBO widened to bills/expenses, customers, class per division | 5, 3, 6 | L |
| P7 | Scheduling proposals + MCP | 45–56 | `drying_predictions`; `schedule.propose` over skills/certs/availability/equipment/drying/travel, crew-lead approvals, MCP over streamable HTTP propose-only, phone agent on packages | 4 | L |

### 7.2 E0 — Fix production now (weeks 1–2, S)

**Goal.** Nothing ships on a jammed spine or a dead lane, and nothing changes shape yet. Every item is a fact read from production on 2026-09-06 and a one-PR fix.

| Item | Why (today) | Fix | Owner verifies |
|---|---|---|---|
| Reconnect Gmail and QB Time; interim `integration_health_v0` + owner SMS | Gmail dead since 09-01 (`invalid_grant` every 15 min), QB Time dead since ~09-04 (refresh token invalid); `cron.job_run_details` reports both as succeeded because `net.http_post` is asynchronous **(live)** | re-authorise both; `integration_health_v0`, a view over the three existing token tables and the newest successful row per lane (`capture_events` `emailPull`, `time_entries` `qbtime`, `sms_messages`); a 15-minute check that texts the owner through today's `sendSms` with a 24 h resend-guard row — one of the HTTP crons that keeps its Vault wrapper (below) until P1. The view is replaced, not evolved: from P1 every adapter call writes `integration_runs` and `integration_health` is the view over `integration_runs ⋈ connections` (§2.6) | revoke the Gmail token in a test → one text arrives; a second revoke inside 24 h sends nothing |
| CI test gate + BUILD/SW lockstep | `BUILD = "v165"` (`apps/field/js/config.js:25`) vs `CACHE = "roybal-field-v166"` (`apps/field/sw.js:6`) shipped in PR #181; `npm test` chains with `&&` and stops at file 2 (`package.json:11`; `apps/field/package.json:10`); the only workflow deploys with no test step and still lists a deleted branch (`.github/workflows/deploy-field.yml:10-19`) | fix the pair by hand (the generated constant arrives with P3.0, §7.5); a `pull_request` workflow running every suite without `&&` chains, `build.test.mjs` a required check; the deploy job depends on it | a PR with a failing test cannot deploy; `main` is green |
| Gate the proxy actions reachable with the publishable key | `exchangeCode`, `disconnect`, `syncJobcodes`, `getTimesheets`, `getUsers`, `getCurrentTotals` have no `requireUser` (`supabase/functions/qb-time-proxy/index.ts:747-979`); `pushInvoice` consults the role only to widen CRM linkage and pushes for any caller (`supabase/functions/qbo-proxy/index.ts:309-313`); the key is served on the public site (`apps/field/js/config.js:8`) | every action requires a user JWT resolved to `admin`/`office`, or the cron secret where a cron is the caller | `curl` with the publishable key to `pushInvoice` and `getTimesheets` returns 401 |
| Sweep the zombie proposals + expiry filter | three `boardEdit` rows `pending` since 2026-07-25, expired 2026-07-26 **(live)**, counted forever against `MAX_LIVE_PROPOSALS = 3` (`qb-time-proxy/index.ts:285,499`) | set them `expired`; count only `status='pending' and expires_at > now()`; a 5-minute expiry sweep | the next QB Time run proposes again; `select count(*) from pending_actions where status='pending' and expires_at < now()` = 0 |
| Twilio status callback + reconcile queued rows | `sendSms` inserts `pending` and settles to Twilio's accept status (`supabase/functions/roybal-notify/index.ts:343-368`), which is `queued`; 174 of 175 outbound rows are frozen there **(live)**; `supabase/config.toml:53-54` claims a status-callback handler that does not exist | a `/status` route on `roybal-notify` (it moves into `roybal-webhooks` at P1) settling `delivered`/`failed` by SID; a one-time reconcile of the 174 rows against Twilio | send a test text; its row reaches `delivered` |
| Delete Magicplan | `magicplan-proxy` has no callers and no auth and spends a paid key (`supabase/functions/magicplan-proxy/index.ts:44-70`); `magicplan-webhook` is unreachable behind `verify_jwt=true` | delete both functions, the `MAGICPLAN_*` secrets and the `config.toml` pins | `supabase functions list` shows 12 |
| Cron secrets out of `cron.job` | all seven HTTP jobs embed the cron secret and the publishable key as literals in `cron.job.command` **(live)**; migrations 233/236/244 scrape them back out | store both in Vault; re-register each job with a SQL wrapper that reads `vault.decrypted_secrets` at call time | `select command from cron.job` contains no `Bearer` or `cron_` literal |
| Arm `min_field_build` | seeded 0 (`supabase/migrations/218_sync_rpcs_and_build_gate.sql:55`), so the fleet at v165 ×4, v155, v153 **(live)** is never asked to update; the recipe exists (`219_revoke_direct_field_writes.sql:32`) | set it to 163 [A-7.2] so the two stale devices receive the P0002 "update required" (`218:82-85`) and self-update through the service worker | `sync_clients` shows six devices at v165+ within a day |
| Ledgers insert-only for clients | `ai_usage` is `for all to authenticated using(true)` (`supabase/migrations/201_ai_usage.sql:78-79`), so are `capture_events` (`200_ai_backbone.sql:235-237`) and `time_entries` (`102_time_entries.sql:32`) — any crew login can delete the month's AI spend or payroll hours | `ai_usage`, `capture_events`: revoke UPDATE/DELETE from `authenticated`; `time_entries`: revoke DELETE and gate UPDATE to `admin`/`office` — the board's manual-hours editor upserts with `merge-duplicates` (`apps/board/js/data.js:255-269`) and is an office surface [A-7.3] | a tech JWT `DELETE` on `ai_usage` affects 0 rows; the board still saves an hours edit under the owner's login |
| `capture_events` retention | 3,500 of 4,504 rows are `emailPull` heartbeats **(live)**; no purge exists | a nightly purge of `emailPull` rows older than 30 days, in the shape of the `blob_history` purge (`215_blob_history.sql:199-218`) | the row count drops; nothing else changes |

**What changes.** No tables, no packages. Twelve edge functions instead of fourteen; one new workflow; nine cron rows re-registered; one `app_settings` value; four policy sets. **Data migration.** None. **Rollback.** Each item is its own PR and reverts alone; the build gate rolls back by setting the value to 0. **What people feel.** Crews: the two stale devices see "update required" once, then nothing. Office: the Gmail and QB Time cards stop lying and the owner gets a text when a lane dies. **Ceiling.** These are repairs, not structures. The one structure introduced — `integration_health_v0` — is deliberately disposable: P1 replaces it with the `integration_runs` table written by every adapter call, pull or push, and the `integration_health` view over `integration_runs ⋈ connections` with the alert rule of three consecutive failures or no successful run in twice the expected cadence (§2.6; ADR-15). `connections` carries `division_id` from its first migration, so a lane per division is a row, not code.

### 7.3 P1 — Thin backbone and smoke test (weeks 3–6, M)

**Goal.** The contract exists in Postgres and one real lane rides it end to end — proposal → approval (inbox or SMS, both recorded without the worker) → worker execution → event — with negative tests proving the permission model.

**What changes.**

- *Migrations and environments:* `supabase db dump` of production into `0000_baseline.sql`; the 70 journal files archived; `supabase migration repair` marks every live entry reverted and the baseline applied — before any CLI points at prod; `supabase db reset` on the baseline is the CI replay test; a second Supabase project seeded from `pg_dump` with scrubbed data becomes staging [A-0.6].
- *Tables:* `proposals`, `events`, `jobs_queue`, `outbox`, `agent_runs`, `agents` — seeded with six rows, `agent:billing`, `agent:projection`, `agent:outbox`, `agent:brief`, `agent:phone`, `agent:web`; the principals of later lanes are added by the phase that first runs them (§3.7) — `agent_authority`, `role_permissions`, `approval_policies`, `operation_catalog`, `model_routes`, `model_prices` (seeded from the provider price sheet at deploy time; no per-token figure lives in this document, §2.7), `prompts`, `api_tokens`, `submittals` (the one shape of §3.7: `kind`, ordered `document_version_ids uuid[]`, nullable `packet_document_version_id`, `carrier_id`, `adjuster_contact_id`, `to_contact_id`, `channel`, `sent_at`, `sent_by`, `outbox_id`, `external_ref`, `parent_submittal_id`, `status`), `divisions` (one row); the integration spine — `connections` (one row per provider and division, `token_ref` into Vault, `expected_cadence`), `integration_runs` (one row per adapter call, `kind pull|push|refresh|webhook`, `ok`, `error`, `rows_affected`, `external_ref`, written whether the call ran from `jobs_queue` or from the `outbox`) and the `integration_health` view over `integration_runs ⋈ connections` (`last_ok_at`, `last_error`, `consecutive_failures`, `token_expires_at`); `worker_heartbeats`; `external_refs` (the outbox deliverer needs it for the migrated lanes' Gmail ids); `ai_caps` and `web_sessions` as renames of the existing cap and web-agent rate state; `org_id` defaulted on every table created here if the owner takes the tenancy recommendation (Decision record §7).
- *SQL:* `emit_event`, `op_propose`, `op_proposal_approve`, `op_proposal_decline`, `enqueue`, `claim_job`, `ai_reserve`/`ai_settle`, `current_principal()`; the role-enum migration in three steps — N adds the values; N+1 in one transaction redefines `_sync_guard` (`218_sync_rpcs_and_build_gate.sql:67-69`; `226_sync_client_telemetry.sql:45-49`) and every other role gate to accept both vocabularies, remaps `profiles.role` through an explicit per-row table — `branden@roybalconstruction.com` → `owner`, the second live `admin` profile → `office` by default, `tech` → `crew`, the two machine viewers → `agents` rows — and asserts `push_project` still resolves; N+2 drops the old names a week later. The owner confirms both `admin` mappings in the admin UI before the role claim is armed (a confirmation item in 04). The Custom Access Token hook adds `principal_id`, `role`, `division_id` and `org_id` — never assigned job ids; crew reads stay division-wide until `job_assignments` and the `assigned_job_ids()` initplan helper land in P3 (§2.4) [A-5.4].
- *Operations:* about five, each a SQL function shipped by migration with its definition in `packages/domain/ops` — new files, since P1 moves nothing — `proposal.approve`, `proposal.decline`, `email.send`, `sms.send`, `job.set_stage`; the registry (`defineOp`) emits the `operation_catalog` rows and the dispatcher's route table; `_shared/` (one CORS, one envelope, `current_principal()`) is hand-written until P3.0 builds it from the packages.
- *Functions:* `roybal-webhooks` — Twilio inbound, the status callback moved from E0, and `/alert`. It handles the owner's "YES 12" / "NO 12" **synchronously**: trust level B is allowed to call exactly two SQL ops, `op_proposal_approve` and `op_proposal_decline`, as the principal resolved from the sender's number, allowlisted approvers only (§2.5; §4 step 6) — so an approval records without the worker and only a `runtime:"worker"` execution waits for it. The `roybal-ops` dispatcher shell with its route table generated from the registry; `sendSms` becomes an outbox row.
- *Worker:* `roybal-worker` on Fly, its own app at ≥1 GB [A-0.1]. The skeleton is a queue poller with lease columns, the outbox deliverer (email through a Gmail adapter, SMS through Twilio — inline under `services/worker/adapters/` until P3.0 lifts them into `packages/integrations`) under idempotency keys, and a 30 s heartbeat into `worker_heartbeats`; every adapter call writes an `integration_runs` row. It imports the workspace directly under Node's type-stripping; no bundler [A-1.8]. The dead-worker alert is worker-independent by construction: pg_cron runs a SQL check over `worker_heartbeats` every 5 minutes and, when the newest heartbeat is older than 10 minutes, calls `net.http_post` to `roybal-webhooks /alert` with a service-secret header read from Vault; that function texts the owner synchronously through the Twilio adapter, with a 24 h resend guard. This is the one documented exception to "pg_cron never calls HTTP"; the outbox is not used for it (§2.5).
- *Apps:* an Approvals inbox in `apps/admin` (the first route of the office app that arrives in P5) scoped mine ∪ my role in my division ∪ unassigned in my division, beside "text YES 12", not instead of it.
- *Lanes migrated:* the overdue-invoice reminder (`supabase/functions/roybal-brief/index.ts:249-299` → `pending_actions.emailSend` → `gmail-proxy/index.ts:317-381`) now proposes `email.send`; the adjuster-email draft (`roybal-ai-office/index.ts:760-782`) files an `email.send` proposal instead of filling a textarea; a `submittals` row is written whenever the email carries a document reference.
- *Not in P1:* no esbuild, no service-worker generation, no package extraction, no generated `BUILD` — all of it is P3.0 (§7.5), the first sub-step of the phase that first needs a generated build. P1's effort stays M because of that: about four weeks of the owner's third-time at the keyboard, of which the squash-and-repair day, the three role migrations and the Fly worker are the fixed costs.

**Data migration.** `pending_actions` is kept and read through a compatibility view over `proposals` until P2; the brief writes `proposals` behind a flag. No blob key changes. The cron rows for the migrated lanes become SQL-only `jobs_queue` inserts; the remaining HTTP crons keep their Vault wrapper from E0 (the heartbeat check above is the one HTTP job that stays for good).

**Unblocks.** Capability 5 (a send with a submittal record) and capability 6 (the approval, audit and queue backbone every later automation uses).

**Owner verifies.** Approve the same reminder twice from the inbox and once by SMS → exactly one email, one `outbox` row `delivered`, one `submittals` row when a document was attached, one `integration_runs` row per delivery and `integration_health` `ok` for the Gmail lane. A crew JWT calling `op_proposal_approve` is refused. `events` shows propose → approve → execute with the approver's principal, and `approved_by_kind` is never null. Kill the worker mid-delivery → after restart the lease expires, the row is re-claimed, the email still goes once. Scale the worker to zero and text "YES n" → the approval records through `op_proposal_approve` and the send waits; the owner's dead-worker text arrives within about 15 minutes through `/alert`, not through the outbox. The role remap table in migration N+1 lists all eleven profiles, and the two `admin` rows are confirmed in the UI before the claim is armed. `supabase migration list` agrees on both sides and `supabase db reset` passes in CI. Every device in `sync_clients` pushed successfully during the role-enum window.

**Rollback.** A flag routes the brief back to `pending_actions` (kept until P2); the worker can be scaled to zero — approvals still record from the inbox and by SMS, sends queue. The role migration's N+1 step is one transaction; if it fails nothing is remapped.

**What people feel.** Crews: nothing. Office: an inbox beside the SMS code, and the adjuster email arrives as a proposal to approve rather than text to copy. Owner: staging exists, a CI gate stands between a PR and production, and a dead worker texts within minutes.

**Ceiling.** The contract tables hold to 100+ people, hundreds of proposals a day and several thousand queue jobs a day on one worker — the projection kind that arrives in P3 is the load to watch (§7.12); the worker's ceiling is availability — one machine, one region, no HA — not throughput; `events` stays one heap to low millions (§7.12); `integration_runs` grows one row per adapter call and is kept.

### 7.4 P2 — First new capability (weeks 7–10, M)

**Goal.** `billing.reconcile` v0 runs nightly on the worker over mitigation jobs and files one `invoice.review_gaps` proposal per job with evidence; the QB Time pull moves onto the queue so the detector's labor input is known-fresh.

**What changes.** *Packages:* `packages/domain/detect/billing.reconcile.ts` (pure, in the `completeness.js` style; it imports the equipment-class regexes from `apps/field/js/dryingcalc.js:152-163` by path until P3.0 lifts that module into `packages/domain/engines`) and the ops `billing.reconcile` (worker) and `invoice.review_gaps` (money; approval `office`). Its inputs include `photos.ai.equipment[]` — the tag the photo tagger already writes (`supabase/functions/roybal-ai-office/index.ts:241-248`; stored at `apps/field/js/officeai.js:124-127`) — as a v0 hint (§7.13). *Worker:* a `billing.reconcile` queue kind scheduled nightly by a `jobs_queue` insert; the QB Time `pullAllLinked` and `clockinSweep` actions run as queue kinds through an inline adapter in the worker (lifted to `packages/integrations/qbtime` at P3.0), each call writing an `integration_runs` row so `integration_health` sees the pull lane. *Migrations:* a unique index on `time_entries ((data->>'qbTimesheetId'))` — dedupe is app-side today (`qb-time-proxy/index.ts:600-611`) and the table's only index is `updated_at` (`102_time_entries.sql`) — plus expression indexes on `qbJobcodeId` and `date`. *Apps:* a weekly unbilled list in the inbox.

**Data migration.** None to rows. Execution of an approved `invoice.review_gaps` appends the flagged lines to the blob's office-owned `invoices[]` through the worker's key-owner write-back: read the row, set only `invoices`, push with the rev just read, retry once on `merged`. Invoices are office-edited only (`apps/field/js/model.js:26`), so no crew device races that key.

**Unblocks.** Capability 2 at the resolution today's data allows.

**Owner verifies.** Run the detector over three closed mitigation jobs the owner picks [A-7.4]; it surfaces the misses the owner already knows about and invents nothing; every finding carries dates, counts and entry ids the owner can open. Approve one → the invoice gains the lines; approve it again → nothing. Inserting a duplicate `qbTimesheetId` into `time_entries` fails. `integration_health` shows `qbtime` with `last_ok_at` inside its cadence after the first queued pull.

**Rollback.** Disable the queue kind; open proposals expire on the sweep. The write-back never touches a crew-owned key, so there is nothing to repair.

**What people feel.** Crews: nothing. Office: a weekly list of unbilled and undocumented items per job, and QB Time hours that are current again. Owner: the first dollar the backbone found.

**Ceiling.** The detector's contract survives P3 and P4 unchanged — it reads placements instead of counts and writes `line_items` instead of blob lines; its resolution is bounded by the capture shapes until P3.

### 7.5 P3 — Toolchain, then domain tables and projections (weeks 11–18, L)

**Goal.** The toolchain lands first, so that the builds this phase ships are generated builds; then the facts agents must read become rows without changing how crews capture them, and the office's entities are born in tables.

**What changes.**

- *P3.0 — toolchain, the first sub-step; nothing else in P3 starts before it:* `tools/build.mjs` — esbuild bundling per app from `packages/*`; `config.generated.js` with `BUILD = "v" + git rev-list --count HEAD` (a monotonic integer, because `_sync_guard` strips non-digits and compares as `int`, `218_sync_rpcs_and_build_gate.sql:79-84`; 489 at `1e04694`) and `COMMIT` as the short SHA for display and telemetry; the service-worker precache generated from the bundle graph; the lockstep test now asserts the generated constant; the verbatim package moves of §1.10 — `fincalc`, `dryingcalc`, `completeness`, `dryingwatch`, `buildwatch`, `calibration`, `convert`, `xactimate`/`xlsx`, `selections`, `ai`, `myweekcalc`, `schedule` + `schedulewatch`, `phasematch`, `emailmatch`, `payments`, `approve`, `guards`, `digest`/`crewdigest`/`weekly` into `packages/domain/engines` with their tests; `supa.js` into `packages/db`; the three `schedule.js` copies collapse to one import; the worker's inline adapters lift into `packages/integrations`; `packages/integrations/llm/client.ts` becomes the one file that names a model — `packages/ui/tokens.css` with one navy [A-1.1]; `tsc --checkJs`, Biome and the §8.2 invariants as required checks. The `roomIds` build below must be a generated build — that is why the toolchain sits here and not in P1.
- *Tables:* `jobs` — `unified_jobs` renamed **in place** (`alter table … rename`) with a compatibility view named `unified_jobs` created in the same transaction and kept until every reader has moved (phone agent, brief, portal, field `spine.js`, old PWA builds; §3.5) — `claims`, `carriers`, `policies`, `claim_adjusters`, `rooms`, `equipment_units`, `equipment_placements`, `equipment_reservations` (created here, first written in P7), `moisture_locations`, `moisture_readings`, `dry_standards`, `drying_days`, `crews`, `crew_members` (`principal_id` nullable, `contact_id` mandatory, `contact_prefs{digestOptOut, appOptOut, paperTimesheet}`), `skills`, `crew_member_skills`, `certifications`, `availability_patterns`, `availability_exceptions`, `phase_skill_requirements`, `job_assignments` with the `STABLE` SECURITY DEFINER `assigned_job_ids()` helper every crew-facing policy calls once per statement (§2.4), `locations`, `jobs.lat/lng`, `captures` — the ingest envelope from here on, one `kind` enum `voice|photo_ai|ocr_meter|ocr_receipt|barcode|serial|signature|email_pull` (§6.16) — and `eval_runs` with the first prompt version; `time_entries` gains real columns beside `data`, including `source qbtime|manual|board|paper`; `division_id` lands on jobs, crews, crew members, equipment units, proposals and settings (`connections` has carried it since P1).
- *Era 0 → `legacy`:* the March tables that collide by name — `jobs`, `rooms`, `moisture_readings`, `equipment_logs`, `line_items`, `invoices`, `photos`, `documents` (single-digit rows, no live writer) — move to schema `legacy` in the migration before the new ones are created; their triggers and the 18-value `job_status` enum go with them.
- *Blob, additive only:* `roomIds: [{id, name, aliases[]}]` registered in `ID_COLLECTIONS` (`apps/field/js/merge.js:32-36`) and `id_cols` (`243_losstypes_merge_union.sql:16-19`) in the same PR under the CI parity test — the 2,033-case harness referenced at `243:30-36` is recovered from PR history first; `rooms[]` is never rewritten (it unions by value in both engines, `merge.js:180-185`; `243:100-125`).
- *Worker:* `job.project` (pure `extractProject()` in `packages/domain/extract`), enqueued by an AFTER UPDATE trigger on `field_projects` with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` and a 60 s `run_after`, so a burst yields at most one projection per job per minute; `job.project_repair(job_id)`, idempotent; the SQL `field_photos` trigger (`224_photo_projection_trustworthy.sql:195-219`) stays until the worker projection is zero-diff for 30 days. `capture_events` becomes read-only after this phase: the voice-ingest envelope moves to `captures`, and the legacy ledger is folded into `captures` + `events` in P5.
- *Field app:* new builds write `roomIds` beside `rooms[]`; an IndexedDB `outbox` store for non-blob ops, drained after each successful pull through the ops door; an optional scan screen — QR/Code-128 labels per `equipment_units.asset_tag`, `equipment.place/remove` and `capture.record` ops with a photo, `BarcodeDetector` where present and a WASM decoder in a Web Worker on the company iPad; a scan of a job's label also grants that crew member the 24 h walk-on read (§2.4); the spine writer (`apps/field/js/spine.js:23-40`) repointed at an op in the same PR as the rename and the view — old builds that still POST get a 4xx that `spine.js:83` already swallows as `{skipped:true}`.
- *Compute:* Micro → Small if the trigger metric fires (§7.12).

**Data migration — the whole story for this phase.**

1. *Backfill by projection.* Every live blob (15) and its `blob_history` pre-images run through `extractProject()`; rows carry `origin='blob'`, `source_ref`, `blob_rev`. Verification is count parity per job (photos 729, moistureMaps 14, dryingLogs 7, rooms 9 **(live)**) — the check migration 221 passed for photos (450 → 450, `docs/Sync_Rearchitecture_Plan.md:217-221`).
2. *Arbitration.* Blob wins for capture-owned sections; rows are rebuildable; nothing reads rows for a section until it has been zero-diff for 14 days.
3. *Rooms identity with device gating.* `min_field_build` is raised to the first generated build that writes `roomIds` **before** any consumer relies on the key [A-7.5], so every device receives the P0002 "update required" (`218:82-85`) and updates through the service worker; the projection resolves every free-text `room` (six write sites, `apps/field/js/model.js:173,210,324,421,586,275`) through `roomIds.aliases` per job; unresolved strings land in a review list, never in a guess. Rollback: stop reading `roomIds`; nothing was rewritten.
4. *Spine.* The 33 `unified_jobs` rows (all `status='new'` **(live)**) keep their ids: the table is renamed in place, never copied, so the `contacts` links (`229:31`), the portal and the phone agent are untouched and nothing is backfilled; the view with the old column list keeps the five field sites, the phone agent, brief, portal and notify working until each reader moves (P5–P7). §3.5 reads the same way.
5. *Crew.* The `crew_members` blob (11 rows) becomes columns. `contact_id` is mandatory for every member and `principal_id` is nullable: the one-time bind matches on email where present (7 of 11 rows carry one **(live)**) and the owner confirms the rest through a reviewed `(crew_member_id, profile_id | null)` table in the migration; the paper-timesheet member may never have a login and keeps `principal_id` null, which `canNotify` treats as opt-out of every app lane. `digestOptOut` (2 rows **(live)**, honored at `supabase/functions/roybal-brief/crewdigest.ts:149`) becomes `contact_prefs.digestOptOut`; the paper-timesheet member gets `paperTimesheet=true` and `appOptOut=true`.
6. *Time entries.* Columns are added beside `data` and backfilled — `source='qbtime'` for the pulled rows, `board` for the office editor's, `manual` or `paper` for the rest; the QB Time writer (`qb-time-proxy/index.ts:215-239`) writes both until every reader is on columns, then `data` becomes derived.
7. *Equipment.* New scans produce placement rows through the outbox; per-day counts in `dryingLogs[].readings[]` keep flowing from old flows and are projected into `drying_days`; nothing forces a crew off counts.
8. *Captures.* `captures` starts empty and fills from new captures only; `capture_events` is not rewritten here — it stays readable as the legacy ledger and is folded into `captures` + `events` in P5.

**Unblocks.** Capabilities 1, 2, 3 and 4 gain the data they read; the reconciliation turns from counts into placement events wherever scans are used.

**Owner verifies.** P3.0 first: the first generated build ships with `BUILD` equal to the commit count and a precache equal to the module graph, `sync_clients` reports it, and every `node --test` suite passes from its new directory. Then zero-diff blob vs rows for 14 days on every live job (a nightly diff event); "dehu-days in the basement on job X" answers from SQL and matches the drying log; every device in `sync_clients` is at or above the `roomIds` build; the scan screen places and removes a unit offline and the rows appear after reconnect with the photo attached; `select count(*) from jobs` = 33 with the original ids and `select * from unified_jobs` returns the old column list; a crew JWT reading `photos` shows one InitPlan and no nested loop over `job_assignments` in `explain`.

**Rollback.** Drop the queue kind and the trigger — the blob is untouched; the view stays; `min_field_build` can be lowered. P3.0 rolls back by publishing the last hand-assembled build; nothing in the database depends on it.

**What people feel.** Crews: one build update ("update required" once), an optional scan screen, and room names that are the same across forms. Office: the claim and crew data exist even though their tabs arrive in P5. Owner: the first honest capacity data — skills, certs, availability — to enter.

**Ceiling.** Per job, not per company: whole-blob writes (INSERT 207 ms mean, max blob 227 kB, five triggers per update **(live)**) under a per-job `FOR UPDATE`; more than ~5 devices editing one job at once, an office feature that must *edit* a capture-owned section, or blobs routinely over ~1 MB move that section to rows plus the outbox, one section at a time. Divisions plus a defaulted `org_id` hold to 100+ people in one legal entity. The toolchain has no scale ceiling; a second product line is what would split the packages.

### 7.6 P4 — Documents (weeks 19–26, L)

**Goal.** Every carrier-facing artifact becomes a numbered, versioned, immutable document rendered headlessly from data, and money documents leave the blob.

**What changes.** *Tables:* `documents`, `document_versions` — the column list of §3.7 and §6.7, including `page_count`, `render_status queued|rendering|rendered|failed`, `rendered_at`, `html_hash`, `pdf_sha256`, `storage_path`, `model_json`, `template_version`; the immutability trigger exempts only the render columns while `render_status <> 'rendered'` — `document_sequences`, `signatures` (the one shape of §3.7: `signer_kind owner|contractor|adjuster|tech|lender|external`, `png_sha256`, `signed_model_hash`, `method in_app|upload|portal`, `captured_by`, `captured_offline`, `device_id`, `ip`, `consent_text_version`, `external_ref`), `payments`, `line_items`, `carrier_profiles`, `external_intents`, `qbo_item_map` (seeded from the 48 QBO items ever used; an unmapped category falls back to the generic service item and flags the line), `price_sources` + `internal_rates`, `change_order_terms`, and `sov_schedules`/`sov_lines`/`draw_requests` [A-7.6]. *Packages:* `packages/documents` — the document models (`buildDocumentModel`), the per-kind templates, and the shared HTML renderer porting `sheet()`/`letterhead()` (`apps/field/js/formkit.js:290-315`) and `print.css` nearly verbatim; `print.css` lives only here, its 13 navy literals replaced by the token from `packages/ui`. *Worker:* the render child process — Chromium, its own memory cap, one render at a time, restart on OOM; the Playwright version is pinned in the worker image, which pins the Chromium build. *Templates, in order:* invoice → certificate of drying → drying-log packet → photo packet → estimate → change order. *Ops:* `document.issue`, `document.supersede`, `document.void`, `document.sign`, `document.rerender`, `qbo.invoice_upsert` keyed on the version with an `external_intents` row before the call and a `DocNumber` lookup on retry. *AI:* the per-line justification runs on the `judgment.line_justification` task class, routed to `claude-opus-5` (§2.7; Decision record §10). *Golden harness:* fixture job → HTML diff and page count per kind, before the first template ships. *PITR and the rebuildable bucket:* PITR is enabled — documents and events are now the legal record — and it covers Postgres, not Storage; the `documents` bucket is rebuildable from `model_json` + `template_version` + the pinned Chromium build, `pdf_sha256` is verified on every re-render, and the soft-delete window is turned on for the `documents` and media buckets [A-6.8] (§6.7).

**Data migration.** Ownership of `invoices[]`, `reconEstimates[]` and `changeOrders[]` flips from blob to rows: the nine, four and two live instances **(live)** are imported into `documents`/`line_items` when the office first opens that job in the documents surface; numbers are re-issued from `document_sequences` — today `INV-${length+1}` repeats after a delete (`apps/admin/js/finactions.js:89,137,250`) — with the old string kept as a legacy number; the field app becomes read-only for those sections and shows the issued PDF (the intent already recorded at `model.js:26`); a read-only `officeDocs: {invoiceNo, status, pdfPath}` summary is written back under the one-owner-per-key rule so old builds still show status. Jobs closed before P4 are not imported [A-7.7]. `drawSchedule`/`lender`/`contractAmount` on the 8 construction jobs **(live)** import the same way into `sov_*`. Signatures captured offline in the blob attach through the projection to the current issued version [A-0.4]. Paid state comes from `payments` rows written by the existing QBO pull rules (`supabase/functions/qbo-proxy/payments.ts:37-65`), each pull writing an `integration_runs` row.

**Unblocks.** Capability 1: documents with identity, versions, per-carrier profiles and a submittal record.

**Owner verifies.** The pipeline PDF of a live invoice equals the on-site print (golden diff, no page-count drift); re-issue makes v2 with the same number and a `supersedes` link; a retried QBO push makes exactly one invoice (kill the worker between the call and the settle; on restart the adapter finds it by `DocNumber`); the photo packet of the largest live job renders inside the child process without restarting the worker; `document.rerender` of an issued version reproduces its `pdf_sha256`; a drafted line's justification is stored on the line instead of discarded (`apps/field/js/forms.js:1484-1488` vs `:1505` today) and `ai_usage` shows it on `claude-opus-5` with a prompt version.

**Rollback.** Per kind: the field print path stays until no open job uses it; a kind whose golden diff fails stays on the blob editor. The import is idempotent, keyed on the blob instance id.

**What people feel.** Crews: Print/Share yields PDFs; the invoice editor, already office-only, is gone from the field app. Office: documents arrive as attachments, numbers never collide, a re-issued invoice is a version. Adjusters: the same packet, now with a version number on it.

**Ceiling.** Hundreds of documents a day per render process; photo packets are the heavy case (cap images per render); over ~1,000 a day or a sub-2 s SLA → a second render machine. ESX/XactAnalysis-native submission stays a human step in Xactimate recorded on the submittal; `price_list` is frozen behind `price_sources` and never expanded.

### 7.7 P5 — Office UI strangling (weeks 27–34, L)

**Goal.** The office's tabs are rebuilt on ops one at a time, direct table writes are revoked behind each op, the board reads a changes feed and applies schedules atomically, and the office gets its first reports and its first screened leads.
