**What changes.** *Apps:* the tabs of `apps/office` — the target home of today's admin and board [A-0.10] — in the order Today/Proposals → Leads → Claims → Documents → Board, each a route on the ops client beside the old route; the `leads` table lands with the Leads tab [A-7.8] together with `lead.screen` (below); the board's three-table poll every 20 s (`apps/board/js/board.js:205-208`; `apps/board/js/data.js:150-163`) is replaced by a changes feed; `schedule.apply` lands one proposal of N assignments atomically; a *Reports* tab (below). *Migrations:* the `219` pattern per table — `coordination_jobs`, `crew_members`, `time_entries`, `contacts`, `portal_*` lose client INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE once every writer of that table is an op; the five hand-rolled rev guards (`apps/board/js/data.js:110-132`; `apps/field/js/boardpush.js:302-320`; `roybal-notify`; `qbo-proxy`; `coordination_job_patch`) collapse into the op; `event_subscriptions` replaces the op-declared fan-out [A-4.2]; `on_call` replaces the owner's cell as the only alert number [A-2.6]; `capture_events` is folded into `captures` + `events` and dropped after the fold is verified; `events` is month-partitioned — the arithmetic of §2.2 puts low millions of rows inside the first year at 100+ people, so the partition arrives with the headcount, here, not "later" (§7.12). *Functions:* the browser halves of the gmail/qbo/qb-time proxies fold into `roybal-ops` action by action behind a flag.

**`lead.screen` (capability 6, intake).** A worker op run on every new `leads` row by `agent:intake` (added in this phase's migration), **propose-only**. *Deterministic signals:* the honeypot and the per-IP and per-subnet caps `roybal-lead` already applies (`supabase/functions/roybal-lead/index.ts:22-23`, `:306-312`); phone and email validity (normalisation, a disposable-domain list); a prior-contact match on `contacts.phone_norm` or email. *Judgment:* one `classify.intake` call — `claude-sonnet-5` at low effort, batch-eligible (§2.7) — returning `{score, reasons}`. *Output:* the `leads` row (`score`, `scam_flags`, `screen_result {score, reasons}`) plus its `captures` row as the ingest envelope; when any hard signal fires, a `lead.triage` proposal for office with the flags as evidence. Nothing auto-declines a lead. Ceiling: per lead, machine-bounded.

**Reporting (capability 6).** Reports are four materialized views over the rows P3 and P4 create, refreshed nightly by a `jobs_queue` kind and on demand, each exposed as a `read` op with `division_id` and date-range inputs (so agents and the brief use the same numbers as the tab) and rendered by the one *Reports* route. No warehouse, no BI tool, no second copy of a number.

| View | Answers | Sources |
|---|---|---|
| `jobs_summary` | per job: stage, division, days in stage, billed vs cost — Σ `billed_lines` − (Σ `time_entries.hours × crew_members.hourly_rate_usd` + Σ `materials.cost_usd` + equipment-days × `internal_rates`) — and open `invoice.review_gaps` findings in dollars at the invoice's own rates | `jobs`, `billed_lines`, `time_entries`, `crew_members`, `materials`, `equipment_placements`, `internal_rates`, `proposals` |
| `ar_aging` | issued-unpaid documents at 30/60/90 with the payer and the last collections step | `documents`, `payments`, `outbox` (`collections:*` keys) |
| `crew_hours` | logged hours ÷ available hours per member per week, split by `source` (so `paper` is visible), days with no entries flagged | `time_entries`, `availability_*`, `schedule_assignments` |
| `automation_costs` | the §2.6 query set: what agents proposed, who approved, what ran, cost by task class, per job | `proposals ⋈ events ⋈ agent_runs ⋈ ai_usage` |

Each view has a pgTAP test on a fixture; the morning brief reads the same views, so the brief and the tab cannot disagree. A `carrier_scorecard` (submittals, approval rate, median days to pay, top `reason_code`s per carrier and adjuster) joins the set in P6 when `carrier_responses` exist. Ceiling: materialized views hold to thousands of jobs on a nightly refresh; a read replica or warehouse export is the step after that, and nothing in the schema changes for either.

**Data migration.** `coordination_jobs.data` — stage, dates, `subtasks[]`, `deps[]`, `crewIds`, `dayCrew`, `leadLog` **(live)** — is projected into `jobs`/`job_phases`/`schedule_assignments`/`leads` when its tab moves; the blob stays the board's read model until the old board route has been silent for 14 days, then it is frozen read-only. The office app stops booting the field sync engine (`apps/admin/js/admin.js:34`) once the last tab reads rows, which ends the whole-company replica in the office browser. `capture_events` rows become `captures` rows (the envelope) and `events` rows (the audit facts) in one migration with count parity asserted; the 30-day `emailPull` purge from E0 has already bounded its size.

**Unblocks.** Capability 6 (intake, collections, reporting surfaces) and the operability of everything before it for more than two office people — the inbox, roles and divisions become things a second office hire can be given.

**Owner verifies.** Per tab: two weeks of daily use on the new route, then 14 days of zero hits on the old route (a route-hit counter in `events` [A-7.9]), then the old code is deleted. A tech JWT can no longer PATCH `coordination_jobs`; the board's schedule survives two tabs open at once without a conflict re-entering `refresh()`; the count of `using(true)` policies on operating tables falls to zero. The Reports tab and the morning brief show the same `ar_aging` total on the same day. A staged scam lead yields a `lead.triage` proposal and no decline. `select count(*) from capture_events` is 0 after the fold and `events` has its first month partition.

**Rollback.** Per tab, the flag returns the route to the old code; each revocation is one migration and re-grant is its rollback; the reporting views are droppable; the `capture_events` fold keeps the source table until parity is asserted.

**What people feel.** Crews: nothing. Office: one tab at a time changes under them, each only after two weeks of daily use; the board stops flickering; a Reports tab that agrees with the brief. Owner: "who may approve" is a row, not a deploy.

**Ceiling.** Role permissions as data hold to 100+ people and ~10 roles; the inbox scoping holds to several office staff across divisions; cross-department SLAs force a workflow layer on top of these tables, never instead of them.

### 7.8 P6 — Communications, carrier learning and scope detection (weeks 35–44, L)

**Goal.** Every message on a claim is a row in one thread, drafts come from claim context, what carriers reject is captured in a form the next draft can learn from, and the scope the carrier approved is compared with what the field observed.

**What changes.** *Tables:* `threads`, `messages` (one direction enum across email/SMS/portal/phone — today `inbound/outbound` and `in/out` disagree), `carrier_responses`, `rejection_reasons`, `learned_rules`, `supplements`, `scope_baselines`, `observations`. *Ops:* `message.draft_reply` (propose; `draft.correspondence` on Sonnet 5), `classify.email` (`claude-sonnet-5` at low effort, batch-eligible, §2.7), `carrier.learn` (weekly, deterministic, propose-only, owner-approved), `collections.schedule` on the outbox with the quiet window in one place — 7 am–8 pm Alaska for every kind by default, crew-line kinds included; the exemption for crew-line kinds is an owner option recorded in 04 and, when taken, a per-kind row in `notification_policies`/`role_permissions`, never a constant — and `scope.compare` (below). *Prompts and evals:* versioned prompt files mirrored to `prompts`; the `line_justification` prompt (on `claude-opus-5`) with `retrieve_rules(carrier_id, codes[])` injected after the cached prefix; an eval set per task class gating activation. *QBO sync, widened:* a confirmed `receipts` row becomes a QBO purchase through a `qbo.purchase_create` outbox channel behind the same adapter, `materials.cost_usd` flows to job costing, `qbo.customer_upsert` is proposed for a contact with no `qbo_customer_id`, and `divisions.qbo_class` is stamped on every pushed line (ADR-15).

**`scope.compare` (capability 3).** A worker queue kind at `packages/domain/detect/scope.compare.ts`, enqueued (a) when an `observations` row is created or confirmed, (b) nightly for every job with an open `scope_baselines` row, and (c) on demand. *Inputs:* the approved estimate version's line items by room and category (`scope_baselines.document_version_id` → frozen `model_json.body.lines[]`) versus the observations since the baseline — `photos.ai` tags (`damage[]`, `materials[]`), daily-log `issues`, change-order text and readings — turned into candidate `observations` rows by `classify.observation` (Sonnet 5 at low effort, batch-eligible) and confirmed by the office before they count. *Deterministic diff first:* rooms observed but absent from the baseline; categories present in confirmed observations with zero baseline quantity; baseline quantities exceeded by projected `rooms.floor_sf`/`perim_lf` where the projection has them; `hidden_damage` and `code_upgrade` observation kinds, which always qualify. *Judgment model second:* one `judgment.supplement` call (`claude-opus-5`, adaptive) writes the reason text and the per-line justification for the candidate delta lines, with `retrieve_rules(carrier_id, codes[])` injected after the cached prefix; quantities never come from the model. *Output:* one `supplement.draft` proposal per (job, baseline) with `evidence_refs` (observation, photo and log ids), the candidate lines and the delta to `approved_amount_usd`, under the idempotency key `scope.compare:{estimate_version_id}:{observation cursor}` — the cursor is the id of the newest qualifying observation, so the same observations never file twice and a new observation supersedes the open row. Office approval creates the `supplements` row and a draft `documents(kind='supplement')`; issue and submittal then follow the §4 flow. Ceiling: one diff per job per night is trivial; the forcing condition is quantity take-off from sketches, a sketch-tooling question, not a backbone one.

**Data migration.** `email_messages` (64), `sms_messages` (181), `portal_messages` (29) and the 76 phone transcripts (in `capture_events` today, in `captures` after the P5 fold) **(live)** are backfilled into `threads`/`messages` keyed to claims by the existing matchers; the old tables stay as provider logs (`sms_messages` remains the SMS log). `photo_shares` and `contact_sessions` keep serving the portal gateway.

**Unblocks.** Capability 5 (communications and per-carrier learning), capability 3 (unforeseen scope), capability 6 (collections).

**Owner verifies.** A rejection reason recorded on Monday changes Tuesday's draft for that carrier (visible in the injected rules and in `ai_usage.prompt_version`); a reminder proposed at 11 pm goes out at 7 am, and so does a crew text unless the owner has taken the exemption option; a staged job with a confirmed hidden-damage observation yields a supplement proposal within a day whose lines cite the observation and photo ids; a confirmed receipt appears in QBO as a purchase with the division's class.

**Rollback.** Drafting ops are propose-only; disabling a queue kind stops the lane; `learned_rules` are individually deactivable rows; the `qbo.purchase_create` channel is a flag.

**What people feel.** Crews: nothing. Office: replies arrive drafted with the claim context filled in; collections stop being a morning task; supplements arrive proposed with their evidence attached. Adjusters: faster, more consistent submittals.

**Ceiling.** Learning is per (carrier, adjuster, code) and is one `GROUP BY` away from a report; a second provider or a model change is a route row.

### 7.9 P7 — Scheduling proposals and MCP (weeks 45–56, L)

**Goal.** The schedule engine becomes a server authority that proposes, crew leads approve their own crews, and Claude Code becomes a first-class propose-only operator.

**What changes.**

- *`drying_predictions` — the method [A-3.3] left open.* Computed by `packages/domain/detect/drying.predict.ts` as a queue job after each projection of a job in `drying`: for every `moisture_locations` row with readings on ≥3 distinct dates, a per-location trend — linear, or exponential where the residual is lower — through the last readings toward `dry_goal_pct` gives the date it crosses the goal; a flat or rising slope yields `null` with `basis.reason = 'not_drying'` (which `dryingwatch` already flags); confidence comes from the residual, floored for three points. The map-level prediction is the latest location date; the job-level prediction is the latest map date plus one calendar day for the verification visit. Locations with fewer than three readings fall back to the IICRC class defaults carried by `dry_standards` — a `typical_days` column seeded from the S500 class ranges and owner-editable [A-7.12] — at a low fixed confidence, so the scheduler never sees an empty value. `basis jsonb` stores the points, slope and residual used, so a proposal can say "predicted dry Tue from 5 readings, −1.8 %/day". Ceiling: a regression per location per projection is microseconds; the forcing condition is GPP-based drying-rate modelling, which changes the formula, not the table.
- *What changes in `schedule.js` — stated, since "runs in the worker over …" is not a design.* The engine today (`apps/board/js/schedule.js`, 887 lines, pure, already consumed by four runtimes and imported from `packages/domain/engines` since P3.0) takes phases with `durationDays`/`estimatedHours`, crew bodies from `crewIds`, one `hoursPerDay`, holidays and a work calendar (`schedule.js:14`, `:58-62`) and produces a forward pass over dependencies. P7 adds four **constraints** and one **objective term** beside the pass, each a pure module with fixture tests next to the existing `apps/board/test/schedule.test.mjs` cases: (1) **skill/cert filters** — a phase may be assigned only a crew whose members cover `phase_skill_requirements` with unexpired `certifications`, a hard filter on candidate crews; (2) **per-person capacity** — `availability_patterns`/`availability_exceptions` replace `hoursPerDay` and the out-days per member, so crew capacity is a per-day sum; (3) **equipment reservations** — `equipment_reservations` are a capacity per unit type per day, so a mitigation phase that needs four dehumidifiers cannot start while the fleet is placed elsewhere; (4) **drying** — a phase whose predecessor is drying takes `drying_predictions.predicted_dry_on` as that predecessor's finish; and the objective is the existing earliest-finish plus a **drive-time penalty** from the geocode — `jobs.lat/lng` and `locations` in distance buckets first (same side of town, cross-town, out of town) [A-0.7] — that discourages splitting one crew across two far jobs in a day. The engine stays deterministic, so `schedule.propose`'s output for a fixed input is itself a fixture.
- *Proposals and approval.* `schedule.propose` files **one proposal row per schedule run** with the assignment set as its params, and `schedule.apply` lands the set atomically — `op_proposal_approve` never applies a subset of a row. The scheduler runs per crew by default, so the row is approvable by that crew's lead (`crew_lead` approves their own crew's assignments); a run that spans crews — the company-wide week the owner asks for — is approved by office.
- *Crew texts.* The daily crew texts read `contact_prefs` and skip `digestOptOut`, `appOptOut` and `paperTimesheet` members — the paper-timesheet crew member is never texted a schedule — and they obey the 7 am–8 pm Alaska window by default like every other kind; the crew-line exemption is the owner option of 04, carried as a per-kind setting when taken (§7.8).
- *MCP.* The worker serves MCP over **streamable HTTP only**, on an internal port behind Fly's proxy, with the tool list the registry generates; Claude Code on the owner's laptop connects to that endpoint with a minted `api_tokens` bearer — directly, or through a 10-line local stdio shim that forwards to it — never a stdio server "in the worker". A session acts as the minting human for reads and is capped at propose for every write; the audit row says "proposed via mcp by <person>".
- *Phone agent.* It imports `packages/*` instead of `COPY`ing `personas.ts` and `schedule.js` (`services/phone-agent/Dockerfile:8-9`) and stays its own Fly app.

**Data migration.** None new; `crewIds`/`dayCrew` on tiles were retired in P5. The three copies of `schedule.js` (board, brief, Docker image) have been one import since P3.0; this phase deletes the last `COPY` lines. `schedule_assignments` is first written by `schedule.propose` here.

**Unblocks.** Capability 4 (AI-proposed employee and job scheduling) and Claude Code as an operator.

**Owner verifies.** A proposed week is approved in one tap and matches the crew texts; a crew lead can approve only a run whose assignment set is their crew's, and a cross-crew run waits for office; an MCP session as the owner — over the HTTP endpoint with a minted token, from Claude Code directly or through the local shim — can list jobs and file a proposal but cannot approve it, and the audit row reads "proposed via mcp by Branden"; the crew member on paper receives no text; a crew text proposed at 9 pm goes out at 7 am unless the exemption option is set; a drying phase's successor moves when a new reading changes `predicted_dry_on`; `ls services/phone-agent` shows no `COPY` of shared files.

**Rollback.** The board keeps rendering the stored schedule; `schedule.propose` and the prediction job are queue kinds that can be disabled; MCP tokens are revocable rows.

**What people feel.** Crews: the daily text matches the board and stops changing under them; crew leads get an approval, not a phone call. Office: the week is proposed, not built. Owner: Claude Code reads the company and proposes work from a terminal.

**Ceiling.** The engine is O(phases × crews × days) and fine for dozens of crews; the forcing conditions are a travel matrix beyond distance buckets [A-0.7] and a request for optimisation across crews (a solver, not a pass). MCP and Agent SDK sessions are per-connection stateful, so a second worker machine needs sticky routing — `fly scale count 2` is true for the queue poller only.

### 7.10 Data migration across the phases: who owns each blob section, and when

| Blob section (live key) | Owner today | Phase | Mechanism | Owner after |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `photos[]` (729) | blob; `field_photos` trigger projection nobody reads | P3 | worker projection; trigger retired after 30 zero-diff days | blob (capture); rows read by office and agents |
| `moistureMaps[]`, `dryingLogs[]`, `constructionLogs[]`, `workAuth`, `contents[]`/`boxes[]`, sketches, `certDrying` | blob | P3 | projection into `moisture_*`, `drying_days`, daily logs; blob wins | blob (capture) |
| `rooms[]` (string list) | blob, union by value | P3 | additive `roomIds[]`; `rooms[]` kept as the derived list; `min_field_build` armed first | blob, id-keyed; rows resolve aliases |
| equipment counts (`readings[].dehu/am/scrub`, `equipment[]`) | blob | P3 | new scans → `equipment_placements` via the field outbox; counts still projected into `drying_days` | rows for scanned units; blob for counts |
| `invoices[]` (9), `reconEstimates[]` (4), `changeOrders[]` (2) | blob, office-edited only | P4 | import-on-open → `documents`/`line_items`; field read-only; `officeDocs` summary written back under one-owner-per-key | rows |
| `drawSchedule`, `lender`, `contractAmount` (8 construction jobs) | blob | P4 [A-7.6] | import → `sov_schedules`/`sov_lines`/`draw_requests` | rows |
| `receipts[]` (0 live) | blob | P3 (`captures` envelope) / P6 (QBO purchase) | `captures` row (`kind='ocr_receipt'`) plus photo; `extract.receipt` online; confirm into `receipts`/`materials`; a confirmed receipt becomes a QBO purchase through the outbox in P6 | rows |
| `claimNo`, `carrier`, `adjuster`, `waterCategory/Class`, `dateOfLoss` | blob → `unified_jobs` text | P3 | projected into `jobs`/`claims`; office edits go to rows and are written back to the blob header under one-owner-per-key | rows (office) |
| `narrative`, `portalShare`, `smsLog`, `photoShares` | blob | P4 / P6 | narrative becomes a document kind; portal share and SMS log become rows in P6 | rows |
| `coordination_jobs.data` (stage, dates, `subtasks`, `deps`, `crewIds`, `dayCrew`, `leadLog`) | board blob, rev-guarded | P5 (P7 for assignments) | projection into `jobs`/`job_phases`/`schedule_assignments`/`leads`; blob frozen after 14 silent days | rows |
| `crew_members.data` | last-write-wins blob | P3 | columns; mandatory `contact_id`, nullable `principal_id` bound by email where present (7 of 11) and owner-confirmed for the rest; `contact_prefs` | rows |
| `time_entries.data` | JSON, QB Time writer | P2 (index) / P3 (columns) | unique `qbTimesheetId`; columns backfilled with `source qbtime\|manual\|board\|paper`; writer dual-writes until readers move | columns |
| `unified_jobs` | table, all `status='new'` | P3 | renamed in place to `jobs` (ids kept, nothing copied); a compatibility view named `unified_jobs` until every reader moves | view, then dropped |
| `capture_events` | ledger, envelope and rate state at once | P3 (read-only) / P5 (fold) | `captures` takes the envelope role from P3; `web_sessions` took the rate state in P1; rows folded into `captures` + `events` in P5, then dropped | `captures` + `events` |
| `pending_actions` | table, 3 zombie rows | P1 | compat view over `proposals`; dropped at P2 after 14 days with no new rows | `proposals` |

Two rules apply to every row of that table. Capture-owned sections are never edited in rows while the blob is their write model — an office feature that must edit one is the forcing condition for moving that section. And the worker never writes a whole blob: it owns one key at a time with the rev it just read, so the Aug 6 stale-device clobber cannot recur through it.

### 7.11 Side-by-side periods and cutover criteria per strangled surface

| Surface | Old path | New path | Side by side from | Cutover criterion | Then |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Approvals | `pending_actions` + SMS "YES n" | `proposals` + inbox (+ SMS handled synchronously in `roybal-webhooks`) | P1 | brief and qb-time write `proposals`; 14 days with no new `pending_actions` rows | drop `pending_actions` (P2) |
| Cron lanes | pg_cron → `net.http_post` | pg_cron → `jobs_queue` insert → worker | P1–P2, per job | the queue kind has run 14 days with `integration_health` (over `integration_runs`) green | unschedule the HTTP job; delete its Vault wrapper — except the 5-minute heartbeat check, the one HTTP job that stays |
| Integration health | `integration_health_v0` over token rows (E0) | `integration_runs` written by every adapter call + the `integration_health` view | P1 | every lane's adapter writes `integration_runs` | drop the interim view |
| Photo projection | SQL trigger | worker `job.project` | P3 | 30 zero-diff days | drop the trigger and the nightly repair cron |
| Field capture rows | blob only | blob + projection | P3 | 14 zero-diff days per section before any reader uses it | readers move; blob unchanged |
| Ingest ledger | `capture_events` | `captures` (envelope) + `events` (facts) | P3 | `capture_events` read-only from P3; fold asserted at P5 with count parity | drop `capture_events` |
| Money documents | blob editors + `window.print()` | `documents` + pipeline PDF | P4, per kind | golden diff passes; no open job uses the old editor for that kind; every live instance imported | field editor read-only; browser print of the same HTML stays for offline |
| QBO push | `qboInvoiceId` in the blob, a fresh invoice when absent (`qbo-proxy/index.ts:345-352`) | outbox keyed on document version | P4 | one invoice per version under retry tests | old action deleted |
| Office tabs | `apps/admin` and `apps/board` routes on the field sync replica | routes of `apps/office` on the ops client | P5, per tab | two weeks of daily use; 14 days of zero old-route traffic | delete the old tab; the last tab ends the office replica |
| Board | 20 s three-table poll, client CAS | changes feed + `schedule.apply` | P5 | board writes zero dates for 14 days; every write via ops | revoke direct PATCH on `coordination_jobs`; freeze the blob |
| Edge functions | 14 `{action}` dispatchers | `roybal-ops` + four trust-level functions | P1–P5, per action | an action has run only via `roybal-ops` for 14 days | delete the branch; delete the function and its `config.toml` pin when empty |
| Phone agent | own Fly app with Docker `COPY` | own Fly app importing `packages/*` | P7 | its tests pass on the imported modules | delete the `COPY` lines |

### 7.12 The compute-tier upgrade trigger, with the load arithmetic behind it

The live tier is a 1 GB Micro with two disk-IO incidents (Jul 24, Aug 17), and the plan adds `events`, `jobs_queue` polling, `outbox`, `integration_runs`, projections and later `messages` to it. Micro → Small is a P3 step, not a P1 one, and it fires on a metric, not a date: `events + capture_events` over 1M rows, p95 `push_project` over 400 ms after projections land, or a third disk-IO alert, whichever comes first.

**The arithmetic (§2.2 carries the full table).** The projection is the first real load: at ~300 active jobs, a 45 s sync cadence would yield on the order of 500k projection jobs a day if every push enqueued one, so `job.project` is debounced per job to at most one run per minute — still ≈430k rows a day in the worst case — which is why `jobs_queue` is partition-free but indexed on `(status, run_after)` and purged daily: `job.project` done rows drop at the next purge (≤24 h), every other kind keeps a 14-day window [A-5.8]. Semantic events at 100+ people run ~15–20k a day, so `events` reaches low millions within about twelve months of that headcount — month partitioning is therefore a **P5** item (§7.7), not "later", and it comes earlier if the compute upgrade fires first; it is still deliberately absent from P1 because it is an operational lift a solo owner has not done yet. Retention keeps the rest honest: `outbox` rows purge after 90 days (`sms_messages` stays the SMS log); `capture_events` `emailPull` rows after 30 days from E0, the table read-only after P3 and folded into `captures` + `events` in P5; `ai_usage`, `agent_runs`, `integration_runs` and `events` are kept.

### 7.13 First shipped automation

**The settled choice.** Two things ship in sequence and both are on the roadmap by name. The *backbone smoke test* (P1, week 6) is not new automation: it migrates the two lanes whose execute legs already exist — the overdue-invoice reminder and the adjuster-email draft — onto `proposals` and the `outbox`, and it writes the first `submittals` rows. The *first shipped automation* (P2, week 10) is **mitigation billing reconciliation v0**: `billing.reconcile` at `packages/domain/detect/billing.reconcile.ts`, deterministic and zero-LLM, filing one `invoice.review_gaps` proposal per job.

**The operation.** `billing.reconcile` is a worker queue kind (`runtime:"worker"`, action type `read`) scheduled nightly and runnable on demand. It runs per mitigation job whose board stage is `drying` or `final`, or that is closed with an invoice not yet `sent`/`paid`; stage comes from `coordination_jobs.data.stage` **(live)** until the `jobs` spine lands in P3 [A-7.10], because `unified_jobs.status` is never advanced — the spine writer sets no status (`apps/field/js/spine.js:23-40`) and all 33 rows read `new` **(live)**. Its output is a proposal of operation `invoice.review_gaps` (action type `money`, approval `office`, amount field = the sum of priced deltas) under the idempotency key `invoice.review_gaps:<jobId>:<invoiceId>:<hash of findings>`, so the same findings never file twice and changed findings supersede the open row.

**Inputs it reads today.**

- Per-day equipment counts: `dryingLogs[].readings[].dehu/am/scrub` on dated psychrometric rows (`apps/field/js/model.js:353-361`; live sample `dehu:"2", am:"20", scrub:"1"`), summed over dated rows into unit-days by class; `dryingLogs[].equipment[]` rows add per-unit days when `placed`/`removed` are filled (`model.js:350-352`; the derivation at `apps/field/js/narrative.js:57-79`).
- Invoice lines: `invoices[].items[] {qty, desc, unit, price}` (live sample), classified by the same regexes that classify equipment types today (`apps/field/js/dryingcalc.js:152-163`), because no line carries a code (`model.js:420-422`) — the AI's `code` and `basis` are dropped at `apps/field/js/forms.js:1484-1488`.
- Labor: `time_entries.data.hours` per `fieldProjectId`/`jobId` as written by the QB Time pull (`supabase/functions/qb-time-proxy/index.ts:215-239`; 1,193 rows **(live)**), against Σ qty of lines with `unit='HR'`.
- Monitoring visits: dated rows per `moistureMaps[].readings[]` (`narrative.js:42-55`), against monitoring-visit lines.
- Category: `waterCategory` (present on all 15 live jobs) drives the Cat-3 package assertions that exist today only as prompt prose (`supabase/functions/roybal-ai-office/index.ts:539-541`: containment, negative air / HEPA scrubber, floor protection, HEPA vacuuming, antimicrobial, PPE consumables).
- Photos, as hints: `photos[].ai.equipment[]` (air movers, LGR dehumidifier, air scrubber, heater — the tag the photo tagger already writes, `roybal-ai-office/index.ts:248`, stored at `apps/field/js/officeai.js:127`) and `ai.materials[]`, which are unconsumed today. Two deterministic checks need no model: (a) a class that appears in a `during`-stage photo's `ai.equipment[]` on a date with no count for that class in the drying log is flagged `photographed_not_logged` with the photo id — a hint that the log is short, never a count; (b) a photo whose `ai.materials[]` names a class no line bills — antimicrobial, containment, HEPA — is flagged `documented_not_billed` with the photo id. A room named on a line with zero photos at any stage is flagged `no_photo_evidence`, the single most common adjuster pushback on a mitigation invoice. All three land in `findings[]` with `evidence_refs` and no price; when `photos.line_item_id` exists (P4) they become joins.
- Sent state: `submittals` rows written since P1 and, for older invoices, outbound `email_messages` rows — "was this invoice already sent" is an input the detector needs and the datum capabilities 1, 3 and 5 are blocked on.

**The proposal it files and the evidence it attaches.** One row per job: `{jobId, invoiceId, findings:[{class, documented, billed, delta, unit, suggested_desc, rate_source, evidence_refs}], undocumented_days:[…], sent:{submittal_id|null}}`. Evidence is ids and dates the office can open: drying-log row ids and dates per class, `time_entries` ids and hours, moisture-map ids and reading dates, photo ids with their tags, the Cat-3 checklist with the `waterCategory` field, the submittal or email row. A priced delta uses the rate already on that invoice for the same class (the live sample: $32.25 per air-mover-day, $85.05 per dehumidifier-day, $106 per scrubber-day) and is left unpriced when no such line exists — never from `price_list` [A-7.11]. Execution of an approved proposal appends the flagged lines to the blob's office-owned `invoices[]` through the key-owner write-back until P4, then writes `line_items` rows with `justification` and `evidence_refs`, without changing the proposal's contract.

**Its limits, stated on every proposal.** It is an internal leak detector, not carrier-grade justification. Typed count strings are not evidence a carrier accepts, and a photo tag is a hint, not a count; per-unit identity does not exist until scans (P3); materials and consumables are never captured; receipts are 0 rows; units are inconsistent on the invoices themselves (the live sample bills air movers in `EA` and scrubbers in `DA`). Jobs whose drying logs have rows on only some drying days — the live sample has 07-31 and 08-03 only — are flagged "undocumented days" between `dryoutStart` and `dryoutFinish` (`model.js:345`), not "unbilled": the live invoice bills 215 air-mover-days against a log of 20 per day, and the honest finding is that the log cannot support the quantity in either direction, which is exactly what an adjuster would say.

**How it upgrades when placements exist.** The contract is fixed; the inputs get better. At P3, documented unit-days come from `equipment_placements` (asset, room, placed/removed, photo) wherever scans were used and from `drying_days` elsewhere; monitoring visits come from `moisture_readings` by location and date; the photo hints become joins on `photos.room_id` and `placement_id`. At P4, execution writes `line_items` rows and the `judgment.line_justification` task class — routed to `claude-opus-5` with adaptive thinking by the seed routes (§2.7; Decision record §10) — drafts the per-line justification text stamped with a prompt version, with `retrieve_rules` injecting carrier rules once P6 has recorded any. That is the point where the same job stops being a leak detector and becomes the justification input capability 1 needs.

**Why the alternatives come second.**

- *Collections.* It needs Gmail alive and QBO payments trusted; the Gmail lane was dead five days with cron reporting success **(live)**, and it exercises no domain model. Its execute leg — the reminder lane — rides P1 as the smoke test, so nothing is lost by not calling it the first automation; `collections.schedule` proper lands at P6 on the outbox with quiet hours in one place.
- *Scheduling.* The engine is pure and tested, but its inputs stop at bodies × one shift: no skills, certs, availability patterns, equipment inventory or travel exist — `crew_members` carries `role` as free text and one `bioCerts` string **(live)**. A schedule proposed before P3 would be proposed from nothing.
- *Adjuster-email drafting.* It already exists (9 live calls) and its send leg exists server-side, which is exactly why it is the P1 smoke test — but it creates no new capability, so it is not the first automation; its lasting contribution is the `submittals` row.
- *Packet QA.* The completeness gate already runs in-app (29 pure rules in `apps/field/js/completeness.js`); a server copy proves nothing new and touches no cash.
- *Agent-drafted invoice lines with LLM justification first.* Without placements and photos the justification would be prose over count strings; it lands at P4 on top of the detector.

**Why not as a browser chip.** Built as chip number eighteen it would be the seventh propose/execute pattern, executed in a tab, with a discarded basis and a best-effort audit stamp. Built as the first queue kind on the backbone it is the shape every later automation reuses, and it costs nothing per run.

### 7.14 The two paths not taken

**Evolve in place as the primary path.** It would have added the same contract tables and a thin operations function over the existing blobs, fixed auth table by table across 48 policy sets, and kept both merge engines. Nobody would have felt anything, and the security and health fixes would have landed in weeks — the same weeks E0 takes here. But every new operation would have stayed blob-shaped and browser-executed: the JS↔SQL merge twin becomes permanent instead of retiring one section at a time, nothing forces a crew, division or claim entity into existence, the office replica and the 20 s poll stay, and the north star's unit of work — one line, one reading, one placement, one submittal — remains unrepresentable. The repo's own history is the argument: four eras were each added beside the last, and by month twelve the company would have more features at the same distance from an AI-operated back office, with more to port when the backbone finally arrived. It lost because the things that must change — the blob as the record, execution in tabs, email-string permissions — are things a company of 100 cannot run on, and every month on that path raises the cost of leaving it.

**Clean-sheet rewrite with migration.** Data migration is genuinely cheap — 15 jobs, 743 kB, 748 photos, 1,193 time entries, 40 contacts is an afternoon of scripts. Behaviour migration is not: 22 form renderers whose conventions carriers pay on, a 638-line sync engine with four incidents of tuition (the Aug 6 stale-device clobber among them), `print.css` rules learned from adjuster pushback, a service-worker update path that finally works, the board, the admin, the portal, the phone agent and fourteen functions, all rebuilt to parity before a single crew could switch — 8 to 14 months of a solo owner running two products, then a big-bang cutover for iPads on job-site LTE. It would have made nothing possible sooner, because the backbone tables are additive on both paths, and the parts a rewrite discards are the best parts of the tree. The last rewrite here — React era 0 to static era 1 — left about twenty dead tables, two dead functions and a lockfile of ghosts. It lost because the crews already trust this field app, the sync engine is the best module in the repo, and the strangler captures the rewrite's one real advantage — a clean typed model — with new tables at a fraction of the cost, precisely because the live data is small.

---

## 8. Guardrails against re-fragmentation

Four eras were added beside each other and none was retired (`docs/architecture/00-SYSTEM-INVENTORY.md` §1.3). The rules that kept the product running live in 103 file-header comments and in a memory file outside the tree; the root has no `CLAUDE.md`, no lint, no typecheck, and one workflow that deploys without testing (`.github/workflows/deploy-field.yml:12-19`). This section is the package that stops era five: a `CLAUDE.md` to commit, the lint/typecheck/CI rules with their file names, the import boundaries, the PR checklist, five runbooks, and the migration discipline including the one-time squash and history repair. Every rule names the file that breaks it on `main@1e04694`. The package is additive and lands in the order the phases need it (§8.2, last paragraph; §1.8): `CLAUDE.md`, the PR template, the runbooks, the test gate and the migration discipline at E0–P1; Biome, `checkJs`, the invariants, the drift guards and the generated build at **P3.0 "toolchain"**, the first sub-step of P3 (§7.5; ADR-14) — never P1, which is the contract tables, ~5 SQL operations, the `roybal-ops` shell, the worker skeleton, `submittals` and the smoke test, effort M (Decision record §15). Each **Today** note is deleted when its seam closes. Package names throughout are the one vocabulary fixed in §1.1 — `packages/{domain,db,documents,ui,integrations,ai}`, `apps/{field,office,portal,site}`, `services/{worker,phone-agent}` — and every Biome scope, folder rule and import boundary below keys on those paths and no others.

**Ceiling of the package as a whole:** holds to 100+ people and to any number of Claude Code sessions working in parallel, because every rule is a check a machine runs, not a convention a person remembers. What forces change: a second implementation language (a second registry and a second lint) or a second repository (the invariants assume one tree).

### 8.1 CLAUDE.md

Commit verbatim at the repo root. `.claude/` is gitignored (`.gitignore:18`); the root is not, and there is no `CLAUDE.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md` or `AGENTS.md` there today. It is written for the target layout; the **Today** lines describe `main` and are removed as each seam closes. The block already carries the reconciled decisions the completeness pass asked for: §1.1's package vocabulary, `COMMIT` for the SHA constant (ADR-14), quiet hours as a flat window with the crew-line exemption an owner option (§2.4), `model_prices` seeded from the provider price sheet (Decision record §10), the worker-liveness alert as the one HTTP call pg_cron keeps (§2.5), and SMS approval as a synchronous call from `roybal-webhooks` (§2.4).

````markdown
# Roybal Construction — CLAUDE.md

Read this first. `docs/architecture/00-SYSTEM-INVENTORY.md` is the audited
map of what exists; `docs/architecture/03-TARGET-ARCHITECTURE-AND-ROADMAP.md`
is where it is going; this file is the rules. Lines marked **Today:** describe
`main` as it is and are deleted when the seam they describe closes.

## What this is

One product for a Fairbanks restoration/mitigation + general contractor, and
the backbone that lets agents run its back office: an agent reads state and
proposes, a human approves, the system executes and records.

Surfaces: **field** (`apps/field`, offline-first PWA; IndexedDB is the working
copy, `field_projects.data` is one JSONB blob per job, `push_project` is the
only door), **office** (`apps/office` from P5 — the CRM home: Approvals inbox,
Leads, Claims, Documents, Board, Reports; until then `apps/admin` at `/admin`
and `apps/board` at `/board` are its source and keep publishing), **portal**
(`apps/portal`, Vercel; customers and adjusters reach only the `roybal-portal`
gateway), **site** (`apps/site`, Astro, Cloudflare Pages).

Runtimes, by what runs where:
- **Postgres** (Supabase `djpgvcvhvgrzgaziruze`) holds the contract:
  `proposals`, `events`, `jobs_queue`, `outbox`, `agents`, `agent_authority`,
  `agent_runs`, `role_permissions`, `approval_policies`, `operation_catalog`,
  `model_routes`, `model_prices`, `connections`, `integration_runs`,
  `worker_heartbeats`. Every short, DB-only operation is a SECURITY DEFINER
  function `op_<name>` that validates, checks permission, writes and emits its
  event in ONE transaction. pg_cron only inserts `jobs_queue` rows — never
  HTTP — with ONE documented exception: every 5 minutes a SQL check reads
  `worker_heartbeats`, and when the newest row is older than 10 minutes it
  `net.http_post`s `roybal-webhooks /alert` (service-secret header read from
  Vault), which texts the owner synchronously through the Twilio adapter. That
  alert never rides the outbox, because the worker is the outbox's deliverer.
- **Five edge functions**, grouped by who may call them: `roybal-ops`
  (authenticated app: assistant turns — the 2-round tool cap stays, comment at
  `roybal-ai-office/index.ts:1527`, loop guard at `:1586` — OAuth exchange,
  credential probes), `roybal-webhooks` (signed Twilio inbound + status
  callback + `/alert`; the owner's `YES n` / `NO n` is handled HERE,
  synchronously — the function may call exactly two SQL ops,
  `op_proposal_approve` and `op_proposal_decline`, as the principal its
  sender's number resolves to, allowlisted approvers only, so approval never
  waits for the worker), `roybal-web-agent` and `roybal-lead` (anonymous www,
  self-protecting), `roybal-portal` (share tokens / `contact_sessions`).
- **`roybal-worker`** (Node 22 on Fly, its own app, ≥1 GB): queue poller,
  outbox deliverer, 30 s heartbeat, agent loops (Claude Agent SDK), the MCP
  endpoint — streamable HTTP ONLY; Claude Code on a laptop connects with a
  minted `api_tokens` bearer, directly or through a 10-line local stdio shim
  that forwards to that endpoint; there is no stdio server in the worker —
  PDF render in an isolated child process, integration pulls, blob→row
  projection. Holds nothing that matters in memory.
- **`roybal-phone`** (Node 22 on Fly, 256 MB): the Twilio ConversationRelay
  socket. Never co-hosted with the worker — a packet render must never be
  able to drop a live call.

**Today:** 14 edge functions, no worker; pg_cron calls HTTP with the cron
secret and the publishable key as literals inside `cron.job.command`, and
`cron.job_run_details` reports "succeeded" for lanes that have been dead for
days because `net.http_post` is asynchronous. The five-function shape lands
across P1–P2.

## One app line

There is ONE app line, on `main`. `apps/web`, `apps/mobile` and
`packages/shared` (React/Expo) were deleted 2026-08-13 (PR #117). Never
recreate them; never "fix" their `extraneous` entries in `package-lock.json`.

**Today:** admin and board import 14 field modules by relative URL
(`../../js/core.js`, `supa.js`, `config.js` … — 58 import lines;
`apps/admin/js/admin.js:8-11`, `apps/board/js/data.js:13-14`) and the field
app imports the board's engine back (`apps/field/js/myweekcalc.js:17-18`).
This resolves only because the deploy lays the three directories out at `/`,
`/admin`, `/board` (`.github/workflows/deploy-field.yml:36-38`). Until
`packages/*` and esbuild land (P3.0 "toolchain", the first sub-step of P3 —
not P1): no 59th `../../` import; do not move or rename the 14 shared field
modules; keep `apps/board/js/schedule.js` pure ESM with no imports (it is
Docker-COPY'd into the phone agent, `services/phone-agent/Dockerfile:8-9`).

## Layout (target; the tree converges on it from P3.0 — these names and no others)

```
packages/domain/          pure, runtime-neutral: no DOM, no fetch, no Deno, no process
  engines/                the verbatim calc modules with their tests: schedule, schedulewatch, myweekcalc, fincalc,
                          dryingcalc, completeness, dryingwatch, buildwatch, phasematch, emailmatch, approve, payments, guards …
  schemas/                JSON Schema for every op input/output, blob key, table row; model.js factories beside them
  vocab/                  stages, job types, materials, channels, lead-log kinds, loss types, SETTINGS_ROW_ID, COMPANY
  policies/               quietHours (7 am–8 pm AK for EVERY kind by default), canNotify (opt-outs), spend caps, approval defaults
  ops/                    THE operation registry: <area>.<verb>.ts (+ <area>.<verb>.sql when runtime:"sql")
  extract/                extractProject(blob) → rows (the projection); toUnifiedRow
  detect/                 billing.reconcile.ts · scope.compare.ts · drying.predict.ts · lead.screen.ts
  ports.ts                the interfaces a worker/edge handler receives (render, llm, outbox, vendors) — declared here, implemented below
packages/db/              generated Supabase types + THE one data client (forBrowser / forFunction / forService): auth + refresh-on-401,
                          typed reads, ops.invoke/propose, the field app's IndexedDB/offline adapter (the outbox store), log
packages/documents/       models/<kind>.ts (buildDocumentModel, pure) · templates/<kind>.ts · renderHtml · letterhead · print.css
                          (its ONLY home; imports tokens from packages/ui) · print.js (the one window.print()) · __golden__/
packages/ui/              tokens.css (ONE navy), app.css, h(), formkit editor helpers, app chrome, emblem
packages/integrations/    adapters behind interfaces + fakes: twilio, gmail, qbo, qbtime, deepgram; llm/client.ts is the ONLY file
                          that names a model id; price_sources; every call, pull or push, writes an integration_runs row
packages/ai/              routing policy loader (model_routes/model_prices), prompts/<key>.v<N>.md (immutable once merged),
                          evals/<task_class>/, personas + per-persona tool allowlists, metering helpers (ai_reserve/ai_settle),
                          tool-definition generation from the ops registry (Anthropic tools[], MCP list)
apps/{field,office,portal}/   renderers + routing only; bundled by esbuild from packages/* (admin and board are office's source until P5)
apps/site/                Astro marketing site; joins CI as a build check
services/worker/          roybal-worker (Fly)      services/phone-agent/   roybal-phone (Fly; imports packages/* via the workspace at P7)
supabase/functions/       the five functions; each ≤150 lines: resolve principal → call an op → respond;
                          _shared/ hand-written in P1, built by esbuild from db + domain + ai from P3.0
supabase/migrations/      0000_baseline + forward-only timestamped files; replays from zero in CI
supabase/migrations_archive/   the 70 journal files, read-only history
supabase/cron_register.sql   run by CI after every deploy; SQL-only jobs_queue inserts + the ONE worker-liveness net.http_post; no secrets
supabase/tests/           pgTAP (RLS on, no using(true), search_path, permission matrix, one InitPlan for crew reads)
tools/                    build.mjs, serve.mjs, invariants/*.test.mjs, drift.test.mjs, ops-catalog.mjs, current-defs.mjs
```

The rule that survives renames: **engines and operations sit one hop below
everything that has a UI or a network; nothing imports upward.** A handler
that needs a renderer, a vendor or a model receives it as a port
(`packages/domain/ports.ts`), injected by the worker lane or by `roybal-ops`.

## Rules (non-negotiable — each is a CI check; see tools/invariants)

1. **Every write is an operation.** A write = `defineOp` in
   `packages/domain/ops/<area>.<verb>.ts`: `name@version`, `actionType`
   (read|capture|job|money|comms|external|schedule|admin), JSON Schema in and
   out (`additionalProperties:false`), `runtime` (sql|edge|worker), an
   idempotency key computed from the input, an approval policy, `emits[]`.
   Short DB-only ops run as `op_<name>` SQL functions. No PATCH/POST from a
   click handler; no direct table INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE from any app.
   **Today's violators:** the board's client CAS
   (`apps/board/js/data.js:110-132`), the field's board writes
   (`apps/field/js/boardpush.js:302-322`), admin finance chips writing
   IndexedDB (`apps/admin/js/finactions.js:109`), `qbo-proxy`'s service-role
   PATCH around the merge (`supabase/functions/qbo-proxy/index.ts:429-444`).
   Exception, kept on purpose: `push_project` is the field blob's door and is
   registered in the catalog as `job.push_blob`; its body never changes.
2. **One data-access pattern.** Reads and writes reach Supabase only through
   `packages/db` — the one client with its three factories and, in the field
   bundle, the IndexedDB/offline adapter whose `outbox` store holds non-blob
   ops until the next successful pull (P3). No `/rest/v1`, `/rpc/`,
   `/storage/v1`, `/auth/v1` or `/functions/v1` literal anywhere else.
   **Today:** `rest(` at 51 field / 25 admin / 6 board sites; raw `fetch` with
   no 401 retry in `apps/field/js/narrative.js:391`, `officeai.js:41`,
   `voice.js:58`; 38 `rest/v1` sites in
   `supabase/functions/roybal-portal/index.ts`; supabase-js in 4 functions
   and raw fetch in 10.
3. **Every state change emits an event** through `emit_event()` in the same
   transaction; `events` is append-only for every role. Blob pushes emit NO
   semantic events (`blob_history` is the pre-image). `captures` (kind
   `voice|photo_ai|ocr_meter|ocr_receipt|barcode|serial|signature|email_pull`)
   is the ingest envelope from P3 on; `capture_events` is the legacy ledger —
   read-only after P3, folded into `captures` + `events` in P5. **Today:**
   `capture_events` and `ai_usage` are deletable by any crew login
   (`supabase/migrations/201_ai_usage.sql:78-79`); the chip audit is a
   best-effort client PATCH (`apps/field/js/assist.js:211-223`).
4. **Every external side effect is an outbox row** with a unique idempotency
   key, delivered by the worker; the provider's callback settles it; every
   adapter call — a pull run from `jobs_queue`, a push run from the outbox, a
   webhook — ends by writing an `integration_runs` row. The one send that is
   not an outbox row is the worker-liveness text above, because it must not
   depend on the worker it reports on. **Today:** `sendSms`, `sendEmail` and
   `pushInvoice` have no key (`roybal-notify/index.ts:334-368`,
   `gmail-proxy/index.ts:340-349`, `qbo-proxy/index.ts:345-356`); 174 of 175
   SMS rows are frozen `queued`.
5. **Agents propose; humans approve; the system executes.** A write op is
   exposed to any model only as `propose_<name>`. MCP sessions are
   propose-only whatever the human's role. For money/comms/external the
   approver is never the proposer. Auto-approval records
   `approved_by_kind='policy'` and the policy row id. The owner's SMS `YES n`
   is `op_proposal_approve` called synchronously by `roybal-webhooks`, so
   approval never waits for the worker; only a `runtime:"worker"` execution
   does. **Today:** 14 of 17 chip kinds execute in a browser tab
   (`apps/field/js/assist.js:179-193`); approval is one phone number
   (`roybal-notify/index.ts:400-401`); three zombie `pending_actions` rows
   have blocked new proposals since 2026-07-26.
6. **No business logic in renderers.** Totals ladders, O&P, 7-day equipment
   flags, dry standards, stage transitions and quiet hours live in
   `packages/domain`; document numbers come from `packages/documents/models`
   over those engines. **Today:** three totals ladders
   (`apps/field/js/fincalc.js:27-38`, `formkit.js:240-248`,
   `supabase/functions/roybal-brief/digest.ts:16-24`); the equipment flag ×3
   (`forms.js:634`, `forms.js:695`, `dryingwatch.js:71-75`); `invoice()`
   binds, pushes to QBO and paints print nodes in one closure
   (`forms.js:1378-1912`).
7. **Copies are forbidden.** No second copy of `schedule.js` (×3: board,
   `roybal-brief`, Docker COPY), of the merge rules (`merge.js` ↔
   `merge_project_blobs`; `ID_COLLECTIONS` in five places), of price tables
   (`LLM_PRICES` ×5 + `roybal-web-agent/guards.ts:254-256`), of `zip.js`
   (field ↔ portal), of CORS / `x-cron-secret` blocks (×11 / ×6), of the
   stage list (×9 files), of the settings uuid (×7 files), of quiet hours
   (`America/Anchorage` ×9 files, hard-coded window at
   `apps/admin/js/campaigns.js:39`). Needed in two places ⇒ it goes in a
   package. Until a copy is deleted (the price tables at P1, the module
   copies at P3.0, the phone agent's at P7), `tools/drift.test.mjs` guards
   it byte-for-byte in CI. The one twin kept on purpose is
   `apps/field/js/merge.js` ↔ `merge_project_blobs` (Decision record §6);
   `merge.js` never moves and the parity corpus is its guard.
8. **The blob changes additively only.** A new blob key lands in
   `ID_COLLECTIONS` (`apps/field/js/merge.js:32-36`) and in `id_cols` of a
   new `merge_project_blobs` migration (`243_losstypes_merge_union.sql:16-19`)
   in the SAME PR; CI parses the SQL and compares. `rooms[]` is never
   rewritten (add `roomIds`). Office-owned entities — claims, carriers,
   policies, submittals, documents, line items, estimates/invoices/COs,
   schedule, contacts — are born in tables and never written into the blob.
   The worker writes back only the one key it owns (`officeDocs`), with the
   rev it just read, and retries once on `merged`.
9. **AI is routed, never named.** Task classes map to models in
   `model_routes` (FK to `model_prices`); `model_prices` is seeded from the
   provider price sheet at deploy time, never from a number typed into
   source or into this document. The route loader, the versioned prompts
   (`packages/ai/prompts/<key>.v<N>.md`, immutable once merged, id in
   `ai_usage`), the metering helpers around `ai_reserve`/`ai_settle` and the
   tool-definition generator live in `packages/ai`; a model id literal
   appears only in the SDK adapter `packages/integrations/llm/client.ts` and
   in the seed rows. `judgment.*` (per-line justification included) routes
   to `claude-opus-5`; `draft.*`, `chat.*`, `extract.*` to `claude-sonnet-5`;
   `classify.*` to `claude-sonnet-5` at low effort, batch-eligible (Haiku 4.5 only where an eval justifies it for a bulk backfill). Every call passes
   through `ai_reserve`/`ai_settle`. No forced `tool_choice: {type:"tool"}` —
   Fable 5.1 rejects it; use `tool_choice: auto` with `strict:true` schemas
   or `output_config.format`. **Today:** ten env knobs with hard-coded
   defaults across seven files (`roybal-ai-office/index.ts:63-68`); four
   forced-tool sites (`roybal-ai-office/index.ts:148` inside `forcedTool()`,
   called 15×; `roybal-ai-ingest/index.ts:411`; `qb-time-proxy/aiphase.ts:123`;
   `roybal-portal/index.ts:665`); `guards.ts:254-256` prices two models
   wrongly.
10. **Vocabularies are data.** Stages, types, materials, channels, lead-log
    kinds and loss types come from `packages/domain/vocab`. **Today:** the
    phone agent writes `type:"mitigation"`
    (`services/phone-agent/tools.mjs:131`), which is not in the board's
    list (`apps/board/js/board.js:30-38`) and renders as "Other"
    (`board.js:127`).
11. **No silent catch.** A `catch` rethrows, returns a typed failure, or
    logs through `packages/db/log`. **Today:** 26 empty `catch {}` blocks in
    `apps/field/js` and 5 in `apps/board/js` (59 counting comment-only
    bodies).
12. **File headers stay.** Every non-test source file opens with the
    `/* ====== */` WHY-block: what it is, what it must not do, who depends on
    it. 103 of 103 today; keep it 100%.
13. **Migrations are forward-only, timestamped, applied by CI.** Never edit
    an applied file; never paste into the SQL editor; never MCP
    `apply_migration` for schema; no secret, key or `net.http_post` in a
    migration; no `auth.email()` in a policy; no `using(true)` for
    `authenticated` on a new table. Cron lives only in
    `supabase/cron_register.sql`, which carries exactly one `net.http_post`
    (the worker-liveness check, header from Vault) and no literal secret.
    Procedure: `docs/architecture/MIGRATIONS.md`.
14. **Quiet hours and opt-outs are one definition.** Every human-facing send —
    customer or crew; text, email, push, AI call, schedule digest — runs
    07:00–20:00 America/Anchorage from
    `packages/domain/policies/quietHours` (the `SMS_QUIET_START/END` secrets
    are NOT set in production; the code default rules). No kind is assumed
    exempt: the brief states the window as a flat constraint, so the
    exemption for crew-line kinds (`crewSchedule`, `onOurWay`, `crewLine`)
    is an OWNER OPTION, listed as a confirmation item in 04, and once taken
    it is a per-kind setting in `role_permissions.conditions` /
    `agent_authority.conditions` (a `notification_policies` row per division
    if the owner wants it that fine) — never a constant. Every crew-facing
    send also goes through `canNotify(member, kind)`, which honors
    `crew_members.contact_prefs` (`digestOptOut`, `appOptOut`,
    `paperTimesheet`) and treats a member with no login (`principal_id` null) as
    opted out of every app lane. Leonard Sheldon works off-app. This is a
    test, not a comment. **Today:** `digestOptOut` is honored only at
    `roybal-brief/crewdigest.ts:149`; the window is implemented three ways
    (`roybal-notify/index.ts:217-232`, `qb-time-proxy`,
    `apps/admin/js/campaigns.js:36-39`).
15. **Xactimate / Verisk.** `price_list` (2,959 rows) is frozen behind
    `price_sources`. Do not add rows, build pricing engines on it, or expose
    it through the portal or any tool. ESX files are encrypted — work from
    PDFs. Xactimate-native submission is a human step recorded on the
    submittal (`channel`, `format='xactimate'`, `external_ref`).

## Checks and tests

- `npm run check` = Biome + `tsc --noEmit` (checkJs over apps, strict TS over
  packages and services) + `deno check` over the five functions + the
  invariants suite (`tools/invariants/*.test.mjs`) + drift guards.
- `npm test` = `node --test` over `apps/**`, `packages/**`,
  `supabase/functions/**`, `services/**` — one runner, no `&&` chain.
  **Today:** `package.json:11` and `apps/field/package.json:10` chain with
  `&&` and stop at file 2; `main` is red (`apps/field/js/config.js:25` v165
  vs `apps/field/sw.js:6` v166, `apps/field/test/build.test.mjs:17`); the
  phone agent's 13 tests and three field files are unwired.
- Engines: fixture and property tests. Ops: input validates, ≥1 event
  emitted, idempotent on replay, refused for a principal without the
  capability. Renderers: jsdom smoke. Documents: golden HTML. SQL: replay
  from the baseline + pgTAP assertions in `supabase/tests/`.
- Both must be green before a PR is mergeable; branch protection enforces it.

## Deploy

| What | How | Gotcha |
|---|---|---|
| field `/`, office (`/admin`, `/board` until P5) | `ci.yml` on merge → `tools/build.mjs` → GitHub Pages | `BUILD`, `COMMIT` and the SW precache manifest are generated at P3.0; never hand-edit, never commit them. **Today:** hand-bumped; the assembly step of `deploy-field.yml` survives as the publish job, with the test gate in front of it from E0 |
| five edge functions | `ci.yml` → `supabase functions deploy` from `config.toml` | **MCP/dashboard deploys ignore `config.toml`** — see the verify_jwt trap |
| migrations | `ci.yml`: staging `db push` → tests → prod `db push` | never the SQL editor, never MCP `apply_migration` |
| cron | `supabase/cron_register.sql`, run by CI after `db push` | SQL-only `jobs_queue` inserts plus the one worker-liveness `net.http_post`; no secrets in it |
| `roybal-worker` | `ci.yml` → `fly deploy --config services/worker/fly.toml` | one machine, one region; render runs in a child process with its own memory cap; MCP over streamable HTTP only |
| `roybal-phone` | manual `fly deploy --config services/phone-agent/fly.toml --dockerfile services/phone-agent/Dockerfile .` from the repo root | own app, 256 MB, in-memory rate limits (`tools.mjs:19-27`) — never scale to 2 |
| portal | Vercel project **`roybal-restoration-app-web`** | do not delete; it IS portal.roybalconstruction.com |
| site | `npm run site:build`, then `npx wrangler pages deploy apps/site/dist --project-name roybal-site-pages --branch main` | manual; root `wrangler.jsonc` describes an unused Worker |

## The verify_jwt trap

`supabase functions deploy` defaults `verify_jwt` to TRUE. The anonymous and
webhook functions (`roybal-lead`, `roybal-web-agent`, `roybal-webhooks`,
`roybal-portal`; today also `roybal-voice`, `roybal-notify`, `roybal-ai-*`)
MUST stay `false` and authenticate in code. The pins live in
`supabase/config.toml`; on 2026-08-13 an MCP bulk deploy flipped two of them
and killed the www quote form for a day (`config.toml:1-21`). After ANY
deploy not done by CI, run
`supabase functions list --project-ref djpgvcvhvgrzgaziruze -o json` and diff
against `config.toml`. CI's `functions-config` job does this on every PR that
touches `supabase/functions/**` or `config.toml`.

## Build tag

`BUILD` is a MONOTONIC INTEGER, never a SHA. `_sync_guard` strips non-digits
and compares the reported build to `min_field_build` as an `int`
(`caller_build := regexp_replace(p_build, '\D', '', 'g')::int`,
`218_sync_rpcs_and_build_gate.sql:79-84`; redefined identically at
`226_sync_client_telemetry.sql:57-59`), so a SHA would compare as garbage
digits or parse to `null` and bypass the gate; and the service worker's cache
name must carry the same number or a tablet parked on a form runs old JS
under a new cache key (`config.js:19-24`). `tools/build.mjs` (P3.0) generates
both from git — `BUILD = "v" + git rev-list --count HEAD` (489 at `1e04694`;
monotonic on `main` because merges only add commits; the digits are what the
gate compares) plus a separate `COMMIT` constant with the short SHA, for
display and telemetry — and emits the SW precache manifest from the bundle
graph. `config.generated.js` is a build output, never committed. **Today:**
five hand-bumped constants (`config.js:25`, `sw.js:6`,
`apps/board/index.html:16,53`, `app.js:577`), one guard, never run in CI
(E0 fixes v165/v166 by hand and puts `build.test.mjs` behind the PR gate);
the precache omits `photoshare.js` and `calibration.js` and includes the
deprecated `pricing.js` (`sw.js:8-32`).

## Other standing decisions

- Brand: navy `#0f1b2d` + orange `#f26a21`. The `#16395a` in
  field/board/admin/portal CSS is drift; fix toward `#0f1b2d`, never back.
- Company name is Roybal Construction LLC; "Roybal Restoration" strings are
  legacy (`apps/admin/js/admin.js:2`, `apps/field/package.json:5`).
- Photo deletes propagate only via `deletedIds` tombstones; a "missing
  photos" complaint is NOT fixed by sign-out/sign-in.
- Board settings sync through the reserved row
  `00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000001` — import `SETTINGS_ROW_ID`.
- `unified_jobs` → `jobs` at P3 is an IN-PLACE RENAME (`alter table … rename`)
  plus a read-only compatibility VIEW named `unified_jobs`, created in the
  same transaction and kept until every reader has moved (phone agent, brief,
  portal, field `spine.js`, old PWA builds) — never a copy; its one writer
  (`apps/field/js/spine.js:23-40`) moves to `job.sync_spine` in the same PR.
- The legacy JWT keys are dead; `UNAUTHORIZED_INVALID_JWT_FORMAT` means a
  stored legacy token.
- The worker executes every op as one of the named `agents` principals
  seeded in P1 — `agent:billing`, `agent:projection`, `agent:outbox`,
  `agent:brief`, `agent:phone`, `agent:web` — under `agent_authority`,
  deny-by-default, propose-only, choosing the principal per queue kind from
  a table in `services/worker`, never a default. Password-grant machine
  users (`phone-agent@`, `office-brief@`) remain only until the phone agent
  imports packages (P7).
- Crew read scope is never in the JWT: the claim carries `principal_id`,
  `role`, `division_id`, `org_id`; crew-facing policies call
  `assigned_job_ids()` in initplan form plus the 24 h walk-on rule (§2.4).
````

### 8.2 Lint, typecheck and CI

The repo has zero lint, zero typecheck and a deploy-only workflow (`00-SYSTEM-INVENTORY.md` §1.2). The build decision is esbuild bundling per app from `packages/*` with Biome for lint and format; the no-build import-map variant lost because it leaves the two most recent production defects in place — the hand-bumped tag drift that shipped red in PR #181 and the hand-maintained precache manifest (`apps/field/sw.js:8-32`). **When:** the test gate and the lockstep test at E0; the migration replay, the catalog diff and the deploy chain at P1; the bundler, the generated `BUILD`/`COMMIT`, the generated service worker, `checkJs`, Biome, the import boundaries, the drift guards and the verbatim package moves at **P3.0 "toolchain"** (§1.7, §1.8, ADR-14) — P1 carries none of them (Decision record §15). Everything below is written against paths, so it can be authored before the `packages/` move and keeps working when files migrate; the scopes name the §1.1 vocabulary only.

**Toolchain (five tools, all pinned exactly in the root `package.json`).**

| Tool | Command | What it covers | Why not something else |
|---|---|---|---|
| esbuild | `node tools/build.mjs` | one bundle per app (field; office — admin and board until P5; portal) from `packages/*`; generates `BUILD` (the integer) and `COMMIT` (the SHA) into `config.generated.js` and the SW precache manifest from the bundle graph; 200 ms | the hand-bumped tag (`config.js:25` vs `sw.js:6`) and the hand-listed precache are the last two production defects |
| TypeScript | `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` | `checkJs` + JSDoc over `apps/*/js/**`, strict TS over `packages/**` and `services/**` | `tsconfig.base.json:1-14` declares `strict`, `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`, `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` and has zero consumers; 69 client files carry 0 `@ts-check` |
| Deno | `deno check supabase/functions/*/index.ts` | the five functions under `supabase/functions/deno.json` (`strict`, one import map: `std` pinned to one version, `supabase-js` exact) | 26 `.ts` files are executed only via `node --experimental-strip-types`, which strips and never checks; `std@0.168.0` ×11 vs `0.177.0` ×3; `supabase-js@2` major-only ×4; `deno.lock` has no consumer |
| Biome | `biome check .` | format + the built-in rules below | one binary, one `biome.json`; no ESLint plugin tree to maintain |
| node --test | `node --test` over four globs | every suite in one runner | `package.json:11` and `apps/field/package.json:10` stop at file 2 on the first `&&` failure |

Type-check rules: every source file gets `// @ts-check` under its header in one bulk PR (P3.0); that PR may sprinkle `/** @type {any} */` with a `// TODO(types)` marker, and `tools/invariants/todo-types.test.mjs` asserts the count only decreases. JSDoc typedefs for blob keys live in `packages/domain/schemas/` (today the "schema" is the union of `model.js` factories, `merge.js` and 80 observed live keys). `packages/db/types.gen.ts` is produced by `supabase gen types typescript --local` against the replayed baseline in CI and must match the committed file — the schema-drift detector (no generated types exist anywhere today, inventory §9). `personas.ts` moves to `packages/ai/` (its 17 prose `ACTION_DEFS` become op schemas in P1, the first five with the smoke test; the file itself moves at P3.0) and is checked once by `tsc` instead of being strip-imported by Node and Deno separately.

**Biome (`biome.json`).** Built-in rules only, each tied to a violation that exists today [A-8.1]:

| Rule | Scope (`overrides`) | Breaks today |
|---|---|---|
| `suspicious/noEmptyBlockStatements` | everywhere | 26 empty `catch` blocks in `apps/field/js`, 5 in `apps/board/js` — counted as catch blocks whose body is empty or comment-only, on multiple lines as well as one (`catch {}` alone greps 8 and 5); 39 in the field app if `.catch(() => {})` swallowers are included. The allowlist is generated once by `tools/empty-catches.mjs` so the number is reproducible |
| `style/noRestrictedGlobals`: `fetch` | everywhere except `packages/db/**`, `packages/integrations/**`, `apps/field/js/supa.js` (until it moves into `packages/db` at P3.0) | raw `fetch` in `narrative.js:391`, `officeai.js:41`, `voice.js:58`; 10 functions hand-roll PostgREST |
| `style/noRestrictedGlobals`: `document`, `window`, `localStorage`, `indexedDB`, `Deno`, `process` | `packages/domain/**`, `packages/documents/**` (except `packages/documents/print.js`, the one `window.print()`), `packages/ai/**` | `h()` needs `document` (`apps/field/js/core.js:8-9`); nothing under `packages/` exists yet — the rule is what keeps `packages/domain` pure once it does |
| `style/noRestrictedImports`: `../../js/*`, `../../board/*` | `apps/**` | 58 import lines across admin, board and `myweekcalc.js:17-18` |
| `style/noRestrictedImports`: `**/packages/db/client*`, `**/integrations/**`, `**/packages/ai/**` | `apps/*/js/screens/**`, `apps/*/js/forms*`, `packages/documents/**` (not `apps/**` — screens may not reach the DB directly, but each app's data layer imports `packages/db` by design, §8.3) | renderers call ops, never the DB, a vendor or a model (`forms.js:3336-3910` issues eleven network calls from a renderer); a template reads a `DocumentModel` and nothing else |
| `style/noRestrictedImports`: `**/apps/**`, `**/integrations/**`, `**/db/**`, `**/documents/**`, `**/ai/**` | `packages/domain/**` | domain imports nothing above it; a handler reaches a renderer, a vendor or a model only through `ports.ts` |
| `style/noRestrictedImports`: `**/apps/**` | `packages/**`, `services/**`, `supabase/functions/**` | nothing imports an app |
| formatter: 2-space, 100 cols, double quotes | everywhere | matches the existing code; format-on-PR is a check, not an auto-commit |

**Invariants (`tools/invariants/*.test.mjs`).** Biome cannot match string literals or SQL, so the pattern rules run as `node --test` source-grep checks — the technique `supabase/functions/roybal-web-agent/guards.test.mjs:318-321` already uses to assert `index.ts` touches no `/rest/v1` path. One file per rule; each prints the offending `path:line`; each carries an allowlist of today's violators that may only shrink. The suite lands at P3.0 with today's violators allowlisted, except `migration-hygiene` and `webhooks-op-allowlist`, which are required from P1 because the baseline and `roybal-webhooks` are their first inputs.

| Test | Asserts | Breaks today |
|---|---|---|
| `no-raw-supabase-url` | no `/rest/v1`, `/rpc/`, `/storage/v1`, `/auth/v1`, `/functions/v1` literal outside `packages/db`, `packages/integrations` | `supa.js:74-86` (allowlisted until it moves), `roybal-portal/index.ts` ×38, three raw-fetch field files |
| `no-rest-in-apps` | no `rest(` call outside `packages/db` | field 51, admin 25, board 6 |
| `write-is-operation` | `.insert(`/`.update(`/`.upsert(`/`.delete(` on the DB client, or `method: "PATCH"|"POST"|"DELETE"` against `rest/v1`, only inside `packages/domain/ops/**` handlers, `packages/integrations/**` (outbox delivery) and migrations | `data.js:110-132`, `boardpush.js:302-322`, `finactions.js:109`, `qbo-proxy/index.ts:429-444` |
| `no-model-id-literal` | `claude-`, `nova-`, `aura-` only in `packages/integrations/llm/client.ts`, `packages/integrations/deepgram/client.ts` and the `model_routes`/`model_prices` rows of `supabase/seed.sql` | 8 files (`roybal-ai-office`, `-ingest`, `-narrative`, `-portal`, `qb-time-proxy`, `roybal-web-agent` ×2, `services/phone-agent/config.mjs`) |
| `no-forced-tool-choice` | `tool_choice: { type: "tool"` nowhere; `tool_choice` literal only in `llm/client.ts` | the four sites in rule 9 |
| `no-price-table` | no per-MTok price literal anywhere in source: `supabase/seed.sql` seeds `model_routes` and the `model_prices` keys (model id, provider, context, thinking mode, forced-tool flag); the per-MTok columns are filled from the provider price sheet by the deploy step (Decision record §10), never typed into the repo | `LLM_PRICES` ×5, `guards.ts:254-256` |
| `no-settings-uuid-literal` | `00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000001` only in `packages/domain/vocab` | 7 files |
| `no-stage-list-literal` | no array literal containing `"on_hold"` outside `packages/domain/vocab/stages` | 9 files |
| `no-quiet-hours-math` | `America/Anchorage` and `>= 7 && … < 20` only in `packages/domain/policies/quietHours` and one date formatter; no `kind` exemption in code — a per-kind exemption is a `role_permissions.conditions` / `agent_authority.conditions` / `notification_policies` row | 9 files + `campaigns.js:39` |
| `no-print-outside-pipeline` | `window.print()` only in `packages/documents/print.js` | `apps/field/js/app.js:1552,1625,1936,2400,2413,2458` |
| `blob-key-registered` | every key a `model.js` factory writes is in `ID_COLLECTIONS`, `FORM_SLOTS` or the scalar list, AND in the `id_cols`/`form_slots` arrays of the LAST migration defining `merge_project_blobs` (parsed by regex) | `merge.test.mjs:224-226` checks JS against JS only; the SQL twin is unguarded |
| `migration-hygiene` | in any migration newer than the baseline: no `net.http_post`, no `sb_publishable_`/`cron_`/`__CRON_SECRET__`/`__ANON_KEY__` literal, no `auth.email()` inside `create policy`, no `using (true)` for `authenticated`, `set search_path` on every function, one `create or replace function` per name per file; in `supabase/cron_register.sql`: no literal secret and exactly one `net.http_post`, in the `worker-liveness` job, its header read from `vault.decrypted_secrets` | `202/206/207/209/211` placeholders, `233:45-51`/`236`/`244` scrapes, 26 email fences in `204`/`205`, 18 `using(true)` tables, `merge_project_blobs` defined in 217/241/243 |
| `webhooks-op-allowlist` | `supabase/functions/roybal-webhooks/**` names no `op_*` function other than `op_proposal_approve` and `op_proposal_decline`, and no `rpc/` path other than those two and `emit_event`/`enqueue` — the test §2.4 requires ("no other op is reachable from the function") | the function does not exist yet; `roybal-notify/index.ts:400-443` is its source |
| `header-comment` | first token of every non-test source file is a `/* ===` block of ≥3 lines | 103/103 today; keeps it so |
| `no-cross-app-file` | no second `config.js` with the Supabase URL; one `tools/serve.mjs --app <name>`; no header containing "VERBATIM COPY" or "keep in sync" past its `removeBy` | `apps/portal/js/config.js:5-6`, four `serve.mjs`, `apps/portal/js/zip.js:3-5`, 47 "keep in sync" comments |
| `op-registry` | every `defineOp` has a description, schemas with `additionalProperties:false`, an idempotency function, ≥1 `emits` for non-read types, a test file beside it, and a `role_permissions` seed row or explicit deny; every `comms` op whose audience can be crew calls `canNotify` and passes `quietHours` | the 17 prose `ACTION_DEFS` (`personas.ts:246-362`) have none of these |

**Drift guards (`tools/drift.test.mjs`).** One file, byte-equality per entry, each printing the `cp` that fixes it and carrying a `removeBy` PR reference; an entry whose guarded path no longer exists fails the job so dead guards are deleted, not forgotten. Entries: `apps/board/js/schedule.js` ↔ `supabase/functions/roybal-brief/schedule.js` (exists at `crewdigest.test.mjs:18-23`, moves here; gone at P3.0 when both import `packages/domain/engines/schedule`); the Docker `COPY` lines in `services/phone-agent/Dockerfile:8-9` point at the canonical paths (gone at P7); `apps/field/js/zip.js` ↔ `apps/portal/js/zip.js` ignoring the header (gone at P3.0); `LLM_PRICES` across five files (gone at P1 when `model_prices` lands and `guards.ts:254-256` reads it); the quiet-hours constants across notify, qb-time, voice, campaigns (gone at P3.0 when the functions import `policies/quietHours` through `_shared/`); `postbuild.mjs:57-65` ↔ `resolveEndpoint()` in `apps/site/src/data/site.ts`.

**CI: `.github/workflows/ci.yml`** (on `pull_request`, and on push to `main`). All jobs are required checks from the day each lands; branch protection on `main` requires them and blocks direct pushes [A-8.2].

| Job | Runs | Trigger |
|---|---|---|
| `check` | `npm ci`, `biome check`, `tsc --noEmit`, `deno check`, `npm run invariants`, `npm run drift` | always (P3.0; `migration-hygiene` and `webhooks-op-allowlist` alone from P1) |
| `test` | `node --test "apps/**/*.test.mjs" "packages/**/*.test.{mjs,ts}" "supabase/functions/**/*.test.mjs" "services/**/*.test.mjs"` — one runner, no `&&` chain, and no suite excluded: it picks up `services/phone-agent`'s 13 tests (unwired today — the root `package.json:11` and `apps/field/package.json:10` chains never reach them), the two unwired field tests, the edge functions' own suites, and it moves `apps/field/js/calibration.test.mjs` (deployed to the public origin today) into `test/` | always (E0) |
| `build` | `tools/build.mjs` per app; `tools/build.test.mjs` asserts the generated `BUILD` equals the generated SW cache tag, the manifest equals the bundle graph, and — when anything under `apps/{field,admin,board,office}` changed — the number is strictly greater than `origin/main`'s (a rebuild of the same commit yields the same `BUILD`) | always (P3.0; until then the E0 lockstep test asserts the hand-set pair) |
| `migrate` | `supabase start` on the runner [A-8.3], `supabase db reset` on the baseline + forward files, `supabase test db` (pgTAP in `supabase/tests/`: RLS on every table, no `using(true)` for `authenticated` outside the shrinking allowlist, `search_path` set on all functions, every `operation_catalog` op has a `role_permissions` row or an explicit deny, `push_project` still resolves `_sync_guard(text)`, and — once `job_assignments` exists (P3) — `explain` of a crew read of `photos` shows one InitPlan over `assigned_job_ids()` and no nested loop), `supabase gen types` diff, then `tools/merge-parity.test.mjs` — the 2,033-case randomized corpus (recovered from PR history first) run through `merge_project_blobs` via psql and compared to `apps/field/js/merge.js`. Two replays, not one: the PR replay is the runner's local stack, and on merge the same baseline + forward files are applied to the **seeded staging project** (§8.6 step 7 — a second Supabase project loaded from `pg_dump`, **never Supabase branching**, which would replay the journal that cannot replay and bills per branch-hour, ADR-13 option (b)) before any file reaches production | when `supabase/**`, `apps/field/js/merge*`, or `packages/domain/ops/**/*.sql` changed; still required (P1) |
