| `ops-catalog` | `tools/ops-catalog.mjs` emits `operation_catalog` rows, Anthropic tools, MCP list, typed client, route tables, `role_permissions` seeds and `docs/PERMISSIONS.md`; fails on any uncommitted diff, on a `.sql` body whose hash has no migration, and — on `main` — on a deployed `op_*` signature that differs from the catalog | when `packages/domain/ops/**` changed (P1) |
| `documents` | golden HTML diff + page count per document kind against `packages/documents/__golden__/` | when `packages/documents/**` changed (P4) |
| `functions-config` | parse `supabase/config.toml`; `supabase functions list -o json` with a read-only token [A-8.3]; fail on any `verify_jwt` mismatch | when `supabase/functions/**` or `config.toml` changed (E0) |
| `site` | `npm run site:build` (already runs `postbuild.mjs` + `check-parity.mjs`), plus `astro check` once `@astrojs/check` is installed (`apps/site/package.json:11` names it; it is not installed) | when `apps/site/**` changed |

On merge to `main` the `deploy` job runs, in this order, stopping at the first failure: staging `supabase db push` against the seeded staging project (§8.6 step 7; never branching) → `cron_register.sql` on staging → `npm test` against staging → prod `supabase db push` → `cron_register.sql` on prod → `supabase functions deploy` for the five functions from `config.toml` (never MCP) → `ops-catalog` publish → bundles to GitHub Pages with `test/`, READMEs, `package.json`, `serve.mjs` excluded → `fly deploy` the worker → record `app_settings.latest_field_build`. A weekly `jobs_queue` row proposes raising `min_field_build` to latest−2 — a low-risk early proposal kind. `deploy-field.yml` is retired into `ci.yml` in three steps: E0 puts the test gate in front of its assembly step (`:35-38`, kept as the publish job) and drops the trigger on the deleted branch (`:13`); P1 adds the migration and deploy chain; P3.0 replaces the directory copy with `dist/` from `build.mjs`.

**Ceilings.** Biome + `tsc --checkJs` + source-grep invariants hold to 100+ people and to any number of concurrent agent sessions; what forces change is a second language (a second lint) — do not add one. One `ci.yml` with two environments holds to 100+; a second region or a second Supabase project adds per-environment config, nothing structural. The `migrate` job's runner-local stack takes ~3 minutes; if CI wall-clock becomes the constraint, the replay moves to the staging project (serial, no Docker) — a config change. **Option left to the owner (lost in the build decision, recorded here):** no-build import maps + JSDoc/`checkJs` + these same invariants — zero toolchain, but the tag lockstep and the precache stay human duties, which is exactly what failed in PR #181.

### 8.3 Folder rules and import boundaries

Layers, top to bottom, in the §1.1 vocabulary. An arrow means "may import"; anything not listed is forbidden and enforced by the Biome `overrides` and the `no-cross-app-file` invariant.

```
apps/*                    ──►  packages/documents, packages/domain (engines, vocab, policies, schemas, ops client stubs), packages/ui
apps/*                    ──►  packages/db (reads + auth session + the offline adapter; writes only by calling an op)
services/worker           ──►  packages/domain, packages/documents, packages/integrations, packages/ai, packages/db
services/phone-agent      ──►  packages/domain, packages/db            (P7; Docker COPY until then)
supabase/functions/*      ──►  packages/domain, packages/integrations, packages/ai, packages/db   (as _shared/ — hand-written in P1, built by esbuild from P3.0)
packages/ai               ──►  packages/domain (ops registry, schemas), packages/db (model_routes, ai_usage), packages/integrations (llm)
packages/documents        ──►  packages/domain (engines, vocab), packages/ui (tokens.css — print.css imports it)
packages/integrations     ──►  packages/domain (types, policies, ports), packages/db (ledger, outbox, integration_runs rows)
packages/ui               ──►  packages/domain (vocab)
packages/domain/ops       ──►  packages/domain/{engines,schemas,vocab,policies,extract,detect,ports}
packages/domain/engines   ──►  nothing (pure; Date and Math only)
packages/db               ──►  packages/domain (schemas, vocab), the generated types and the platform client
```

`packages/domain` never imports upward, so a handler with `runtime:"edge"|"worker"` that needs a renderer, a vendor or a model receives it as a `ctx` argument typed by `packages/domain/ports.ts` (`render`, `llm`, `outbox`, the vendor interfaces); `packages/documents`, `packages/ai` and `packages/integrations` implement those ports and the worker lane or `roybal-ops` injects them [A-8.5]. That is how `document.render` reaches `packages/documents` and an agent loop reaches `packages/ai` without a cycle, and why a handler is testable with fakes.

Where each kind of thing lives, with naming. The registry file per operation is the one the decision record fixes (Decision record §3); the rest follow it [A-8.4].

| Thing | Path | Naming and rule |
|---|---|---|
| Engine (pure computation) | `packages/domain/engines/<name>.ts` | noun: `schedule`, `schedulewatch`, `myweekcalc`, `fincalc`, `dryingcalc`, `completeness`, `dryingwatch`, `buildwatch`, `phasematch`, `emailmatch`, `approve`, `payments`, `guards` …; header says "pure — no DOM, no fetch"; moved verbatim with their tests at P3.0 (§1.10). `merge.js` is not an engine: it stays in `apps/field/js`, byte-for-byte, as the twin of `merge_project_blobs` |
| Detector | `packages/domain/detect/<area>.<verb>.ts` | `billing.reconcile.ts` (P2), `scope.compare.ts` (P6), `drying.predict.ts` (P7), `lead.screen.ts` (P5); deterministic over row inputs, `findings[]` with `evidence_refs`; the judgment half is a `ports.llm` call the worker lane supplies — never `packages/domain/billing/reconcile` |
| Operation | `packages/domain/ops/<area>.<verb>.ts` (+ `<area>.<verb>.sql` when `runtime:"sql"`) | `equipment.place.ts`, `document.issue.ts`, `proposal.approve.ts`; one `defineOp` default export; a `<area>.<verb>.test.ts` beside it; the `.sql` body is the `op_<area>_<verb>(p_principal_id uuid, p_input jsonb)` function and ships only through a generated migration `supabase/migrations/<ts>_op_<area>_<verb>.sql`; the directory exists from P1 (its five ops are new code, not a move) |
| Port | `packages/domain/ports.ts` | interfaces only: `Render`, `Llm`, `Outbox`, `Twilio`, `Gmail`, `Qbo`, `QbTime`, `Deepgram`; every implementation has a fake beside it in the implementing package |
| Schema | `packages/domain/schemas/<name>.json` | JSON Schema is the source of truth (not zod): feeds `pg_jsonschema` at `op_propose` [A-0.2], the handler, Anthropic `tools[]` with `strict:true`, and MCP verbatim; row types re-export from `packages/db/types.gen.ts`, never hand-copied |
| Vocabulary | `packages/domain/vocab/<name>.ts` | frozen arrays + label maps: `stages`, `jobTypes`, `materials`, `channels`, `leadLogKinds`, `lossTypes`, `SETTINGS_ROW_ID`, `COMPANY` |
| Policy | `packages/domain/policies/<name>.ts` | `quietHours` (07:00–20:00 AK for every kind by default; exemptions are rows, not code), `canNotify` (reads `contact_prefs`; null `crew_members.principal_id` = opted out of app lanes), `spendCaps`, `approvalDefaults`; data, tested |
| Prompt | `packages/ai/prompts/<key>.v<N>.md` | immutable once merged; mirrored to the `prompts` table by CI; `ai_usage.prompt_id/prompt_version` come from the filename |
| Eval | `packages/ai/evals/<task_class>/` | fixtures + expectations; `npm run eval -- <class>` against staging, recorded in `eval_runs` (P3, with the first prompt version); CI runs the zero-cost schema-validity subset; a full run gates activating a prompt version |
| Route loader, metering, tools | `packages/ai/{routes,metering,tools,personas}.ts` | reads `model_routes` (60 s cache) and `model_prices` through `packages/db`; `reserve()`/`settle()` around `ai_reserve`/`ai_settle`; generates Anthropic `tools[]` and the MCP list from the ops registry (read ops as-is, writes as `propose_<name>`, `strict:true`); never names a model id |
| Document model | `packages/documents/models/<kind>.ts` | `buildDocumentModel(kind, ids) → DocumentModel`; pure; every number the template shows is on the model; totals via `packages/domain/engines/fincalc`; the models are assembly over the engines and compute nothing themselves (§6.2) |
| Template + renderer | `packages/documents/templates/<kind>.ts`, `packages/documents/{render.ts,print.css,letterhead.ts,print.js}` | `renderHtml(model, template, carrierProfile)` returns a string in browser and Node; `sheet()`/`letterhead()` ported from `apps/field/js/formkit.js:290-315`; `print.css` ported nearly verbatim (`apps/field/css/print.css:6,20,25-30,82-87,111,238-259`) — this is its ONLY home, it imports `tokens.css` from `packages/ui`, and a change to it must pass the golden harness; `print.js` holds the one `window.print()` |
| Integration | `packages/integrations/<vendor>/{client,mapping,index,fake}.ts` | `twilio`, `gmail`, `qbo`, `qbtime`, `deepgram`; the only place a vendor URL or SDK appears; implements the port; every outbound call is an outbox row; every call — pull from `jobs_queue`, push from the outbox, or webhook — ends by writing an `integration_runs` row (`connection_id`, `kind pull|push|refresh|webhook`, `started_at`, `finished_at`, `ok`, `error`, `rows_affected`, `external_ref`) |
| LLM client | `packages/integrations/llm/client.ts` | the SDK adapter and the only file matching `claude-`; the official `@anthropic-ai/sdk`, never raw `fetch`; `tool_choice: auto` + strict schemas; adaptive thinking + `output_config.effort`; prefix-ordered caching; Batches API lane; the model it calls is what `packages/ai` resolved from `model_routes` |
| DB client | `packages/db/{client,auth,principal,ledger,outbox,offline,log}.ts`, `types.gen.ts` | three factories: `forBrowser(session)`, `forFunction(req)` (user JWT → `current_principal()` once), `forService(principalId)` (service role + explicit `p_principal_id`, worker and webhooks only); `offline.ts` is the field app's IndexedDB adapter and its `outbox` store (P3); refresh-on-401 lives here once instead of `supa.js:74-86` plus three raw-fetch files |
| Renderer | `apps/<app>/js/screens/<screen>.js` | `render<Screen>(container, state, actions)`; may hold view state, never domain state; header lists the ops it calls |
| Edge function | `supabase/functions/<slug>/index.ts` + `_shared/` (hand-written in P1, built from P3.0) | ≤150 lines: parse → resolve principal → call an op → respond `{ok, data}`; one CORS, one envelope, one `json()`; `roybal-webhooks` may name only `op_proposal_approve`/`op_proposal_decline` (`webhooks-op-allowlist`) |
| Worker lane | `services/worker/lanes/<lane>.ts` | `queue`, `heartbeat`, `outbox`, `agent`, `mcp` (streamable HTTP only), `render` (child process), `projection`, `integrations`; P1 ships `queue` + `outbox` + `heartbeat`; leases, reservations and sessions live in Postgres, never in a `Map`; the principal per queue kind comes from `lanes/index.ts` |
| Migration | `supabase/migrations/<YYYYMMDDHHMMSS>_<verb>_<object>.sql` | see §8.6; ops bodies are generated, never hand-written twice |
| Cron | `supabase/cron_register.sql` | `cron.schedule(...)` whose command is `insert into jobs_queue (kind, run_after, idempotency_key) …`; run by CI after every deploy; idempotent; the one exception is the `worker-liveness` job whose command is the 5-minute `worker_heartbeats` check that `net.http_post`s `roybal-webhooks /alert` with a header read from `vault.decrypted_secrets` |
| Test | beside the file, `<name>.test.{mjs,ts}`; SQL in `supabase/tests/*.sql` (pgTAP) | `node --test`; jsdom + fake-indexeddb for field screens; no hand-rolled `ok()` harness in new files (27 exist) |

Three things that are not allowed to exist: a second file holding the Supabase URL (`apps/portal/js/config.js:5-6` duplicates field's), a `serve.mjs` per app (one `tools/serve.mjs --app` replaces four), and a Docker `COPY` of source from another directory (`services/phone-agent/Dockerfile:8-9`; the workspace import replaces it at P7).

**Ceiling.** One domain package and one registry hold to 100+ people; what forces a split is a second product line with disjoint vocabulary, not headcount. The deploy-coupling cost is real and stated: an op change rebuilds the app bundles, `_shared/`, the five functions and the worker — at 200 ms per bundle and one CI run, acceptable indefinitely.

### 8.4 PR checklist

Paste into `.github/pull_request_template.md`. A box stays unchecked only with a written reason.

```
## What changed / why (2–4 lines)

## Guardrails
- [ ] `npm run check` and `npm test` are green locally (not "green except…")
- [ ] No new `fetch`, `rest(`, `/rest/v1`, `/functions/v1` outside packages/db or packages/integrations
- [ ] Every new write is a `defineOp` in packages/domain/ops with schema, actionType, idempotency, approval, emits[], and a test
- [ ] No business logic added to a renderer (apps/*/js, packages/documents templates)
- [ ] No new copy of an engine, price table, vocab list, CORS/json helper, settings uuid, quiet-hours math, or model id
- [ ] Every catch logs, throws, or returns; no `catch {}`
- [ ] Header block present on every new/renamed source file and still true on changed ones

## If apps/field, apps/admin, apps/board, or apps/office changed
- [ ] BUILD/COMMIT and the SW manifest come from `tools/build.mjs` in CI — not hand-edited, not committed (Today, before P3.0: BUILD in config.js, CACHE in sw.js, and every `?v=` bumped together; `build.test.mjs` green)
- [ ] Field screens render from IndexedDB with the network off; non-blob writes go through the field outbox (P3+) or degrade to "needs connection"

## If the job blob changed
- [ ] New key is additive; registered in ID_COLLECTIONS/FORM_SLOTS AND in a new merge_project_blobs migration in this PR
- [ ] No office-owned entity written into the blob; worker write-back touches only the key it owns

## If supabase/migrations changed
- [ ] One new timestamped file; no edits to applied files; replays from the baseline (migrate job green)
- [ ] New table: real FKs, `deleted_at`, `(actor_kind, actor_id)`, `org_id` default + `division_id` where scoped, RLS on, policies via the claims (principal_id, role, division_id, org_id) and — for crew scope — `assigned_job_ids()` in initplan form, no `using(true)` for authenticated, no `auth.email()`
- [ ] Every new function has `set search_path`; SECURITY DEFINER justified in a comment; EXECUTE revoked from anon/public where it must be
- [ ] No secret, key, cron header or `net.http_post` in the file; cron changes go in cron_register.sql (whose only `net.http_post` is the worker-liveness job)
- [ ] types.gen.ts regenerated and committed

## If packages/domain/ops changed
- [ ] `name@version` bumped if the input schema change is breaking; old handler kept while open proposals reference it
- [ ] role_permissions seed row (or explicit deny) present; docs/PERMISSIONS.md regenerated (ops-catalog job green)
- [ ] Negative test: a principal without the capability is refused; replay with the same idempotency key is a no-op

## If supabase/functions changed
- [ ] config.toml verify_jwt entry present (functions-config job green); deployed by CI, never MCP/dashboard
- [ ] Shared helpers from _shared/, not copied; function body ≤150 lines
- [ ] roybal-webhooks reaches no op beyond op_proposal_approve / op_proposal_decline (webhooks-op-allowlist green)

## If anything sends to a human or a vendor
- [ ] The send is an outbox row with an idempotency key; a callback or poll settles it; the adapter writes an integration_runs row
- [ ] Every send respects quietHours (07:00–20:00 AK; test present); a per-kind exemption is a conditions/notification_policies row, never code; crew lanes also go through canNotify (test present)

## If AI/prompt changed
- [ ] New prompt version file under packages/ai/prompts (never an edit); eval case added; task class exists in model_routes
- [ ] No model id, price, or forced tool_choice in the change; spend goes through ai_reserve/ai_settle
- [ ] Does not read or expose price_list (Verisk EULA)

## Docs
- [ ] docs/architecture, MIGRATIONS.md, or a runbook updated if a rule, path, or deploy step changed
- [ ] Memory-worthy decision noted in the PR body for the owner's memory file
```

### 8.5 How we do X — five runbooks

Each runbook is: steps, files touched, tests required, and how an agent gets access. The last item is the same in all five, so it is stated once and then only its specifics: **agents get access through the registry, never through a table.** Read ops become Anthropic and MCP tools as-is; write ops are exposed only as `propose_<name>`; a machine principal holds nothing until the owner adds an `agent_authority` row through `agent_authority.grant` (an event), and MCP tokens (`api_tokens`) are capped at propose for every write regardless of the minting human's role [A-0.3].

#### 1. New table

1. **Design the row.** Relational columns for everything queried or joined; JSONB only for the per-job blob that already exists. Real foreign keys with `on delete` behaviour — every era-1/2/3 core table has zero FKs today (`00-SYSTEM-INVENTORY.md` §6.1). One soft-delete idiom, `deleted_at timestamptz` (six coexist: `deleted bool`, `deleted_at`+`purged_at`, `merged_into`, `revoked_at`, `enabled`, JSON `archived`/`deletedIds`). One actor idiom, `(actor_kind, actor_id)` (four coexist). `uuid` job references, never `text` (`email_messages.job_id` and `pending_actions.job_id` are text). `org_id uuid not null default '<this-org>'` on spine and money tables; `division_id` on anything a second crew, calendar, mailbox or QBO class would partition. Where §3.7 fixes one definition for an entity (`signatures`, `submittals`, `captures`, `document_versions`, `time_entries.source`), that definition is the row.
2. **Migration.** `supabase migration new create_<table>`. In it: `create table`, indexes on every FK, `alter table … enable row level security`, `grant select … to authenticated` (writes are NOT granted — they arrive through `op_*`), policies written against the four JWT claims (`principal_id`, `role`, `division_id`, `org_id`) through `current_principal()` / `current_org()` in the `(select …)` initplan form and — for anything crew reads — `job_id = any ((select assigned_job_ids()))`, the `STABLE` SECURITY DEFINER helper that evaluates once per statement and carries the 24 h walk-on rows (§2.4; ADR-04): assigned job ids are never in the claim and never joined per row. A `touch_updated_at` trigger from `_shared`, and a comment line `-- matrix: <table> | owner: rwd | office: rw | crew_lead: r(assigned) | crew: r(assigned) | viewer: r | agent: r`. Ledgers (`events`, `ai_usage`, `integration_runs`, and `capture_events` until it is folded into `captures` + `events` in P5) are insert-only for everyone but the service role.
3. **Types and schema.** `npm run db:types` regenerates `packages/db/types.gen.ts`; commit it. Add `packages/domain/schemas/<table>.json`.
4. **Operations.** At minimum `<area>.create`, `<area>.update`, `<area>.delete` in `packages/domain/ops/`, each with an `actionType`, an idempotency function and `emits[]`; short ones are `runtime:"sql"` with the body beside them. Read ops (`<area>.get`, `<area>.list`, paged with `hasMore`) if an agent or screen needs them — bare `limit=N` reads already drop rows (`roybal-ai-office/index.ts:1409-1411` vs 1,193 `time_entries`). An aggregate that a tab, the brief and an agent all need is a SQL view (materialized at hundreds of jobs — `jobs_summary`, `ar_aging`, `crew_hours`, `automation_costs` in P5) behind a `read` op, so no surface keeps its own number (§7.7).
5. **Permissions.** `role_permissions` seed rows are generated from the op definitions by `ops-catalog`; the pgTAP matrix test fails until every new op has a row or an explicit deny.
6. **Projection, if the data originates in the blob.** Add the section to `packages/domain/extract/extractProject()`; rows carry `origin='blob'`, `source_ref`, `blob_rev`; `job.project_repair` rebuilds them; blob wins for capture-owned sections.
7. **Tests required.** Replay (`migrate` job); pgTAP matrix; an op test with two JWTs proving a `crew` principal cannot do what the matrix says only `office` can; for a crew-readable table, the `explain` assertion (one InitPlan, no nested loop over `job_assignments`); an idempotency replay test; a schema-validity test; a projection zero-diff test if step 6 applies.
8. **Agent access.** The read ops appear in the tool list automatically; the agent's RLS scope is whatever `current_principal()` resolves for the `agents` row; no `agent_authority` row means propose-only on the write ops; never widen a policy for a machine.

Files touched: `supabase/migrations/<ts>_create_<table>.sql`, `packages/db/types.gen.ts`, `packages/domain/schemas/<table>.json`, `packages/domain/ops/<area>.*.ts` (+ `.sql`), generated `docs/PERMISSIONS.md`, `packages/domain/extract/` if projected, tests.

#### 2. New screen

1. **Which app.** Crew-facing → `apps/field`; office (CRM, approvals, documents, reports) and scheduling → `apps/office` [A-0.10] — until P5, `apps/admin` (the CRM home, strangled tab by tab) and `apps/board` are its source; customer or adjuster → `apps/portal`. No fourth internal app.
2. **Ops first.** Write or find the operations the screen calls. If you catch yourself typing `rest(` or a PATCH in the screen file, stop: that is an operation. The five hand-rolled rev guards (`data.js:110-132`, `boardpush.js:302-320`, `roybal-notify:501-510`, `qbo-proxy:429-431`, `coordination_job_patch`) are what "just one PATCH" turns into. Every op the screen calls is a registry entry that already carries its idempotency key, its `emits[]` and its `role_permissions` row; the screen adds none of the three and may not compensate for a missing one. What the screen can show is whatever RLS grants the signed-in principal — a control that must disappear for a role is a `role_permissions` row and a policy, never a hidden button, and a read a role may not have is refused by the database even when the renderer asks.
3. **Renderer.** `apps/<app>/js/screens/<screen>.js` exporting `render<Screen>(container, state, actions)`; `h`/`clear` from `packages/ui`, labels from `packages/domain/vocab`, formatting from `packages/documents/format`. Header comment: what it shows, which ops it calls, what it must not do.
4. **Route.** The app's hash router: field `#/p/:id/…`, office/admin `#/<tab>`, board `#view=`. Nothing to add to the service worker — from P3.0 the precache manifest is generated from the bundle graph. **Today:** register statically imported modules in `sw.js` `CORE` (`photoshare.js` and `calibration.js` are missing, `sw.js:8-32`) and bump `config.js` BUILD with `sw.js` CACHE.
5. **Offline (field).** The screen renders from IndexedDB with the network off; non-blob writes go into the `packages/db` offline adapter's IndexedDB `outbox` store (P3) keyed by the op's idempotency key and drained after the next successful pull; until then a network-only action shows "needs connection" instead of a blank — today `boardpush.js:204` and `spine.js:83` silently return `{skipped:true}`.
6. **Approvals.** A chip the assistant renders is a `proposals` row; tapping it calls `proposal.approve`; a non-money chip is self-approvable by the signed-in proposer when they hold execute; `sendText` keeps its synchronous iOS tap window (`assist.js:229-236`) but records the proposal first.
7. **Tests required.** jsdom smoke render with a fixture; one interaction test per op the screen triggers, asserting the op was called with the right input (not that the DB changed); an offline render test for field screens.
8. **Agent access.** The assistant "opens" or "fills" a screen by proposing the same ops the screen calls; it never scripts the DOM. Nothing new to expose.

Files touched: `apps/<app>/js/screens/<screen>.js`, the router file, `<screen>.test.mjs`, ops if new. (**Today** also: `sw.js` CORE, `config.js` BUILD, `sw.js` CACHE.)

#### 3. New document type

1. **Model.** `packages/documents/models/<kind>.ts`: `buildDocumentModel("<kind>", ids)` → `DocumentModel`, pure; totals through `packages/domain/engines/fincalc`; dates through one formatter; every number the template shows exists on the model — templates never compute and models are assembly over the engines, computing nothing themselves (today `invoice()` computes, pushes to QBO and paints print nodes in one closure, `forms.js:1378-1912`).
2. **Template.** `packages/documents/templates/<kind>.ts`: `(model, carrierProfile) → html`, using the shared `letterhead()`/`sheet()` and the `print.css` tokens (from `packages/ui/tokens.css`), page-break classes, and the print-only div mirror for free text (bare textareas clip in print — the field-textarea rule).
3. **Register.** `packages/documents/kinds.ts`: `{kind, label, numberPrefix, signable, packetOrderDefault, portalVisible, aiDraftable}`. `document_sequences` gets a row per `(org_id, kind, year)` — numbers are never reused (today `INV-${length+1}` repeats after a delete, `apps/admin/js/finactions.js:89,137,250`). The packet builder, the portal projection, the brief and the field "Documents" list read this registry; none keep their own list.
4. **Lifecycle is generic.** `document.issue`, `document.render`, `document.supersede`, `document.void`, `document.sign` already exist; a new kind needs a new op only if it takes inputs the generic ones do not. Each of them emits its event in the same transaction (`document.issued`, `document.rendered`, `document.superseded`, `document.voided`, `document.signed`), each computes its idempotency key from the input (`document.issue:<job_id>:<kind>:<model_hash>` — never the sequence, which is allocated inside the issuing transaction (§6.6, §4 step 7) and so cannot be known by the caller, so a double tap on a slow connection issues one number), and each already carries its `role_permissions` rows — a kind only the office may issue is a separate op with its own row and its own key, never a branch inside a generic handler. Issued versions are immutable — the trigger on `document_versions` exempts only the render columns (`page_count`, `render_status queued|rendering|rendered|failed`, `rendered_at`, `html_hash`, `pdf_sha256`, `storage_path`) and only while `render_status <> 'rendered'` (§6.7); a post-issue edit is a supersede (same number, v+1); QBO follows via the outbox keyed `qbo.<kind>:<document_id>:v<n>` with an `external_intents` row before the call. `document.render` runs in the worker's render lane, which supplies the `Render` port from `packages/documents`.
5. **Carrier variants.** Branch on `carrier_profiles.format` and `packet_order`; a pushback-prone line gets its justification when `(carrier, code|category)` matches `learned_rules` or the default list — text from the `judgment.line_justification` route (`claude-opus-5`), frozen at issue. ESX/XactAnalysis output is out of scope by decision; the pipeline emits PDF + structured JSON and the `submittals` row (one per sending: `kind`, `document_version_ids[]` ordered, `packet_document_version_id`, `carrier_id`, `adjuster_contact_id`, `to_contact_id`, `channel email|portal|carrier_portal|mail|api`, `format`, `sent_at`, `sent_by`, `outbox_id`, `external_ref`, `parent_submittal_id`, `status` — §3.7, §6.9) records what the carrier required and how it was sent, a human Xactimate upload included.
6. **Print.** Browser printing of the same HTML goes through `packages/documents/print.js` — the one `window.print()`. No new `app.js:1552`-style page. PDF renders in the worker's render child process under a pinned Playwright/Chromium build, so an issued version re-renders from `model_json` + `template_version` to the same `pdf_sha256` and the `documents` bucket is rebuildable rather than backed up (§6.7).
7. **Blob.** Nothing. Documents live in tables. **Today (before P4):** a document kind still edited in the blob must be registered in `ID_COLLECTIONS` and the SQL twin, and its `invoiceNo`-style number must not come from array length.
8. **Tests required.** Model test with a fixture job (totals, empty states); golden HTML + page-count fixture in `packages/documents/__golden__/<kind>.html` (reviewed diff on change; the harness exists before the first template ships); a registry test that the packet includes it when flagged; a supersede test (same number, v2, `supersedes_version_id`); for signable kinds, a sign test (unsigned v1 → signed v2 with a `signatures` row in the §3.7 shape: `signer_kind`, `png_sha256`, `signed_model_hash`, `method`, `captured_offline`).
9. **Agent access.** If AI drafts it: a `draft.<kind>` task class in `model_routes`, a prompt file `packages/ai/prompts/draft.<kind>.v1.md`, an eval set under `packages/ai/evals/draft.<kind>/`; the agent proposes `document.issue` and never issues; every drafted line keeps its `justification` and `evidence_refs` — today the model's `basis` is rendered once and discarded (`forms.js:1484-1488` vs `:1505`).

Files touched: `packages/documents/models/<kind>.ts`, `packages/documents/templates/<kind>.ts`, `packages/documents/kinds.ts`, `packages/documents/__golden__/<kind>.html`, a migration seeding `document_sequences`, prompt + eval under `packages/ai` if drafted, tests.

#### 4. New integration

1. **Adapter.** `packages/integrations/<vendor>/client.ts` (auth, base URL, retries, 429 backoff), `mapping.ts` (vendor shape ↔ domain schema, pure), `index.ts` (the narrow verbs the domain may call — the implementation of the vendor's port in `packages/domain/ports.ts`), `fake.ts` (the fake every test uses). The only place the vendor's URL or SDK appears — today Anthropic is a raw `fetch` in seven files and every vendor likewise.
2. **Connection, not token row.** A `connections(provider, division_id, org_id, account, token_ref, token_expires_at, expected_cadence)` row replaces "latest row wins" (`qbo-proxy/index.ts:76-77`, `gmail-proxy/index.ts:110-111`, `qb-time-proxy/index.ts:107-112` all `.limit(1).single()`); `token_ref` points at Vault and token columns are `using(false)` service-role only, as `gmail_tokens`/`qbo_tokens`/`qb_time_tokens` are; refresh happens in the client; a second mailbox or QBO company is a row.
3. **Health.** Every adapter call — a pull run from `jobs_queue`, a push run from the outbox, a refresh, an inbound webhook — ends by writing an `integration_runs` row (`connection_id`, `kind pull|push|refresh|webhook`, `started_at`, `finished_at`, `ok`, `error`, `rows_affected`, `external_ref`). `integration_health` is a VIEW over `integration_runs ⋈ connections` (`last_ok_at`, `last_error`, `consecutive_failures`, `token_expires_at`, `status`) — a table populated by every call sees the pulls an outbox-joined view could not (§2.6; ADR-15). The alert rule: three consecutive failures OR no successful run within 2× the connection's expected cadence; a 15-minute SQL rule texts the owner through the outbox with a 24 h resend guard, and a token expiring within 7 days is surfaced on the admin card and in the brief. When the failing lane is the worker itself, the alert takes the worker-independent path — the pg_cron `worker_heartbeats` check that posts to `roybal-webhooks /alert` (§2.5). E0's interim is a simpler view over the three token tables and the newest successful row per lane, replaced (not evolved) when the table lands in P1. The Gmail lane was dead five days with the admin card green (`apps/admin/js/gmailconnect.js:64-68` renders "Connected" on row-exists); QB Time died with cron reporting success.
4. **Operations.** `<vendor>.<verb> (two segments, underscores inside the verb: `qbo.invoice_upsert`, `qbtime.pull`)` ops with `runtime:"worker"` and `actionType:"external"`; every outbound side effect is an `outbox` row with an idempotency key and, for calls that create remote records, an `external_intents` row before the call and a lookup-by-natural-key on retry (the QBO `DocNumber` rule); `external_refs` is the single home for the provider's ids. Each verb emits its event (`integration.pulled`, `integration.pushed`, `integration.failed`) and gets a `role_permissions` row from `ops-catalog` — the office may run a pull by hand, crew may not — and the machine principal that runs it on the queue holds propose-only until an `agent_authority` row says otherwise.
5. **Inbound.** A webhook route inside `roybal-webhooks`: HMAC verification, idempotency on the provider's message id, an `integration_runs` row of kind `webhook`, then an `events`/`jobs_queue` insert only — no business logic in the function, and no `op_*` call beyond the two SMS-approval ops that route already holds (`webhooks-op-allowlist`). A Twilio status callback is the first such route (absent today; `config.toml:53-54` claims one).
6. **Schedules.** A `cron_register.sql` entry that inserts a `jobs_queue` row; never `net.http_post`, never a header literal — the migrations `202/206/207/209/211` embed placeholders and `233/236/244` scrape the live `cron.job` row. The `worker-liveness` job is the one entry that posts, and no integration adds a second.
7. **Secrets.** Names listed in `packages/integrations/<vendor>/env.ts`; read once at boot by the worker or function and fail loudly if missing; never in `config.js`, a migration, or `cron.job.command`.
8. **Tests required.** Mapping tests on recorded (redacted) fixtures; a client test against a `node:http` fake covering 401-refresh and 429; an outbox test proving a retried delivery with the same key sends once; an `integration_runs`-row test (one row per call, `ok=false` with `error` on failure) and a view test that three failures flip `status`; a quiet-hours and `canNotify` test if it sends to people; a webhook HMAC + replay test.
9. **Agent access.** The read verbs become tools; `external` write verbs are propose-only until an `agent_authority` row with conditions (`max_amount_usd`, `kinds`, `quiet_hours`, `division_ids`) widens one — the recorded path from propose to auto that today has no mechanism.

Files touched: `packages/integrations/<vendor>/*`, `packages/domain/ports.ts` (the interface), `packages/domain/ops/integration.<vendor>.*.ts`, a migration (`connections` row, `external_refs.system` value, `external_intents` if it creates remote records), `supabase/functions/roybal-webhooks/routes/<vendor>.ts` if inbound, `supabase/cron_register.sql`, tests.

#### 5. New role or permission

1. **Vocabulary.** Roles are `owner, office, crew_lead, crew, viewer, agent` on `profiles.role`, plus the external principals `customer` (portal token / `contact_sessions`) and `adjuster` (`photo_shares` token) confined to the portal gateway. Machines are `agents` rows, never a `profiles.role` and never an email in a policy — a third machine account today inherits full `using(true)` rights because the 26 fences name `phone-agent@`/`office-brief@` (`204_phone_agent_rls.sql:12-31`, `205_brief_machine_rls.sql:16-23`), and new signups default to `tech` (`216_individual_logins.sql:71-77`). A crew member is a `crew_members` row with a mandatory `contact_id` and a nullable `principal_id` (§3.2) — only 7 of the 11 roster rows carry an email **(live)**, and the paper-timesheet member may never have a login; the one-time bind matches on email where present and the owner confirms the rest through a reviewed table in the P3 migration (§3.7).
2. **A new permission is data, not a deploy.** `role.grant(role, operation, capability)` is an owner-only op that writes `role_permissions` and emits `permission.granted` in the same transaction; its idempotency key is `(role, operation, capability)`, so re-running a grant is a no-op and emits no second event; the admin Access page shows the matrix; CI's pgTAP snapshot asserts every catalog op has a row or an explicit deny, and `docs/PERMISSIONS.md` is regenerated from the rows. Amount thresholds are `approval_policies(action_type, division_id, max_amount_usd, approver_role)` rows [A-0.3]; a per-kind quiet-hours exemption is a `conditions` value on the same rows (§2.4), never code. **Owner's option — code as matrix:** the seed rows in `packages/domain/ops` become the only source and every change is a PR; drift-free, and viable while the office is two people; at 100+ with per-person overrides the deploy per change dominates.
3. **A new role value** is the one case that needs migrations, and the sequence is fixed because `push_project` calls `_sync_guard` on every push and `_sync_guard` admits `role in ('tech','admin','office')` (`218:67-69`, `226:45-49`): migration N adds the enum value (`ALTER TYPE … ADD VALUE` cannot run inside the transaction that uses it); migration N+1, in one transaction, redefines `_sync_guard` and every other role gate (`coordination_job_patch` `246:47-52`, `contact_merge`, `contact_resolve`, `contact_mark_review_asked`, `restore_photo`, the `field_photos` policies `221:113-137`) to accept both vocabularies, remaps `profiles.role` from an explicit `(profile_id, old_role, new_role)` table reviewed in the PR — never a blanket update: the P1 migration maps `branden@roybalconstruction.com` to `owner` and the second live `admin` profile to `office` by default, and the owner confirms both in the admin UI before the role claim is armed (a confirmation item in 04) — and asserts in a `DO` block that `push_project` still resolves `_sync_guard(text)`; migration N+2 drops the old names a week later. No push fails at any instant.
4. **Claims.** The Custom Access Token hook adds exactly four claims — `principal_id`, `role`, `division_id`, `org_id`; assigned job ids are NOT in the claim. `current_principal()` reads the claims and falls back to a `profiles` lookup for old tokens; helpers are `STABLE` and use the `(select …)` initplan form; office and owner predicates stay claim-only. Crew scoping is the one lookup the claim cannot carry, so every crew-facing policy calls the `STABLE` SECURITY DEFINER helper `assigned_job_ids()` — one initplan evaluation per statement, not per row, over `job_assignments` (schedule assignments plus explicit adds) unioned with the walk-on rows: crew may read any job in their division with stage in the active set for 24 h after scanning its QR/asset label or being added by a crew lead, recorded by the self-executed op `job.claim_walk_on(job_id)`, which notifies the office — reads widen through a recorded op, never an ad-hoc policy edit (stated identically in §2.4 and ADR-04; `job_assignments` lands with crews in P3). A demotion also revokes sessions [A-0.8].
5. **Client.** `packages/db/auth.ts` exposes `currentRole()` from the claim; renderers hide what the role cannot do; ops re-check; the DB is the last word. No client has a role concept today (`grep -rn '\brole\b' apps/field/js` finds only chat-message roles).
6. **Machines and MCP.** `agent_authority.grant(agent, operation, capability, conditions, expires_at)` widens a machine; revoke is an event too. An MCP token is minted in the admin (`api_tokens`: principal, scopes, expiry, hash) and acts as the minting human for reads, propose-only for writes; the audit row says "proposed via mcp by <person>". The worker serves MCP over streamable HTTP only; Claude Code on a laptop presents that token directly or through a 10-line local stdio shim that forwards to the endpoint — there is never a stdio server in the worker.
7. **Tests required.** The pgTAP matrix snapshot; an op test per changed cell with a JWT of that role against the local stack; a renderer test that the control is absent for a forbidden role; for a new role value, the N+1 `DO` assertion and a push test with a `crew` JWT before and after; for a crew-scoped table, the `explain` assertion of one InitPlan and no nested loop.
8. **Agent access.** A new agent = an `agents` row + `agent_authority` rows; nothing else. Widening is reviewed like any permission and recorded as an event.

Files touched: none for a permission (rows via `role.grant`); for a role value: three migrations, `packages/db/auth.ts`, affected renderers and ops, `supabase/tests/permissions.sql` expectations regenerated by script, tests.

### 8.6 Migration discipline, the baseline squash and history repair

**The problem, precisely.** `supabase/migrations` is a journal, not a schema: 70 files vs 71 live entries; `005-012` and `20260713231439_price_list_ddl.sql` are one-line placeholders whose SQL was never committed; files 203–246 were applied live under timestamp versions so the CLI would treat 44 files as unapplied; six live entries have no file (five `admin_fix_cron_*` and the "237" that rotated cron headers, which exists only on an unmerged branch); `105_labor_log_billing_gate.sql:10-14` updates tables that `200` creates; `233:45-51`, `236:38-44`, `244:33-39` scrape the cron secret and keys out of the live `morning-brief` row and `raise exception` on a fresh database; `202/206/207/209/211` embed `__CRON_SECRET__`/`__ANON_KEY__` placeholders; `merge_project_blobs` is defined in three files and `push_project` in three; at least eight live tables, two buckets and the live enum values (`freeze`, 18 job statuses) have no DDL; 22 file headers say "SQL editor" and one says MCP `apply_migration`. `supabase db reset` fails at `105`. There is no way to stand up a staging database that matches production.

**The one scheme (from the squash onward; Decision record §11, ADR-13).**

- **Timestamped only:** `YYYYMMDDHHMMSS_<verb>_<object>.sql`, created with `supabase migration new`. The `NNN_` scheme ends with the squash.
- **Append-only:** a file applied anywhere (staging counts) is never edited. Fix forward.
- **Idempotent where cheap** (`create … if not exists`, `drop policy if exists` before `create policy`), but correctness is proven by replay, not idempotence.
- **One definition per function per file**, and the current body of every `op_*` function is generated from `packages/domain/ops/<area>.<verb>.sql` — `git log -S` is no longer how you find what is live; `tools/current-defs.mjs` prints the latest definition of any function for review.
- **No secrets, ever:** no literal keys, no scraping `cron.job`, no placeholders. Cron is not in migrations at all; it is `supabase/cron_register.sql`, run by CI after every deploy, whose jobs insert `jobs_queue` rows. During the transition the last HTTP job reads its header from `vault.decrypted_secrets` (the `vault` extension is installed and unused today); the one HTTP job that stays for good is the `worker-liveness` check — every 5 minutes over `worker_heartbeats`, `net.http_post` to `roybal-webhooks /alert` when the newest heartbeat is older than 10 minutes, its service-secret header read from Vault the same way (§2.5; ADR-06). The Vault entries are created once per environment by the owner and documented.
- **Seed vs data:** `supabase/seed.sql` holds only what a fresh environment needs to boot — the settings row `00000000-…-01`, `app_settings.min_field_build`, `phase_templates`/`required_forms`/`field_requirements`, `model_routes` and the `model_prices` keys (the per-MTok columns are filled from the provider price sheet at deploy time, never typed into the seed), and the six P1 `agents` rows — `agent:billing`, `agent:projection`, `agent:outbox`, `agent:brief`, `agent:phone`, `agent:web` (later lanes' principals are added by the migration of the phase whose lane first runs under them, §3.7). The 2,959 `price_list` rows (3,021 lines in `110_price_list_data.sql`) are data, not seed: out of the baseline, out of CI, loaded once per environment by an operator, frozen behind `price_sources` and never grown.
- **Every migration asserts its own invariants where they matter:** `do $$ begin assert (select relrowsecurity from pg_class where relname='x'); end $$;`.
- **Replay is the test:** the `migrate` job runs `supabase db reset` on the baseline plus forward files, then `supabase test db` (pgTAP in `supabase/tests/`), the merge-parity corpus, and the `gen types` diff. Green replay is required to merge anything under `supabase/`.
- **Applying to production:** only `supabase db push` from CI on merge to `main`, after staging passed, with `--dry-run` output posted to the PR first. Never the SQL editor; never MCP `apply_migration` (MCP stays for read-only inspection).

**The baseline squash and history repair (do once, at P1, in this order).**

1. **Freeze.** Declare a one-day schema freeze [A-8.6]. Take a dashboard backup and record its id in the PR. Merge or confirm the effect of the missing "237" (`crm/clockin-crew-line`, commit `9b32807`); the five `admin_fix_cron_*` entries are live and will be captured by the dump.
2. **Dump the truth.** `supabase db dump --project-ref djpgvcvhvgrzgaziruze --schema public,storage -f baseline.sql` — policies, functions, triggers, grants, live enum values, buckets. Dump `cron.job` separately for reference and strip every cron definition from the baseline (they carry literal secrets and are re-registered by `cron_register.sql`). Generate `docs/architecture/baseline-inventory.json` (tables, policies, functions, triggers, buckets, enum values) by script for later diffing.
3. **Scrub.** Confirm the baseline contains no `price_list` rows, no `blob_history`, no `capture_events` rows (the table's DDL stays: it is the legacy ledger, read-only after P3 and folded into `captures` + `events` in P5), no key or secret literal (the `migration-hygiene` invariant runs on it). Keep the 26 email fences and the `using(true)` policies in the baseline — the fences die in the P1 permission migration that introduces `agents`, and the `using(true)` policies are revoked table by table behind their ops through P5 (§7.7), not in the squash. Era-0 tables stay as-is (honest) and move to schema `legacy` in P3.
4. **Archive.** `git mv supabase/migrations supabase/migrations_archive` (read-only history, kept). Create `supabase/migrations/0000_baseline.sql` from the scrubbed dump (the CLI records it under the timestamp version the file carries) and `supabase/seed.sql` per the seed rule.
5. **Repair production's history BEFORE any CLI points at prod.** `supabase migration repair --status reverted <every one of the 71 live versions>` [A-8.7], then `supabase migration repair --status applied <baseline version>`. Production's schema does not change; only `supabase_migrations.schema_migrations` does. Verify: `supabase migration list` shows the baseline applied on both sides and nothing pending; `supabase db push --dry-run` reports nothing to apply; `supabase db diff --linked` is empty. Until this step is done, no `db push` may be run against prod by anyone.
6. **Replay locally.** `supabase start && supabase db reset` must succeed. Run `supabase test db`. Regenerate `types.gen.ts`. Diff the replayed inventory JSON against the production inventory from step 2: zero differences other than row counts.
7. **Stand up staging.** A second Supabase project (not branching — branching replays the journal that cannot replay), seeded by `pg_dump` of schema plus scrubbed data, with duplicated function secrets and its own Vault entries [A-0.6]. `cron_register.sql` runs there too. Point CI's staging steps at it.
8. **Commit the procedure.** `docs/architecture/MIGRATIONS.md` holds this list, the Vault entry names, the "never edit applied files" rule, and the "never SQL editor / never MCP apply_migration" rule; the 22 "SQL editor" headers and the `__CRON_SECRET__` docs are deleted; `deno.lock` is regenerated from the new `deno.json`.

From that day, every schema change is one new timestamped file, and replay in CI is what makes "it works on prod" a fact instead of a memory.

**Compute and retention are part of the discipline.** Micro → Small is a P3 step with a trigger metric — `events + capture_events` over 1M rows, p95 `push_project` over 400 ms after projections land, or a third disk-IO alert, whichever first. `jobs_queue` is partition-free, indexed on `(status, run_after)`, and its done rows are purged **daily** — `job.project` rows at the next purge, every other kind after 14 days — because the arithmetic demands it: ~300 active jobs pushing on the 45 s cycle would be ≈500k projection enqueues/day if unbatched, and the per-job debounce to at most one per minute still allows ≈430k/day worst case (§2.2; ADR-06). `outbox` rows purge after 90 days; `capture_events` `emailPull` rows after 30 (3,500 of 4,504 live rows) until the table is folded into `captures` + `events` in P5; `events` and `ai_usage` are kept. `events` stays one heap to low millions of rows — which 100+ people reach within ~12 months (~15–20k events/day) — so month partitioning is a **P5 item**, not "later", and lands earlier at ~5M rows or with the compute upgrade, whichever first. PITR is enabled at P4 when documents and events become the legal record; PITR covers Postgres only, and the `documents` bucket is rebuildable from `model_json` + `template_version` under the pinned Chromium build, `pdf_sha256` verified on every re-render (§6.7).

**Ceilings.** Two environments and one deploy discipline hold to 100+ people; a second region or project adds per-environment config and Vault entries, nothing structural. Replay time grows with the forward-file count; at ~200 forward files a second squash (same procedure, no history repair needed because the CLI's ledger is already honest) keeps CI under five minutes.

### 8.7 Ceilings and options, in one place

| Guardrail | Holds to | What forces change | Option left to the owner |
|---|---|---|---|
| Biome + `tsc --checkJs` + source-grep invariants (P3.0) | 100+ people; any number of parallel agent sessions | a second implementation language or a second repo | none — a second language is refused |
| esbuild `build.mjs` with generated integer `BUILD`, `COMMIT` and precache manifest (P3.0) | any fleet size; 200 ms build | a second product line splits `packages/` | no-build import maps + drift tests (cheaper to operate, keeps lockstep and precache as human duties — the PR #181 failure) |
| One `ci.yml`, deploy gated on green, prod schema only by `db push` from CI | 100+; two environments | a second region/project → per-env config | replay on the runner's local stack (default, ~3 min) vs against staging (serial, no Docker) |
| Ops registry as the only write door; per-op SQL bodies generated; ports for everything above domain | 100+ people, hundreds of ops | nothing structural; a partner API adds a gateway, not a second registry | — |
| `role_permissions` as data + CI snapshot; `assigned_job_ids()` initplan for crew reads | several office staff, ~10 roles, per-person overrides; one lookup per statement at any headcount | per-customer isolation → `org_id` claim; SLAs → workflow layer | code-as-matrix (drift-free, every change a deploy; fine while the office is two people) |
| pg_cron SQL-only with the one `/alert` exception; `roybal-webhooks` limited to two ops | 100+ | a second liveness signal that must not depend on the worker → a second allowlisted route, never a second mechanism | — |
| Drift guards with `removeBy` | until the last copy is deleted (price tables P1, module copies P3.0, phone agent P7) | — | — |
| Merge-parity corpus in CI | until the last capture-owned section leaves the blob | more than ~5 devices editing one job, or an office feature that must edit a capture section → that section moves to rows | — |
| Baseline + forward-only + staging project | 100+; ~200 forward files before a second squash | second region/project | staging plan tier (Free suffices at this data size; Pro if PITR or larger compute is wanted) [A-0.6] |

### 8.8 Assumptions in this section

| Tag | Assumption | Consequence if wrong |
|---|---|---|
| [A-8.1] | Biome (one binary, formatter + linter) is acceptable as the single lint/format tool | swap for ESLint + Prettier; the rules transfer, the config file changes |
| [A-8.2] | `checkJs` with JSDoc types on `packages/*` is enough type safety without converting the apps to TypeScript | if not, the apps convert file by file at P3.0; the CI step is the same |
| [A-8.3] | GitHub Actions minutes on the current plan cover a PR run (tests + migration replay) of roughly 6–10 minutes | if not, the migration replay moves to a nightly job and the PR keeps the test gate |
| [A-8.4] | A second Supabase project for staging is acceptable at its plan cost | if not, replay runs against a local `supabase start` in CI and staging is skipped, losing the rehearsal for RLS and cron |
| [A-8.5] | The owner will paste `CLAUDE.md` and the PR template into the repo (this review is read-only and wrote neither) | without them the rules exist only in this document and no agent reads them |
| [A-8.6] | Claude Code sessions honour a committed `CLAUDE.md` reliably enough to make it the primary convention channel | if not, the invariant tests in CI remain the only enforcement, which is why every rule here has one |
| [A-8.7] | The file-header comment convention (100 % of source files today) is worth keeping alongside `CLAUDE.md` | if it lapses, the per-file "why" is lost and only the global rules remain |
